         │    • Evaluates AuthorizationPolicy (is caller allowed?)                │
         │    • Records: request count, latency, HTTP status code                 │
         │    • Emits: inbound trace span (joins outbound trace)                  │
         │    • Forwards to your Django container on localhost                    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    2.6  Istio Key Terms and Vocabulary Glossary
         - Service Mesh: the network of proxies + control plane
         - Sidecar: the Envoy proxy container injected alongside your app
         - Data Plane: the collection of all Envoy sidecar proxies
         - Control Plane (istiod): the central component that configures proxies
         - VirtualService: Istio CRD for traffic routing rules
         - DestinationRule: Istio CRD for traffic policies (mTLS, load balancing)
         - Gateway: Istio CRD for ingress/egress to the mesh
         - ServiceEntry: Istio CRD to register external services in the mesh
         - PeerAuthentication: Istio CRD for mTLS mode per namespace/workload
         - AuthorizationPolicy: Istio CRD for access control (who can call what)
         - Workload: a Kubernetes deployment/pod managed by Istio
         - mTLS: Mutual TLS — both sides authenticate each other with certificates
         - xDS API: the protocol istiod uses to configure Envoy proxies
         [GLOSSARY TABLE: Term | What it does | anihpj example | YAML resource name]

         | Term               | What it is           | anihpj example                      |
         | ──────────────────-│──────────────────────│─────────────────────────────────── │
         | VirtualService     | Traffic routing rules│ Send 10% traffic to jobpost-api:v2 │
         | DestinationRule    | Traffic policies     │ Round-robin LB for user-service     │
         | Gateway            | Mesh ingress         │ Accept *.anihpj.com HTTPS traffic   │
         | ServiceEntry       | Register external svc│ Allow calls to external-payments-api│
         | PeerAuthentication | mTLS mode            │ Enforce mTLS in anihpj namespace    │
         | AuthorizationPolicy| Access control       │ Only jobpost-api → user-service     │
         | Sidecar (resource) | Proxy scope config   │ Limit what services jobpost sees    │

         [ISTIO CRD RELATIONSHIP MAP — how the resources link together]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                     │
         │           TRAFFIC IN                                                │
         │   Internet ──▶ [ Gateway ] ──▶ matches host + port                 │
         │                     │                                               │
         │                     ▼                                               │
         │             [ VirtualService ] ──▶ routing rules:                  │
         │               • match URI /jobs/*                                   │
         │               • weight: 90% → v1,  10% → v2                       │
         │               • timeout: 10s / retries: 3                          │
         │                     │                                               │
         │                     ▼                                               │
         │            [ DestinationRule ] ──▶ policies per subset:            │
         │               • subset: v1 → labels: version: v1                  │
         │               • subset: v2 → labels: version: v2                  │
         │               • loadBalancer: ROUND_ROBIN                          │
         │               • circuitBreaker: consecutiveErrors: 5               │
         │                     │                                               │
         │                     ▼                                               │
         │          [ Pods: jobpost-api:v1 ]  and  [ Pods: jobpost-api:v2 ]   │
         │                                                                     │
         │  SECURITY LAYER (applied alongside traffic rules):                  │
         │    [ PeerAuthentication ] ──▶ mTLS mode for namespace/workload      │
         │    [ AuthorizationPolicy ] ──▶ who can call what                    │
         │    [ RequestAuthentication ] ──▶ JWT token validation               │
         │                                                                     │
         │  EXTERNAL SERVICES:                                                 │
         │    [ ServiceEntry ] ──▶ registers external APIs in the mesh        │
         │      e.g. payments.stripe.com → treated as mesh service            │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    2.7  Before vs After Istio — A Day in the Life of anihpj
         - DEFINITION: "before vs after" is the operational delta Istio
           creates — the same anihpj team and the same 7 services, but every
           cross-cutting network concern moves out of Django code and into
           mesh YAML that one engineer can own
         - Why it matters: this is the business case you give your team
           lead — Istio is not "one more feature", it is REMOVED TOIL:
           fewer custom libraries, fewer 2am log-grepping sessions
         - How it works: the sidecars absorb retries, mTLS, metrics,
           routing and access control, so a change that used to touch seven
           codebases becomes a single kubectl apply
         - The "zero code change" promise, precisely: views.py, models.py,
           urls.py, the Dockerfile and the CI/CD pipeline stay untouched —
           the only thing that grows is the folder of Istio CRD manifests

         [BEFORE vs AFTER ISTIO — SAME TASK, TWO VERY DIFFERENT DAYS]
         ┌──────────────────┬───────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
         │  DAILY TASK      │  🔴 BEFORE ISTIO           │  🟢 AFTER ISTIO            │
         ├──────────────────┼───────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
         │  Deploy v2       │  big-bang release — pray  │  weight: 5 canary —       │
         │                  │  nothing breaks tonight   │  rollback = one YAML edit │
         │  Debug latency   │  grep logs across 7 pods  │  Jaeger: every hop timed  │
         │                  │  "where is it slow?"      │  "DB query = 1.1s!"       │
         │  Secure traffic  │  hand-rolled TLS in each  │  one PeerAuthentication   │
         │                  │  Django service           │  = mesh-wide mTLS         │
         │  Retry logic     │  7 teams, 7 different     │  retries: attempts: 3 in  │
         │                  │  implementations          │  one VirtualService       │
         │  Circuit breaker │  none — one slow DB call  │  outlierDetection in the  │
         │                  │  takes down everything    │  DestinationRule          │
         │  Metrics         │  Prometheus client coded  │  Envoy auto-emits; scrape │
         │                  │  into every service       │  istio-agent :15020       │
         │  Rate limiting   │  custom Django middleware │  EnvoyFilter at the proxy │
         │                  │  — fragile, per-service   │  — language-agnostic      │
         └──────────────────┴───────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘

         [A TUESDAY ON-CALL — skills-service degrades to 8.1% errors / 1240ms]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  BEFORE ISTIO:                                                         │
         │   09:00  pager fires — skills-service error rate hits 8.1%             │
         │   09:10  ssh + grep through 7 pods of logs, no correlation IDs         │
         │   10:40  found it — search-service hammers skills-service in a loop    │
         │   11:30  hotfix the retry loop, redeploy, write up the incident        │
         │   COST:  ~2.5 hours, 3 engineers, users saw errors the whole time      │
         │                                                                        │
         │  AFTER ISTIO:                                                          │
         │   09:00  same spike — the Kiali edge search→skills turns red           │
         │   09:02  Jaeger trace shows the retry storm from search-service        │
         │   09:05  apply outlierDetection (circuit breaker) → cascade stops      │
         │   09:15  skills-service recovers — most users never noticed            │
         │   COST:  15 minutes, 1 engineer, blast radius contained by the mesh    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         KEY: same team, same services — the mesh moves the fix from code to YAML

    2.8  Istio's 9 Capabilities — What Each One Does For You
         - DEFINITION: Istio's whole feature set condenses into 9 named
           capabilities — memorize them and you can translate any plain-
           English requirement ("encrypt traffic", "canary this release")
           into the right CRD instantly
         - Why it matters: ICA questions describe a capability in words and
           ask you to pick or write the matching resource — this list is
           the translation table
         - Each entry pairs the capability with the CRD that delivers it
           and a one-line DEMO you can run against anihpj in minutes

         [THE 9 CAPABILITIES — EACH WITH A 60-SECOND anihpj DEMO]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. AUTO mTLS                        CRD: PeerAuthentication           │
         │     encrypts every call; SPIFFE certs auto-rotate every 24h            │
         │     DEMO: open Kiali → padlock icons on every anihpj edge              │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  2. TRAFFIC ROUTING                  CRD: VirtualService               │
         │     route by URI, header or weight — overrides K8s round-robin         │
         │     DEMO: apply 90/10 weights → watch Kiali split traffic live         │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  3. RETRIES                          CRD: VirtualService (retries:)    │
         │     auto-retry transient failures, with a per-try timeout              │
         │     DEMO: kill a user-service pod → Grafana shows retries, no 5xx      │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  4. TIMEOUTS                         CRD: VirtualService (timeout:)    │
         │     hard cap on waiting — Envoy answers 504 when it fires              │
         │     DEMO: inject a 10s delay → callers get 504 at exactly 5s           │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  5. CIRCUIT BREAKING                 CRD: DestinationRule              │
         │     ejects failing hosts (outlierDetection) — no cascades              │
         │     DEMO: overload skills-service → watch the circuit open in Kiali    │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  6. FAULT INJECTION                  CRD: VirtualService (fault:)      │
         │     inject delays/aborts on purpose to prove resilience works          │
         │     DEMO: delay 30% of user-service calls by 7s → verify timeouts      │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  7. METRICS (RED)                    built-in + Telemetry CRD          │
         │     rate, errors, duration auto-emitted for every service              │
         │     DEMO: Grafana → jobpost-api at 847 req/s, 2.1% err, p99 234ms      │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  8. DISTRIBUTED TRACING              built-in + Telemetry CRD          │
         │     stitches spans into one end-to-end timeline (B3 headers)           │
         │     DEMO: Jaeger → one GET /jobs/ trace across 5 anihpj hops           │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  9. ACCESS CONTROL                   CRD: AuthorizationPolicy          │
         │     L7 allow/deny by identity, method, path or JWT claims              │
         │     DEMO: deny-all + allow jobpost-api → skills-service curl = 403     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - Memory hook: encrypt = PeerAuthentication; route, retry, timeout
           and fault = VirtualService; load-balancing and circuit-breaking =
           DestinationRule; ingress = Gateway; external = ServiceEntry;
           allow/deny = AuthorizationPolicy

    2.9  Deep Dive — How the Mutating Admission Webhook Injects Sidecars
         - DEFINITION: a Mutating Admission Webhook is a Kubernetes API-
           server hook that may REWRITE an object after kubectl apply but
           before it is stored in etcd — Istio registers one named
           istio-sidecar-injector
         - Why it matters: injection is not magic and not a controller that
           watches pods — it happens exactly ONCE, at Pod CREATE time;
           that is why labeling a namespace never retrofits running pods
         - How it works: the API server POSTs an AdmissionReview to
           istiod's webhook endpoint; the reply is a JSON patch that adds
           the istio-init and istio-proxy containers to the Pod spec
         - → consequence: after enabling injection, delete the pods so the
           Deployment recreates them and the webhook finally sees them

         [SIDECAR INJECTION — THE 5-STEP WEBHOOK FLOW]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ① kubectl apply -f jobpost-api.yaml                                   │
         │     → API server receives the Pod CREATE request (not yet in etcd)     │
         │  ② API server calls Istio's registered MutatingWebhook:                │
         │     POST https://istiod.istio-system.svc:443/inject (AdmissionReview)  │
         │  ③ Webhook decides — inject only if BOTH are true:                     │
         │     (a) namespace anihpj carries label istio-injection=enabled         │
         │     (b) pod does NOT carry sidecar.istio.io/inject: "false"            │
         │  ④ Webhook returns a JSON patch that ADDS to the Pod spec:             │
         │     + initContainer istio-init  (writes the iptables redirect rules)   │
         │     + container istio-proxy     (the Envoy sidecar)                    │
         │     + volumes for certificates and the Envoy bootstrap config          │
         │  ⑤ API server persists the MODIFIED Pod → scheduler places it →        │
         │     kubelet starts BOTH containers → kubectl shows READY 2/2           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [⚠️ PITFALL — POD SETTING OVERRIDES NAMESPACE LABEL]
         ┌───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬────────────┐
         │  NAMESPACE LABEL          │  POD-LEVEL SETTING           │  SIDECAR?  │
         ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │  istio-injection=enabled  │  (none)                      │  ✅ YES     │
         │  istio-injection=enabled  │  sidecar.istio.io/inject:    │  ❌ NO      │
         │                           │  "false"  ← pod setting WINS │            │
         │  (no label)               │  label sidecar.istio.io/     │  ✅ YES     │
         │                           │  inject=true (pod opt-in)    │            │
         │  (no label)               │  (none)                      │  ❌ NO      │
         └───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┴────────────┘
         RULE: the pod-level setting ALWAYS beats the namespace label
         - modern opt-in/out uses the POD LABEL sidecar.istio.io/inject
           ("true"/"false"); the older annotation is still honored — and it
           still overrides the namespace label

         [WEBHOOK HEALTH CHECK — THE THREE COMMANDS TO RUN]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  kubectl get mutatingwebhookconfiguration istio-sidecar-injector       │
         │    → must exist with WEBHOOKS: 1 (deleted = NO pod gets a sidecar)     │
         │  kubectl get ns anihpj --show-labels | grep istio-injection            │
         │    → istio-injection=enabled must appear on the namespace              │
         │  kubectl delete pods -l app=jobpost-api -n anihpj                      │
         │    → recreate pods so the webhook can inject (CREATE-time only!)       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    2.10  Deep Dive — Istio vs Cilium: Sidecar Proxy vs eBPF
         - DEFINITION: Cilium is a CNI + service mesh built on eBPF —
           sandboxed programs that run INSIDE the Linux kernel, which means
           there is no sidecar container at all
         - Istio's model: a userspace Envoy proxy per pod, traffic steered
           into it by iptables; Cilium's model: kernel hooks on sockets and
           network devices — packets never detour through a proxy process
         - Why it matters: architects will ask "why sidecars at all?" —
           the answer is L7 depth: Envoy fully decodes HTTP/2, gRPC and JWT
           claims in userspace, which kernel eBPF cannot match today
         - The ICA exam is Istio-only, but the trade-off vocabulary
           (userspace vs kernel, per-pod overhead vs near-zero) appears in
           conceptual questions

         [ISTIO vs CILIUM — ARCHITECTURE COMPARISON]
         ┌──────────────┬────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
         │  DIMENSION   │  ISTIO — SIDECAR PROXY     │  CILIUM — eBPF KERNEL      │
         ├──────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │  Data plane  │  Envoy proxy per pod       │  eBPF programs inside      │
         │              │  (userspace, C++)          │  the Linux kernel          │
         │  Control     │  istiod (xDS server)       │  cilium-agent + operator   │
         │  plane       │                            │                            │
         │  Injection   │  mutating webhook adds     │  none — there is no        │
         │              │  the sidecar container     │  sidecar to inject         │
         │  Intercept   │  iptables REDIRECT/TPROXY  │  eBPF socket/TC/XDP hooks  │
         │  Encryption  │  mTLS with SPIFFE X.509    │  IPSec or WireGuard        │
         │              │  certs (application layer) │  (network layer)           │
         │  Identity    │  SPIFFE ID per K8s         │  numeric identity per      │
         │              │  ServiceAccount            │  pod label set             │
         │  L7 routing  │  full: path, header,       │  limited: Gateway API,     │
         │              │  weight, method, JWT       │  HTTP header matching      │
         │  CNI role    │  needs a separate CNI      │  IS the CNI — also         │
         │              │  (Calico, Flannel, ...)    │  replaces kube-proxy       │
         │  Overhead    │  ~100-128Mi RAM + CPU      │  near zero (kernel         │
         │              │  per sidecar               │  memory only)              │
         │  Kernel      │  any modern Linux          │  Linux 5.10+ with eBPF     │
         └──────────────┴────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

         [WHEN TO CHOOSE WHICH — DECISION GUIDE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  CHOOSE ISTIO WHEN:                                                    │
         │   ✅ you need rich L7 traffic management — canary, retries,             │
         │      circuit breaking, fault injection, per-route timeouts             │
         │   ✅ compliance wants SPIFFE mTLS identity + AuthorizationPolicy        │
         │   ✅ you want the full observability stack (Kiali/Grafana/Jaeger)       │
         │   ✅ anihpj-style microservices with per-service traffic policy         │
         │                                                                        │
         │  CHOOSE CILIUM WHEN:                                                   │
         │   ✅ performance-critical or edge workloads (near-zero overhead)        │
         │   ✅ you already run Cilium as your CNI — mesh is a flag away           │
         │   ✅ you want CNI + NetworkPolicy + mesh in one binary                  │
         │   ✅ kernels are 5.10+ everywhere and L7 needs are simple               │
         │                                                                        │
         │  WHY ENVOY STAYS IN USERSPACE: deep protocol decoding (HTTP/2          │
         │  streams, gRPC methods, JWT claims, header mutation) is not            │
         │  practical inside kernel eBPF today — that L7 depth is exactly         │
         │  what Istio sells                                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    2.11  Deep Dive — xDS: How istiod Programs Every Envoy
         - DEFINITION: xDS ("anything Discovery Service") is the family of
           gRPC streaming APIs Envoy uses to receive configuration; istiod
           is the xDS server and every sidecar is a client
         - Why it matters: every Istio CRD you kubectl apply ends its life
           as an xDS push — when "my VirtualService does nothing", you are
           debugging this pipeline
         - How it works: istiod watches CRDs plus Services/Endpoints via
           K8s informers → computes a per-proxy view of the mesh → streams
           it over ONE gRPC connection (ADS) on istiod port 15012
         - Envoy applies pushes HOT — no restart, no dropped connections;
           a change propagates mesh-wide in ~1-3 seconds (eventual
           consistency)

         [THE xDS FAMILY — WHAT EACH DISCOVERY SERVICE CONFIGURES]
         ┌───────────────────┬────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
         │  xDS SERVICE      │  WHAT IT CONFIGURES        │  DRIVEN BY (SOURCE)     │
         ├───────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │  LDS  Listener    │  ports Envoy listens on    │  Gateway, Sidecar       │
         │  RDS  Route       │  HTTP routing tables       │  VirtualService         │
         │  CDS  Cluster     │  backend service pools     │  DestinationRule        │
         │  EDS  Endpoint    │  real pod IPs per cluster  │  K8s Endpoints          │
         │  SDS  Secret      │  TLS certs + trust bundle  │  istiod CA (SPIFFE)     │
         │  ADS  Aggregated  │  all of the above in ONE   │  istiod's default mode  │
         │                   │  ordered gRPC stream       │                         │
         └───────────────────┴────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘

         [xDS CONNECTION LIFECYCLE — FROM BOOTSTRAP TO HOT RELOAD]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  PHASE 1 — BOOTSTRAP                                                   │
         │    Envoy starts → reads /etc/istio/proxy/envoy-rev.json                │
         │    → learns istiod's address (istiod.istio-system.svc:15012),          │
         │      its own node metadata, and where to fetch certs (SDS)             │
         │    → opens ONE mTLS-protected gRPC stream to istiod                    │
         │                                                                        │
         │  PHASE 2 — INITIAL SYNC                                                │
         │    Envoy requests LDS → RDS → CDS → EDS → SDS in order;                │
         │    istiod answers with this pod's full scoped configuration            │
         │    → Envoy applies everything and starts proxying (~1-3s)              │
         │                                                                        │
         │  PHASE 3 — HOT RELOAD (steady state)                                   │
         │    you apply a VirtualService → istiod recomputes → pushes the         │
         │    delta → Envoy swaps routes in milliseconds — no restart,            │
         │    no dropped connections                                              │
         │                                                                        │
         │  PHASE 4 — HEARTBEAT + RECONNECT                                       │
         │    Envoy ACKs/NACKs every push; if the connection drops it keeps       │
         │    the LAST KNOWN GOOD config and retries with backoff                 │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ENVOY CLUSTER NAMES — outbound|PORT|SUBSET|HOST]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  FORMAT:  direction|port|subset|hostname                               │
         │                                                                        │
         │  outbound|8000||jobpost-api.anihpj.svc.cluster.local                   │
         │    → all jobpost-api pods on port 8000 (no subset)                     │
         │  outbound|8000|v2|jobpost-api.anihpj.svc.cluster.local                 │
         │    → ONLY the v2 subset — the canary routing target                    │
         │  outbound|5432||postgres.anihpj.svc.cluster.local                      │
         │    → the PostgreSQL database service on 5432                           │
         │  outbound|443||api.stripe.com                                          │
         │    → an external API registered via ServiceEntry                       │
         │  inbound|8000||                                                        │
         │    → traffic entering THIS pod on port 8000                            │
         │                                                                        │
         │  The name is the GLUE: a VirtualService "subset: v2" resolves to       │
         │  the cluster containing |v2| — which only exists if a                  │
         │  DestinationRule defines that subset. Name mismatch = broken routes.   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ISTIO CRD → xDS → ENVOY OBJECT — TRANSLATION MAP]
         ┌─────────────────────┬──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ISTIO CRD          │  xDS     │  ENVOY OBJECT GENERATED               │
         ├─────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Gateway            │  LDS     │  Listener (open ports, TLS mode)      │
         │  VirtualService     │  RDS     │  RouteConfiguration (match → route)   │
         │  DestinationRule    │  CDS     │  Cluster (LB, outlier, connPool, TLS) │
         │  ServiceEntry       │  CDS+EDS │  Cluster + Endpoints for external svc │
         │  PeerAuthentication │  LDS+SDS │  TLS transport socket (mTLS mode)     │
         │  AuthorizationPolicy│  LDS     │  HTTP filter: envoy.filters.http.rbac │
         │  Sidecar            │  LDS+CDS │  scoped listeners + clusters          │
         │  EnvoyFilter        │  any     │  raw config patch (last resort)       │
         └─────────────────────┴──────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

         [WHAT HAPPENS WHEN ISTIOD DIES — THE DATA PLANE SURVIVES]
         ┌─────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  DURING THE OUTAGE      │  EFFECT                                      │
         ├─────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Existing traffic       │  ✅ keeps flowing — every Envoy runs on       │
         │                         │     its cached last-known-good config        │
         │  New pods               │  ❌ stuck — no webhook injection, no          │
         │                         │     certificates, no xDS bootstrap           │
         │  kubectl apply new CRDs │  ⚠️ stored in etcd but NOT pushed — no        │
         │                         │     effect until istiod returns              │
         │  istioctl proxy-status  │  🟡 proxies drift from SYNCED to STALE        │
         │  Cert rotation (24h)    │  ⚠️ paused — an outage longer than the        │
         │                         │     cert TTL WILL break mTLS                 │
         │  Recovery               │  ✅ Envoys reconnect with backoff and         │
         │                         │     resync automatically (eventual           │
         │                         │     consistency — no restart needed)         │
         └─────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - production hardening: run istiod with 3 replicas plus a
           PodDisruptionBudget (min-available: 2) so pod creation and cert
           rotation never depend on a single control-plane pod

         [DEBUGGING THE xDS PIPELINE — FIVE COMMANDS TO KNOW]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  istioctl proxy-status                                                 │
         │    → per-proxy sync state: SYNCED / STALE / NOT SENT                   │
         │  istioctl proxy-config listeners deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj          │
         │    → are 15001 (outbound) and 15006 (inbound) bound?                   │
         │  istioctl proxy-config routes deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj             │
         │    → did the VirtualService become a route? check the weights          │
         │  istioctl proxy-config clusters deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj           │
         │    → does outbound|8000|v2|jobpost-api... exist? (DR applied?)         │
         │  istioctl proxy-config endpoints deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj          │
         │    → do subsets resolve to real pod IPs? empty = silent 503s           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 2: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After this chapter, in your daily work you will:
      • Know exactly what Istio's 10+ features are and which solves which problem
      • Understand the difference between K8s (manages containers) and
        Istio (manages communication between containers)
      • Be able to trace a request through Envoy sidecars step by step
      • Know the Istio CRDs by name and purpose (VirtualService,
        DestinationRule, Gateway, etc.)
      • Understand that Envoy is the "engine" and istiod is the "brain"
      • Know that sidecar injection is AUTOMATIC — your Dockerfile never changes
      • Have the vocabulary to discuss service mesh with your team:
        sidecar, data plane, control plane, xDS, mTLS, SPIFFE, CRD

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 2: ICA EXAM TIPS & PRACTICE QUESTIONS
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS:
      • "Encrypt service-to-service traffic" = PeerAuthentication STRICT
      • "Route traffic based on URL path" = VirtualService with http.match.uri
      • "Define mTLS and load balancing policies" = DestinationRule
      • "Accept external HTTPS traffic" = Gateway
      • "Enable sidecar injection" = kubectl label namespace istio-injection=enabled
      • "Sidecar never appeared" = webhook + namespace label + recreate pods
      • "External API blocked under REGISTRY_ONLY" = ServiceEntry
      • "Did my config reach Envoy?" = istioctl proxy-status + proxy-config

    Q1: What command enables automatic Istio sidecar injection for the anihpj namespace?
    A1: kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled

    Q2: Which Istio CRD would you use to split traffic 90/10 between two versions?
    A2: VirtualService with the weight field on route destinations.

    Q3: What is the primary role of the istio-ingressgateway?
    A3: It is the entry point for external traffic INTO the mesh. It is an Envoy
        proxy that matches Gateway CRD rules for host, port, and TLS.

    Q4: How does Envoy get configured by istiod?
    A4: Via the xDS API (gRPC streaming). istiod watches CRD changes, converts
        them to Envoy configuration, and pushes to all sidecar proxies in ~1-3s.

    Q5: What label must every Deployment pod have for Istio canary routing to work?
    A5: version: v1 (or v2). DestinationRule subsets match pods by these labels.

    Q6: During a cleanup someone deletes the istio-sidecar-injector
        MutatingWebhookConfiguration. New anihpj deployments now start 1/1 READY
        with no istio-proxy container. Diagnose and recover.
    A6: The webhook is the ONLY component that adds sidecars, and it acts at Pod
        CREATE time — recreate it, then recycle the pods:

        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  kubectl get mutatingwebhookconfiguration istio-sidecar-injector     │
        │    → "not found" confirms the webhook is gone                        │
        │  istioctl install --set profile=demo -y                              │
        │    → reinstall recreates the webhook (its config ships with istiod)  │
        │  kubectl delete pods -l app=jobpost-api -n anihpj                    │
        │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj          → READY 2/2 again               │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: injection happens only when a Pod CREATE passes through the webhook
        — no webhook means no sidecars, no matter what the namespace label says.

    Q7: Production flips outboundTrafficPolicy.mode to REGISTRY_ONLY and
        jobpost-api's payment calls to api.stripe.com start failing with 502.
        Fix it and give every Stripe call a 5-second timeout.
    A7: Register the host with a ServiceEntry, then attach a VirtualService:

        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                      │
        │  kind: ServiceEntry                                      │
        │  metadata:                                               │
        │    name: stripe-payments                                 │
        │    namespace: anihpj                                     │
        │  spec:                                                   │
        │    hosts:                                                │
        │    - api.stripe.com        ← host to unblock             │
        │    ports:                                                │
        │    - number: 443                                         │
        │      name: https                                         │
        │      protocol: HTTPS                                     │
        │    location: MESH_EXTERNAL ← outside the cluster         │
        │    resolution: DNS         ← resolve via DNS, not EDS    │
        │  ---                                                     │
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                      │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                    │
        │  metadata:                                               │
        │    name: stripe-timeout                                  │
        │    namespace: anihpj                                     │
        │  spec:                                                   │
        │    hosts:                                                │
        │    - api.stripe.com                                      │
        │    http:                                                 │
        │    - timeout: 5s           ← give up after 5s            │
        │      route:                                              │
        │      - destination:                                      │
        │          host: api.stripe.com                            │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: REGISTRY_ONLY drops any destination missing from the mesh registry;
        the ServiceEntry adds api.stripe.com, and once registered all Istio
        policies (timeout/retry/CB) can target it. For L7 policies to bite, let
        the sidecar originate TLS (app calls :80, DestinationRule upgrades to
        :443) — otherwise HTTPS is an opaque tunnel to Envoy.

    Q8: Inside the jobpost-api pod, Django calls http://user-service:8080/ and
        gets 503s even though user-service pods are Running. Walk the
        iptables/Envoy path and prove where the request dies.
    A8: istio-init's iptables rules redirect ALL app outbound traffic to Envoy's
        15001 listener; inbound traffic reaches the app via 15006. A 503 from
        the outbound Envoy usually means its cluster has no healthy endpoints:

        ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl proxy-config listeners deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj      │
        │    → confirm 15001 (outbound) and 15006 (inbound) are bound        │
        │  istioctl proxy-config clusters deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj       │
        │    → does outbound|8080||user-service.anihpj.svc... exist?         │
        │  istioctl proxy-config endpoints deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj      │
        │    → HEALTHY endpoints for user-service? an empty list = your 503  │
        └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: 15001/15006 are Envoy's capture ports (15020 serves the merged
        metrics Prometheus scrapes); if the endpoints table is empty, the
        Service selector or subset labels do not match the running pods.

    Q9: istiod crashes at 14:00 and stays down for an hour. What happens to
        (a) existing traffic, (b) new pods, (c) CRDs applied during the outage —
        and how do you harden the control plane afterwards?
    A9: (a) ✅ keeps flowing — every Envoy runs on cached last-known-good config;
        (b) ❌ stuck — no injection, no certificates, no xDS bootstrap;
        (c) ⚠️ stored in etcd but NOT pushed until istiod returns (proxies STALE).

        ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl proxy-status       → STALE during outage, SYNCED after  │
        │  kubectl scale deployment istiod -n istio-system --replicas=3     │
        │  kubectl create pdb istiod-pdb -n istio-system \                  │
        │      --selector=app=istiod --min-available=2                      │
        └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: control-plane failure never interrupts the data plane — that
        separation is a core ICA fact. HA = 3 istiod replicas plus a
        PodDisruptionBudget so at least 2 survive voluntary disruptions.

    Q10: Trace GET /jobs/ end-to-end through the mesh in 12 steps and state
         Envoy's per-hop overhead versus business-logic time.
    A10: ① istio-ingressgateway accepts — Gateway CRD matches host/port/TLS
         ② VirtualService routes — URI /jobs/*, weights 90% v1 / 10% v2
         ③ DestinationRule applies — LB pick + circuit-breaker check
         ④ iptables in the jobpost-api pod redirects inbound to Envoy :15006
         ⑤ inbound Envoy terminates mTLS (verifies the caller's certificate)
         ⑥ AuthorizationPolicy evaluated — is this caller allowed?
         ⑦ metrics + trace span recorded, forward to localhost:8000
         ⑧ Django handles /jobs/, queries PostgreSQL (psql) — 50-200ms
         ⑨ Django's call to user-service hits iptables → Envoy :15001
         ⑩ outbound Envoy applies retry/timeout, opens the mTLS tunnel
         ⑪ user-service's Envoy repeats ⑤-⑦ on its side
         ⑫ responses return the same path; spans close, metrics recorded
         Envoy adds ~3-5ms per hop against 50-200ms of business logic (≈2-5%)
         — the price of mTLS, retries, timeouts, metrics, tracing and authz.


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 3: ISTIO ARCHITECTURE — HOW IT ALL WORKS                            🟢
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Chapter 3: Istio Architecture Deep Dive
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    3.1  The Two Planes: Data Plane and Control Plane
         - Data Plane = all the Envoy sidecar proxies running alongside your Pods
         - Control Plane = istiod (one binary that manages everything)
         - Data plane: handles actual traffic (millions of requests per second)
         - Control plane: handles configuration (not in the hot path)
         [ARCHITECTURE DIAGRAM: Full Istio architecture with both planes labeled]

         [ISTIO ARCHITECTURE — MASTER DIAGRAM]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                       KUBERNETES CLUSTER                             │
         │                                                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │            🧠 CONTROL PLANE (istio-system namespace)          │   │
         │  │                                                              │   │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │   │
         │  │  │                    istiod                            │   │   │
         │  │  │  ┌─────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌─────────────────┐  │   │   │
         │  │  │  │  Pilot  │   │ Citadel  │   │     Galley      │  │   │   │
         │  │  │  │ (Traffic│   │  (Certs  │   │  (Config valid/ │  │   │   │
         │  │  │  │  config)│   │ + mTLS)  │   │   conversion)   │  │   │   │
         │  │  │  └─────────┘   └──────────┘   └─────────────────┘  │   │   │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │   │
         │  │                           │                                 │   │
         │  │                    xDS API (gRPC)                           │   │
         │  │                           │                                 │   │
         │  └───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                             │                                       │
         │  ┌───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │         🔷 DATA PLANE (Envoy sidecars in every Pod)        │   │
         │  │                                                              │   │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────┐               │   │
         │  │  │ Pod: jobpost-api  │   │ Pod: user-service │               │   │
         │  │  │ ┌────────┐┌─────┐│   │ ┌──────┐ ┌─────┐ │               │   │
         │  │  │ │ Django ││Envoy││   │ │Django│ │Envoy│ │               │   │
         │  │  │ │  :8000 ││proxy│◀──────▶:8000 │ │proxy│ │               │   │
         │  │  │ └────────┘└─────┘│   │ └──────┘ └─────┘ │               │   │
         │  │  └──────────────────┘   └──────────────────┘               │   │
         │  │                                                              │   │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────┐               │   │
         │  │  │ Pod: skills-svc   │   │ Pod: auth-service │               │   │
         │  │  │ ┌────────┐┌─────┐│   │ ┌──────┐ ┌─────┐ │               │   │
         │  │  │ │  App   ││Envoy││   │ │ App  │ │Envoy│ │               │   │
         │  │  │ │        ││proxy││   │ │      │ │proxy│ │               │   │
         │  │  │ └────────┘└─────┘│   │ └──────┘ └─────┘ │               │   │
         │  │  └──────────────────┘   └──────────────────┘               │   │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.2  istiod — The Control Plane (Deep Dive)
         - istiod is a single binary that replaced 3 separate components (v1.5+)
         - Old architecture: Pilot + Citadel + Galley (separate pods)
         - New architecture: all merged into one istiod pod
         - What istiod contains:
           a) Pilot: reads Istio CRDs (VirtualService, DestinationRule), converts
              them to Envoy xDS configuration, pushes to all sidecar proxies
           b) Citadel (now: Certificate Authority): issues and rotates mTLS
              certificates for every service in the mesh (SPIFFE/X.509 certs)
           c) Galley (now: Config Validation): validates Istio YAML before applying,
              watches Kubernetes API for changes
         - istiod runs in istio-system namespace
         [istiod INTERNALS DIAGRAM — what happens inside istiod]

         [istiod INTERNAL ARCHITECTURE — what lives inside the single binary]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                        istiod Pod                                 │
         │   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │   PILOT sub-component (Traffic Config Manager)           │    │
         │   │   • Watches Kubernetes API for:                          │    │
         │   │     - VirtualService changes → convert to Envoy RDS      │    │
         │   │     - DestinationRule changes → convert to Envoy CDS     │    │
         │   │     - Gateway changes → convert to Envoy LDS             │    │
         │   │     - Service/Endpoint changes → convert to Envoy EDS    │    │
         │   │   • Pushes config to Envoy proxies via gRPC/xDS stream   │    │
         │   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │   CITADEL sub-component (Certificate Authority)          │    │
         │   │   • Issues SPIFFE X.509 certificates to every workload   │    │
         │   │   • Certificate identity: spiffe://cluster.local/        │    │
         │   │     ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api-service-account            │    │
         │   │   • Rotates certs automatically (default: 24h)           │    │
         │   │   • Stored as Kubernetes Secrets (auto-mounted)          │    │
         │   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │   GALLEY sub-component (Config Validation + Injection)   │    │
         │   │   • Validates Istio YAML before it reaches Pilot         │    │
         │   │   • Runs the Mutating Admission Webhook for injection     │    │
         │   │   • Rejects bad config (prevents misconfiguration)       │    │
         │   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                                   │
         │   External interfaces:                                            │
         │   • Port 15010: xDS gRPC (plaintext, for mesh-internal only)     │
         │   • Port 15012: xDS gRPC (mTLS, for production)                  │
         │   • Port 15014: Control plane metrics (Prometheus scrape)         │
         │   • Port 443:   Webhook (sidecar injection requests)             │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ISTIOD EVOLUTION — old vs new architecture]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
         │  OLD (Istio < 1.5)               │  NEW (Istio 1.5+)                │
         ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Pod: pilot (100m/256Mi)         │  Pod: istiod (single binary)     │
         │  Pod: citadel (50m/128Mi)        │  All three merged into one       │
         │  Pod: galley (50m/128Mi)         │  Simpler: 1 deployment to manage │
         │ Pod: sidecar-injector (50m/128Mi)│  Easier upgrades and scaling     │
         │  Total: 4 pods to manage         │  Total: 1 pod (or 3 for HA)      │
         │  Complex communication between   │  In-process calls (faster)       │
         │  them over gRPC                  │  Less failure modes              │
         └──────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

    3.3  The Envoy Proxy — Deep Dive
         - Envoy is not just a proxy — it is a "programmable" proxy
         - istiod configures Envoy via the xDS API (Discovery Services):
           • LDS (Listener Discovery Service): which ports to listen on
           • RDS (Route Discovery Service): how to route requests
           • CDS (Cluster Discovery Service): which upstream services exist
           • EDS (Endpoint Discovery Service): which pod IPs are behind a service
         - Envoy's capabilities used by Istio:
           • HTTP request/response manipulation
           • Automatic metrics generation (RED metrics: Rate, Errors, Duration)
           • Distributed tracing header injection (x-b3-traceid, etc.)
           • mTLS termination and origination
           • Circuit breaking and outlier detection
           • Request retries with backoff
           • Fault injection (delays, aborts)
         [xDS PROTOCOL DIAGRAM: istiod → xDS → Envoy configuration flow]

         [HOW istiod CONFIGURES ENVOY — xDS flow]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                        │
         │  You apply YAML:                                       │
         │  kubectl apply -f virtual-service.yaml                 │
         │         │                                             │
         │         ▼                                             │
         │  Kubernetes API Server stores it in etcd              │
         │         │                                             │
         │         ▼                                             │
         │  istiod watches for CRD changes (via informers)       │
         │         │                                             │
         │         ▼                                             │
         │  istiod converts VirtualService → Envoy xDS config    │
         │         │                                             │
         │         ▼  (gRPC streaming, persistent connection)    │
         │  istiod PUSHES config to every affected Envoy proxy   │
         │         │                                             │
         │         ▼                                             │
         │  Envoy proxies update their routing tables in-memory  │
         │  ← No restart needed. Live config update! →           │
         │                                                        │
         │  Total time from kubectl apply to traffic routing:    │
         │  ~1-3 seconds 🚀                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     VISUAL: A SINGLE REQUEST — FULL JOURNEY THROUGH THE ISTIO MESH
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TRACE: User clicks "View Job #42" on anihpj website               │
         │                                                                     │
         │  STEP  TIME    COMPONENT              ACTION                       │
         │  ──── ─────── ────────────────────── ────────────────────────────  │
         │   ①    0ms     Browser                GET /jobs/42/                 │
         │                   │                                                │
         │   ②    2ms     Istio Gateway          TLS termination               │
         │                   │  Envoy matches Gateway CRD                     │
         │                   │  Routes to VirtualService                      │
         │                   ▼                                                │
         │   ③    4ms     jobpost-api Pod        Envoy inbound:               │
         │                   ┌─────────────────┐  • Verify mTLS (if internal) │
         │                   │ 🔷 Envoy Sidecar │  • Check AuthorizationPolicy │
         │                   │   (inbound)      │  • Start trace span          │
         │                   │   port 15006     │  • Record request count      │
         │                   └────────┬────────┘  • Forward to Django :8000   │
         │                            │                                       │
         │   ④    5ms     Django Container       views.get_job(id=42)          │
         │                   │  (port 8000)       • Django code runs           │
         │                   │                     • Calls: user-service,      │
         │                   │                       skills-service            │
         │                   ▼                                                │
         │   ⑤    8ms     Envoy Sidecar (out)    Django calls user-service:   │
         │                   ┌─────────────────┐  • iptables redirect → Envoy  │
         │                   │ 🔷 Envoy Sidecar │  • Envoy evaluates VS rules  │
         │                   │   (outbound)     │  • Selects pod via LB        │
         │                   │   port 15001     │  • Initiates mTLS handshake  │
         │                   └────────┬────────┘  • Adds x-b3-traceid header  │
         │                            │                                       │
         │                            │  🔒 mTLS encrypted tunnel             │
         │                            ▼                                       │
         │   ⑥   12ms    user-service Pod        Envoy inbound receives:       │
         │                   ┌─────────────────┐  • Terminates mTLS            │
         │                   │ 🔷 Envoy Sidecar │  • Verifies SPIFFE cert      │
         │                   │   (inbound)      │  • Records inbound span      │
         │                   └────────┬────────┘  • Forwards to Django :8000   │
         │                            │                                       │
         │   ⑦   15ms    Django Container       GET /api/users/42/             │
         │                   │                     • Returns author JSON       │
         │                   ▼                                                │
         │   ⑧   45ms    Response returns        • Envoy records: latency=33ms │
         │                   (back through both   • Status: 200 OK             │
         │                    Envoy proxies)       • Response size: 1.2KB      │
         │                                                                     │
         │   ⑨   50ms    Django (jobpost)        Now calls skills-service:     │
         │                   │                     • Same Envoy hop pattern     │
         │                   ▼                                                │
         │   ⑩   55ms    skills-service Pod      GET /api/skills/?ids=1,2,3   │
         │                   │                     • This service is slow ⚠️    │
         │                   │                     • DB query: 185ms!          │
         │                   ▼                                                │
         │   ⑪  245ms    Response returns         • Envoy records: latency=190ms│
         │                                                                     │
         │   ⑫  250ms    jobpost-api Pod          Django assembles response:   │
         │                   │                     • Merges user + skills data  │
         │                   ▼                                                │
         │   ⑬  255ms    Response → Browser       • Envoy records final span   │
         │                                         • Total: 255ms              │
         │                                         • Traces sent to Jaeger     │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════    │
         │  KEY TAKEAWAYS FROM THIS TRACE:                                     │
         │                                                                     │
         │  🟢 Gateway + jobpost-api: 4ms overhead (negligible)               │
         │  🟢 user-service call: 33ms total (healthy)                        │
         │  🔴 skills-service call: 190ms (75% of total time!) ← bottleneck   │
         │                                                                     │
         │  Without Istio tracing: you'd only know "page took 255ms"          │
         │  With Istio: you know EXACTLY that skills-service DB is slow       │
         │                                                                     │
         │  Envoy adds ~3-5ms per hop. Business logic + DB = 240ms.           │
         │  Verdict: ~4% overhead for full encryption + observability ✅       │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.4  Automatic Sidecar Injection — How It Works
         - How Istio injects Envoy without changing your Deployment YAML
         - Kubernetes Mutating Admission Webhook: Istio registers a webhook
         - When a Pod is created, Kubernetes calls istiod's webhook FIRST
         - istiod modifies the Pod spec on the fly — adds the istio-proxy container
         - Also adds an init container (istio-init) that sets up iptables rules
         - iptables rules: redirect ALL inbound/outbound traffic through Envoy
         - Enable injection for a namespace: kubectl label namespace anihpj
           istio-injection=enabled
         - Or per-deployment: add annotation sidecar.istio.io/inject: "true"
         [INJECTION FLOW DIAGRAM: Pod creation → webhook → sidecar added]

         [SIDECAR INJECTION FLOW]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                        │
         │  kubectl apply -f jobpost-deployment.yaml              │
         │                       │                               │
         │                       ▼                               │
         │         Kubernetes API Server                          │
         │                       │                               │
         │  "Before I create this Pod, call the Admission         │
         │   Webhooks registered in the cluster"                 │
         │                       │                               │
         │                       ▼                               │
         │  Istio Mutating Admission Webhook (istiod)             │
         │  "I see istio-injection=enabled on this namespace"     │
         │  "Let me modify the Pod spec..."                       │
         │                       │                               │
         │                       ▼                               │
         │  Modified Pod spec returned:                           │
         │  + initContainers: [istio-init]  ← sets iptables      │
         │  + containers: [istio-proxy]     ← Envoy sidecar      │
         │                       │                               │
         │                       ▼                               │
         │  Pod is created with sidecar already inside it        │
         │  Your original jobpost container: UNCHANGED ✅         │
         │                                                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.5  iptables — How Traffic Is Intercepted
         - iptables is a Linux firewall that redirects network packets
         - istio-init container runs as root to set iptables rules:
           • ALL outbound traffic → redirected to Envoy port 15001
           • ALL inbound traffic → redirected to Envoy port 15006
           • Envoy process itself is excluded (avoid infinite loop)
         - This is completely transparent to your Django app
         - Django thinks it's connecting directly to user-service:80
         - In reality: Django → iptables → Envoy → mTLS tunnel → remote Envoy → user-service
         [IPTABLES REDIRECT DIAGRAM showing the invisible interception]

         [IPTABLES PACKET JOURNEY — step by step through the network stack]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  OUTBOUND (jobpost-api calling user-service):                      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ① Django code:  requests.get("http://user-service/api/users/")   │
         │  ② OS resolves:  user-service → ClusterIP 10.96.45.23:80          │
         │  ③ Packet enters: Linux network stack, destination 10.96.45.23:80 │
         │  ④ iptables rule: "OUTPUT chain → redirect to 127.0.0.1:15001"   │
         │  ⑤ Envoy receives packet on port 15001 (outbound listener)        │
         │  ⑥ Envoy looks up: "who is 10.96.45.23?" → it is user-service    │
         │  ⑦ Envoy applies:  routing rules, timeout, retry, circuit breaker │
         │  ⑧ Envoy initiates mTLS: presents jobpost-api certificate         │
         │  ⑨ Packet sent to: user-service Pod IP:15006 (not :80!)           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  INBOUND (user-service receiving):                                 │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ① Packet arrives at user-service Pod:15006                       │
         │  ② iptables rule: "PREROUTING → redirect to 127.0.0.1:15006"     │
         │  ③ Envoy receives on port 15006 (inbound listener)                │
         │  ④ Envoy verifies: mTLS certificate from jobpost-api Envoy       │
         │  ⑤ Envoy checks:   AuthorizationPolicy (is jobpost-api allowed?)  │
         │  ⑥ Envoy records:  request metric (latency timer starts)          │
         │  ⑦ Envoy forwards: to 127.0.0.1:8000 (Django on localhost)        │
         │  ⑧ Django sees:    normal HTTP request, no TLS needed!            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  KEY INSIGHT: Django never sees TLS. Envoy handles it all.        │
         │  Django talks plain HTTP on localhost. Envoy handles encryption    │
         │  between pods. Your code changes: ZERO. ✅                         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [IPTABLES RULES SET BY istio-init — the actual commands]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Rules added by istio-init container (simplified):               │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Redirect ALL outbound traffic to Envoy port 15001               │
         │  iptables -t nat -A OUTPUT -p tcp \                                │
         │    ! --dport 15001 \                   ← skip Envoy's own traffic  │
         │    -m owner ! --uid-owner 1337 \       ← skip Envoy process (uid) │
         │    -j REDIRECT --to-ports 15001                                    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Redirect ALL inbound traffic to Envoy port 15006                │
         │  iptables -t nat -A PREROUTING -p tcp \                            │
         │    -j REDIRECT --to-ports 15006                                    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Exception: do NOT redirect traffic from Envoy itself            │
         │  # (Envoy runs as UID 1337 — exempt from redirection)              │
         │  iptables -t nat -A OUTPUT -m owner \                              │
         │    --uid-owner 1337 -j RETURN                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.6  Istio Components in the Cluster (What Pods Run)
         - istio-system namespace contains:
           • istiod — the control plane (1 deployment, 1-3 replicas for HA)
           • istio-ingressgateway — handles inbound traffic (LoadBalancer service)
           • istio-egressgateway — handles outbound traffic (optional)
           • Kiali — service mesh visualization dashboard
           • Prometheus — metrics scraping (can use existing)
           • Grafana — dashboards (can use existing)
           • Jaeger/Zipkin — distributed tracing
         [TABLE: Component | Namespace | Purpose | Resource usage]

         | Component             | Namespace    | Purpose                                |
         | ──────────────────────│──────────────│────────────────────────────────────── │
         | istiod                | istio-system | Control plane — configures all proxies  |
         | istio-ingressgateway  | istio-system | Ingress to the mesh (LoadBalancer)      |
         | istio-egressgateway   | istio-system | Controlled egress from mesh (optional)  |
         | kiali                 | istio-system | Mesh topology + traffic visualization   |
         | prometheus            | istio-system | Metrics collection from Envoy proxies   |
         | grafana               | istio-system | Metrics dashboards (pre-built for Istio)|
         | jaeger                | istio-system | Distributed tracing (trace spans)       |

         [ISTIO COMPONENTS — memory & CPU resource budget for anihpj]
         ┌──────────────────────────┬────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Component               │  Resources      │  Scales with...                  │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  istiod (1 replica)      │  500m / 2Gi    │  Number of services (CRDs)       │
         │  istiod (HA: 3 replicas) │  1.5c / 6Gi   │  3x for high availability        │
         │  istio-ingressgateway    │  100m / 128Mi  │  Traffic volume (scale out)      │
         │  istio-egressgateway     │  100m / 128Mi  │  External call volume            │
         │  Each Envoy sidecar      │  100m / 128Mi  │  Number of Pods in mesh          │
         │  Prometheus              │  500m / 2Gi    │  Metrics retention + cardinality │
         │  Grafana                 │  100m / 256Mi  │  Dashboard complexity            │
         │  Jaeger (all-in-one)     │  500m / 512Mi  │  Trace volume (dev only)         │
         │  Kiali                   │  200m / 256Mi  │  Service graph complexity        │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  anihpj total (7 svcs)   │  ~3c / 8Gi    │  7 sidecars + control plane      │
         └──────────────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

         [NETWORKING FLOW — how traffic enters the anihpj mesh]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │   Internet / Client                                                │
         │        │                                                          │
         │        ▼  DNS: api.anihpj.com → LoadBalancer IP                  │
         │   [Cloud LoadBalancer]  ← AWS ALB / GCP LB / minikube NodePort   │
         │        │                                                          │
         │        ▼  Port 443 (HTTPS terminated here or passed through)      │
         │   [istio-ingressgateway Pod]                                       │
         │        │  • Envoy proxy (no Django here — pure proxy)             │
         │        │  • Matches Gateway CRD rules                             │
         │        │  • Matches VirtualService CRD routes                     │
         │        ▼                                                          │
         │   [jobpost-api Pod]  ← Envoy sidecar intercepts first            │
         │        │  Envoy verifies mTLS (if internal caller)                │
         │        │  Django container receives plain HTTP on :8000            │
         │        ▼                                                          │
         │   [user-service Pod]  ← another mTLS hop                         │
         │   [skills-service Pod]                                             │
         │   [auth-service Pod]                                               │
         │        ↕                                                          │
         │   ALL hops: encrypted + observed + controlled ✅                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.7  Sidecar Debugging Checklist — When Things Go Wrong
         - DEFINITION: sidecar debugging = a fixed, ordered routine that tells
           you WHY a pod is not meshed, not synced, or not routing traffic
         - Why it matters: most "Istio is broken" tickets on anihpj are one of
           six repeat symptoms → memorize the symptom-to-first-command mapping
         - How it works: every check reads LIVE state — either from the
           Kubernetes API (labels, webhook, events) or from Envoy itself
           (istioctl proxy-config reads the proxy's in-memory tables)
         - Golden order: INJECTION → SYNC → CONFIG → TRAFFIC
           → never start by editing YAML blindly; observe first, change second
         - The modern toolbox (Istio 1.22+) — commit these five to memory:
           • istioctl proxy-status               → is every proxy SYNCED?
           • istioctl x describe pod <pod>       → one-pod mesh summary
             (shows matching VirtualServices, DestinationRules, mTLS mode)
           • istioctl proxy-config <section> <pod> → Envoy's live config
             (sections: listener / route / cluster / endpoint / secret)
           • kubectl logs <pod> -c istio-proxy   → Envoy runtime errors
           • istioctl analyze -n anihpj          → static config lint
         - ⚠️ "istioctl authn tls-check" was REMOVED years ago — never answer
           with it; use istioctl x describe pod / proxy-config secret instead

         [SIDECAR SYMPTOM CHECKLIST — the six repeat offenders in anihpj]
         ┌────┬────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
         │ □  │ Symptom (what you see)         │ First command to run       │
         ├────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ □1 │ Pod READY 1/1 — no sidecar     │ kubectl get ns anihpj      │
         │    │ container was ever injected    │   --show-labels            │
         ├────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ □2 │ Pod 2/2 but follows OLD routes │ istioctl proxy-status      │
         │    │ minutes after kubectl apply    │   → any STALE / NOT SENT?  │
         ├────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ □3 │ Calls to a service return 503  │ istioctl proxy-config      │
         │    │ "no healthy upstream"          │   endpoint deploy/<name>   │
         ├────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ □4 │ Plaintext caller gets TCP      │ istioctl x describe pod    │
         │    │ connection RESET (not 403!)    │   <pod> → mTLS STRICT?     │
         ├────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ □5 │ istio-proxy CrashLoopBackOff   │ kubectl logs <pod>         │
         │    │ or pod never leaves Init state │   -c istio-proxy -n anihpj │
         ├────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ □6 │ mTLS errors: unknown identity, │ istioctl proxy-config      │
         │    │ certificate expired            │   secret deploy/<name>     │
         └────┴────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

         [QUICK-DEBUG COMMAND TABLE — exact commands for deploy/jobpost-api]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. Is every proxy in the mesh synced with istiod?             │
         │  istioctl proxy-status                                           │
         │     → want: CDS/LDS/EDS/RDS all "SYNCED" for every workload      │
         │                                                                  │
         │  # 2. One-pod summary: routes, mTLS mode, matching VS/DR         │
         │  istioctl x describe pod jobpost-api-7d9f8b6c5-x2k4p -n anihpj   │
         │                                                                  │
         │  # 3. Envoy's live tables (LDS / RDS / CDS / EDS / certs)        │
         │  istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj     │
         │  istioctl proxy-config route    deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj     │
         │  istioctl proxy-config cluster  deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj     │
         │  istioctl proxy-config endpoint deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj     │
         │  istioctl proxy-config secret   deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj     │
         │                                                                  │
         │  # 4. Envoy runtime logs (rejected config, TLS handshake fails)  │
         │  kubectl logs deploy/jobpost-api -c istio-proxy -n anihpj        │
         │                                                                  │
         │  # 5. Static lint of all Istio config in the namespace           │
         │  istioctl analyze -n anihpj                                      │
         │     → want: "No validation issues found"                         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - REAL SCENARIO (anihpj, day 12): skills-service came back 1/1 after
           a node drain. Checklist □1 → namespace label was fine; □2 → the pod
           was missing from istioctl proxy-status entirely → it had been
           recreated WHILE the injection webhook was down during an istiod
           upgrade. Fix: kubectl delete pod → recreated 2/2, SYNCED in ~3s ✅

    3.8  Inside Envoy — Proxy Configuration Hierarchy
         - DEFINITION: Envoy stores its running config as four stacked tables —
           Listeners (LDS), Routes (RDS), Clusters (CDS), Endpoints (EDS)
         - Why it matters: every istioctl proxy-config command reads exactly
           ONE of these tables — the hierarchy tells you WHICH one to read
         - How a request walks the hierarchy (top → bottom):
           • LDS: "a packet hit 15001/15006 — which listener owns it?"
           • RDS: "which route matches this Host header + path?"
           • CDS: "route points at a cluster — does that cluster exist?"
           • EDS: "which healthy pod IPs are members of that cluster?"
         - Istio CRD → xDS mapping (memorize):
           → Gateway → LDS   → VirtualService → RDS
           → DestinationRule → CDS   → Service/Endpoints → EDS
         - DEBUG BOTTOM-UP: endpoints first (no healthy pods = nothing else
           matters), then cluster, then route, then listener last

         [ENVOY CONFIG HIERARCHY — the four xDS tables inside every sidecar]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  REQUEST ENTERS ENVOY                                            │
         │      │                                                           │
         │      ▼                                                           │
         │  ┌─ 1. LDS — LISTENERS ─────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │ "Which ports do I own?" → 15001, 15006, 15021, 15090     │    │
         │  │ Fed by: Gateway CRDs + mesh defaults                     │    │
         │  │ Read: istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/jobpost-api  │    │
         │  └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                               ▼                                  │
         │  ┌─ 2. RDS — ROUTES ────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │ "Which rule matches Host + path?" (VirtualService)       │    │
         │  │ Fed by: VirtualService CRDs                              │    │
         │  │ Read: istioctl proxy-config route deploy/jobpost-api     │    │
         │  └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                               ▼                                  │
         │  ┌─ 3. CDS — CLUSTERS ──────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │ "Which upstream services and subsets exist?"             │    │
         │  │ Fed by: DestinationRule CRDs + Service registry          │    │
         │  │ Read: istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api   │    │
         │  └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                               ▼                                  │
         │  ┌─ 4. EDS — ENDPOINTS ─────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │ "Which healthy pod IPs back that cluster?"               │    │
         │  │ Fed by: K8s Endpoints (istiod bypasses kube-proxy)       │    │
         │  │ Read: istioctl proxy-config endpoint deploy/jobpost-api  │    │
         │  └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                               ▼                                  │
         │  PACKET LEAVES → mTLS to the chosen pod's Envoy on :15006        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [BOTTOM-UP DEBUG ORDER — always start at EDS, finish at LDS]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Symptom: calls to skills-service time out or return 503         │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 1 — EDS (endpoints): are there healthy pod IPs?            │
         │    istioctl proxy-config endpoint deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj   │
         │    ❌ empty or UNHEALTHY → no ready pods / outlier ejection       │
         │       (skills-service at 8.1% errors gets ejected!) — STOP HERE  │
         │    ✅ healthy IPs listed → continue to STEP 2                     │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 2 — CDS (clusters): does the target cluster exist?         │
         │    istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj    │
         │    ❌ no outbound|8090||skills-service... → DestinationRule /     │
         │       subset problem                                             │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 3 — RDS (routes): is your VirtualService rule present?     │
         │    istioctl proxy-config route deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj      │
         │    ❌ rule missing → VS not applied, or host typo                 │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 4 — LDS (listeners): is Envoy even listening?              │
         │    istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj   │
         │    ❌ 15001/15006 missing → injection or iptables broken          │
