         │                                                                  │
         │  All four ✅ but still failing? → kubectl logs -c istio-proxy     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.9  Deep Dive — Envoy Filter Chain & Full Request Lifecycle
         - DEFINITION: a filter chain is Envoy's internal assembly line —
           every packet passes an ordered list of filters before it may leave
         - Why it matters: when an Istio feature "does nothing", the usual
           cause is that the packet never reached the filter implementing it
         - Three filter layers, in fixed order:
           • Listener filters → connection-level peeking (tls_inspector asks
             "TLS or plaintext?"; original_dst recovers the real destination
             address that iptables rewrote)
           • Network filters  → L4, run for ALL traffic (http_connection_
             manager for HTTP, tcp_proxy for raw TCP, rbac for authorization)
           • HTTP filters     → L7, ONLY when http_connection_manager runs
             (jwt_authn, cors, fault, stats, router — router is ALWAYS last)
         - → this is why TCP services get no retries/fault-injection: those
           live in HTTP filters that plain TCP traffic never reaches
         - The sidecar's port map (memorize for the exam):
           • 15001 outbound capture   • 15006 inbound capture
           • 15021 health/readiness   • 15090 raw Envoy stats
           • 15020 istio-agent — merged app + Envoy metrics; Prometheus
             scrapes THIS port, not 15090

         [ENVOY FILTER CHAIN — inbound packet through jobpost-api's sidecar]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  packet arrives on :15006 (iptables PREROUTING redirect)         │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  LISTENER FILTERS ("what is this connection?")                   │
         │   • tls_inspector → TLS or plaintext? (STRICT mTLS + plaintext   │
         │                     = TCP connection RESET, never an HTTP 403)   │
         │   • original_dst  → recover real destination iptables rewrote    │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  NETWORK FILTERS (L4 — run for ALL traffic)                      │
         │   • istio_authn             → verify SPIFFE peer certificate     │
         │   • rbac                    → enforce AuthorizationPolicy        │
         │   • http_connection_manager → hand HTTP traffic to L7 filters    │
         │     (tcp_proxy instead if raw TCP, e.g. PostgreSQL on 5432)      │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  HTTP FILTERS (L7 — HTTP/gRPC only)                              │
         │   • jwt_authn → validate JWT (RequestAuthentication)             │
         │   • cors      → cross-origin rules                               │
         │   • fault     → inject delays/aborts (VirtualService fault)      │
         │   • stats     → record RED metrics (rate/errors/duration)        │
         │   • router    → ALWAYS LAST: pick route → cluster → endpoint     │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  forward to 127.0.0.1:8000 (Django, plain HTTP on localhost)     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [FULL REQUEST LIFECYCLE — browser → gateway → sidecar → Django]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🌐 Browser: GET https://api.anihpj.com/jobs/42/                  │
         │        │  public TLS on :443                                     │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  [istio-ingressgateway]  (standalone Envoy, no app container)    │
         │    • LDS: listener bound by the Gateway CRD                      │
         │    • terminates public TLS, matches VirtualService route         │
         │    • RDS: /jobs/* → cluster outbound|8000||jobpost-api...        │
         │        │  🔒 mTLS: gateway cert ↔ jobpost-api sidecar cert        │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  [jobpost-api pod — INBOUND side]                                │
         │    • iptables PREROUTING → Envoy :15006                          │
         │    • filter chain: tls_inspector → istio_authn → rbac → HTTP     │
         │    • forwards to Django on 127.0.0.1:8000 (plain HTTP)           │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  Django view runs → needs the author → calls user-service        │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  [jobpost-api pod — OUTBOUND side]                               │
         │    • iptables OUTPUT (UID != 1337) → Envoy :15001                │
         │    • RDS route for user-service → CDS cluster → EDS pod IP       │
         │    • 🔒 new mTLS tunnel to user-service sidecar :15006            │
         │        │                                                         │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  [user-service pod] :15006 → filters → app on :8080              │
         │        │  response retraces the same path in reverse             │
         │        ▼                                                         │
         │  🌐 Browser receives 200 OK — every hop encrypted + measured      │
         │                                                                  │
         │  SIDE PORTS (never in the request path):                         │
         │   :15021 kubelet health probes    :15090 raw Envoy stats         │
         │   :15020 istio-agent merges app + Envoy metrics for Prometheus   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - REAL SCENARIO (anihpj, day 19): a fault-injection rule "did
           nothing" for search-service traffic. Root cause: the Service port
           was named "tcp-search", so Envoy installed only tcp_proxy and the
           fault HTTP filter never saw a request. Renaming the port to
           "http-search" switched the chain to L7 — the fault fired instantly

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 3: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After this chapter, in your daily work you will:
      • Visualize the two planes: data plane (sidecars doing the work)
        and control plane (istiod configuring everything)
      • Know that istiod merged 3 components into 1 binary (simpler ops!)
      • Understand how xDS pushes config from istiod to Envoy in ~1-3 seconds
      • Know exactly what happens during sidecar injection (mutating webhook)
      • Understand iptables interception — your code thinks it talks directly
        to user-service:80, but Envoy intercepts and encrypts everything
      • Know what pods run in istio-system and what each one does
      • Be able to check proxy sync status: istioctl proxy-status

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 3: ICA EXAM TIPS & QUESTIONS
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS (Foundation — feeds all 5 domains):
      • "Check if all proxies are synced" = istioctl proxy-status
      • "What port does Envoy listen on for inbound traffic?" = 15006
      • "How does traffic get redirected to Envoy?" = iptables rules by istio-init
      • "What runs in istio-system namespace?" = istiod, ingressgateway, addons
      • "Traffic not routing — debug order?" = endpoint → cluster → route →
        listener (bottom-up through EDS / CDS / RDS / LDS)
      • "Verify a workload certificate" = istioctl proxy-config secret
        (istioctl authn tls-check was REMOVED — never pick it)
      • "Which port does Prometheus scrape?" = 15020 (istio-agent, merged
        app + Envoy metrics; 15090 is the raw Envoy stats port)

    Q1: What command checks if all Envoy proxies are synchronized with istiod?
    A1: istioctl proxy-status

    Q2: What are the three sub-components merged into the single istiod binary?
    A2: Pilot (traffic config), Citadel (certificates/mTLS), Galley (config validation/injection)

    Q3: How does the istio-init container redirect traffic to Envoy?
    A3: By setting iptables rules: outbound → port 15001, inbound → port 15006.
        Envoy's own traffic (UID 1337) is exempt from redirection.

    Q4: New pods in the anihpj namespace suddenly come up 1/1 with no sidecar,
        although the namespace still carries istio-injection=enabled. What do
        you check first, and how do you prove injection works end-to-end?
    A4: Check the webhook object: kubectl get mutatingwebhookconfiguration
        istio-sidecar-injector. If it is missing or its CA bundle is broken,
        the API server never calls istiod, so Pods are persisted unmodified.
        Prove it end-to-end with a server-side dry run:
        kubectl apply --dry-run=server -o yaml -f pod.yaml | grep istio-proxy
        → the output must contain the injected istio-proxy container.
        Why: injection happens ONLY at Pod creation, inside the admission
        chain — if the webhook is gone, nothing else can add the sidecar.

    Q5: Why do the iptables rules written by istio-init exempt traffic owned
        by UID 1337, and what exactly would break without that exemption?
    A5: UID 1337 is the user the istio-proxy (Envoy) container runs as. The
        rule "-m owner ! --uid-owner 1337 -j REDIRECT --to-ports 15001"
        redirects everyone EXCEPT Envoy. Without it, Envoy's own outbound
        packets would be redirected back into Envoy on :15001 → an infinite
        redirect loop; no packet would ever leave the pod. Verify with:
        kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -c istio-proxy -n anihpj -- id
        → uid=1337(istio-proxy).

    Q6: You applied a VirtualService 10 minutes ago, but one jobpost-api pod
        still routes with the old rules. istioctl proxy-status marks that
        proxy STALE. What does STALE mean and how do you recover?
    A6: STALE = the proxy is connected but has not acknowledged istiod's
        latest xDS push (SYNCED = ACKed latest; NOT SENT = istiod could not
        deliver at all). Normal propagation is ~1-3s, so minutes = a stuck
        stream. Recover: confirm the pod can reach istiod on port 15012, then
        force a reconnect — kubectl exec <pod> -c istio-proxy -n anihpj --
        curl -X POST localhost:15000/quitquitquit (Envoy restarts and
        re-subscribes to xDS) — or simply kubectl delete pod <pod> -n anihpj.

    Q7: Prove that the jobpost-api workload really holds a valid SPIFFE
        identity certificate, and state the exact SPIFFE URI you expect.
    A7: Dump what Envoy is serving: istioctl proxy-config secret
        deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -o json, base64-decode the "default"
        cert and read it with openssl x509 -noout -text — the URI SAN must be
        spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api (trust domain /
        namespace / service account). istioctl x describe pod <pod> confirms
        the effective mTLS mode. Never answer "istioctl authn tls-check" —
        that command was removed years ago.

    Q8: What does istioctl proxy-config secret deploy/user-service -n anihpj
        show, and how do you read certificate health from its output?
    A8: It lists the workload's SDS secrets: "default" (the workload cert)
        and "ROOTCA", with STATUS, SERIAL NUMBER, NOT BEFORE / NOT AFTER.
        Healthy = STATUS "VALID CERT" with NOT AFTER within ~24h (istiod
        rotates workload certs daily). "CERT NOT FOUND" or an expired window
        means the SDS stream from istio-agent to istiod is broken → check
        kubectl logs <pod> -c istio-proxy for SDS/CA errors.

    Q9: A VirtualService fault-injection rule (HTTP 500 abort) has no effect
        on notification-service, though the identical pattern works for
        jobpost-api. The Service port is named "tcp-notify". Why, and what
        is the fix?
    A9: The port name tells Istio the protocol. "tcp-*" → Envoy installs only
        the tcp_proxy network filter, so the HTTP filter chain (fault, cors,
        jwt_authn, router) NEVER runs — L4 traffic cannot use L7 features.
        Fix: rename the port to "http-notify" (or set appProtocol: http) and
        re-apply; the listener then uses http_connection_manager and the
        fault filter fires.

    Q10: jobpost-api gets 503 "no healthy upstream" when calling
         skills-service. Give the bottom-up istioctl sequence you would run
         and what each step rules out.
    A10: Walk the xDS hierarchy from the bottom:
         ① istioctl proxy-config endpoint deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj —
           empty/UNHEALTHY → no ready pods or outlier ejection (likely here:
           skills-service degraded at 8.1% errors / 1240ms gets ejected)
         ② istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj —
           outbound|8090||skills-service... missing → DestinationRule/subset
         ③ istioctl proxy-config route deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj —
           rule absent → VirtualService not applied, or host typo
         ④ istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj —
           15001/15006 missing → injection or iptables broken
         Why bottom-up: if EDS is empty nothing above it matters — this
         ordering finds the root cause in the fewest commands.


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 4: INSTALLING ISTIO — GETTING STARTED                               🟢
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Chapter 4: Installing and Configuring Istio
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    4.1  Prerequisites and Cluster Requirements
         - Kubernetes version: 1.28+ recommended (1.22+ minimum)
         - kubectl configured and pointing to your cluster
         - Cluster sizing for Istio overhead:
           • istiod: ~500m CPU, 2Gi memory (1 replica)
           • Each Envoy sidecar: ~100m CPU, 128Mi memory
           • For anihpj with 7 services: add ~700m CPU, ~1Gi RAM overhead
           • For a total anihpj cluster: ~4 CPUs, 8GB RAM minimum
         - Supported platforms: minikube, k3s, kind, Docker Desktop K8s,
           EKS, GKE, AKS, OpenShift, on-prem clusters
         - Quick cluster readiness check:
           kubectl cluster-info
           kubectl get nodes  → all nodes should be Ready
           kubectl top nodes  → check available resources
         [RESOURCE CALCULATOR: table showing overhead per service count]

         [RESOURCE CALCULATOR — mesh overhead by service count]
         ┌──────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────────┬────────────────┐
         │ Services │ Sidecar  │ Sidecar  │ istiod       │ TOTAL mesh     │
         │ (pods)   │ CPU      │ RAM      │ (replicas)   │ overhead       │
         ├──────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┼────────────────┤
         │    5     │   500m   │  640Mi   │ 500m/2Gi (1) │ ~1.0 CPU/2.6Gi │
         │ 7 anihpj │   700m   │  896Mi   │ 500m/2Gi (1) │ ~1.2 CPU/2.9Gi │
         │   10     │  1000m   │ 1.25Gi   │ 500m/2Gi (1) │ ~1.5 CPU/3.3Gi │
         │   25     │  2500m   │  3.2Gi   │ 1 CPU/4Gi (2)│ ~3.5 CPU/7.2Gi │
         │   50     │  5000m   │  6.4Gi   │ 1 CPU/4Gi (2)│ ~6 CPU/10.4Gi  │
         └──────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────────┴────────────────┘
         - Math: each sidecar ≈ 100m CPU + 128Mi RAM; istiod baseline is
           500m CPU + 2Gi RAM → run 2 istiod replicas beyond ~20 services
         - Budget this overhead ON TOP of the application containers: for
           anihpj (7 services) a 4 CPU / 8GB cluster is comfortable

    4.2  Installing istioctl (The Istio CLI)
         - istioctl: Istio's command-line tool (like kubectl but for Istio)
         - Installation on all platforms (Linux/Mac/Windows)
         - Key istioctl commands overview:
           • istioctl install: install Istio on the cluster
           • istioctl analyze: validate Istio configuration
           • istioctl proxy-status: check proxy sync status
           • istioctl proxy-config: inspect Envoy config
           • istioctl dashboard: open Kiali, Grafana, Jaeger in browser
         [CODE BLOCK: istioctl installation commands for each OS]

         [INSTALLING istioctl]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Linux / macOS:                                      │
         │  curl -L https://istio.io/downloadIstio | sh -         │
         │  cd istio-1.22.0                                       │
         │  export PATH=$PWD/bin:$PATH                            │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Windows (PowerShell):                               │
         │  Invoke-WebRequest https://istio.io/downloadIstio      │
         │    -OutFile istio.zip                                   │
         │  Expand-Archive istio.zip                              │
         │  $env:PATH += ";.\istio-1.22.0\bin"                    │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Verify:                                             │
         │  istioctl version                                      │
         │  → client version: 1.22.0                             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.3  Istio Installation Profiles
         - Istio has built-in installation profiles for different use cases:
           • default: production-grade (ingressgateway + istiod), minimal tools
           • demo: all features for learning (adds Kiali, Jaeger, Prometheus, Grafana)
             ⚠️ demo profile is for LEARNING only — NOT production!
           • minimal: control plane only (no gateway), for CI/CD pipeline tests
           • remote: for multi-cluster setups, connects to external control plane
           • empty: nothing pre-configured, for advanced custom installs
           • ambient: sidecar-free mode (ztunnel + waypoint proxy), lower overhead
           • preview: experimental features for testing upcoming releases
         - For this guide: use demo profile to get all observability tools
         - For production later: use default profile + add tools via Helm separately
         [PROFILE COMPARISON TABLE: what each profile includes]

         | Component             | default | demo | minimal | remote |
         | ──────────────────────│─────────│──────│─────────│────────│
         | istiod                | ✅      | ✅   | ✅      | ❌     |
         | istio-ingressgateway  | ✅      | ✅   | ❌      | ❌     │
         | istio-egressgateway   | ❌      | ✅   | ❌      | ❌     |
         | Kiali                 | ❌      | ✅   | ❌      | ❌     |
         | Jaeger                | ❌      | ✅   | ❌      | ❌     |
         | Prometheus            | ❌      | ✅   | ❌      | ❌     |
         | Grafana               | ❌      | ✅   | ❌      | ❌     |
         | Best for              | Prod   | Learn| CI/CD  | Multi  |

         [PROFILE SELECTION DECISION — which profile to use when]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  Are you learning / developing? ──── YES ──▶ demo profile         │
         │                                              (all tools included)  │
         │  ↓ NO                                                              │
         │  Is this production?  ──────────── YES ──▶ default profile        │
         │                                              (then add tools via   │
         │                                               Helm separately)     │
         │  ↓ NO                                                              │
         │  Is this a CI/CD pipeline test? ── YES ──▶ minimal profile        │
         │                                              (fastest install)     │
         │  ↓ NO                                                              │
         │  Is this a secondary cluster? ──── YES ──▶ remote profile         │
         │                                              (connects to primary)  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [EXPECTED OUTPUT after: istioctl install --set profile=demo -y]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ✔ Istio core installed                                            │
         │  ✔ Istiod installed                                                │
         │  ✔ Egress gateways installed                                       │
         │  ✔ Ingress gateways installed                                      │
         │  ✔ Installation complete                                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system                                  │
         │  NAME                                    READY  STATUS   RESTARTS  │
         │  istiod-7d7f9c7b8c-x4k2z               1/1    Running  0         │
         │  istio-ingressgateway-6d99ff9d4-9xklq   1/1    Running  0         │
         │  istio-egressgateway-7f9ddb899-z2p8k    1/1    Running  0         │
         │  grafana-6b7857866c-bj84p               1/1    Running  0         │
         │  jaeger-7f78b6fb65-hlfgp                1/1    Running  0         │

    4.4  Step-by-Step: Install Istio for anihpj
         [COMPLETE INSTALLATION WALKTHROUGH]
         Step 1: Verify cluster is ready
           kubectl cluster-info
           kubectl get nodes
         Step 2: Install Istio with demo profile
           istioctl install --set profile=demo -y
         Step 3: Verify installation
           kubectl get pods -n istio-system
           kubectl get svc -n istio-system
         Step 4: Enable sidecar injection for anihpj namespace
           kubectl create namespace anihpj
           kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled
           kubectl get namespace anihpj --show-labels
         Step 5: Deploy anihpj services
           kubectl apply -f anihpj/k8s/
         Step 6: Verify sidecars were injected
           kubectl get pods -n anihpj
           → Each pod should show 2/2 containers (app + istio-proxy)
         Step 7: Open observability dashboards
           istioctl dashboard kiali
           istioctl dashboard grafana
           istioctl dashboard jaeger
         [CODE BLOCKS with expected output at each step]

    4.5  Verifying Istio is Working
         - Check proxy sync status:
           istioctl proxy-status
           → All pods should show "SYNCED" next to istiod
         - Check a specific proxy's config:
           istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj
         - Analyze your configuration for problems:
           istioctl analyze -n anihpj
         - Generate a traffic test and watch Kiali:
           kubectl exec -it deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- curl user-service/users/
         [EXPECTED OUTPUT SCREENSHOTS with annotations]

         [EXPECTED OUTPUT — istioctl proxy-status: every column SYNCED]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ istioctl proxy-status                                           │
         │  NAME                             CDS     LDS     EDS     RDS      │
         │  jobpost-api-7d4f8b9c.anihpj      SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED   │
         │  user-service-6c9d8f7b.anihpj     SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED   │
         │  skills-service-5b8c7d6f.anihpj   SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED   │
         │  ingressgateway-6d9.istio-system  SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED  SYNCED   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  CDS/LDS/EDS/RDS = the four xDS config channels Envoy receives     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  SYNCED   → proxy holds the latest istiod config          ✅        │
         │  STALE    → istiod pushed config, proxy did not ACK it    ⚠        │
         │  NOT SENT → istiod has no config for this proxy           ⚠        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [EXPECTED OUTPUT — istioctl analyze: clean run vs a warning]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ istioctl analyze -n anihpj                                      │
         │  ✔ No validation issues found when analyzing namespace: anihpj.    │
         │    ← the output you WANT: config is internally consistent          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # What a WARNING looks like instead (bad port name):              │
         │  $ istioctl analyze -n anihpj                                      │
         │  Warning [IST0118] (Service anihpj/jobpost-api) Port name          │
         │    api-port (port: 8000, targetPort: 8000) doesn't follow          │
         │    the naming convention of Istio port.                            │
         │    ← fix: rename the Service port to "http", re-apply, re-run      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [EXPECTED OUTPUT — in-mesh curl test through the sidecars]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ kubectl exec -it deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \              │
         │      curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}\n" \                    │
         │      user-service:8080/users/                                      │
         │  200                                                               │
         │   ← 200 = the request left jobpost-api's Envoy, crossed the        │
         │     mesh (mTLS), entered user-service's Envoy, reached Django      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  $ kubectl exec -it deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \              │
         │      curl -s user-service:8080/users/ | head -c 60                 │
         │  [{"id":1,"username":"recruiter01","skills":["python","dj          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ✅ Open Kiali now — a green edge jobpost-api → user-service        │
         │     appears on the service graph within ~15 seconds                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.6  Understanding the anihpj Deployment with Istio
         - Complete YAML for all anihpj services with Istio labels
         - Required label: app: jobpost-api version: v1 (Istio uses these)
         - Port naming convention: must name ports "http", "grpc", "tcp"
         - Kubernetes Service selectors and how Istio uses them
         [ANNOTATED YAML: Complete Deployment + Service YAML for jobpost-api]

         [ANNOTATED Deployment YAML for anihpj with Istio]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: apps/v1                                   │
         │  kind: Deployment                                      │
         │  metadata:                                             │
         │    name: jobpost-api                                   │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                   │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    replicas: 2                                         │
         │    selector:                                           │
         │      matchLabels:                                      │
         │        app: jobpost-api    # ← Istio uses "app" label │
         │        version: v1         # ← Required for canary!   │
         │    template:                                           │
         │      metadata:                                         │
         │        labels:                                         │
         │          app: jobpost-api                              │
         │          version: v1                                   │
         │      spec:                                             │
         │        containers:                                     │
         │        - name: jobpost                                 │
         │          image: anihpj/jobpost:v1                      │
         │          ports:                                        │
         │          - name: http          # ← Must be "http"!    │
         │            containerPort: 8000 # ← Port naming matters│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ANNOTATED Service YAML for jobpost-api — named port "http"]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: v1                                        │
         │  kind: Service                                         │
         │  metadata:                                             │
         │    name: jobpost-api                                   │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                   │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    selector:                                           │
         │      app: jobpost-api   # ← matches Deployment label   │
         │    ports:                                              │
         │    - name: http         # ← MUST start with "http"!    │
         │      port: 8000         # ← what callers dial          │
         │      targetPort: 8000   # ← containerPort in the pod   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - Replicate the same shape for every anihpj service — only name
           and port change: user-service :8080, skills-service :8090,
           location-service :8070, auth-service, search-service,
           notification-service — ALL with port name "http"
         - If any port name is wrong, Istio treats that service as raw
           TCP: no retries, no canary %, no per-route metrics, no tracing

    4.7  Installation Profiles — Which One for Which Scenario
         - DEFINITION: a profile is a named, pre-configured IstioOperator
           spec shipped inside istioctl — a preset that decides which
           components are installed and how they are tuned
         - Why it matters: the wrong profile either wastes resources
           (demo in production) or strands you without tools (default in
           a lab → "no Kiali pods found" when opening the dashboard)
         - How it works: istioctl install --set profile=<name> loads the
           preset, merges your --set overrides, renders manifests, applies
         - Explore profiles before choosing:
           • istioctl profile list              → names of all 7 profiles
           • istioctl profile dump demo         → full IstioOperator preset
           • istioctl profile diff default demo → exactly what demo adds

         [PROFILE → SCENARIO MAP — all 7 built-in profiles]
         ┌─────────┬──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
         │ Profile │ Installs                 │ Use it when...          │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ default │ istiod + ingressgateway  │ Production baseline;    │
         │         │                          │ add tools via Helm      │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ demo    │ istiod + ingress +       │ Learning, labs, ICA     │
         │         │ egress + Kiali, Jaeger,  │ practice — NEVER prod   │
         │         │ Prometheus, Grafana      │                         │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ minimal │ istiod only              │ CI/CD pipeline tests,   │
         │         │                          │ edge/IoT, tight RAM     │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ remote  │ injection webhook only   │ Secondary cluster using │
         │         │ (no local istiod)        │ an external istiod      │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ empty   │ nothing at all           │ Base for fully custom   │
         │         │                          │ IstioOperator builds    │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ ambient │ istiod + ztunnel + CNI   │ Sidecar-free mesh; big  │
         │         │ (no sidecars)            │ RAM savings per pod     │
         ├─────────┼──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ preview │ default + experimental   │ Testing upcoming        │
         │         │ features enabled         │ features — never prod   │
         └─────────┴──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘
         - Real anihpj scenario: the dev cluster runs demo (all dashboards,
           100% trace sampling); production runs default with Kiali,
           Prometheus and Grafana installed separately via Helm and trace
           sampling dialed down to 1%

    4.8  Deep Dive — What Each Profile REALLY Installs: IstioOperator API and the Helm Pipeline
         - DEFINITION: IstioOperator (install.istio.io/v1alpha1) is the
           CRD that describes a COMPLETE Istio installation — every
           profile is just a pre-filled IstioOperator document
         - Why it matters: every --set flag you pass to istioctl install
           maps 1:1 to an IstioOperator field — understand this CRD and
           you understand ALL installation customization
         - How it works: istioctl merges profile + overrides, renders the
           BUNDLED Helm charts to plain Kubernetes YAML, applies it, then
           waits for readiness — Helm itself is never required
         - GitOps tip: istioctl manifest generate -f op.yaml > all.yaml
           produces the fully rendered YAML to review/commit before apply

         [INSTALL PIPELINE — istioctl → IstioOperator → manifests → pods]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  istioctl install --set profile=demo -y                        │
         │      │                                                         │
         │      ▼ 1. PROFILE RESOLUTION                                   │
         │  loads built-in preset: manifests/profiles/demo.yaml           │
         │      │                                                         │
         │      ▼ 2. MERGE OVERRIDES                                      │
         │  profile + every --set flag → one final IstioOperator spec     │
         │      │                                                         │
         │      ▼ 3. HELM RENDERING (charts bundled inside istioctl)      │
         │  each component (istiod, gateways) = a chart rendered to       │
         │  plain K8s YAML: Deployments, Services, RBAC, webhooks         │
         │      │                                                         │
         │      ▼ 4. CRDs + NAMESPACE                                     │
         │  creates istio-system + ~30 CRDs (VirtualService, Gateway…)    │
         │  + MutatingWebhookConfiguration (the sidecar injector)         │
         │      │                                                         │
         │      ▼ 5. APPLY + PRUNE                                        │
         │  server-side apply of every rendered resource                  │
         │      │                                                         │
         │      ▼ 6. WAIT + VERIFY                                        │
         │  istiod 1/1, gateways 1/1, webhooks answering                  │
         │  → "✔ Installation complete"                                   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ANNOTATED IstioOperator YAML — customizing the install]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: install.istio.io/v1alpha1                         │
         │  kind: IstioOperator                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                     │
         │    name: anihpj-istio                                          │
         │    namespace: istio-system                                     │
         │  spec:                                                         │
         │    profile: demo                 # ← starting preset           │
         │    meshConfig:                   # ← global mesh settings      │
         │      accessLogFile: /dev/stdout                                │
         │      enableTracing: true                                       │
         │    components:                                                 │
         │      pilot:                      # ← "pilot" = istiod          │
         │        k8s:                                                    │
         │          replicaCount: 1                                       │
         │          resources:                                            │
         │            requests: {cpu: 500m, memory: 2048Mi}               │
         │      ingressGateways:                                          │
         │      - name: istio-ingressgateway                              │
         │        enabled: true                                           │
         │        k8s:                                                    │
         │          service: {type: LoadBalancer}                         │
         │                                                                │
         │  # Install it:   istioctl install -f operator.yaml -y          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PER-PROFILE COMPONENT MATRIX — what actually lands on cluster]
         ┌──────────────────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┐
         │ Component        │ def  │ demo │ min  │ rem  │ emp  │ amb  │ pre  │
         ├──────────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
         │ istiod           │  ✅   │  ✅   │  ✅   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ✅   │  ✅   │
         │ ingressgateway   │  ✅   │  ✅   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ✅   │
         │ egressgateway    │  ❌   │  ✅   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │
         │ ztunnel + CNI    │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ✅   │  ❌   │
         │ Kiali/Jaeger/    │  ❌   │  ✅   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │  ❌   │
         │ Prom/Grafana     │      │      │      │      │      │      │      │
         ├──────────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
         │ pods installed   │  2   │  7   │  1   │  0   │  0   │ 3+   │  2   │
         │ control-pl. RAM  │ .6Gi │ 2Gi  │ .3Gi │  0   │  0   │ 1Gi  │ .6Gi │
         └──────────────────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┘
         - Legend: def=default, min=minimal, rem=remote, emp=empty,
           amb=ambient, pre=preview
         - demo's observability addons ship in the Istio release under
           samples/addons/ — the profile wires them together for the lab

    4.9  Troubleshooting — Common Installation Issues
         - DEFINITION: install troubleshooting = working the stack
           bottom-up: scheduler → control plane → webhook → sidecar →
           config → traffic
         - Why it matters: 90% of "Istio is broken" reports right after
           an install are one of the issues below — each has a 30-second
           check and a one-line fix
         - Golden rule: kubectl describe the failing object FIRST — the
           Events section usually names the cause outright
         - Pre-flight: run istioctl x precheck BEFORE install/upgrade —
           it validates versions, permissions and existing webhooks
           without changing anything on the cluster

         [INSTALL ISSUES — SYMPTOM → CAUSE → FIX]
         ┌───────────────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
         │ Symptom           │ Root cause          │ Fix                     │
         ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ istio-system pods │ cluster out of CPU/ │ kubectl describe pod →  │
         │ stuck Pending     │ RAM for requests    │ Events; add nodes or    │
         │                   │                     │ use minimal profile     │
         ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl install  │ API server cannot   │ allow 15017 to istiod;  │
         │ hangs, webhook    │ reach the injection │ istioctl x precheck,    │
         │ timeout           │ webhook (port 15017)│ then retry the install  │
         ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ app pods show 1/1 │ no injection label, │ kubectl label ns anihpj │
         │ instead of 2/2    │ or pods created     │ istio-injection=enabled │
         │                   │ before the label    │ + rollout restart       │
         ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl analyze  │ bad port names, VS  │ fix the named object;   │
         │ prints warnings   │ subset without DR,  │ re-run analyze until    │
         │                   │ selector mismatch   │ no issues reported      │
         ├───────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ proxy-status is   │ istiod unreachable  │ verify istiod Running,  │
         │ STALE / NOT SENT  │ from pod, port 15012│ NetworkPolicies; then   │
         │                   │                     │ restart the stale pod   │
         └───────────────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘
         - Nuclear option (labs only): istioctl uninstall --purge -y, then
           delete the istio-system namespace and reinstall from scratch
         - Plaintext-to-STRICT gotcha: a non-mesh pod calling a STRICT-mTLS
           port gets a TCP connection reset — NOT an HTTP 403; if curl says
           "connection reset by peer" right after install, check mTLS mode

    4.10  Installing Istio with Helm — The Alternative to istioctl
         - DEFINITION: Helm is Kubernetes' package manager — it installs,
           upgrades and removes a bundle of Kubernetes manifests (a
           "chart") as ONE tracked release, with helm install / upgrade /
           rollback / uninstall as the lifecycle verbs
         - Why the exam names it separately from istioctl: istioctl
           bundles these SAME charts internally and applies them for you
           (see 4.8) — installing "with Helm" means running helm
           yourself against the charts Istio publishes on its own
           repository, a different day-2 workflow over identical
           underlying manifests
         - Why it matters in production: a Helm release is versioned and
           diffable (helm diff upgrade, helm rollback <rev>) — exactly
           the primitives GitOps controllers like Argo CD and Flux
           reconcile against; istioctl has no release object at all
         - How it works: Istio publishes THREE separate charts — install
           them in dependency order:
           • base    → the ~30 Istio CRDs + ClusterRoles ONLY, nothing
             runs yet
           • istiod  → the control plane; needs base's CRDs already on
             the cluster or every VirtualService/Gateway apply fails
           • gateway → a data-plane gateway (e.g. istio-ingress); needs
             istiod already Running to receive its xDS config
         → Install out of order and the next chart's pods CrashLoop on
           "no matches for kind VirtualService", or a gateway pod hangs
           waiting for a control plane that was never installed

         [INSTALLING ISTIO WITH HELM — the three charts, in order]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. Add the Istio Helm repo and refresh it                      │
         │  helm repo add istio \                                            │
         │      https://istio-release.storage.googleapis.com/charts          │
         │  helm repo update                                                 │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # 2. base chart — CRDs + ClusterRoles only (no pods yet)         │
         │  helm install istio-base istio/base -n istio-system \             │
         │      --create-namespace                                           │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # 3. istiod chart — the control plane (wait for Ready)           │
         │  helm install istiod istio/istiod -n istio-system --wait          │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # 4. gateway chart — data-plane ingress gateway                  │
         │  helm install istio-ingress istio/gateway -n istio-system         │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # Order matters: base → istiod → gateway                         │
         │  #   (CRDs first, then control plane, then data plane)            │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Every --set flag maps to a dotted path inside that specific
           chart's values.yaml — --set pilot.resources.requests.cpu=500m
           is IstioOperator's spec.components.pilot.k8s.resources by
           another name, just scoped to the istiod chart alone
         - helm status / helm list replace istioctl's install summary —
           STATUS: deployed is Helm's version of "install complete";
           REVISION climbs by one on every future upgrade

         [CUSTOMIZING THE HELM INSTALL — overrides + verification]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Override any chart value at install time with --set            │
         │  # (maps 1:1 to a path in that chart's values.yaml)               │
         │  helm install istiod istio/istiod -n istio-system --wait \        │
         │      --set pilot.resources.requests.cpu=500m \                    │
         │      --set pilot.resources.requests.memory=2048Mi                 │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # Prefer a file once you need more than 1-2 overrides:           │
         │  helm install istiod istio/istiod -n istio-system \               │
         │      --wait -f istiod-values.yaml                                 │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # Verify the release (Helm's equivalent of proxy-status):        │
         │  helm status istiod -n istio-system                               │
         │  STATUS: deployed        REVISION: 1                              │
         │                                                                   │
         │  helm list -n istio-system                                        │
         │  NAME    NAMESPACE     REVISION  STATUS    CHART                  │
         │  istiod  istio-system  1         deployed  istiod-1.22.0          │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ISTIOCTL vs HELM — which one for which job]
         ┌────────────────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
         │ Scenario           │ istioctl            │ Helm                │
         ├────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Quick lab / demo   │ fastest: 1 command  │ 3 installs, more    │
         │ profile install    │                     │ setup               │
         ├────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Production, GitOps │ no release object   │ values.yaml is      │
         │ (Argo CD / Flux)   │ to diff             │ diffable + versioned│
         ├────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Need a rollback    │ uninstall + re-     │ helm rollback       │
         │                    │ install from scratch│ istiod 1            │
         ├────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ CI/CD smoke test   │ profile=minimal -y  │ 3x helm install     │
         └────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

         - Real anihpj scenario: the dev/lab cluster installs fast with
           istioctl install --set profile=demo -y; the production
           pipeline instead commits istiod-values.yaml to git and lets
           Argo CD run helm upgrade --install on every merge — same
           three charts underneath, a completely different day-2 story
         - 🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: Installing Istio with istioctl or Helm is a
           named exam topic — you may be asked to install via EITHER
           method, so know both command sequences cold.

    4.11  Installing Istio in Ambient Mode — The Sidecar-Free Exam Topic
         - DEFINITION: sections 4.1-4.9 installed Istio in SIDECAR mode —
           every meshed pod runs its own Envoy container injected next
           to the app (2/2 READY). AMBIENT MODE removes that per-pod
           proxy: a shared ztunnel DaemonSet (one per node) supplies
           mTLS and L4 policy to every ambient pod, with an optional
           per-namespace waypoint proxy added only if L7 features are
           needed. Same mesh, same certificates — a different install.
         - This section covers only the INSTALLATION exam topic: how to
           turn ambient mode on and prove it took effect. → for the full
           ztunnel / waypoint / HBONE architecture, see 16.4-16.6.

         [INSTALLING AMBIENT MODE — install, label, verify]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. Install control plane + ztunnel with the ambient profile    │
         │  istioctl install --set profile=ambient -y                        │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # 2. Opt anihpj into ambient (NOT istio-injection=enabled!)      │
         │  kubectl label namespace anihpj istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient   │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # Contrast — sidecar mode uses a DIFFERENT namespace label:      │
         │  #   kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled       │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # 3. Verify the ambient data plane (ztunnel, no per-app sidecar) │
         │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system                                 │
         │  NAME                       READY  STATUS   AGE                   │
         │  istiod-7d7f9c7b8c-x4k2z    1/1    Running  3m                    │
         │  ztunnel-4kxpq              1/1    Running  3m  ← 1/node          │
         │  ztunnel-9mvwt              1/1    Running  3m                    │
         │                                                                   │
         │  # 4. Verify anihpj app pods — NO istio-proxy container           │
         │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj                                       │
         │  NAME                          READY  STATUS   RESTARTS           │
         │  jobpost-api-7d4f8b9c-x2k4z    1/1    Running  0                  │
         │  user-service-6c9d8f7b-m3j5y   1/1    Running  0                  │
         │                                ↑ 1/1 is CORRECT here — by design  │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - The two namespace labels are NOT interchangeable — they drive
           TWO DIFFERENT data planes:
           • istio-injection=enabled          → sidecar mode (per-pod Envoy)
           • istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient  → ambient mode (ztunnel only)
         → Applying BOTH labels, or the wrong one, is the single most
           common ambient-install mistake — pick exactly one per namespace

         [WHICH MODE DID THE EXAM ASK FOR?]
         ┌──────────────────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
         │ Signal in the ask    │ Sidecar mode        │ Ambient mode        │
         ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Pods READY column    │ 2/2 expected        │ 1/1 expected        │
         ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Namespace label      │ istio-injection=    │ istio.io/dataplane- │
         │ used                 │ enabled             │ mode=ambient        │
         ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Install flag         │ profile=demo/       │ profile=ambient     │
         │                      │ default             │                     │
         ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Vocabulary in the    │ "sidecar", "inject",│ "ztunnel",          │
         │ question             │ "istio-proxy"       │ "waypoint", "HBONE" │
         └──────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

         - Real anihpj scenario: notification-service and
           location-service (internal, low-traffic, mTLS-only) go
           ambient with just the dataplane-mode label and no waypoint;
           jobpost-api and skills-service (public, canary weights,
           circuit breakers) stay on sidecar, or move to ambient PLUS a
           waypoint — see 16.6 for the full per-namespace decision guide
         - 🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: Installing Istio in Sidecar or Ambient Mode
           is a named exam topic — know the install command AND the
           verification signal for each mode.

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 4: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After this chapter, in your daily work you will:
      • Install Istio on any cluster with a single command
      • Know which profile to use: demo for learning, default for production
      • Enable sidecar injection with one label: kubectl label namespace
      • Verify injection worked: pods show 2/2 containers
      • Check proxy sync: istioctl proxy-status (all must be SYNCED)
      • Validate config: istioctl analyze -n anihpj
      • Open dashboards: istioctl dashboard kiali/grafana/jaeger
      • Know the critical rules: port names must be "http"/"grpc"/"tcp",
        pods need "app" and "version" labels, services need the "app" selector

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 4: ICA EXAM TIPS & QUESTIONS
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS (Installation, Upgrade & Config — 20% of exam):
      • "Install Istio with demo profile" = istioctl install --set profile=demo -y
      • "Enable injection for namespace" = kubectl label ns X istio-injection=enabled
      • "Verify injection worked" = kubectl get pods → 2/2 containers
      • "Check Istio config for errors" = istioctl analyze -n <namespace>

    Q1: What command installs Istio with all observability tools included?
    A1: istioctl install --set profile=demo -y

    Q2: How do you verify that a pod has the Istio sidecar injected?
    A2: kubectl get pods → look for 2/2 under READY (app container + istio-proxy)

    Q3: What port naming convention does Istio require for protocol detection?
    A3: Port names must start with the protocol prefix: "http", "grpc", "tcp",
        "tls", etc. Istio uses port names (not port numbers) to determine the protocol.

    Q4: A production cluster gives you only 4 CPUs / 8GB RAM for anihpj's
        7 services. Which profile do you install, and does it fit?
    A4: istioctl install --set profile=default -y
        default = istiod + ingressgateway only (~600Mi vs demo's ~2Gi of
        control-plane RAM). Overhead: istiod 500m/2Gi + 7 sidecars
        700m/896Mi ≈ 1.2 CPU / 2.9Gi → fits, leaving room for the apps.

    Q5: Prepare the anihpj namespace so a future v1→v2 canary of
        jobpost-api will work. What must be in place BEFORE any
        VirtualService is written?
    A5: kubectl create namespace anihpj
        kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled
        Then deploy pods carrying BOTH labels app: jobpost-api and
        version: v1, and name the Service port "http". DestinationRule
        subsets select on the version label — no label, no canary.

    Q6: After installing Istio and deploying anihpj, prove the mesh is
        actually routing traffic — not just that containers run.
    A6: (1) kubectl get pods -n anihpj → all 2/2
        (2) istioctl proxy-status → every proxy SYNCED
        (3) istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj
            → upstream services listed
        (4) kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- curl -s
            user-service:8080/users/ → HTTP 200
        (5) istioctl dashboard kiali → green edge on the graph
        2/2 only proves the sidecar container runs; steps 2-5 prove config
        was pushed and traffic really flows through Envoy.

    Q7: Install the demo profile BUT with istiod requesting 1 CPU / 4Gi
        and access logs on stdout — as one reviewable file.
    A7: Create operator.yaml (kind: IstioOperator, apiVersion:
        install.istio.io/v1alpha1) with spec.profile: demo,
        spec.meshConfig.accessLogFile: /dev/stdout, and
        spec.components.pilot.k8s.resources.requests: {cpu: 1000m,
        memory: 4096Mi} — then: istioctl install -f operator.yaml -y
        A file is reviewable, diffable and GitOps-friendly; every --set
        flag maps to the same IstioOperator fields.

    Q8: Remove Istio COMPLETELY from a cluster (CRDs included) and return
        the anihpj pods to plain Kubernetes.
    A8: istioctl uninstall --purge -y
        kubectl delete namespace istio-system
        kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection-
        kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj
        --purge removes ALL Istio resources including CRDs; the restart
        recreates pods without sidecars (the injection webhook is gone).

    Q9: Before installing on a shared cluster, verify compatibility and
        detect conflicting meshes/webhooks WITHOUT changing anything.
    A9: istioctl x precheck
        It validates the Kubernetes version, required permissions, and
        existing webhooks/installations, printing actionable warnings.
        Read-only — safe to run against production clusters.

    Q10: You labeled anihpj AFTER deploying; pods still show 1/1. Fix it,
         and prove injection now works without redeploying everything.
    A10: kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj
         The webhook fires only at Pod CREATION — labels are never
         retroactive. Proof: kubectl apply --dry-run=server a test pod in
         anihpj and grep the returned YAML for "istio-proxy".


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 5: TRAFFIC MANAGEMENT — CONTROLLING HOW SERVICES COMMUNICATE        🟡
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     VISUAL: TRAFFIC FLOW MAP — WHICH ISTIO RESOURCE HANDLES WHAT
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  COMPLETE REQUEST JOURNEY — External User → anihpj Pod              │
         │                                                                     │
         │  🌐 Internet                                                        │
         │       │  GET https://api.anihpj.com/jobs/                          │
         │       ▼  Host: api.anihpj.com                                       │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
         │  │  ☁️ Cloud LoadBalancer (AWS ALB / GCP LB / MetalLB)      │       │
         │  │  Forwards TCP :443 to istio-ingressgateway NodePort      │       │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘       │
         │                             │                                       │
         │                             ▼  TCP :443                             │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
         │  │  🌐 GATEWAY Resource: anihpj-gateway                     │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  "I accept traffic on port 443 for host                 │       │
         │  │   api.anihpj.com with TLS certificate"                 │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  ✅ Host matches: api.anihpj.com                        │       │
         │  │  ✅ Port matches: 443 (HTTPS)                           │       │
         │  │  ✅ TLS termination: SIMPLE mode                        │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  → Passes decrypted HTTP to VirtualService              │       │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘       │
         │                             │                                       │
         │                             ▼  HTTP (decrypted)                     │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
         │  │  🚦 VIRTUALSERVICE Resource: jobpost-api-vs              │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  "Now I route this request:"                            │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  Match: uri prefix /jobs/  → YES ✅                     │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  Route options:                                         │       │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │       │
         │  │  │  subset: v1  →  weight: 90  →  90% traffic  │       │       │
         │  │  │  subset: v2  →  weight: 10  →  10% traffic  │       │       │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘       │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  Policies applied HERE:                                 │       │
         │  │  • timeout: 10s                                         │       │
         │  │  • retries: attempts: 3, perTryTimeout: 2s             │       │
         │  │  • fault: (none)                                        │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  → Chose subset v1 (90% weight)                         │       │
         │  │  → Forwards to DestinationRule for policy lookup        │       │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘       │
         │                             │                                       │
         │                             ▼  "send to jobpost-api, subset v1"     │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
         │  │  📋 DESTINATIONRULE Resource: jobpost-api-dr             │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  "subset v1 = pods with labels: version=v1"             │       │
         │  │  "Now I apply destination policies:"                    │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  Policies applied HERE:                                 │       │
         │  │  • loadBalancer: LEAST_CONN                             │       │
         │  │    → picks pod with fewest active connections           │       │
         │  │  • circuitBreaker: consecutiveErrors: 5                 │       │
         │  │    → checks: has this pod failed 5 times?              │       │
         │  │  • tls: mode: ISTIO_MUTUAL                              │       │
         │  │    → opens mTLS tunnel to destination pod               │       │
         │  │  • connectionPool: maxConnections: 100                  │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  → Selected: Pod jobpost-api-v1-abc (LEAST_CONN)        │       │
         │  │  → Circuit breaker: CLOSED (healthy) ✅                  │       │
         │  │  → mTLS tunnel established 🔒                            │       │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘       │
         │                             │                                       │
         │                             ▼  mTLS encrypted                       │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
         │  │  📦 KUBERNETES SERVICE: jobpost-api                      │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  "My selector: app=jobpost-api"                         │       │
         │  │  "My endpoints: 10.244.1.5:8000, 10.244.2.3:8000, ..." │       │
         │  │  (Istio overrides K8s default round-robin)              │       │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘       │
         │                             │                                       │
         │                             ▼  mTLS → Envoy inbound                 │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
         │  │  🫛 POD: jobpost-api-v1-abc                              │       │
         │  │                                                         │       │
         │  │  ┌──────────────────────┐  ┌────────────────────────┐  │       │
         │  │  │ 🔷 Envoy (inbound)   │  │ 🐍 Django Container    │  │       │
         │  │  │ port 15006           │  │ port 8000              │  │       │
         │  │  │                      │  │                        │  │       │
         │  │  │ 1. Terminate mTLS 🔓 │  │ 6. Process GET /jobs/  │  │       │
         │  │  │ 2. Verify SPIFFE cert│──▶│ 7. Query PostgreSQL   │  │       │
         │  │  │ 3. AuthzPolicy check │  │ 8. Return JSON         │  │       │
         │  │  │ 4. Record metrics    │◀──│                        │  │       │
         │  │  │ 5. Forward to :8000  │  │                        │  │       │
         │  │  └──────────────────────┘  └────────────────────────┘  │       │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘       │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════    │
         │  RESOURCE RESPONSIBILITY SUMMARY:                                   │
         │  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  Gateway     │  WHO can enter? (host, port, TLS)            │   │
         │  │  VirtualSvc  │  WHERE to route? (path, weight, headers)     │   │
         │  │  DestRule    │  HOW to send? (LB, mTLS, circuit breaker)    │   │
         │  │  K8s Service │  WHAT pods exist? (endpoint discovery)        │   │
         │  │  PeerAuth    │  ENCRYPT? (mTLS mode per namespace)          │   │
         │  │  AuthzPolicy │  ALLOWED? (who can call this service)        │   │
         │  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 5: Gateway — The Front Door to Your Mesh
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    5.1  What is an Istio Gateway?
         - A Gateway is the entry point for traffic FROM OUTSIDE the mesh
         - It replaces (or works alongside) a Kubernetes Ingress
         - Deployed as its own Pod: istio-ingressgateway (Envoy-based)
         - More powerful than Ingress: supports header manipulation, mTLS,
           custom ports, weight-based routing, etc.
         - Two types:
           • IngressGateway: external traffic INTO the mesh
           • EgressGateway: controlled traffic OUT of the mesh (to external APIs)
         [GATEWAY POSITION DIAGRAM: internet → Gateway → VirtualService → Pods]

         [GATEWAY POSITION — INTERNET → LB → EDGE ENVOY POD → MESH]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ISTIO GATEWAY PATH (anihpj)          PLAIN K8S INGRESS PATH       │
         │  ───────────────────────────          ──────────────────────       │
         │  🌐 Internet                          🌐 Internet                  │
         │      │ GET https://api.anihpj.com         │ any Host header        │
         │      ▼                                    ▼                        │
         │  Cloud LoadBalancer (TCP :443)        Cloud LoadBalancer           │
         │      │                                    │                        │
         │      ▼                                    ▼                        │
         │  🔷 istio-ingressgateway POD          📦 ingress-controller POD    │
         │     (standalone Envoy proxy)          (nginx / traefik / ...)      │
         │     programmed by Gateway CRD         programmed by Ingress        │
         │      │ decrypt TLS, match host            │ host/path rules        │
         │      ▼                                    ▼                        │
         │  ═══ ENTERS THE MESH 🔒 ═══           K8s Service (kube-proxy)     │
         │      │ VirtualService routing,            │                        │
         │      │ retries, mTLS, metrics             ▼                        │
         │      ▼                                Pod (no sidecar powers)      │
         │  jobpost-api Pod 🔷 (sidecar)                                      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  → SAME position in the network path — but the Istio edge pod      │
         │    is a FULL Envoy: every mesh feature applies from hop one        │
         │  → a K8s Ingress pod is only an HTTP router — mesh policy stops    │
         │    at its door — no mTLS, no weights, no fault injection           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [GATEWAY vs KUBERNETES INGRESS COMPARISON]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  KUBERNETES INGRESS          ISTIO GATEWAY             │
         │  ──────────────────          ─────────────────         │
         │  ✅ Simple host/path routing  ✅ Same + more            │
         │  ❌ No header manipulation    ✅ Full header control     │
         │  ❌ No mTLS termination       ✅ mTLS at the edge        │
         │  ❌ No weighted routing       ✅ % traffic split        │
         │  ❌ Limited TLS options       ✅ Advanced TLS/SNI        │
         │  ❌ No egress control         ✅ Egress gateway          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: the Gateway CRD is pure CONFIGURATION — the thing that
           actually moves packets is the istio-ingressgateway POD (an Envoy)
           → the CRD's selector labels bind the config to that pod
           → wrong labels = kubectl accepts the YAML but NO Envoy ever
             receives it (a silent failure — the classic gateway gotcha)
         - The Gateway resource itself may live in ANY namespace; putting it
           in istio-system is merely a common convention, not a requirement

    5.2  Gateway YAML — Accepting Traffic for anihpj
         - How to write a Gateway resource for anihpj.example.com
         - port 80 (HTTP) and port 443 (HTTPS with TLS certificate)
         - selector: istio: ingressgateway (which Envoy handles this)
         - hosts: ["anihpj.example.com"] (which hostnames to accept)
         [ANNOTATED Gateway YAML with every field explained]

         [COMPLETE ANNOTATED Gateway YAML for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3                          │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: anihpj-gateway          # ← name this gateway            │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      istio: ingressgateway       # ← which Envoy pod handles this │
         │    servers:                                                        │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 80               # ← accept HTTP on port 80        │
         │        name: http                                                  │
         │        protocol: HTTP                                              │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "anihpj.example.com"     # ← only accept this hostname     │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 443              # ← accept HTTPS on port 443      │
         │        name: https                                                 │
         │        protocol: HTTPS                                             │
         │      tls:                                                          │
         │        mode: SIMPLE            # ← TLS termination at gateway     │
         │        credentialName: anihpj-tls-cert  # ← K8s secret with cert │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "anihpj.example.com"                                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Applying the YAML is only half the job — always verify the config
           actually reached the edge Envoy:
           • kubectl get gateway → proves the API server ACCEPTED it, nothing
             more (a Gateway with a wrong selector still shows up here)
           • istioctl proxy-config listener → proves the edge Envoy is
             actually LISTENING (the test that matters)

         [VERIFY THE GATEWAY LANDED IN THE EDGE ENVOY — two-step check]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # step 1 — resource accepted? (necessary, NOT sufficient)         │
         │  kubectl get gateway anihpj-gateway -n anihpj                      │
         │  # NAME             AGE                                            │
         │  # anihpj-gateway   12s   ← exists in etcd, says nothing more      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # step 2 — did the edge Envoy get it? (the real test)             │
         │  istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/istio-ingressgateway \      │
         │      -n istio-system                                               │
         │  # ADDRESS  PORT  MATCH  DESTINATION                               │
         │  # 0.0.0.0  8080  ALL    Route: http.80   ← :80 server pushed      │
         │  # 0.0.0.0  8443  SNI    Route: https.443 ← :443 + TLS pushed      │
         │  # (pod ports 8080/8443 sit behind service ports 80/443)           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # step 2 empty? → selector matched no pod. Check the labels:      │
         │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system -l istio=ingressgateway          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.3  How Gateway Connects to VirtualService
         - Gateway: "I accept traffic on port 80 for anihpj.example.com"
         - VirtualService: "Route it to jobpost-api service on port 8000"
         - They are linked by the gateways field in VirtualService
         - Two-step pattern: Gateway defines port/host, VS defines routing
         [FLOW DIAGRAM: request → Gateway → VirtualService → Service → Pod]

         [GATEWAY + VIRTUALSERVICE — the mandatory two-resource pattern]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │   STEP 1: Gateway resource says "I accept this traffic"           │
         │   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │
         │   │  Gateway: anihpj-gateway                             │        │
         │   │  • listen on port 80/443                             │        │
         │   │  • for host: anihpj.example.com                      │        │
         │   │  • on: istio-ingressgateway pod                      │        │
         │   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
         │                          │                                         │
         │                          │ ← traffic arrives here                 │
         │                          ▼                                         │
         │   STEP 2: VirtualService says "now route it like this"            │
         │   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │
         │   │  VirtualService: jobpost-vs                          │        │
         │   │  • gateways: [anihpj-gateway]   ← reference gateway │        │
         │   │  • hosts: [anihpj.example.com]                       │        │
         │   │  • match: uri /jobs/*                                │        │
         │   │  • route: to: jobpost-api:8000                       │        │
         │   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
         │                          │                                         │
         │                          ▼                                         │
         │          Kubernetes Service: jobpost-api                           │
