         │                          │                                         │
         │                          ▼                                         │
         │          Pod: jobpost-api (with Envoy sidecar)                     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ⚠️  KEY RULE: Gateway WITHOUT VirtualService = traffic DROPPED    │
         │     VirtualService without Gateway = only mesh-internal routing    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - REAL SCENARIO — anihpj launch morning: the team applied the
           Gateway, DNS cutover went live... and every request to
           api.anihpj.com returned 404. kubectl showed the Gateway healthy.
           → cause: the VirtualService was still sitting in a git branch —
             the Gateway was accepting traffic with ZERO routing rules
           → fix took 30 seconds: kubectl apply the VirtualService with
             gateways: [anihpj-gateway] — the 404s stopped instantly
           → lesson: a Gateway alone NEVER routes; treat Gateway + VS as
             one deployment unit and always apply them together

    5.4  Common Gateway Patterns — Production-Ready Configurations
         - DEFINITION: a gateway "pattern" is a ready-made spec.servers[]
           recipe — the three below cover ~95% of real clusters and are the
           exact configs the ICA exam asks you to write from scratch
         - Why it matters: gateways fail silently (wrong selector, missing
           secret, absent VirtualService) → starting from a known-good
           pattern removes whole classes of launch-day surprises for anihpj
         - How it works: every pattern is just a different combination of
           port.protocol and the tls: block inside ONE Gateway resource
           • Pattern 1 HTTP-only    → dev clusters, quick demos
           • Pattern 2 HTTPS SIMPLE → the production baseline
           • Pattern 3 HTTP→HTTPS   → production + automatic 301 upgrade
         - All YAML below uses apiVersion networking.istio.io/v1 — the GA
           API since Istio 1.22; prefer it for everything you write today

         [PATTERN 1 — HTTP-ONLY GATEWAY (dev / demos, NEVER production)]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: anihpj-gateway-dev                                        │
         │    namespace: anihpj            # ← any namespace works            │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      istio: ingressgateway      # ← default edge pod's label       │
         │    servers:                                                        │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 80               # ← plaintext HTTP, no tls: block  │
         │        name: http                                                  │
         │        protocol: HTTP                                              │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "dev.anihpj.example.com" # ← keep dev hostnames explicit    │
         │  # ⚠️ credentials, cookies, JWTs travel UNENCRYPTED — dev only     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PATTERN 2 — HTTPS WITH credentialName (production baseline)]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: anihpj-gateway-https                                      │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      istio: ingressgateway                                         │
         │    servers:                                                        │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 443              # ← TLS port                       │
         │        name: https                                                 │
         │        protocol: HTTPS                                             │
         │      tls:                                                          │
         │        mode: SIMPLE             # ← terminate TLS at the edge      │
         │        credentialName: anihpj-tls-cert  # ← K8s TLS secret         │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "api.anihpj.com"                                            │
         │  # the secret must sit in the EDGE POD's namespace (istio-system   │
         │  # in the default install), whatever namespace the Gateway is in:  │
         │  #   kubectl create secret tls anihpj-tls-cert \                   │
         │  #     --cert=server.crt --key=server.key -n istio-system          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PATTERN 3 — HTTP→HTTPS REDIRECT via httpsRedirect: true (301)]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: anihpj-gateway                                            │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      istio: ingressgateway                                         │
         │    servers:                                                        │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 80                                                  │
         │        name: http                                                  │
         │        protocol: HTTP                                              │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "api.anihpj.com"                                            │
         │      tls:                                                          │
         │        httpsRedirect: true      # ← every HTTP request → 301       │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 443                                                 │
         │        name: https                                                 │
         │        protocol: HTTPS                                             │
         │      tls:                                                          │
         │        mode: SIMPLE                                                │
         │        credentialName: anihpj-tls-cert                             │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "api.anihpj.com"                                            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - What the client sees with Pattern 3 — the redirect never reaches
           any VirtualService; the gateway Envoy answers it directly:

         [REDIRECT ROUND-TRIP — WHAT ACTUALLY GOES OVER THE WIRE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  T=0ms:  GET http://api.anihpj.com/jobs/  ──────▶ gateway :80      │
         │  T=1ms:  ◀────── 301 Moved Permanently                             │
         │          Location: https://api.anihpj.com/jobs/                    │
         │  T=2ms:  client re-issues the request over TLS ──▶ gateway :443    │
         │  T=5ms:  TLS handshake (SIMPLE) → VirtualService → jobpost-api     │
         │  ✅ one extra round trip on FIRST contact; browsers cache the 301  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.5  Deep Dive — TLS Modes: SIMPLE vs MUTUAL vs PASSTHROUGH
         - DEFINITION: tls.mode on a Gateway server decides WHERE the TLS
           tunnel ends and WHO must prove their identity with a certificate
         - Why it matters: pick wrong and you either break every client
           (MUTUAL without client certs), lose end-to-end encryption
           guarantees, or double-terminate TLS — the ICA tests all modes
         - How it works: the gateway Envoy either TERMINATES the handshake
           itself (SIMPLE / MUTUAL — needs a cert secret) or forwards the
           still-encrypted bytes onward (PASSTHROUGH — no secret at all)

         [TLS MODE MATRIX — WHO HOLDS CERTS, WHO GETS VERIFIED]
         ┌──────────────────┬──────────┬──────────────┬───────────────────────┐
         │ tls.mode         │ GW cert? │ client cert? │ secret data keys      │
         ├──────────────────┼──────────┼──────────────┼───────────────────────┤
         │ SIMPLE           │ yes      │ no           │ tls.crt + tls.key     │
         │ MUTUAL           │ yes      │ REQUIRED     │ tls.crt + tls.key     │
         │                  │          │              │   + ca.crt            │
         │ PASSTHROUGH      │ no       │ no           │ none (SNI routing)    │
         │ AUTO_PASSTHROUGH │ no       │ no           │ none (SNI → cluster)  │
         └──────────────────┴──────────┴──────────────┴───────────────────────┘

         - Use cases, one line each:
           • SIMPLE           → public HTTPS for api.anihpj.com (the default)
           • MUTUAL           → B2B partners must present a client cert 🔒
           • PASSTHROUGH      → jobpost-api pod holds its own certificate;
                                the gateway must never see plaintext
           • AUTO_PASSTHROUGH → multi-cluster east-west gateways; the SNI
                                alone encodes the destination — no
                                VirtualService needed to route it
         - Secret contents differ by mode — the #1 exam trap:
           • SIMPLE: tls.crt + tls.key (kubectl create secret tls ...)
           • MUTUAL: tls.crt + tls.key + ca.crt (the CA that signed the
             CLIENT certificates — forget ca.crt and NO client can connect)
           • PASSTHROUGH / AUTO_PASSTHROUGH: no credentialName, no secret

         [SNI ROUTING — HOW PASSTHROUGH ROUTES WITHOUT DECRYPTING]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. client opens TCP to gateway :443, sends TLS ClientHello        │
         │     → the SNI field ("api.anihpj.com") is UNENCRYPTED by design    │
         │  2. gateway Envoy peeks at the SNI — nothing else is readable      │
         │  3. SNI matched against Gateway servers[].hosts                    │
         │  4. VirtualService tls: rules match on sniHosts → pick the         │
         │     destination service                                            │
         │  5. the encrypted stream is piped to the jobpost-api pod byte-     │
         │     for-byte — the POD's own certificate finishes the handshake 🔒 │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # VirtualService snippet used with PASSTHROUGH:                   │
         │  tls:                                                              │
         │  - match:                                                          │
         │    - port: 443                                                     │
         │      sniHosts: ["api.anihpj.com"]     # ← route key = SNI          │
         │    route:                                                          │
         │    - destination:                                                  │
         │        host: jobpost-api                                           │
         │        port:                                                       │
         │          number: 8000                                              │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [HANDSHAKE WALKTHROUGH — THE THREE MODES SIDE BY SIDE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SIMPLE — one-way TLS (public HTTPS default)                       │
         │  client ──ClientHello─────────▶ gateway                            │
         │  client ◀──server cert───────── gateway  client verifies gateway   │
         │  client ──encrypted HTTP─────▶ gateway → DECRYPTS → routes plain   │
         │           (then re-encrypts pod-to-pod with mesh mTLS 🔒)          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  MUTUAL — two-way TLS at the edge (B2B / partner APIs)             │
         │  client ◀──server cert───────── gateway  client verifies gateway   │
         │  client ──client cert────────▶ gateway  gateway verifies client    │
         │           against ca.crt from the secret                           │
         │  😈 no cert / untrusted cert → handshake FAILS, no HTTP at all     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  PASSTHROUGH — no termination at the edge (end-to-end TLS)         │
         │  client ──TLS bytes──────────▶ gateway ──TLS bytes──▶ backend pod  │
         │  gateway reads only the SNI; the BACKEND pod presents the cert     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.6  Deep Dive — Kubernetes Gateway API vs Istio APIs
         - DEFINITION: the Kubernetes Gateway API (gateway.networking.k8s.io)
           is the vendor-neutral SIG-Network standard for ingress + routing —
           one CRD set implemented by Istio, Envoy Gateway, nginx, GKE, ...
         - Why it matters: Istio treats it as the FUTURE default traffic API;
           new Istio docs lead with it, and the ICA may show either style
         - How it works: install the Gateway API CRDs, create a Gateway with
           gatewayClassName: istio, then attach HTTPRoute resources to it
           via parentRefs — Istio programs Envoy from those resources
           → big behavioral difference: an istio-class Gateway API Gateway
             AUTO-PROVISIONS its own Envoy Deployment + Service (you no
             longer pre-install a shared istio-ingressgateway pod)

         [CONCEPT MAP — ISTIO API ↔ KUBERNETES GATEWAY API]
         ┌──────────────────┬────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
         │ concept          │ Istio API              │ Kubernetes Gateway API   │
         ├──────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ edge entry point │ Gateway                │ Gateway                  │
         │ API group        │ networking.istio.io/v1 │ gateway.networking.k8s.io│
         │ HTTP routing     │ VirtualService (http:) │ HTTPRoute                │
         │ TCP / TLS routes │ VS tcp: / tls: blocks  │ TCPRoute / TLSRoute      │
         │ gRPC routing     │ VirtualService (http:) │ GRPCRoute                │
         │ traffic split    │ route[].weight         │ backendRefs[].weight     │
         │ header rewrite   │ headers.request.add    │ RequestHeaderModifier    │
         │ route → gateway  │ gateways: [gw-name]    │ parentRefs: [gw-name]    │
         │ edge proxy pod   │ pre-installed, shared  │ auto-provisioned per GW  │
         └──────────────────┴────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

         [GATEWAY API IN ACTION — anihpj EDGE, 90/10 CANARY INCLUDED]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1                          │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: anihpj-gw                                                 │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    gatewayClassName: istio      # ← Istio implements this Gateway  │
         │    listeners:                                                      │
         │    - name: http                                                    │
         │      hostname: "*.anihpj.com"   # ← wildcard listener              │
         │      port: 80                                                      │
         │      protocol: HTTP                                                │
         │      allowedRoutes:                                                │
         │        namespaces:                                                 │
         │          from: Same             # ← only routes from this ns       │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1                          │
         │  kind: HTTPRoute                                                   │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: jobpost-route                                             │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    parentRefs:                                                     │
         │    - name: anihpj-gw            # ← binds route to the Gateway     │
         │    hostnames: ["api.anihpj.com"]                                   │
         │    rules:                                                          │
         │    - matches:                                                      │
         │      - path:                                                       │
         │          type: PathPrefix                                          │
         │          value: /jobs           # ← same idea as a VS uri prefix   │
         │      backendRefs:                                                  │
         │      - name: jobpost-api        # ← 90/10 canary, GW-API style     │
         │        port: 8000                                                  │
         │        weight: 90                                                  │
         │      - name: jobpost-api-v2                                        │
         │        port: 8000                                                  │
         │        weight: 10                                                  │
         │  # applying this creates Deployment/anihpj-gw-istio +              │
         │  # Service/anihpj-gw-istio automatically — check with:             │
         │  #   kubectl get deploy,svc -n anihpj | grep anihpj-gw             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Adoption guidance for anihpj (and for the exam):
           • Gateway API CRDs are NOT bundled with Istio or Kubernetes —
             kubectl apply the release manifest from the gateway-api repo
             BEFORE creating any Gateway of class istio
           • both API families coexist happily in one mesh — migrate the
             edge hostname-by-hostname, no big-bang rewrite required
           • DestinationRule-level policy (circuit breaking, LB algorithm,
             subsets) still comes from Istio APIs — keep them alongside
           • mesh-internal (east-west) routing with HTTPRoute exists too:
             set parentRefs to kind: Service (the GAMMA initiative)
           • rule of thumb: NEW clusters → start with Gateway API at the
             edge; existing anihpj configs → migrate opportunistically

         🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: "Securing Edge Traffic with TLS" is graded under
         the Securing Workloads domain (25%), NOT Traffic Management — but
         it is TAUGHT here in 5.2 (credentialName + TLS block) and 5.5
         (SIMPLE/MUTUAL/PASSTHROUGH modes) because the Gateway resource is
         where edge TLS actually lives. If the exam asks you to secure
         edge traffic, come back to 5.2/5.5, not Chapter 12 (which covers
         MESH-INTERNAL mTLS between sidecars, a different problem).


Chapter 6: VirtualService — Smart Traffic Routing
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    6.1  What is a VirtualService?
         - A VirtualService is Istio's routing rules for a service
         - It overrides Kubernetes' default routing (simple round-robin)
         - Defines: WHERE traffic goes, HOW MUCH traffic, WITH WHAT conditions
         - Works for traffic from:
           • External (via Gateway)
           • Internal service-to-service calls within the mesh
         - Attaches to a Kubernetes Service by name
         [VIRTUALSERVICE CONCEPT DIAGRAM: traffic fork based on rules]

         [VIRTUALSERVICE — THE ROUTING BRAIN: hosts → http → match → route]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  VirtualService "jobpost-api-vs"   (namespace: anihpj)               │
         │                                                                      │
         │  hosts:                     ← WHOSE traffic these rules govern       │
         │  - jobpost-api                (the Kubernetes Service name)          │
         │      │                                                               │
         │      ▼                                                               │
         │  http:                      ← ordered rule list, walked TOP → BOTTOM │
         │      │                                                               │
         │      ├─ RULE 1  match: header x-user-type = premium                  │
         │      │          route: → jobpost-api subset v2        (A/B test)     │
         │      │                                                               │
         │      ├─ RULE 2  match: uri prefix /jobs/                             │
         │      │          route: → v1 weight 90 / v2 weight 10  (canary)       │
         │      │                                                               │
         │      └─ RULE 3  (no match block = catch-all default)                 │
         │                 route: → jobpost-api subset v1    (everyone else)    │
         │                                                                      │
         │  A request forks at the FIRST rule whose match succeeds — Envoy      │
         │  stops evaluating right there (first-match-wins, detailed in 6.9)    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: a VirtualService is a routing TABLE, not a proxy —
           istiod compiles it into each Envoy's route config, so rules add
           ZERO extra network hops
         - Golden rule: VirtualService = WHERE traffic goes (the GPS route);
           DestinationRule = HOW it behaves once it gets there (Chapter 7)

    6.2  Basic Routing — Send All Traffic to One Version
         - jobpost-api has v1 and v2 deployments running simultaneously
         - By default, Kubernetes alternates between both
         - VirtualService: "Send 100% of traffic to subset: v1"
         - Subset is defined in DestinationRule (covered next)
         [YAML + TRAFFIC FLOW DIAGRAM: all traffic pinned to v1]

         [PIN-TO-V1 YAML — annotated (current API: networking.istio.io/v1)]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1  ← "v1" since Istio 1.22 │
         │  kind: VirtualService                                        │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: jobpost-api-vs                                      │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    hosts:                                                    │
         │    - jobpost-api        ← attach to this K8s Service name    │
         │    http:                                                     │
         │    - route:             ← no match block = applies to ALL    │
         │      - destination:                                          │
         │          host: jobpost-api                                   │
         │          subset: v1     ← 100% pinned to v1; the subset is   │
         │                           defined in a DestinationRule (Ch 7)│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [REQUEST FLOW — before vs after the pin-to-v1 rule]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  BEFORE (plain Kubernetes Service — round-robin over ALL pods):  │
         │                                                                  │
         │   browser ─▶ jobpost-api Service ──┬──▶ v1 pod   (≈50%)          │
         │                                    └──▶ v2 pod   (≈50%)  ⚠️      │
         │   users randomly land on the untested v2                         │
         │                                                                  │
         │  AFTER (VirtualService pins subset v1):                          │
         │                                                                  │
         │   browser ─▶ Envoy sidecar ────────────▶ v1 pod   (100%) ✅      │
         │              v2 pods receive ZERO traffic until YOU flip the rule│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.3  Weight-Based Traffic Splitting (Canary Deployments)
         - Deploy jobpost-api:v2 (new version with better job search)
         - Start with 5% traffic to v2, 95% to v1
         - Gradually shift: 10%→25%→50%→100% as confidence grows
         - Roll back instantly by changing the weight — no downtime
         [YAML WALKTHROUGH + VISUAL: traffic percentage split diagram]

         [WEIGHT-BASED ROUTING — jobpost-api canary for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1               │
         │  kind: VirtualService                                  │
         │  metadata:                                             │
         │    name: jobpost-api-vs                                │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                   │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    hosts:                                              │
         │    - jobpost-api                                       │
         │    http:                                               │
         │    - route:                                            │
         │      - destination:                                    │
         │          host: jobpost-api                             │
         │          subset: v1    ← DestinationRule defines this  │
         │        weight: 90      ← 90% to stable version        │
         │      - destination:                                    │
         │          host: jobpost-api                             │
         │          subset: v2    ← New version being tested      │
         │        weight: 10      ← 10% canary traffic            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Weights are RELATIVE — Envoy normalizes them: 90+10, 9+1 and
           900+100 all produce the same 90%/10% split (sum need not be 100)
         - Per request, Envoy draws a random number 0-99: 0-89 → v1,
           90-99 → v2; over 1,000 requests that converges to ≈900/≈100
         - ⚠️ If a route names a subset that no DestinationRule defines,
           ALL traffic to that destination returns 503 — apply the
           DestinationRule first (or in the same kubectl apply -f)

    6.4  Header-Based Routing (A/B Testing)
         - Route specific users to a specific version based on HTTP headers
         - Example: if header x-user-type: premium → send to jobpost-api:v2
         - Example: if header x-canary: true → send to new anihpj UI
         - Useful for: beta testers, internal teams, feature flags
         [YAML + DECISION DIAGRAM: header present → v2, no header → v1]

         [A/B TESTING FLOW — header-based routing decision]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Incoming request to jobpost-api                                   │
         │                    │                                               │
         │          ┌─────────▼──────────┐                                   │
         │          │  Envoy checks:      │                                   │
         │          │  Header present?   │                                   │
         │          │  x-user-type:      │                                   │
         │          │  premium?          │                                   │
         │          └────────┬────────────┘                                   │
         │                   │                                                │
         │          ┌────────┴────────┐                                       │
         │         YES               NO                                       │
         │          │                 │                                       │
         │          ▼                 ▼                                       │
         │  [jobpost-api:v2]   [jobpost-api:v1]                               │
         │  (new UI / features)  (stable production)                          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  YAML that makes this work:                                        │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    http:                                                           │
         │    - match:                                                        │
         │      - headers:                          ← check header           │
         │          x-user-type:                                              │
         │            exact: premium                ← match value             │
         │      route:                                                        │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: jobpost-api                                         │
         │          subset: v2                      ← premium users → v2     │
         │    - route:                              ← default (no match)     │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: jobpost-api                                         │
         │          subset: v1                      ← everyone else → v1     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [A/B TESTING USE CASES for anihpj]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
         │  Header / Condition                  │  Route to                   │
         ├──────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
         │  x-user-type: premium                │  jobpost-api:v2 (premium UI)│
         │  x-canary: "true"                    │  jobpost-api:v2 (canary)    │
         │  x-internal-user: "1"               │  skills-svc:v2 (beta feats) │
         │  user-agent: contains "Mobile"       │ jobpost-api mobile-optimized│
         │  cookie: session=staging             │  all services:v2 staging    │
         │  Authorization: JWT with role=admin  │  admin-service              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

    6.5  URI-Based and Path-Based Routing
         - Route /jobs/ → jobpost-api
         - Route /users/ → user-service
         - Route /skills/ → skills-service
         - Single VirtualService can route by prefix, exact, regex
         [YAML: VirtualService routing all anihpj endpoints]

         [PATH-BASED ROUTING — all anihpj services through one VS]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    http:                                               │
         │    - match:                                            │
         │      - uri:                                            │
         │          prefix: /jobs/        → jobpost-api:8000      │
         │    - match:                                            │
         │      - uri:                                            │
         │          prefix: /users/       → user-service:8080     │
         │    - match:                                            │
         │      - uri:                                            │
         │          prefix: /skills/      → skills-service:8090   │
         │    - match:                                            │
         │      - uri:                                            │
         │          prefix: /locations/   → location-service:8070 │
         │    - route:                                            │
         │      - destination:            → default: jobpost-api  │
         │          host: jobpost-api                             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Rules are checked top → bottom, first match wins: keep the MOST
           specific prefix first and the default (no match) route LAST
         - Strip a prefix before forwarding with rewrite: match prefix
           /api/v2/ + "rewrite: uri: /" delivers /api/v2/users/123 to the
           upstream as /users/123 — the app never sees the version prefix

    6.6  Retries — Automatic Failure Recovery
         - Without Istio: if user-service returns a 503, anihpj fails
         - With Istio VirtualService retries: automatically retry up to 3 times
         - Configure: attempts, perTryTimeout, retryOn (which error codes)
         - Important: retries only safe for idempotent requests (GET, not POST)
         - anihpj use case: retry GET /users/{id}/ if user-service is momentarily down
         [RETRY FLOW DIAGRAM: first call fails → Envoy retries → success]

         [RETRY TIMELINE — how Envoy handles a failing user-service call]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Without retries (default Kubernetes):                             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  T=0ms:   jobpost-api → user-service → ❌ 503 Service Unavailable │
         │  T=1ms:   Envoy returns 503 to Django                             │
         │  T=2ms:   Django returns 500 to user browser                       │
         │  💥 User sees error even though user-service recovers in 200ms    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  With Istio retries (attempts: 3, perTryTimeout: 2s):             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  T=0ms:   jobpost-api → user-service → ❌ 503 (attempt 1)         │
         │  T=100ms: Envoy retries: → user-service → ❌ 503 (attempt 2)      │
         │  T=200ms: Envoy retries: → user-service → ✅ 200 OK (attempt 3)  │
         │  T=201ms: Django receives response normally                        │
         │  ✅ User never sees an error — blip absorbed by retry              │
         │                                                                    │
         │  YAML config that makes this happen:                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    http:                                                           │
         │    - retries:                                                      │
         │        attempts: 3           ← try up to 3 times                  │
         │        perTryTimeout: 2s     ← each attempt has 2s timeout        │
         │        retryOn: 5xx,gateway-error,reset  ← what errors to retry  │
         │      route:                                                        │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: user-service                                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Production-grade retryOn set: 5xx,connect-failure,refused-stream,
           reset — covers server errors, TCP connect failures and resets
         - Between tries Envoy inserts a small growing backoff (25ms+), so
           retries are spaced out, not machine-gunned at the failing service

         [VERIFY RETRY CONFIG — ask Envoy what it actually received]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  istioctl proxy-config route deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj \    │
         │    -o json | grep -B2 -A4 retryPolicy                          │
         │  # → "numRetries": 3, "perTryTimeout": "2s",                   │
         │  #   "retryOn": "5xx,gateway-error,reset"                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.7  Timeouts — Don't Wait Forever
         - Without Istio: your Django code hangs waiting for slow services
         - With Istio: set a global timeout per service (e.g., 5s for skills-svc)
         - Request takes >5s → Envoy returns 408/504, doesn't hang Django
         - anihpj use case: skills-service timeout 3s — fast degradation
         [YAML + TIMELINE DIAGRAM: timeout cut-off visualization]

         [TIMEOUT TIMELINE — how Istio prevents anihpj from hanging]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Without timeout (default):                                        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  T=0s:    jobpost-api → skills-service (slow, database overloaded)│
         │  T=10s:   Still waiting...                                         │
         │  T=30s:   Still waiting... Django thread is BLOCKED                │
         │  T=60s:   Django default timeout → returns error                   │
         │  💥 30-60 seconds of blocked threads = thread pool exhaustion      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  With Istio timeout (timeout: 3s):                                 │
         │                                                                    │
         │  T=0s:    jobpost-api → skills-service                             │
         │  T=3s:    ⏱️ Envoy timeout fires: returns 504 Gateway Timeout     │
         │  T=3.01s: Django gets 504, returns graceful degraded response      │
         │  ✅ Fast failure instead of slow hang — thread pool protected      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  COMBINED retry + timeout pattern for anihpj:                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
         │  │  timeout: 10s          ← total budget for the request    │     │
         │  │  retries:                                                 │     │
         │  │    attempts: 3                                            │     │
         │  │    perTryTimeout: 3s   ← 3 tries × 3s = 9s max          │     │
         │  │    retryOn: 5xx                                           │     │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
         │  Math: 3 attempts × 3s each = 9s, under the 10s total timeout ✅  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.8  Fault Injection — Testing Resilience
         - Intentionally inject failures to test how anihpj handles them
         - Two types:
           • Delay: "delay 50% of requests to user-service by 7 seconds"
           • Abort: "return HTTP 500 for 10% of requests to auth-service"
         - WHY: validate that timeouts, retries, circuit breakers work
         - How to inject: add fault block to VirtualService
         [FAULT INJECTION YAML + what it reveals about your system]

         [FAULT INJECTION YAML — testing anihpj resilience]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Test 1: Delay 30% of requests to user-service       │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    http:                                               │
         │    - fault:                                            │
         │        delay:                                          │
         │          fixedDelay: 7s     # inject 7 second delay   │
         │          percentage:                                   │
         │            value: 30        # affect 30% of requests  │
         │      route:                                            │
         │      - destination:                                    │
         │          host: user-service                            │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Test 2: Abort 10% of requests to auth-service       │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    http:                                               │
         │    - fault:                                            │
         │        abort:                                          │
         │          httpStatus: 500    # return 500 error        │
         │          percentage:                                   │
         │            value: 10        # affect 10% of requests  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Start small (value: 10) and raise gradually — watch p99 latency
           and error-rate dashboards while the fault is live
         - Expected result for anihpj: with the 7s delay active, the 3s
           timeout from 6.7 fires first and Django degrades gracefully
           instead of hanging — if it does NOT, you found a real bug cheaply
         - ⚠️ Fault injection cannot share a VirtualService with retries or
           timeout — keep a separate fault-only VS for test days (see 6.11)
         - 🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: "Using Fault Injection" is its own NAMED exam
           subtopic under Traffic Management (35%) — it is graded
           separately from resilience features (retries/timeouts/circuit
           breaking), even though you use it to TEST them

         [FAULT INJECTION TESTING WORKFLOW — a real resilience test day]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  STEP 1: baseline — capture normal Grafana numbers first        │
         │    847 req/s, 2.1% errors, p99 234ms  (write these down)        │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 2: apply a SMALL fault (delay: 3s @ value: 10) on a       │
         │    fault-only VirtualService, NOT the production one             │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 3: watch — does the CALLER'S timeout/retry/circuit        │
         │    breaker absorb it? p99 should rise but stay < timeout,       │
         │    error rate should stay near 2.1% (retries hide the delay)    │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 4: raise the fault (delay: 7s @ value: 30, or             │
         │    abort: 500 @ value: 10) — this is where real bugs show:      │
         │    • p99 exceeds the timeout → caller hangs (missing timeout)   │
         │    • error rate spikes 1:1 with abort % → no retries configured │
         │    • ALL callers fail together → no circuit breaker isolating   │
         │      the bad subset                                             │
         │                                                                  │
         │  STEP 5: remove the fault-only VS — never leave fault injection │
         │    active outside a test window                                 │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.9  Route Matching Precedence — How Envoy Chooses Routes
         - DEFINITION: when a VirtualService lists several http rules, Envoy
           walks them TOP → BOTTOM and uses the FIRST rule whose match
           succeeds — then STOPS. There is no "best match", only first match
         - Why it matters: the #1 VirtualService bug is a catch-all route
           placed ABOVE a specific route — the specific rule becomes dead
           code that never fires
         - The three uri match styles, most → least precise:
           • exact:  /healthz         → matches ONLY /healthz
           • prefix: /api/v1/         → matches /api/v1/jobs, /api/v1/users…
           • regex:  ^/api/v[12]/.*   → pattern match (CPU-costly, use last)
         - AND vs OR: conditions inside ONE match entry are ANDed; separate
           entries in the match list (or separate rules) are ORed
         - Ordering recipe: exact matches first → longer prefixes before
           shorter → regex → the catch-all default (no match block) LAST

         [FIRST-MATCH-WINS TRAP — why "my route isn't working"]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  REQUEST: GET /api/v1/users/123        (no special headers)        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ❌ BROKEN ORDER — catch-all placed first:                         │
         │     RULE 1: (no match = catch-all)          → v1  ◀ swallows ALL   │
         │     RULE 2: prefix /api/v1/ + x-canary hdr  → v2  ◀ NEVER reached  │
         │     symptom: canary testers keep landing on v1 "for no reason"     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ✅ CORRECT ORDER — specific first, general last:                  │
         │     RULE 1: uri exact /healthz              → health handler       │
         │     RULE 2: prefix /api/v1/ + x-canary hdr  → v2                   │
         │     RULE 3: prefix /api/v1/                 → v1 90% / v2 10%      │
         │     RULE 4: (no match = catch-all)          → v1                   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  the request above, walked through the CORRECT order:              │
         │     RULE 1: /api/v1/users/123 ≠ /healthz           → skip          │
         │     RULE 2: prefix ✓ but x-canary header missing   → skip          │
         │     RULE 3: prefix ✓ and no extra condition        → MATCH, STOP   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [MATCH TYPES — everything a VirtualService rule can match on]
         ┌───────────────┬──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
         │  Match on     │  YAML example            │  Typical use         │
         ├───────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │  uri exact    │  exact: /healthz         │  health, metrics     │
         │  uri prefix   │  prefix: /jobs/          │  path/API routing    │
         │  uri regex    │  regex: ^/api/v[12]/.*   │  variable patterns   │
         │  headers      │  x-canary: {exact: "1"}  │  A/B, canary users   │
         │  queryParams  │  version: {exact: v2}    │  ?version=v2 switch  │
         │  method       │  method: {exact: POST}   │  verb-based routing  │
         │  sourceLabels │  app: jobpost-api        │  route by CALLER     │
         │  port         │  port: 8000              │  multi-port services │
         └───────────────┴──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

         [COPY-PASTE PATTERNS — the five VirtualService moves you reuse]
         ┌──────────────────┬───────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
         │  Pattern         │  Use case             │  Key fields                 │
         ├──────────────────┼───────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
         │  Weighted canary │  90% v1 / 10% v2      │  weight: 90 + weight: 10    │
         │  Header A/B test │  premium users → v2   │  headers: x-user-type:      │
         │                  │                       │    exact: premium           │
         │  Path routing    │  /jobs/ → jobpost-api │  match: uri: prefix: /jobs/ │
         │  Fault injection │  chaos / resilience   │  fault: delay: | abort:     │
         │  Retry + timeout │  absorb 5xx blips     │  retries: {attempts: 3}     │
         │                  │                       │  + timeout: 5s              │
         └──────────────────┴───────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

    6.10  Sidecar CRD — Controlling Proxy Scope and Performance
         - DEFINITION: the Sidecar resource (networking.istio.io/v1) limits
           WHICH services an Envoy proxy is told about — its egress scope
         - Default behavior: EVERY sidecar receives config for EVERY service
           in the mesh — 500 services = 500 clusters inside EACH Envoy
         - jobpost-api actually calls only user-service, skills-service and
           auth-service; carrying the other 497 is pure memory + push waste
         - A Sidecar with no workloadSelector applies to ALL workloads in
           its namespace; config pushes get smaller AND faster
         - Not a security control by itself — it trims what the proxy KNOWS,
           it does not authenticate anything (pair with AuthorizationPolicy)
         - Exam cue: "reduce per-sidecar memory" or "limit reachable
           services" → the answer is the Sidecar CRD, not NetworkPolicy

         [SIDECAR EGRESS SCOPING — trim jobpost-api's view of the mesh]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: Sidecar                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: default          ← convention for namespace-wide scope    │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    egress:                                                         │
         │    - hosts:                                                        │
         │      - "./user-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local"    ← only the     │
         │      - "./skills-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local"  ← services     │
         │      - "./auth-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local"    ← truly used   │
         │      - "istio-system/*"   ← keep mesh infrastructure reachable     │
         │  # "./x" = service x in THIS namespace; "ns/*" = all of ns         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [MEMORY BEFORE / AFTER — Sidecar scoping on a 500-service mesh]
         ┌───────────────────────────┬─────────────┬──────────────────────┐
         │  Per-sidecar metric       │  Without    │  With Sidecar        │
         ├───────────────────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │  Clusters in Envoy config │  500+       │  ~10                 │
         │  Envoy memory (RSS)       │  ~300 MB    │  ~50 MB              │
         │  Pushed config size       │  100%       │  ~10% (90% smaller)  │
         │  Push time per change     │  seconds    │  milliseconds        │
         │  Config blast radius      │  whole mesh │  3 declared services │
         └───────────────────────────┴─────────────┴──────────────────────┘

         [VERIFY THE SHRINK — count clusters Envoy knows about]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj \  │
         │    | wc -l        # before: 500+ clusters → after: ~15         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.11  Application vs Mesh-Level Failure Recovery — Avoiding Conflicts
         - Your Django code AND Istio both recover from failures — and they
           do it INDEPENDENTLY: neither layer knows the other exists
         - Classic conflict: Django's requests.get(..., timeout=2) gives up
           at 2s, BEFORE Istio's 3s timeout — Envoy's retry helps nobody
         - Golden rule: app timeout ≥ (Istio attempts + 1) × perTryTimeout
           plus a buffer — or delete the app timeout and let Istio own it
         - Retry multiplication: app retries 3× × Envoy retries 3× = up to
           9 hits on an already-struggling user-service (a retry storm)
         - Decide ONE owner per concern: timeouts/retries in the mesh
           (visible, tunable without redeploys); business fallbacks (cached
           skills list, "try later" page) stay in Django

         [TIMEOUT CONFLICT — Django timeout=2s vs Istio timeout=3s]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Django view (application layer):                                  │
         │    requests.get("http://user-service:8080/users/",                 │
         │                 timeout=2)           ← app gives up after 2s       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  VirtualService (mesh layer):                                      │
         │    timeout: 3s                       ← Envoy's total budget        │
         │    retries:                                                        │
         │      attempts: 1                                                   │
         │      perTryTimeout: 2s               ← the retry needs ~2s more    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  What ACTUALLY happens when user-service stalls:                   │
         │  T=0.0s  Django sends request → its sidecar forwards it            │
         │  T=2.0s  Django's OWN timeout fires → Timeout exception 💥         │
         │          Envoy is still mid-attempt; its retry is now pointless    │
         │  T=3.0s  Envoy's timeout fires… into a connection Django closed    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  FIX (pick ONE owner for the timeout):                             │
         │    requests.get(..., timeout=5)      ← ≥ (1+1)×2s + buffer ✅      │
         │    …or remove Django's timeout and let the mesh enforce 3s         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [FAULT INJECTION vs RETRY/TIMEOUT — a documented limitation]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ❌ INVALID — fault + retries/timeout in ONE VirtualService:       │
         │     http:                                                          │
         │     - fault:   {delay: …}      injected faults IGNORE the retry    │
         │       retries: {attempts: 3}   and timeout policy sitting next     │
         │       timeout: 5s              to them — they will not "absorb"    │
         │                                your own injected failure           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ✅ WORKING PATTERN — two VirtualServices, swapped on demand:      │
         │     user-service-prod.yaml  → retries + timeout   (production)     │
         │     user-service-test.yaml  → fault block only    (chaos test)     │
         │     kubectl delete -f prod.yaml && kubectl apply -f test.yaml      │
         │     …run the experiment, then swap back                            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.12  Deep Dive — Retry Budget Math: Why Your Timeouts Fail
         - The #1 resilience misconfiguration: retry settings that can
           never finish inside the total timeout budget
         - Formula to memorize:  timeout ≥ (attempts + 1) × perTryTimeout
         - The "+1" is the INITIAL try — the attempts field counts only the
           RETRIES stacked on top of it, and nearly everyone forgets that
         - Envoy also inserts a small growing backoff (25ms+) between tries
           — leave headroom rather than budgeting to the exact second
         - Symptom of a blown budget: 504s plus FEWER retries in the metrics
           than you configured — the last tries were cut off mid-flight

         [RETRY BUDGET WORKSHEET — attempts: 2, perTryTimeout: 1s]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  FORMULA   timeout ≥ (attempts + 1) × perTryTimeout                │
         │            needed here: (2 + 1) × 1s = 3s                          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ❌ timeout: 2s — watch the budget blow up:                        │
         │  T=0.0s  try 1   starts ── stalls ── perTryTimeout at T=1.0s       │
         │  T=1.0s  retry 1 starts ── stalls ── perTryTimeout at T=2.0s       │
         │  T=2.0s  GLOBAL timeout fires → 504.  Retry 2 NEVER RUNS 💥        │
         │          ("attempts: 2" silently behaved like "attempts: 1")       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ✅ timeout: 4s — every try fits (3s needed ≤ 4s budget):          │
         │  T=0.0s  try 1   ── fails at 1.0s                                  │
         │  T=1.0s  retry 1 ── fails at 2.0s                                  │
         │  T=2.0s  retry 2 ── succeeds at 2.4s → 200 OK ✅                   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  anihpj audit — the 6.7 combo (timeout: 10s, attempts: 3,          │
         │  perTryTimeout: 3s) passes the naive 3×3s = 9s check, but the      │
         │  strict formula needs (3+1)×3s = 12s > 10s ⚠️ → the last retry     │
         │  can get cut short.  Fix: perTryTimeout: 2s, because               │
         │  (3+1)×2s = 8s ≤ 10s ✅                                            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ICA EXAM TRAP — spot the broken budget in seconds]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Given:   attempts: 3, perTryTimeout: 2s, timeout: 5s              │
         │  Math:    (3 + 1) × 2s = 8s needed, only 5s budget → ❌ broken     │
         │  Fixes:   raise timeout to ≥ 8s   OR   drop attempts to 1          │
         │           ((1 + 1) × 2s = 4s ≤ 5s ✅)                              │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Chapter 7: DestinationRule — Traffic Policies and Load Balancing
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    7.1  What is a DestinationRule?
         - DestinationRule defines POLICIES for traffic going to a specific service
         - Works AFTER VirtualService routing decides WHERE to send traffic
         - VirtualService = the "GPS route" (which road to take)
         - DestinationRule = the "driving rules" (speed limit, lane choice)
         - Defines: load balancing algorithm, connection pool size, circuit breaker,
           TLS mode, and named subsets (for canary routing)
         [VS + DR RELATIONSHIP DIAGRAM]

         [VIRTUALSERVICE + DESTINATIONRULE — how they work together]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  REQUEST: GET /jobs/                                               │
         │            │                                                       │
         │            ▼                                                       │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐                 │
         │  │  VirtualService: "WHERE should this go?"     │                 │
         │  │  → match /jobs/* → destination: jobpost-api  │                 │
         │  │  → weight: 90% → subset: v1                  │                 │
         │  │  → weight: 10% → subset: v2                  │                 │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬───────────────────┘                 │
         │                             │  "I chose subset: v1"               │
         │                             ▼                                      │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐                 │
         │  │  DestinationRule: "HOW should we send it?"   │                 │
         │  │  → subset v1 = pods with version: v1 label   │                 │
         │  │  → loadBalancer: LEAST_CONN                  │                 │
         │  │  → tls: mode: ISTIO_MUTUAL                   │                 │
         │  │  → outlierDetection (circuit breaker):       │                 │
         │  │      consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5             │                 │
         │  └──────────────────────────┬───────────────────┘                 │
         │                             │                                      │
         │                ┌────────────┼──────────────┐                      │
         │                ▼            ▼              ▼                       │
         │          [pod:v1-1]   [pod:v1-2]   [pod:v1-3]                     │
         │          (least conn picks the one with fewest active connections) │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.2  Defining Subsets (Required for Canary)
         - Subsets link labels on Pods to route targets in VirtualService
         - subset: v1 → select Pods with label version: v1
         - subset: v2 → select Pods with label version: v2
         - VirtualService uses "subset: v1" names → DestinationRule resolves them
         [LABEL → SUBSET → VIRTUALSERVICE connection diagram]

         [SUBSET RESOLUTION — how labels on Pods connect to routing]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  YOUR PODS:                        DESTINATIONRULE:               │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Pod jobpost-v1-abc   ──────────▶  subsets:                       │
         │    labels:                         - name: v1                     │
         │      app: jobpost-api               labels:                       │
         │      version: v1     ◀─ matches ──   version: v1                  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Pod jobpost-v2-xyz   ──────────▶  - name: v2                     │
         │    labels:                           labels:                      │
         │      app: jobpost-api                  version: v2                 │
         │      version: v2     ◀─ matches ──                                │
         │                                                                    │
         │  VIRTUALSERVICE uses:              RESULT:                        │
         │    destination:                    Envoy sends to pods matching   │
         │      host: jobpost-api             the label selector for the     │
         │      subset: v1      ─────────────▶ chosen subset ✅              │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ⚠️  COMMON MISTAKE: If pods are missing the version label,       │
         │     the subset resolves to ZERO endpoints → 503 errors!           │
         │     Always add: labels: version: v1 to all your Deployments       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.3  Load Balancing Algorithms
         - Default: ROUND_ROBIN (Kubernetes default, Envoy default)
         - LEAST_CONN: send to the pod with fewest active connections
         - RANDOM: random pod selection (surprisingly effective)
         - PASSTHROUGH: bypass Envoy load balancing
         - CONSISTENT_HASH: stick same user to same pod (session affinity)
           → anihpj use case: user-service sticky sessions by user ID header
         [ALGORITHM VISUALIZATION: traffic distribution comparison]

         [LOAD BALANCING ALGORITHMS — visual comparison]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  3 pods of user-service: [Pod A] [Pod B] [Pod C]                  │
         │  10 incoming requests numbered 1-10                                │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ROUND_ROBIN (default):                                            │
         │  1→A  2→B  3→C  4→A  5→B  6→C  7→A  8→B  9→C  10→A              │
         │  Each pod: ~3-4 requests. Fair, but ignores pod health/load       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  LEAST_CONN (recommended for anihpj):                             │
         │  Pod A: 2 active, Pod B: 0 active, Pod C: 5 active                │
         │  Next request → Pod B (fewest active connections)                  │
         │  Best for: variable-duration requests (some Django views are slow) │
         │                                                                    │
         │  RANDOM:                                                           │
         │  1→C  2→A  3→A  4→B  5→C  6→A  7→B  8→C  9→B  10→A              │
         │  Statistically uniform at scale. Simplest. Works well in practice │
         │                                                                    │
         │  CONSISTENT_HASH (by header):                                     │
         │  User 42 → always → Pod A (same session, warm cache)              │
         │  User 99 → always → Pod B                                         │
         │  User 7  → always → Pod C                                         │
         │  Best for: user-service with per-user caches                      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  CONSISTENT_HASH YAML for user-service:                           │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                                  │
         │      loadBalancer:                                                 │
         │        consistentHash:                                             │
         │          httpHeaderName: x-user-id  ← route by this header       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [WHEN TO USE WHICH ALGORITHM — decision guide for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────┬───────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
         │  Service           │  Algorithm    │  Why                         │
         ├────────────────────┼───────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
         │  jobpost-api       │  LEAST_CONN   │  Mix of fast/slow views      │
         │  user-service      │  CONSISTENT   │  Per-user cache warmth       │
         │  skills-service    │  ROUND_ROBIN  │  Uniform stateless queries   │
         │  auth-service      │  RANDOM       │  Very fast, stateless        │
         │  search-service    │  LEAST_CONN   │  Variable search complexity  │
         └────────────────────┴───────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

         - NAMING UPDATE (Istio 1.22+): LEAST_CONN above is the legacy
           alias — modern Istio calls the same algorithm LEAST_REQUEST,
           and LEAST_REQUEST (not ROUND_ROBIN) is Istio's ACTUAL default
           when you configure no loadBalancer at all
         - Envoy's own default is ROUND_ROBIN — that is why older docs
           (and the first bullet above) call it "the default"; Istio
           overrides it per-cluster
         - Sticky sessions at scale get two extra algorithm choices,
           RING_HASH and MAGLEV → full 7-algorithm tour in section 7.8

    7.4  Circuit Breaking (Outlier Detection)
         - What is a circuit breaker? (borrowed from electrical engineering)
         - CLOSED: requests flow normally
         - OPEN: service is failing → Envoy STOPS sending requests to it
         - HALF-OPEN: after a pause, try again — if OK, close circuit
         - Configuration in DestinationRule: outlierDetection
           • consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5 → after 5 errors, eject host
           • interval: 30s → check every 30 seconds
           • baseEjectionTime: 30s → keep ejected for 30 seconds
           • maxEjectionPercent: 50 → never eject more than 50% of hosts
         - anihpj use case: if auth-service returns 5 consecutive 503s →
           stop sending requests, return error immediately, spare the service
         [CIRCUIT BREAKER STATE MACHINE DIAGRAM: Closed → Open → Half-Open]

         [CIRCUIT BREAKER STATE MACHINE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                        │
         │  ┌─────────────┐   5 errors    ┌─────────────────┐    │
         │  │   CLOSED    │ ─────────────▶│     OPEN        │    │
         │  │             │               │                 │    │
         │  │  Requests   │               │ Requests fail   │    │
         │  │  flow ✅    │               │ immediately ❌  │    │
         │  │  Success    │               │ (saves service) │    │
         │  └─────────────┘               └────────┬────────┘    │
         │         ▲                               │              │
         │         │ test request             baseEjectionTime   │
         │         │ succeeds                      │              │
         │         │                     ┌─────────┴────────┐    │
         │         └─────────────────────│   HALF-OPEN      │    │
         │                               │                  │    │
         │                               │ Allow 1 test     │    │
         │                               │ request through  │    │
         │                               └──────────────────┘    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.5  Connection Pool Settings
         - Control how many concurrent connections Envoy allows per service
         - HTTP settings: http1MaxPendingRequests, http2MaxRequests
         - TCP settings: maxConnections, connectTimeout
         - anihpj use case: limit skills-service to 100 concurrent connections
           (protect a slow service from being overwhelmed)
         [YAML + DIAGRAM: connection pool as a queue with a max size]

         [CONNECTION POOL — visualizing request queuing]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  skills-service connection pool config:                            │
         │    connectionPool:                                                 │
         │      http:                                                         │
         │        http1MaxPendingRequests: 100   ← queue max                 │
         │        http2MaxRequests: 200           ← concurrent max           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  VISUAL:                                                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Incoming requests ──▶ [=====QUEUE=====|100 max|] ──▶ skills-svc  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Scenario A (normal):  50 requests → queue at 50% — ✅ OK        │
         │  Scenario B (busy):   100 requests → queue FULL → 503 returned   │
         │  Scenario C (spike):  200 requests → 100 queued, 100 rejected    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WHY THIS HELPS: Without limit, 1000 requests queue up in Envoy  │
         │  consuming memory until skills-svc falls over entirely.            │
         │  With limit: 100 get served, rest get fast 503 → caller can retry │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.6  Complete DestinationRule Example for anihpj
         [ANNOTATED YAML: full DestinationRule for jobpost-api with
          v1/v2 subsets, LEAST_CONN load balancing, circuit breaker,
          mTLS policy, and connection pool limits]

         [COMPLETE DestinationRule YAML for jobpost-api]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3                          │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: jobpost-api-dr                                            │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    host: jobpost-api                                               │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                                  │
         │      loadBalancer:                                                 │
         │        simple: LEAST_CONN        ← smart load balancing           │
         │      connectionPool:                                               │
         │        tcp:                                                        │
         │          maxConnections: 100                                       │
         │        http:                                                       │
         │          http1MaxPendingRequests: 50                               │
         │          http2MaxRequests: 100                                     │
         │      outlierDetection:           ← circuit breaker                │
         │        consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5  ← open after 5 errors        │
         │        interval: 30s               ← check every 30s              │
         │        baseEjectionTime: 30s       ← eject bad pod for 30s       │
         │        maxEjectionPercent: 50      ← never eject all pods         │
         │      tls:                                                          │
         │        mode: ISTIO_MUTUAL          ← enforce mTLS                 │
         │    subsets:                                                        │
         │    - name: v1                                                      │
         │      labels:                                                       │
         │        version: v1                ← stable version                │
         │    - name: v2                                                      │
         │      labels:                                                       │
         │        version: v2                ← canary version                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.7  Deep Dive — Circuit Breaker States: CLOSED → OPEN → HALF-OPEN
         - DEFINITION: a circuit breaker is a per-pod health switch inside
           Envoy — it counts failures for every backend pod and "trips"
           (stops sending that pod traffic) once failures cross a threshold
         - Why it matters: without one, every caller keeps hammering a
           dying pod → requests queue behind it → caller threads block →
           the failure CASCADES upstream:
           • one sick auth-service pod → jobpost-api workers stuck waiting
           • jobpost-api runs out of workers → ALL of anihpj goes down
         - How it works: Envoy's outlierDetection analysis runs every
           interval (30s here), ejects pods that crossed the error
           threshold, and re-admits them after a cooling-off period — the
           classic CLOSED → OPEN → HALF-OPEN breaker pattern
         - The canonical anihpj parameters — identical in EVERY example in
           this chapter, memorize them once:
           • consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5 → trip after 5 straight failures
           • interval: 30s → the error-analysis sweep runs every 30 seconds
           • baseEjectionTime: 30s → the first ejection lasts 30 seconds
           • maxEjectionPercent: 50 → at most half the pool ejected at once
         - Repeat offenders sit out longer: ejection time =
           baseEjectionTime × times-ejected (30s → 60s → 90s → ...)
         - Envoy reality check: there is no literal probe request — in
           HALF-OPEN the pod simply rejoins the pool and the next few REAL
           requests are the probe; 5 more errors send it straight back

         [CIRCUIT BREAKER STATE MACHINE — transitions & canonical params]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  STATE 1: CLOSED   (normal — pod is in the load-balancing pool)    │
         │    • every request is forwarded; Envoy counts errors per pod       │
         │    • consecutive-error counter: 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 = threshold!     │
         │    • any single success resets the counter back to 0               │
         │             │                                                      │
         │             │  TRIP CONDITION: consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5         │
         │             │  (5 straight 502/503/504 or TCP connect failures)    │
         │             ▼                                                      │
         │  STATE 2: OPEN     (tripped — pod EJECTED from the pool)           │
         │    • Envoy sends this pod NOTHING; remaining pods take over        │
         │    • callers never block on the sick pod → no cascade upstream     │
         │    • guard rail: maxEjectionPercent: 50 → with 4 pods, at most     │
         │      2 are ever OPEN at once — 2 always keep serving               │
         │             │                                                      │
         │             │  COOL-OFF: baseEjectionTime: 30s expires             │
         │             │  (2nd ejection waits 60s, 3rd waits 90s, ...)        │
         │             ▼                                                      │
         │  STATE 3: HALF-OPEN (probation — pod quietly re-admitted)          │
