         │    • pod rejoins the pool; live traffic is the probe               │
         │    • requests succeed → error count stays 0 → back to CLOSED ✅     │
         │    • 5 more failures → re-ejected → back to OPEN, for longer ❌     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Sweep cadence: the outlier analysis runs every interval: 30s      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [OUTLIER DETECTION TUNING TABLE — meaning, defaults, symptoms]
         ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────┬──────┬───────────────────┐
         │ Field              │ Meaning            │ Safe │ Symptom if wrong  │
         ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
         │ consecutiveGateway │ 502/503/504 + TCP  │ 5    │ 1-2: pods flap on │
         │ Errors             │ connect failures   │      │ one blip; 50: it  │
         │                    │ count toward trip  │      │ never trips at all│
         ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
         │ consecutive5xx     │ app 5xx only; TCP  │ (off)│ conn-refused pod  │
         │ Errors             │ failures are NOT   │      │ is never ejected  │
         │                    │ counted            │      │                   │
         ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
         │ interval           │ how often the      │ 30s  │ 300s: a sick pod  │
         │                    │ ejection analysis  │      │ serves errors for │
         │                    │ sweep runs         │      │ 5 whole minutes   │
         ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
         │ baseEjectionTime   │ length of the 1st  │ 30s  │ 1h: capacity stays│
         │                    │ ejection (x2, x3   │      │ down long after   │
         │                    │ ... on repeats)    │      │ the pod recovers  │
         ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
         │ maxEjectionPercent │ cap on % of pool   │ 50   │ 100: breaker can  │
         │                    │ ejected at the     │      │ eject EVERY pod = │
         │                    │ same time          │      │ self-made outage  │
         ├────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────┼───────────────────┤
         │ minHealthPercent   │ below this healthy │ 0    │ set high on small │
         │                    │ %, ejection is     │ (off)│ pools: ejection   │
         │                    │ suspended entirely │      │ quietly disables  │
         └────────────────────┴────────────────────┴──────┴───────────────────┘

         [REAL SCENARIO TIMELINE — skills-service bad deploy vs the breaker]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Fleet: 4 skills-service pods behind :8090; mesh baseline          │
         │  847 req/s, 2.1% errors, p99 234ms. Pod 3 ships a bad deploy.      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  T=0s     pod 3 starts timing out; service-wide numbers hit        │
         │           8.1% errors / p99 1240ms — dashboards go red             │
         │  T=4s     pod 3 returns its 5th consecutive 503                    │
         │           → circuit OPENS: pod 3 ejected (1/4 = 25% ≤ 50% cap)     │
         │  T=4-34s  3 healthy pods absorb the load; caller-visible           │
         │           errors drop back under 1%                                │
         │  T=34s    baseEjectionTime: 30s expires → HALF-OPEN:               │
         │           pod 3 re-admitted, live requests probe it                │
         │  T=35s    still broken → 5 fast failures → OPEN again              │
         │           (2nd ejection = 60s this time)                           │
         │  T=95s    the bad deploy is rolled back; pod 3 restarts clean      │
         │  T=97s    probes succeed → CLOSED ★ users never saw an outage      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.8  Load Balancing — All 7 Algorithms and the Decision Guide
         - DEFINITION: the load-balancing algorithm is the rule Envoy uses
           to pick WHICH pod gets each request, AFTER VirtualService
           routing and DestinationRule subset selection narrowed the pool
         - Why it matters: the right algorithm hides slow pods, keeps
           caches warm, and survives scaling events; the wrong one sends
           your longest Django queries to the busiest pod
         - Naming update (Istio 1.22+): LEAST_CONN is a DEPRECATED alias —
           the current name is LEAST_REQUEST, and LEAST_REQUEST (not
           ROUND_ROBIN) is Istio's ACTUAL default when nothing is set
         - How LEAST_REQUEST picks: "power of two choices" — sample 2
           random pods, send to the one with fewer active requests;
           near-optimal spread at a fraction of the bookkeeping cost
         - RING_HASH and MAGLEV are not standalone simple: values — they
           are the two hash implementations selectable UNDER
           consistentHash (ketama ring vs Google's Maglev lookup table)

         [ALL 7 LOAD BALANCING ALGORITHMS — the complete Istio menu]
         ┌─────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┬────────────┐
         │ Algorithm       │ How it picks the pod                │ Sticky?    │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ ROUND_ROBIN     │ each pod in turn: 1 → 2 → 3 → 1 ... │ no         │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ LEAST_REQUEST   │ P2C: sample 2 random pods, send to  │ no         │
         │ (THE DEFAULT)   │ the one with fewer active requests  │            │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ RANDOM          │ any pod, uniformly at random        │ no         │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ PASSTHROUGH     │ no LB — connect to the original IP  │ n/a        │
         │                 │ the client itself resolved          │            │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ CONSISTENT_HASH │ hash a request key (header, cookie, │ YES        │
         │                 │ source IP, query param) → same pod  │            │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ RING_HASH       │ consistent-hash impl #1: ketama     │ YES        │
         │ (under c-hash)  │ ring; ~1/N keys move on scaling     │            │
         ├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┤
         │ MAGLEV          │ consistent-hash impl #2: O(1) table │ YES        │
         │ (under c-hash)  │ lookup; evenest spread, big pools   │            │
         └─────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┴────────────┘

         [LB DECISION TREE — pick your algorithm in 4 questions]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Q1: Must the same user/key always land on the same pod?           │
         │      YES → CONSISTENT_HASH on a key (header/cookie/source IP)      │
         │            • medium pool, minimal churn on scale → RING_HASH       │
         │            • large pool, fastest + evenest spread → MAGLEV         │
         │      NO ↓                                                          │
         │  Q2: Do request durations vary a lot? (slow Django views,          │
         │      complex searches mixed with quick lookups)                    │
         │      YES → LEAST_REQUEST — the default; you may set nothing        │
         │      NO ↓                                                          │
         │  Q3: Uniform + stateless, want strict predictable fairness?        │
         │      YES → ROUND_ROBIN        NO → RANDOM (cheapest of all)        │
         │  Q4: Client does its own load balancing (smart gRPC client)?       │
         │      YES → PASSTHROUGH — Envoy steps aside entirely                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ANIHPJ LB GUIDE — service-by-service, reaffirming the 7.3 picks]
         ┌───────────────────────┬─────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
         │ Service               │ Algorithm       │ Why                      │
         ├───────────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ jobpost-api :8000     │ LEAST_REQUEST   │ fast + slow Django views │
         │ user-service :8080    │ CONSISTENT_HASH │ per-user cache warmth —  │
         │                       │ (x-user-id)     │ same user → same pod     │
         │ skills-service :8090  │ ROUND_ROBIN     │ uniform stateless reads  │
         │ location-service :8070│ ROUND_ROBIN     │ uniform geo lookups      │
         │ auth-service          │ RANDOM          │ tiny, fast, stateless    │
         │ search-service        │ LEAST_REQUEST   │ query cost varies wildly │
         │ notification-service  │ ROUND_ROBIN     │ fire-and-forget fan-out  │
         └───────────────────────┴─────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

         - Same picks as section 7.3 — only the spelling is modern:
           LEAST_CONN (legacy alias) → LEAST_REQUEST (current name)

         [ANNOTATED YAML — modern LB config, apiVersion v1 (Istio 1.22+)]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1    ← v1 since Istio 1.22       │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: search-service-lb                                         │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    host: search-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local                   │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                                  │
         │      loadBalancer:                                                 │
         │        simple: LEAST_REQUEST      ← modern name for LEAST_CONN     │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: user-service-lb                                           │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    host: user-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local                     │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                                  │
         │      loadBalancer:                                                 │
         │        consistentHash:                                             │
         │          httpHeaderName: x-user-id   ← the hash key                │
         │          maglev:                                                   │
         │            tableSize: 65537   ← prime; bigger = smoother spread    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [VERIFY — which algorithm is Envoy really using?]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj \    │
         │      --fqdn user-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local -o json \        │
         │      | grep -i lbPolicy                                            │
         │  "lbPolicy": "MAGLEV"     ← the DestinationRule took effect ✅      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.9  Deep Dive — Locality Load Balancing: Region → Zone → Sub-Zone Failover
         - DEFINITION: locality load balancing makes Envoy prefer backend
           pods that are topologically CLOSE to the caller (same zone) and
           spill over to farther pods only when the close ones fail
         - Why it matters (milliseconds AND money): every major cloud
           bills cross-zone traffic, and a same-zone hop is far faster
           than a cross-zone one — at anihpj's 847 req/s peak that is both
           a latency win and a real invoice line
         - Where the location comes from: Kubernetes NODE labels — Istiod
           reads them and stamps every endpoint with a locality string
           "region/zone/subzone"
         - Two modes inside localityLbSetting (mutually exclusive):
           • distribute → YOU set the traffic split per origin locality
           • failover   → Envoy keeps 100% in-zone until pods fail, then
             walks outward: sub-zone → zone → region → anywhere
         - HARD REQUIREMENT ⚠️: locality LB only activates when the same
           DestinationRule ALSO configures outlierDetection — with no
           health signal Envoy cannot know when to fail over, and Istio
           silently ignores your locality settings

         [TOPOLOGY LABEL HIERARCHY — how a pod learns where it lives]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  NODE labels (set by the cloud provider or cluster admin):         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  topology.kubernetes.io/region: eu-west-1        ← REGION          │
         │     │    "which geography" (Dublin)                                │
         │     └──▶ topology.kubernetes.io/zone: eu-west-1a ← ZONE            │
         │             │    "which datacenter building"                       │
         │             └──▶ topology.istio.io/subzone: rack7 ← SUB-ZONE       │
         │                     "which rack/cell" — Istio-only, optional       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  → Istiod stamps every endpoint: "eu-west-1/eu-west-1a/rack7"      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  PREFERENCE ORDER for a caller in eu-west-1/eu-west-1a/rack7:      │
         │  1. same sub-zone  eu-west-1/eu-west-1a/rack7  (fastest, free)     │
         │  2. same zone      eu-west-1/eu-west-1a/*                          │
         │  3. same region    eu-west-1/*                 ($ cross-zone)      │
         │  4. anywhere       *                           ($$ cross-region)   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ANNOTATED YAML — localityLbSetting.distribute for skills-service]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: skills-service-locality                                   │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    host: skills-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local                   │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                                  │
         │      loadBalancer:                                                 │
         │        simple: ROUND_ROBIN        ← 7.3 / 7.8 choice kept          │
         │        localityLbSetting:                                          │
         │          enabled: true                                             │
         │          distribute:              ← weighted-spread mode           │
         │          - from: eu-west-1/eu-west-1a/*  ← callers in zone 1a      │
         │            to:                                                     │
         │              "eu-west-1/eu-west-1a/*": 70  ← keep 70% in-zone      │
         │              "eu-west-1/eu-west-1b/*": 20                          │
         │              "eu-west-1/eu-west-1c/*": 10                          │
         │      outlierDetection:            ← REQUIRED — without this        │
         │        consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5     block, the locality         │
         │        interval: 30s                   settings are silently       │
         │        baseEjectionTime: 30s           IGNORED by Istio            │
         │        maxEjectionPercent: 50                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [THE SILENT TRAP — no outlierDetection means NO failover]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Setup: skills-service pods in 3 zones; callers in eu-west-1a.     │
         │  At T=0 every skills-service pod in zone 1a crashes.               │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITH outlierDetection (correct config):                           │
         │  T=0s    1a pods begin failing — 8.1% errors, p99 1240ms           │
         │  T=~5s   5 consecutive gateway errors → 1a pods ejected            │
         │  T=5s+   locality failover: traffic shifts to 1b / 1c pods ✅       │
         │  T=35s   HALF-OPEN probes keep checking zone 1a for recovery       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITHOUT outlierDetection (the trap):                              │
         │  T=0s    1a pods begin failing                                     │
         │  T=∞     Envoy has NO health signal → keeps preferring the         │
         │          local, DEAD zone → hard errors until a human acts ❌       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  RULE: every DestinationRule that enables localityLbSetting        │
         │  MUST also carry the canonical outlierDetection block (7.4).       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Rollout tip: start with failover mode (nothing to maintain),
           switch to distribute only when you need cost-shaping
           percentages per zone


Chapter 8: ServiceEntry — Connecting to External Services
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    8.1  The Problem: Calling External APIs from Within the Mesh
         - By default, Istio (in REGISTRY_ONLY mode) blocks all external traffic
         - anihpj needs to call: external payment gateway, email service (SendGrid),
           S3 for media files, third-party job board API
         - Without ServiceEntry: HTTP 502 when calling external-api.com
         - 🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: "Connecting In-Mesh Workloads to External
           Workloads and Services" covers BOTH directions — this chapter
           teaches mesh → external (ServiceEntry, below); for the reverse
           direction, an external VM joining the mesh via WorkloadEntry,
           see 16.2
         [BLOCKED TRAFFIC DIAGRAM: egress blocked without ServiceEntry]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  REGISTRY_ONLY mode, NO ServiceEntry for api.stripe.com yet:       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [jobpost-api Pod]                                                 │
         │   app ── "POST https://api.stripe.com/v1/charges" ──▶ (sidecar)    │
         │                                                                    │
         │   [Envoy sidecar] looks up its cluster table:                      │
         │     outbound|443||api.stripe.com ............ NOT FOUND ❌          │
         │     → the request falls into Envoy's BlackHoleCluster              │
         │                                                                    │
         │   plain HTTP call → Envoy answers 502 Bad Gateway                  │
         │   HTTPS/TCP call  → connection dropped — no bytes leave the pod    │
         │                                                                    │
         │   api.stripe.com ......... never contacted (mesh = walled garden)  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  FIX: apply a ServiceEntry → istiod pushes a new Envoy cluster     │
         │       via xDS → the very same call now reaches Stripe ✅            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ISTIO OUTBOUND TRAFFIC MODES — three choices]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  Mode 1: ALLOW_ANY (default in older Istio)                       │
         │  ─────────────────                                                 │
         │  All external traffic passes through uncontrolled                  │
         │  No policies applied to external calls                             │
         │  Easy to set up, less secure                                       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Mode 2: REGISTRY_ONLY (recommended for production)               │
         │  ──────────────────────                                            │
         │  Only registered ServiceEntry hosts allowed out                   │
         │  Unknown external calls: BLOCKED with 502                         │
         │  Forces explicit declaration of all external dependencies          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Mode 3: Egress Gateway (most controlled)                         │
         │  ─────────────────────                                             │
         │  ALL external traffic forced through one egress Pod               │
         │  Central audit point for all outbound traffic                     │
         │  Required for strict compliance environments                       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  For anihpj:                                                       │
         │  Use REGISTRY_ONLY + ServiceEntry for each external API           │
         │  Add Egress Gateway for payment APIs (PCI compliance)             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: outboundTrafficPolicy — the meshConfig switch deciding
           what Envoy does with traffic to hosts OUTSIDE the service registry
           → ALLOW_ANY: pass it through blindly (no mTLS, no metrics, no policy)
           → REGISTRY_ONLY: black-hole it (HTTP 502; raw TCP connection reset)
         - Real scenario: anihpj's first REGISTRY_ONLY rollout — password-reset
           emails silently stopped; sidecar access logs showed 502s against
           cluster "BlackHoleCluster" for api.sendgrid.com → nobody had
           registered SendGrid → ServiceEntry applied → mail queue drained
         - Check which mode YOUR mesh runs before assuming anything:

         [CHECK & SWITCH THE OUTBOUND TRAFFIC MODE — commands]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ kubectl get configmap istio -n istio-system -o yaml \           │
         │      | grep -A1 outboundTrafficPolicy                              │
         │  outboundTrafficPolicy:                                            │
         │    mode: REGISTRY_ONLY            ← anihpj production setting      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # switch (only AFTER ServiceEntries exist — see 8.4):             │
         │  $ istioctl install --set \                                        │
         │      meshConfig.outboundTrafficPolicy.mode=REGISTRY_ONLY -y        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.2  ServiceEntry YAML — Registering External Services
         - Register external.payments-api.com as a known service in the mesh
         - After registration: Envoy applies Istio policies to external calls
         - Specify: hosts, ports, protocol (HTTP, HTTPS, TCP)
         - Now you can add: timeouts, retries, mTLS to external API calls
         [YAML + FLOW DIAGRAM: anihpj → ServiceEntry → external API]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. kubectl apply -f stripe-serviceentry.yaml                      │
         │              │                                                     │
         │              ▼                                                     │
         │  2. istiod adds api.stripe.com to the mesh service registry        │
         │              │                                                     │
         │              ▼                                                     │
         │  3. istiod builds Envoy cluster  outbound|443||api.stripe.com      │
         │     and pushes it to EVERY sidecar in the mesh (xDS/CDS)           │
         │              │                                                     │
         │              ▼                                                     │
         │  4. [jobpost-api Pod] calls https://api.stripe.com                 │
         │     sidecar: "known host!" → timeout/retry/TLS policies applied    │
         │              │                                                     │
         │              ▼                                                     │
         │  5. api.stripe.com answers — and the call now shows up in Kiali,   │
         │     metrics and access logs like any in-mesh request               │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [COMPLETE ServiceEntry YAML for anihpj external services]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Register Stripe payments API                                    │
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3                          │
         │  kind: ServiceEntry                                                │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: stripe-payments                                           │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    hosts:                                                          │
         │    - api.stripe.com              ← external hostname              │
         │    ports:                                                          │
         │    - number: 443                                                   │
         │      name: https                                                   │
         │      protocol: HTTPS                                               │
         │    location: MESH_EXTERNAL        ← tells Istio this is external  │
         │    resolution: DNS               ← resolve via DNS                │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  # Register SendGrid email API                                     │
         │  kind: ServiceEntry                                                │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    hosts: [api.sendgrid.com]                                       │
         │    ports: [{number: 443, name: https, protocol: HTTPS}]           │
         │    location: MESH_EXTERNAL                                         │
         │    resolution: DNS                                                 │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  # Register AWS S3 (for media uploads)                            │
         │  kind: ServiceEntry                                                │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    hosts: ["*.amazonaws.com"]    ← wildcard for all S3 regions    │
         │    ports: [{number: 443, name: https, protocol: HTTPS}]           │
         │    location: MESH_EXTERNAL                                         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: resolution — tells Envoy HOW to find IP addresses for
           the hosts you declared (DNS lookup? fixed list? don't bother?)
         - DEFINITION: exportTo — visibility of this ServiceEntry; the DEFAULT
           is "*" (every namespace in the mesh can route to it)
           → set exportTo: ["."] to keep an entry private to its own
             namespace — least privilege for anihpj-only dependencies
         - Gotcha: with resolution: DNS, Envoy re-resolves periodically — this
           is what keeps calls working when Stripe rotates IPs behind its CDN

         [SERVICEENTRY resolution MODES — DNS vs STATIC vs NONE]
         ┌────────────┬───────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
         │ resolution │ how Envoy finds the IPs   │ anihpj use case           │
         ├────────────┼───────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
         │ DNS        │ re-resolves the hostname  │ api.stripe.com,           │
         │            │ periodically (fresh IPs)  │ api.sendgrid.com — the    │
         │            │                           │ 90% cloud-API case        │
         │ STATIC     │ fixed IPs listed in the   │ PostgreSQL VM at          │
         │            │ ServiceEntry endpoints    │ 10.0.1.50:5432 (psql)     │
         │ NONE       │ no lookup — connect to    │ "*.amazonaws.com"         │
         │            │ the IP the app resolved   │ wildcard passthrough      │
         └────────────┴───────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘

         [VERIFY A NEW ServiceEntry IS LIVE — three quick checks]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. the object exists in the right namespace                     │
         │  $ kubectl get serviceentry -n anihpj                              │
         │  NAME              HOSTS                 LOCATION        AGE       │
         │  stripe-payments   ["api.stripe.com"]    MESH_EXTERNAL   12s       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 2. Envoy actually received the new cluster                      │
         │  $ istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api \              │
         │      -n anihpj | grep stripe                                       │
         │  api.stripe.com  443  -  outbound  STRICT_DNS     ← it's there     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 3. a real call leaves the mesh                                  │
         │  $ kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- curl -s \          │
         │      -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://api.stripe.com/v1      │
         │  401   ← routing + TLS work (401 = Stripe wants an API key)        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.3  Egress Gateway — Controlled Exit from the Mesh
         - Force ALL external traffic through a single egress point
         - Useful for: network policies, compliance, centralized logging of egress
         - anihpj use case: all calls to payment APIs audited through egress gateway
         [EGRESS GATEWAY DIAGRAM: pods → egress pod → external internet]

         [EGRESS GATEWAY TRAFFIC FLOW for anihpj payments]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITHOUT Egress Gateway:                                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [jobpost-api Pod] ──── direct HTTPS ────▶ api.stripe.com         │
         │  [user-service Pod] ─── direct HTTPS ────▶ api.stripe.com         │
         │  [notification Pod] ─── direct HTTPS ────▶ api.sendgrid.com       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Problem: 3 different source IPs, no audit trail, hard to block   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITH Egress Gateway:                                              │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [jobpost-api Pod]   ─┐                                           │
         │  [user-service Pod]   ├──▶ [istio-egressgateway Pod]              │
         │  [notification Pod]  ─┘         │                                 │
         │                                 │ single HTTPS egress             │
         │                                 ▼                                  │
         │                          api.stripe.com / api.sendgrid.com        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Benefits:                                                         │
         │  ✅ One fixed source IP (easier firewall rules at Stripe)          │
         │  ✅ Central audit log of all external calls                        │
         │  ✅ Block external access by stopping the egress gateway           │
         │  ✅ Apply consistent policies (timeouts, retry) at one place       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: istio-egressgateway — a standalone Envoy Deployment in
           istio-system with NO app container; its only job is forwarding
           mesh traffic outward under central control
         - Deploying the Pod alone changes NOTHING — traffic only detours
           through it once Gateway + VirtualService rules are wired (see 8.5)
         - Kill switch drill: scale the egress gateway to 0 replicas and ALL
           routed egress stops instantly — auditors love this demo
         - Note: like ingress Gateways, the egress Gateway resource may live
           in ANY namespace — istio-system is convention, not a requirement

         [INSTALLING THE EGRESS GATEWAY — off by default in most profiles]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # the "default" istioctl profile ships WITHOUT an egress gateway: │
         │  $ istioctl install -y \                                           │
         │      --set "components.egressGateways[0].enabled=true" \           │
         │      --set "components.egressGateways[0].name=istio-egressgateway" │
         │                                                                    │
         │  $ kubectl get pods -n istio-system -l istio=egressgateway         │
         │  NAME                                    READY   STATUS            │
         │  istio-egressgateway-6c9b57d9d4-x2krw    1/1     Running           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # emergency kill switch (blocks all routed egress):               │
         │  $ kubectl scale deploy/istio-egressgateway -n istio-system \      │
         │      --replicas=0                                                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.4  External Service Security Patterns — Production Best Practices
         - DEFINITION: egress security posture — the combined controls deciding
           WHICH external hosts the mesh may reach, WHO inside the mesh may
           reach them, and WHERE that traffic physically exits the cluster
         - Why it matters: a compromised anihpj pod with open egress can ship
           the PostgreSQL user table to any server on the internet 😈
           → egress control is your last line of defense AFTER a breach
         - How it works — three layers stacked, each catching what the layer
           before it missed:
           → Layer 1: REGISTRY_ONLY — unknown hosts are black-holed
           → Layer 2: egress gateway — allowed hosts exit via ONE audited door
           → Layer 3: AuthorizationPolicy ON the egress gateway — only named
             ServiceAccounts may use that door
         - anihpj production pattern: REGISTRY_ONLY mesh-wide, egress gateway
           mandatory for payment traffic, AuthorizationPolicy pinning
           api.stripe.com to the jobpost-api ServiceAccount only

         [THREE-LAYER EGRESS DEFENSE — each layer catches what leaks past]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  [any anihpj Pod] ── wants to call out ──▶                         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  LAYER 1  REGISTRY_ONLY (mesh-wide)                                │
         │    host has a ServiceEntry? ── no ──▶ ❌ black-holed (502/reset)    │
         │    │ yes                                                           │
         │    ▼                                                               │
         │  LAYER 2  egress gateway routing (VirtualService forces detour)    │
         │    traffic may exit ONLY via istio-egressgateway                   │
         │    → logged, single source IP, kill-switchable                     │
         │    │                                                               │
         │    ▼                                                               │
         │  LAYER 3  AuthorizationPolicy AT the gateway                       │
         │    caller is jobpost-api SA AND host is api.stripe.com?            │
         │      yes ──▶ ✅ forwarded to api.stripe.com 🔒                       │
         │      no  ──▶ ❌ 403 RBAC: access denied (attempt logged 😈)          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [AUTHORIZATIONPOLICY ON THE EGRESS GATEWAY — annotated YAML]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                  │
         │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                         │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: egress-stripe-payments-only                               │
         │    namespace: istio-system          ← lives WITH the gateway pods  │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      matchLabels:                                                  │
         │        istio: egressgateway         ← enforced AT the egress Envoy │
         │    action: ALLOW                    ← anything unmatched → denied  │
         │    rules:                                                          │
         │    - from:                                                         │
         │      - source:                                                     │
         │          principals:                ← SPIFFE identity, mTLS-proven │
         │          - cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api                  │
         │      to:                                                           │
         │      - operation:                                                  │
         │          hosts: ["api.stripe.com"]  ← this door opens ONLY to      │
         │                                       Stripe, for ONLY that SA     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PCI / AUDIT RATIONALE — why auditors demand the egress trio]
         ┌──────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ audit requirement            │ mesh mechanism that satisfies it    │
         ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
         │ "deny-by-default outbound"   │ outboundTrafficPolicy REGISTRY_ONLY │
         │ "documented external deps"   │ one ServiceEntry per external API   │
         │ "single controlled egress    │ istio-egressgateway = fixed source  │
         │  point / fixed source IP"    │ IP for Stripe's firewall allowlist  │
         │ "log every outbound call"    │ Envoy access logs on the egress GW  │
         │ "least privilege — only the  │ AuthorizationPolicy on the egress   │
         │  payment app reaches Stripe" │ GW keyed on SPIFFE principal        │
         │ "emergency kill switch"      │ scale egressgateway to 0 replicas   │
         └──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.5  Deep Dive — Egress Gateway Traffic Path Step-by-Step
         - DEFINITION: egress traffic path — the 4-hop journey a request takes
           when the mesh forces it through the egress gateway instead of
           letting the sidecar dial the internet directly
         - Why it matters: mis-wiring ONE resource makes traffic silently
           bypass the gateway — calls still succeed, but the audit trail is
           empty (the worst failure mode: the invisible one)
         - WHICH resources are mandatory (classic ICA trap):
           → ServiceEntry ............ MANDATORY — registers the external host
           → Gateway (egress) ........ MANDATORY — opens the listener on the
             istio: egressgateway Envoy
           → VirtualService .......... MANDATORY — ONE VS with TWO match
             blocks: mesh→gateway, then gateway→external
           → DestinationRule ......... OPTIONAL — only for TLS origination
             (app speaks plain HTTP, gateway upgrades to HTTPS) or to tune
             the sidecar→gateway hop

         [EGRESS TRAFFIC PATH — 4 hops from jobpost-api to api.stripe.com]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  HOP 1  app container → its own sidecar                            │
         │    jobpost-api calls https://api.stripe.com:443                    │
         │    iptables redirects into Envoy's outbound listener :15001        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  HOP 2  sidecar → istio-egressgateway Pod    🔒 mTLS (SPIFFE)       │
         │    VS match {gateways: [mesh]} routes the call to                  │
         │    istio-egressgateway.istio-system.svc.cluster.local:443          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  HOP 3  egress gateway → api.stripe.com                            │
         │    VS match {gateways: [istio-system/egress-stripe]} routes to     │
         │    the ServiceEntry host; gateway resolves DNS, sends to Stripe    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  HOP 4  response returns over the same path                        │
         │    Stripe → egress gateway → sidecar → app container               │
         │    status, headers, body proxied back transparently                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [EGRESS Gateway RESOURCE — opens the door on the egress Envoy]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: egress-stripe                                             │
         │    namespace: istio-system        ← convention (ANY ns works)      │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      istio: egressgateway         ← binds to the EGRESS Envoy,     │
         │    servers:                         not istio: ingressgateway!     │
         │    - port:                                                         │
         │        number: 443                                                 │
         │        name: tls                                                   │
         │        protocol: TLS              ← passthrough listener (HTTPS)   │
         │      hosts:                                                        │
         │      - api.stripe.com                                              │
         │      tls:                                                          │
         │        mode: PASSTHROUGH          ← app's TLS stays intact 🔒       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [THE TWO-MATCH VirtualService — heart of the egress detour]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: VirtualService                                              │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: stripe-through-egress                                     │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    hosts: [api.stripe.com]                                         │
         │    gateways:                                                       │
         │    - mesh                         ← reserved word: every sidecar   │
         │    - istio-system/egress-stripe   ← the egress Gateway above       │
         │    tls:                                                            │
         │    - match:                       ← BLOCK 1: sidecar → gateway     │
         │      - gateways: [mesh]                                            │
         │        port: 443                                                   │
         │        sniHosts: [api.stripe.com]                                  │
         │      route:                                                        │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: istio-egressgateway.istio-system.svc.cluster.local  │
         │          port: {number: 443}                                       │
         │    - match:                       ← BLOCK 2: gateway → internet    │
         │      - gateways: [istio-system/egress-stripe]                      │
         │        port: 443                                                   │
         │        sniHosts: [api.stripe.com]                                  │
         │      route:                                                        │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: api.stripe.com     ← the ServiceEntry host          │
         │          port: {number: 443}                                       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [OPTIONAL DestinationRule — TLS origination at the gateway]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # ONLY needed when the app sends plain HTTP on port 80 and you    │
         │  # want the GATEWAY to upgrade the call to HTTPS toward Stripe:    │
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: stripe-originate-tls                                      │
         │    namespace: istio-system                                         │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    host: api.stripe.com                                            │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                                  │
         │      portLevelSettings:                                            │
         │      - port: {number: 443}                                         │
         │        tls:                                                        │
         │          mode: SIMPLE             ← gateway originates TLS 🔒       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # for plain HTTPS passthrough (panels above) SKIP this resource   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Prove the detour works: the call MUST appear in the egress
           gateway's access log — an empty log means traffic is bypassing it

         [VERIFYING THE DETOUR — the audit log is the proof]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \                  │
         │      curl -s https://api.stripe.com/v1 -o /dev/null                │
         │                                                                    │
         │  $ kubectl logs -n istio-system -l istio=egressgateway \           │
         │      --tail=3 | grep stripe                                        │
         │  [2026-07-02T10:14:07Z] "- - -" ... outbound|443||api.stripe.com   │
         │                          ← the hop-3 entry: detour confirmed ✅     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.6  Deep Dive — DNS Proxying & Protocol Selection
         - DEFINITION: DNS proxying — the istio-agent inside the sidecar
           intercepts the app's DNS queries and answers them from Istio's own
           registry, so ServiceEntry hosts resolve even when kube-dns (or
           public DNS) knows nothing about them
         - Why it matters: without it, a ServiceEntry host that public DNS
           cannot resolve (partner VPN endpoints, multi-network services)
           dies with NXDOMAIN before Envoy ever sees the request
         - Turn it on with ISTIO_META_DNS_CAPTURE: "true" — mesh-wide via
           meshConfig proxyMetadata, or per-Deployment via annotation
         - DEFINITION: address auto-allocation — ServiceEntries without
           spec.addresses receive a unique VIP from 240.240.0.0/16 (class E,
           never routable on real networks); the VIP lets Envoy tell apart
           multiple TCP services that share one port
         - DEFINITION: protocol selection — how Istio decides HTTP vs TCP for
           each port; L7 features (VS routing, retries, per-route metrics)
           only exist once the protocol is identified
         - Pick order — highest wins:
           → 1. appProtocol field on the Service port (Kubernetes 1.18+)
           → 2. port NAME prefix: http-, http2-, grpc-, grpc-web-, tcp-,
             tls-, https- (the prefix is the contract!)
           → 3. automatic detection by Envoy — HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2 only;
             anything unrecognized falls back to opaque TCP
         - Gateways do NOT auto-detect: a Gateway server speaks EXACTLY the
           protocol its YAML declares — no sniffing, ever
         - Real scenario: anihpj's PostgreSQL port was once left unnamed —
           psql connections hung: the sniffer waits for CLIENT bytes, but
           PostgreSQL is a server-first protocol → always name DB ports
           tcp- explicitly (never rely on sniffing for psql)

         [DNS PROXYING FLOW — sidecar answers DNS for ServiceEntry hosts]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WITHOUT DNS proxying:                                             │
         │  app ── "A? api.partner-vpn.net" ──▶ kube-dns ──▶ ❌ NXDOMAIN       │
         │  (the request dies before Envoy can even try to route it)          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITH DNS proxying (ISTIO_META_DNS_CAPTURE=true):                  │
         │  app ── "A? api.partner-vpn.net" ──▶ istio-agent DNS proxy         │
         │        └─▶ host found in the ServiceEntry registry                 │
         │            └─▶ answers 240.240.0.7      ← auto-allocated VIP       │
         │  app connects to 240.240.0.7:443                                   │
         │        └─▶ Envoy: "VIP 240.240.0.7 = that ServiceEntry"            │
         │            └─▶ routed to the real endpoints ✅                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ENABLING DNS PROXYING — mesh-wide and per-pod]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # mesh-wide (IstioOperator):                                      │
         │  apiVersion: install.istio.io/v1alpha1                             │
         │  kind: IstioOperator                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    meshConfig:                                                     │
         │      defaultConfig:                                                │
         │        proxyMetadata:                                              │
         │          ISTIO_META_DNS_CAPTURE: "true"   ← intercept app DNS      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # per-Deployment (pod template annotation):                       │
         │  # annotations:                                                    │
         │  #   proxy.istio.io/config: |                                      │
         │  #     proxyMetadata:                                              │
         │  #       ISTIO_META_DNS_CAPTURE: "true"                            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # opt one ServiceEntry OUT of VIP auto-allocation (label it):     │
         │  #   networking.istio.io/enable-autoallocate-ip: "false"           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PROTOCOL SELECTION — pick order and what each method unlocks]
         ┌──────────────────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
         │ method (pick order)  │ example           │ notes                   │
         ├──────────────────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 1. appProtocol field │ appProtocol: grpc │ highest priority        │
         │ 2. port-name prefix  │ name: http-web    │ http-, http2-, grpc-,   │
         │                      │                   │ grpc-web-, tcp-, tls-,  │
         │                      │                   │ https-                  │
         │ 3. auto-detection    │ (Envoy sniffs)    │ HTTP/1.1 & HTTP/2 only; │
         │                      │                   │ unknown → opaque TCP    │
         │ Gateways             │ protocol: HTTP    │ NO sniffing — exactly   │
         │                      │                   │ what you declare        │
         └──────────────────────┴───────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘

         [ANNOTATED Service PORTS — protocol selection for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  kind: Service                                                     │
         │  metadata: {name: jobpost-api, namespace: anihpj}                  │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    ports:                                                          │
         │    - port: 8000                                                    │
         │      name: http-api        ← prefix → HTTP/1.1, full L7 ✅          │
         │      appProtocol: http     ← explicit — beats the name if both     │
         │    - port: 9090                                                    │
         │      name: grpc-events     ← gRPC over HTTP/2 ✅                    │
         │    - port: 5432                                                    │
         │      name: tcp-postgres    ← declared opaque TCP (psql traffic)    │
         │    - port: 8070                                                    │
         │      name: location        ← no prefix → Envoy must SNIFF; if      │
         │                              the sniffer can't tell → TCP ❌        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTERS 5-8: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS (Traffic Management)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After these chapters, in your daily work you will:
      • Write Gateway YAML to accept external traffic into the mesh
      • Write VirtualService YAML for path-based and weight-based routing
      • Do canary deployments: deploy v2 → route 5% → monitor → ramp up
      • Roll back any deployment instantly by changing one YAML weight
      • Add retries + timeouts to VirtualService (no code changes!)
      • Configure circuit breakers via DestinationRule outlierDetection
      • Set up intelligent load balancing (LEAST_CONN for variable workloads,
        CONSISTENT_HASH for sticky sessions)
      • Register external APIs (Stripe, SendGrid, S3) via ServiceEntry
      • Know the VS+DR mandatory pattern:
        VirtualService = WHERE to route, DestinationRule = HOW to route
      • Remember: port names MUST be "http" or Istio ignores them!

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTERS 5-8: ICA EXAM TIPS & QUESTIONS (Traffic Management — 35%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS — This is 35% of the exam, the single biggest domain.
       You MUST master these:
      • Gateway: accept external traffic → hosts, ports, TLS
      • VirtualService: route traffic → match URI, weight, headers, retries, timeout, fault
      • DestinationRule: policies → subsets, loadBalancer, outlierDetection, tls mode
      • ServiceEntry: external services → hosts, ports, location: MESH_EXTERNAL

    Q1: Create a VirtualService that routes 90% to v1 and 10% to v2 of jobpost-api.
    A1: spec.http.route: destination host=jobpost-api subset=v1 weight=90 +
        destination host=jobpost-api subset=v2 weight=10. Subsets defined in DR.

    Q2: How do you add a 5-second timeout to calls from jobpost-api to user-service?
    A2: In the VirtualService for jobpost-api, add timeout: 5s in the http section.

    Q3: What DestinationRule field enables circuit breaking?
    A3: outlierDetection with consecutiveGatewayErrors, interval, and baseEjectionTime.

    Q4: How do you register an external API (api.stripe.com) in the mesh?
    A4: Create a ServiceEntry with hosts: [api.stripe.com], ports: [{number: 443,
        protocol: HTTPS}], location: MESH_EXTERNAL, resolution: DNS.

    Q5: What's the difference between VirtualService retries and DestinationRule circuit breaker?
    A5: Retries attempt to recover from transient failures (try again). Circuit breaker
        stops ALL requests to a failing service to prevent cascade (stop trying).

    Q6: Security requires ALL jobpost-api → api.stripe.com traffic to exit via
        the egress gateway. Which resources do you create, and which are
        mandatory?
    A6: Mandatory: ServiceEntry (registers api.stripe.com), Gateway with
        selector istio: egressgateway, and ONE VirtualService with TWO match
        blocks — {gateways: [mesh]} routing to
        istio-egressgateway.istio-system.svc.cluster.local, then
        {gateways: [<egress-gw>]} routing to api.stripe.com. DestinationRule
        is OPTIONAL — only needed for TLS origination at the gateway.

    Q7: You must move production from ALLOW_ANY to REGISTRY_ONLY. Give the safe
        order of operations.
    A7: 1) Audit real egress via Envoy access logs/Kiali, 2) create a
        ServiceEntry for EVERY external host found, 3) test while STILL in
        ALLOW_ANY, 4) istioctl install --set
        meshConfig.outboundTrafficPolicy.mode=REGISTRY_ONLY -y, 5) re-test.
        Switching first = instant 502s on every unregistered external call.

    Q8: hosts: ["*.amazonaws.com"] — what does the wildcard cover, and what are
        its limits?
    A8: It covers any subdomain ending in .amazonaws.com (s3.amazonaws.com,
        s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com) but NOT the bare apex amazonaws.com — list
        that separately. "*" is legal only as the LEFTMOST label ("api.*.com"
        is invalid), and a wildcard host cannot be DNS-resolved — pair it with
        resolution: NONE (passthrough) or SNI routing on an egress gateway.

    Q9: When do you choose resolution DNS vs STATIC vs NONE in a ServiceEntry?
    A9: DNS = Envoy re-resolves the hostname periodically (cloud APIs like
        api.stripe.com — the 90% case). STATIC = fixed IPs listed as endpoints
        (anihpj's PostgreSQL VM at 10.0.1.50:5432). NONE = no lookup at all;
        Envoy connects to whatever IP the app already resolved — the required
        pattern for wildcard hosts.

    Q10: Make jobpost-api's Stripe calls resilient: 3s total timeout, 2 retries
        on 5xx, and eject Stripe endpoints after 5 consecutive 5xx. Which
        resources do you write?
    A10: A VirtualService on hosts: [api.stripe.com] with http timeout: 3s and
        retries {attempts: 2, perTryTimeout: 1s, retryOn: 5xx} (budget:
        3 tries × 1s = 3s ≤ 3s ✅), plus a DestinationRule host: api.stripe.com
        with outlierDetection {consecutive5xxErrors: 5, interval: 30s,
        baseEjectionTime: 60s}. This works only because the ServiceEntry made
        Stripe a registry member — external hosts take the full VS/DR surface.

    Q11: anihpj is onboarding a B2B partner that must present a client
        certificate before reaching partner.anihpj.com. You set the Gateway's
        port-443 server to tls.mode: MUTUAL and reuse the TLS secret already
        created for SIMPLE mode (`kubectl create secret tls ...`, holding only
        tls.crt + tls.key). Partner integration tests fail: every connection
        drops mid-handshake. What's missing, and how do you fix it?
    A11: MUTUAL needs a THIRD secret key, ca.crt — the CA that signed the
        PARTNER's client certificates — so the gateway has something to verify
        incoming client certs against. `kubectl create secret tls` only ever
        writes tls.crt + tls.key, so verification has nothing to check and the
        handshake fails before any HTTP is exchanged (5.5's #1 exam trap).
        Rebuild the secret as a generic one carrying all three keys:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  kubectl create secret generic partner-mtls-cert \                  │
        │      --from-file=tls.crt=server.crt \                               │
        │      --from-file=tls.key=server.key \                               │
        │      --from-file=ca.crt=partner-ca.crt \   ← the missing piece      │
        │      -n istio-system                                                │
        │                                                                     │
        │  # Gateway YAML needs NO change — mode and name already correct:    │
        │  tls: {mode: MUTUAL, credentialName: partner-mtls-cert}             │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Q12: The new admin portal needs its own hostname, admin.anihpj.com, with
        its own certificate (admin-tls-cert) — but ops wants it served from the
        SAME istio-ingressgateway Pod and the SAME port 443 that already
        terminates SIMPLE TLS for api.anihpj.com (anihpj-tls-cert). How do you
        add it without touching the existing api.anihpj.com server?
    A12: Add a SECOND servers[] entry to the same Gateway, same port number 443,
        with its own hosts + credentialName — Envoy reads the TLS SNI field to
        pick the right certificate per connection, so one port can safely
        terminate many certs as long as each server's hosts list is distinct
        and each server has a unique name. Never merge two hosts under one
        tls: block — one server = one certificate.
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: Gateway                                                      │
        │  metadata: {name: anihpj-gateway-https, namespace: anihpj}          │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {istio: ingressgateway}                                │
        │    servers:                                                         │
        │    - port: {number: 443, name: https, protocol: HTTPS}  ← existing  │
        │      tls: {mode: SIMPLE, credentialName: anihpj-tls-cert}           │
        │      hosts: ["api.anihpj.com"]                                      │
        │    - port: {number: 443, name: https-admin, protocol: HTTPS}  ← NEW │
        │      tls: {mode: SIMPLE, credentialName: admin-tls-cert}            │
        │      hosts: ["admin.anihpj.com"]      ← SNI picks this cert         │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Q13: The platform team created Gateway anihpj-gateway in namespace
        istio-system. Your team's VirtualService lives in namespace anihpj and
        references it with `gateways: [anihpj-gateway]`. `kubectl apply`
        succeeds and `kubectl get gateway -n istio-system` shows it healthy —
        but every request still 404s. What's wrong?
    A13: An unqualified gateway name resolves inside the VirtualService's OWN
        namespace (anihpj), not the Gateway's — Istio looks for a Gateway
        named anihpj-gateway INSIDE anihpj, finds nothing, and the VS binds to
        no gateway at all. Qualify it with the Gateway's namespace instead:
        gateways: ["istio-system/anihpj-gateway"]. Rule of thumb: whenever
        Gateway and VirtualService live in different namespaces, use the
        <namespace>/<name> form or the reference silently misses.

    Q14: After migrating the edge to Gateway API, the jobpost squad creates
        HTTPRoute jobpost-route in their OWN namespace jobpost-team, with
        parentRefs pointing at Gateway anihpj-gw (namespace anihpj, listener
        allowedRoutes: {namespaces: {from: Same}}). Requests never reach
        jobpost-api, and the HTTPRoute's status shows Accepted: False, reason:
        NotAllowedByListeners. Name two valid fixes.
    A14: `from: Same` restricts the listener to routes living in the Gateway's
        OWN namespace (anihpj) — a route from jobpost-team is rejected before
        routing is even attempted (the Gateway API equivalent of Chapter 5's
        Gateway/VS binding gotcha). Fix 1 — widen the listener:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  listeners:                                                         │
        │  - name: http                                                       │
        │    ...                                                              │
        │    allowedRoutes:                                                   │
        │      namespaces:                                                    │
        │        from: All              ← was: Same                           │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Fix 2 — simplest: create jobpost-route in namespace anihpj instead, so
        it satisfies the existing Same restriction. Either way, re-check
        status: Accepted: True confirms the fix landed.

    Q15: Support tickets say beta testers sending header x-beta-tester: "true"
        always land on search-service v1, even though this VirtualService was
        meant to send them to v2. Find the bug and fix it:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  ❌ BROKEN — catch-all placed first:                                 │
        │  http:                                                              │
        │  - route:                              ← no match = catch-all       │
        │    - destination: {host: search-service, subset: v1}                │
        │  - match:                              ← NEVER reached              │
        │    - headers: {x-beta-tester: {exact: "true"}}                      │
        │    route:                                                           │
        │    - destination: {host: search-service, subset: v2}                │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    A15: Rule order, not the header match, is broken. Envoy walks http[] TOP to
        BOTTOM and stops at the FIRST match; rule 1 has no match block at all
        (a catch-all) and sits FIRST, so it swallows 100% of traffic before
        rule 2's header check ever runs. Fix: move the header-matched rule
        ABOVE the catch-all —
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  ✅ FIXED — specific rule first, catch-all last:                     │
        │  http:                                                              │
        │  - match:                                                           │
        │    - headers: {x-beta-tester: {exact: "true"}}                      │
        │    route:                                                           │
        │    - destination: {host: search-service, subset: v2}                │
        │  - route:                              ← catch-all LAST             │
        │    - destination: {host: search-service, subset: v1}                │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        General rule from 6.9: most specific match first, catch-all (no match
        block) always last.

    Q16: Write a complete VirtualService for skills-service that sends requests
        carrying header x-internal-user: "1" to subset v2 (beta features) and
        routes everyone else to subset v1 — the exact case named in the 6.4
        A/B-testing table but never written out in full.
    A16: Specific match FIRST, catch-all LAST (6.9) — same discipline as Q15:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata:                                                          │
        │    name: skills-service-beta                                        │
        │    namespace: anihpj                                                │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts:                                                           │
        │    - skills-service                                                 │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - match:                        ← specific rule FIRST            │
        │      - headers:                                                     │
        │          x-internal-user:                                           │
        │            exact: "1"                                               │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination:                                                 │
        │          host: skills-service                                       │
        │          subset: v2               ← beta features                   │
        │    - route:                        ← catch-all LAST                 │
        │      - destination:                                                 │
        │          host: skills-service                                       │
        │          subset: v1                                                 │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        subsets v1/v2 must already exist in skills-service's DestinationRule —
        without them this VS applies cleanly but every request 503s (7.2).

    Q17: jobpost-api's public API is versioned at /api/v2/, but the Django app
        itself only knows plain resource paths (/jobs/, /users/) with no
        version prefix. Write the VirtualService that lets external clients
        call https://api.anihpj.com/api/v2/jobs/ while jobpost-api receives
        just /jobs/.
    A17: Match the prefix, then rewrite it away before the route forwards:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata:                                                          │
        │    name: jobpost-api-vs                                             │
        │    namespace: anihpj                                                │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts:                                                           │
        │    - api.anihpj.com                                                 │
        │    gateways:                                                        │
        │    - anihpj-gateway                                                 │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - match:                                                         │
        │      - uri:                                                         │
        │          prefix: /api/v2/                                           │
        │      rewrite:                                                       │
        │        uri: /                 ← strips /api/v2/, replaces with /    │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination:                                                 │
        │          host: jobpost-api                                          │
        │          port: {number: 8000}                                       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        rewrite fires AFTER match, BEFORE the request leaves Envoy —
        /api/v2/jobs/ arrives at jobpost-api as just /jobs/; the app never
        sees the version prefix. Confirm with `istioctl proxy-config route`
        or a live curl -v against the gateway.

    Q18: Before a peak-traffic day, QA must validate skills-service's
        resilience config — timeout: 3s, retries {attempts: 3, perTryTimeout:
        1s} — and then PROVE it survives a real stall using fault injection,
        without touching the production VirtualService. Walk through both
        steps.
    A18: Step 1 — budget check (6.12 formula): timeout ≥ (attempts + 1) ×
        perTryTimeout → needed = (3+1) × 1s = 4s, but timeout is only 3s
        ⚠️ broken — the 3rd retry gets cut off mid-flight. Fix: raise timeout
        to 4s (or drop attempts to 2, needing only (2+1)×1s = 3s).
        Step 2 — prove it without risking prod: per 6.11's two-VS pattern,
        apply a SEPARATE fault-only VirtualService, run the test, remove it:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  # fault-only VS — a TEST-window resource, never combined with the  │
        │  # retry/timeout VS from Step 1 (6.11: fault + retry/timeout in ONE │
        │  # VirtualService don't interact):                                  │
        │  http:                                                              │
        │  - fault:                                                           │
        │      delay: {fixedDelay: 2s, percentage: {value: 20}}               │
        │    route:                                                           │
        │    - destination: {host: skills-service}                            │
        │                                                                     │
        │  # baseline to compare against: 847 req/s, 2.1% errors, p99 234ms   │
        │  # expected: p99 rises but errors stay ~2.1% (retries absorb the    │
        │  # delay); if errors spike instead, the fixed config isn't actually │
        │  # deployed — recheck BEFORE the traffic day                        │
        │  $ kubectl delete -f skills-fault-test.yaml   ← always remove after │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Q19: You add a v3 canary for jobpost-api. The DestinationRule already has a
        `subsets: - name: v3, labels: {version: v3}` entry, and the
        VirtualService sends it 10% of traffic. The v3 Deployment is Running
        and Ready, but that 10% gets 100% 503s. `kubectl get pods
        --show-labels` shows the v3 pods still carrying version: v2. Diagnose
        AND fix it — including how you'd confirm the fix without waiting for
        real user traffic.
    A19: A DestinationRule subset is only a label selector — it does not create
        endpoints, it narrows the existing pool. With the pods mislabeled,
        subset v3 selects ZERO pods, so Envoy has nowhere to send that 10% and
        returns 503 immediately (the classic 7.2 trap). Fix the label, then
        confirm at the Envoy level instead of waiting on real traffic:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  # fix: patch the Deployment's pod-template label to match the      │
        │  # subset selector                                                  │
        │  $ kubectl patch deployment jobpost-api-v3 -n anihpj --type=json \  │
        │      -p '[{"op":"replace",                                          │
        │            "path":"/spec/template/metadata/labels/version",         │
        │            "value":"v3"}]'                                          │
        │                                                                     │
        │  # confirm: does the v3 cluster have ANY endpoints yet?             │
        │  $ istioctl proxy-config endpoint deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj \    │
        │    --cluster "outbound|8000|v3|jobpost-api.anihpj.svc.cluster.local"│
        │  # empty  = diagnosis confirmed (this is why it 503s)               │
        │  # listed = fix confirmed, no need to wait for live traffic         │
