        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Q20: search-service dashboards show p99 climbing to 890ms while the median
        holds at 120ms (mesh baseline: 847 req/s, 2.1% errors, p99 234ms). The
        current DestinationRule sets loadBalancer: {simple: ROUND_ROBIN}, and
        `kubectl top pods` shows one pod pegged at 95% CPU while its siblings
        sit near 20%. What's the likely cause, and what one-line fix would you
        try first?
    A20: ROUND_ROBIN sends every Nth request to a pod in fixed turn regardless
        of how busy it already is — a pod stuck on a few expensive searches
        keeps getting NEW requests queued behind them, exactly the "hot pod,
        high p99, low median" signature. Fix: switch to LEAST_REQUEST (Istio's
        actual default, and the correct 7.8 pick for "request durations vary a
        lot"): trafficPolicy.loadBalancer.simple: LEAST_REQUEST. Verify with
        `istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj --fqdn
        search-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local -o json | grep lbPolicy` →
        "LEAST_REQUEST" confirms Envoy picked it up.

    Q21: auth-service runs only 2 replicas with the canonical outlierDetection
        block (consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5, interval: 30s, baseEjectionTime:
        30s, maxEjectionPercent: 50). Under light packet loss, on-call sees
        repeated 30-second outages: one pod ejects, HALF-OPENs, fails again,
        re-ejects — over and over, each cycle dropping 50% of capacity. What
        would you tune, and why NOT just disable the breaker?
    A21: With a 2-pod pool, ejecting even ONE pod already hits the
        maxEjectionPercent: 50 ceiling — the breaker is working as designed,
        the FLEET is too small for it. Tuning options, cheapest first: (1)
        raise baseEjectionTime (30s→90s) so a flapping pod sits out longer
        instead of re-probing into more failures every 30s, (2) raise
        consecutiveGatewayErrors (5→10) so a few blips don't trip it at all,
        (3) the real fix — scale auth-service to 4+ replicas so a 50%
        ejection still leaves 2 healthy pods serving. Don't disable
        outlierDetection: with it off, a truly dead pod keeps receiving live
        traffic and users see 100% of THEIR requests fail instead of a
        bounded, self-healing 50%.

    Q22: Ops configures localityLbSetting.distribute on skills-service to keep
        70% of eu-west-1a traffic in-zone, and ships the DestinationRule
        WITHOUT an outlierDetection block ("we'll add circuit breaking
        later"). All eu-west-1a pods later crash together. What happens to
        eu-west-1a callers, and what's the one-block fix?
    A22: Nothing fails over — Istio silently IGNORES localityLbSetting on any
        DestinationRule that lacks outlierDetection (7.9's hard requirement:
        no health signal means Envoy has no trigger to spill traffic outward),
        so eu-west-1a callers keep getting routed to the dead local pods
        forever, with zero automatic recovery. Fix: add the canonical breaker
        block to the SAME DestinationRule:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  outlierDetection:                                                  │
        │    consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5                                      │
        │    interval: 30s                                                    │
        │    baseEjectionTime: 30s                                            │
        │    maxEjectionPercent: 50                                           │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Once present, the 5-error trip ejects the dead 1a pods and Envoy
        walks the locality preference outward to 1b/1c automatically.

    Q23: notification-service opens long-lived connections to an internal
        Redis cache on port 6379. The Service port is defined as
        {port: 6379, name: "redis"} with no appProtocol set. Under load, some
        connections hang for several seconds before finally going through;
        packet captures show Envoy waiting before forwarding the first bytes.
        Explain the cause and fix it.
    A23: Protocol selection (8.6) fell through to auto-detection because the
        port name carries no recognized prefix (http-, grpc-, tcp-, tls-,
        https-), and Redis's RESP protocol is neither HTTP/1.1 nor HTTP/2 —
        Envoy's sniffer waits for CLIENT bytes to decide, and that wait is
        exactly the hang. It's the same failure mode as 8.6's psql
        server-first-protocol trap, just for Redis. Fix — name the port so
        Envoy skips sniffing entirely: ports: [{port: 6379, name: tcp-redis}]
        (or set appProtocol: tcp, which outranks the name if both are
        present) — either turns an auto-detect gamble into a guarantee.

    Q24: notification-service currently calls SendGrid directly over plain
        HTTP. Compliance now requires ALL of that traffic to exit through
        istio-egressgateway, upgraded to HTTPS at the gateway (TLS
        origination) instead of staying plaintext end-to-end. The SendGrid
        ServiceEntry from 8.2 already exists (hosts: [api.sendgrid.com], port
        443 HTTPS). Write every ADDITIONAL resource needed, and name which one
        actually performs the HTTP→HTTPS upgrade.
    A24: Three more resources — an egress Gateway, a two-match VirtualService,
        and the DestinationRule that does the real work:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: Gateway                                                      │
        │  metadata: {name: egress-sendgrid, namespace: istio-system}         │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {istio: egressgateway}                                 │
        │    servers:                                                         │
        │    - port: {number: 80, name: http, protocol: HTTP}                 │
        │      hosts: ["api.sendgrid.com"]                                    │
        │  ---                                                                │
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata: {name: sendgrid-through-egress, namespace: anihpj}       │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: [api.sendgrid.com]                                        │
        │    gateways: [mesh, istio-system/egress-sendgrid]                   │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - match: [{gateways: [mesh], port: 80}]     ← BLOCK 1: app→gw    │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination:                                                 │
        │          host: istio-egressgateway.istio-system.svc.cluster.local   │
        │          port: {number: 80}                                         │
        │    - match:                                    ← BLOCK 2: gw→net    │
        │      - gateways: [istio-system/egress-sendgrid]                     │
        │        port: 80                                                     │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination:                                                 │
        │          host: api.sendgrid.com                                     │
        │          port: {number: 443}      ← DR below originates TLS here    │
        │  ---                                                                │
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: DestinationRule                                              │
        │  metadata: {name: sendgrid-originate-tls, namespace: istio-system}  │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    host: api.sendgrid.com                                           │
        │    trafficPolicy:                                                   │
        │      portLevelSettings:                                             │
        │      - port: {number: 443}                                          │
        │        tls:                                                         │
        │          mode: SIMPLE   ← THIS is what upgrades HTTP to HTTPS       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        The Gateway only opens the door and the VirtualService only steers
        traffic through it — neither touches encryption. The
        DestinationRule's tls.mode: SIMPLE is the one field that makes the
        egress gateway originate a real TLS handshake toward SendGrid on the
        way out (same 8.5 pattern used for Stripe, reused here for a plain-
        HTTP caller instead of a PASSTHROUGH one).

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Chapter 9: Metrics — Measuring Every Request
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    9.1  The Observability Pillars: Metrics, Traces, Logs
         - Metrics: numbers over time (e.g., requests/sec, error rate, latency p99)
         - Traces: follow a single request across multiple services
         - Logs: human-readable events from each service
         - Istio provides Metrics + Traces automatically — zero code change!
         [OBSERVABILITY PILLARS DIAGRAM — three overlapping circles]

         │                                                                    │

         │                                                                    │
         │  STEP 2 — Check TRACES (Jaeger):                                  │
         │    • Find a slow trace: total 1,200ms                             │
         │    • jobpost-api → skills-service: 1,150ms!  ← bottleneck span   │
         │    • skills-service → postgres DB: 1,100ms!  ← database is slow  │
         │    → Root cause: database query in skills-service is slow         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  STEP 3 — Check LOGS (EFK/Loki):                                  │
         │    • skills-service logs: "SLOW QUERY: SELECT * FROM skills       │
         │      WHERE user_id=42 took 1,089ms"                               │
         │    • Missing index on user_id column!                             │
         │    → Fix: add index to skills table in PostgreSQL ✅               │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.2  RED Metrics — What Istio Measures Automatically
         - R: Rate — how many requests per second is jobpost-api receiving?
         - E: Errors — what percentage are failing (HTTP 4xx/5xx)?
         - D: Duration — how long do requests take (p50, p95, p99 latency)?
         - Istio's Envoy proxy measures these for EVERY service, EVERY request
         - No Prometheus instrumentation needed in your Django code
         [RED METRICS DASHBOARD MOCKUP for anihpj services]

         [RED METRICS — what Istio gives you for FREE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SERVICE: jobpost-api               LAST 5 MINUTES     │
         │                                                        │
         │  Rate (Requests/sec):   ████████████████ 847 req/s     │
         │  Error Rate:            ██░░░░░░░░░░░░░░  2.1%        │
         │  Duration (p99):        ████░░░░░░░░░░░░  234ms        │
         │                                                        │
         │  SERVICE: user-service              LAST 5 MINUTES     │
         │  Rate:  312 req/s | Errors: 0.3% | p99: 87ms          │
         │                                                        │
         │  SERVICE: skills-service            LAST 5 MINUTES     │
         │  Rate:  89 req/s | Errors: 8.1% ⚠️ | p99: 1,240ms ⚠️  │
         │                     ↑ This service needs attention!   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.3  Prometheus + Istio — How Metrics Are Collected
         - Istio configures Envoy to emit metrics in Prometheus format
         - Prometheus scrapes metrics from every Envoy proxy
         - Key metrics exposed: istio_requests_total, istio_request_duration_milliseconds
         - Configuring scraping interval, retention, alerting rules
         [PROMETHEUS SCRAPING FLOW: Envoy → /metrics → Prometheus → Grafana]

         [METRICS PIPELINE — from request to dashboard]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  1. REQUEST HAPPENS                                                │
         │     User hits GET /jobs/ → jobpost-api                            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  2. ENVOY RECORDS METRICS (automatically, per request)            │
         │     istio_requests_total{source="jobpost-api",                    │
         │       destination="user-service", response_code="200"} += 1       │
         │     istio_request_duration_ms{...} = 87ms                         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  3. PROMETHEUS SCRAPES (every 15s by default)                     │
         │     GET http://jobpost-api-pod:15090/metrics                       │
         │     GET http://user-service-pod:15090/metrics                      │
         │     ... (scrapes ALL pods in mesh)                                 │
         │                                                                    │
         │  4. GRAFANA QUERIES PROMETHEUS                                     │
         │     PromQL: rate(istio_requests_total[5m])                         │
         │     PromQL: histogram_quantile(0.99, istio_request_duration_ms)    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  5. DASHBOARD SHOWS                                                │
         │     jobpost-api: 847 req/s, 2.1% errors, p99=234ms               │
         │                                                                    │
         │  KEY ISTIO METRICS TO KNOW:                                        │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │  istio_requests_total          ← request count              │  │
         │  │  istio_request_duration_ms     ← latency histogram          │  │
         │  │  istio_request_bytes           ← request body size          │  │
         │  │  istio_response_bytes          ← response body size         │  │
         │  │  istio_tcp_connections_opened  ← TCP connection count       │  │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - ⚠️ Scrape-port clarification (exam-accurate): Prometheus does
           NOT scrape Envoy directly by default — it scrapes the
           istio-agent on :15020, which serves MERGED app + Envoy metrics
           • :15020/stats/prometheus → istio-agent, the DEFAULT scrape
             target (injected pods carry prometheus.io/port: "15020")
           • :15090/stats/prometheus → the RAW Envoy stats endpoint,
             served by the Envoy process itself — Envoy metrics ONLY
         - DEFINITION: metrics merging — the istio-agent fetches the
           Django app's own /metrics output AND Envoy's :15090 stats,
           then serves BOTH in one response on :15020 → a single scrape
           per pod collects application + mesh metrics together
         - → when a diagram (like the one above) says "Prometheus scrapes
           :15090", read it as "the Envoy metrics ORIGINATE at :15090" —
           the scrape itself lands on the agent's merged :15020 endpoint

         [SIDECAR METRICS PORTS — :15020 (merged) vs :15090 (raw Envoy)]
         ┌────────────────┬──────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
         │  PORT          │  SERVED BY       │  WHAT YOU GET              │
         ├────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │  :15020        │  istio-agent     │  MERGED app + Envoy stats  │
         │                │  (pilot-agent)   │  ✅ DEFAULT scrape target   │
         ├────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │  :15090        │  Envoy process   │  RAW Envoy stats only      │
         │                │  directly        │  (debugging, no app data)  │
         ├────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │  :8000         │  Django app      │  app's own custom metrics  │
         │                │  (your code)     │  (merged INTO :15020)      │
         └────────────────┴──────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

         [MERGE FLOW — how one scrape collects everything]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Django app :8000/metrics ──────┐                              │
         │                                 ├──► istio-agent :15020        │
         │  Envoy :15090/stats/prometheus ─┘         │  (one merged page) │
         │                                           ▼                    │
         │                             Prometheus scrape (every 15s)      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.4  Grafana Dashboards for Istio
         - Istio ships with pre-built Grafana dashboards:
           • Istio Control Plane Dashboard: istiod health, xDS push latency
           • Istio Mesh Dashboard: global mesh-level traffic overview
           • Istio Service Dashboard: per-service RED metrics, success rate
           • Istio Workload Dashboard: per-deployment metrics
         - anihpj walkthrough: identify which service is the bottleneck
         [GRAFANA DASHBOARD MOCKUP with annotated panels]

         [GRAFANA ISTIO SERVICE DASHBOARD — annotated panel layout]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  Istio Service Dashboard: jobpost-api            LAST 1h     │ │
         │  ├────────────────┬──────────────┬─────────────────────────────┤ │
         │  │  CLIENT RPS    │  SERVER RPS  │  REQUEST SUCCESS RATE       │ │
         │  │   847.3        │   847.3      │  ████████████░ 97.9%        │ │
         │  │   req/s ▲      │   req/s      │                             │ │
         │  ├────────────────┴──────────────┴─────────────────────────────┤ │
         │  │  LATENCY (client)              │  LATENCY (server)          │ │
         │  │  p50:  45ms  ─────────         │  p50: 43ms                 │ │
         │  │  p90:  120ms ──────────────    │  p90: 115ms                │ │
         │  │  p99:  234ms ─────────────────-│  p99: 228ms                │ │
         │  ├───────────────────────────────-┴────────────────────────────┤ │
         │  │  INCOMING REQUESTS BY SOURCE:                               │ │
         │  │  ■ istio-ingressgateway → jobpost-api   612 req/s           │ │
         │  │  ■ search-service → jobpost-api         235 req/s           │ │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── ┤ │
         │  │  OUTGOING REQUESTS BY DESTINATION:                          │ │
         │  │  ■ jobpost-api → user-service    412 req/s  0.1% error      │ │
         │  │  ■ jobpost-api → skills-service   89 req/s  8.1% error ⚠️   │ │
         │  │  ■ jobpost-api → auth-service    312 req/s  0.0% error      │ │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.5  Custom Metrics and Telemetry API
         - Add custom metrics beyond RED: e.g., number of active jobpost listings
         - Istio Telemetry API for customizing what is measured
         - Add custom labels to metrics (e.g., job_category, user_region)
         [YAML: Telemetry resource adding custom dimension to metrics]
         - DEFINITION: Telemetry API — the CRD (telemetry.istio.io/v1)
           that tunes WHAT Envoy reports (metrics, access logs, trace
           sampling) per mesh, per namespace, or per workload — no app
           code changes, no Envoy restart
         - How it works: istiod translates the Telemetry resource into
           Envoy stats config and pushes it over xDS → allow ~30s
           end-to-end (istiod ~5s → xDS push ~5s → next scrape 15s)
         - anihpj scenario: product wants req/s split by job category, so
           ops adds a job_category label sourced from the x-job-category
           header that jobpost-api already sends downstream

         [ANNOTATED YAML — Telemetry: custom dimensions on REQUEST_COUNT]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1    # v1 since Istio 1.22    │
         │  kind: Telemetry                                               │
         │  metadata:                                                     │
         │    name: anihpj-custom-metrics                                 │
         │    namespace: anihpj            ← every workload in anihpj     │
         │  spec:                                                         │
         │    metrics:                                                    │
         │    - providers:                                                │
         │      - name: prometheus         ← the built-in stats provider  │
         │      overrides:                                                │
         │      - match:                                                  │
         │          metric: REQUEST_COUNT      ← istio_requests_total     │
         │          mode: CLIENT_AND_SERVER    ← REPORTER note below      │
         │        tagOverrides:                                           │
         │          job_category:              ← NEW Prometheus label     │
         │            value: request.headers['x-job-category']            │
         │          user_region:               ← 2nd custom dimension     │
         │            value: request.headers['x-user-region']             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - REPORTER note: every request yields TWO copies of each metric —
           the client sidecar reports reporter="source", the server
           sidecar reports reporter="destination"
         - → mode: CLIENT_AND_SERVER applies the tagOverride to BOTH
           copies; use CLIENT or SERVER to touch only one side
         - → in PromQL always pin one side (reporter="destination") or
           your req/s doubles — 847 req/s becomes a phantom 1,694

         [VERIFY THE NEW DIMENSION — apply, drive traffic, query]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ kubectl apply -f telemetry-custom.yaml                      │
         │  $ kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \              │
         │      curl -s -H "x-job-category: devops" \                     │
         │      http://skills-service:8090/api/skills/                    │
         │  $ istioctl dashboard prometheus                               │
         │    PromQL> sum(rate(istio_requests_total{                      │
         │              job_category="devops",                            │
         │              reporter="destination"}[5m]))                     │
         │    → non-empty result = the custom dimension is live ✅         │
         │                                                                │
         │  NOTE: allow ~30s — istiod push (~10s) + next scrape (15s)     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.6  Access Logs — Auditing Every Request
         - DEFINITION: access log — one text line PER REQUEST, written by
           the Envoy sidecar to the istio-proxy container's stdout
         - Why it matters: metrics AGGREGATE (individual requests vanish
           into averages) and traces are SAMPLED (most requests are
           dropped) — access logs keep EVERY request → your audit trail
         - Use them for: security audits ("who hit /admin/ last night?"),
           debugging ONE specific failed request, compliance evidence
         - OFF by default in the production profile — switch on per
           namespace with the Telemetry API (accessLogging), no restart
         - Read them live:
           kubectl logs -f deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -c istio-proxy
         - Ship stdout to Loki / EFK for retention and cross-service
           search over all anihpj services
         - The logged x-request-id also rides the trace headers → paste
           it into Jaeger search to jump from a log line to its trace

         [ACCESS LOG LINE — field-by-field anatomy (default text format)]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ kubectl logs deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -c istio-proxy    │
         │                                                                │
         │  [2026-06-02T10:30:45.360Z] "GET /api/jobs/123 HTTP/1.1" 200   │
         │  - via_upstream - "-" 0 4523 45 43 "10.244.0.1" "curl/8.5.0"   │
         │  "d209e46f-9ed5-9b61-bbdd-43e22662702a" "anihpj.example.com"   │
         │  "10.244.1.5:8000" inbound|8000|| 127.0.0.6:52413 ...          │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  FIELD                          MEANING                        │
         │  [2026-06-02T10:30:45.360Z]     request START time (UTC)       │
         │  "GET /api/jobs/123 HTTP/1.1"   method + path + protocol       │
         │  200                            HTTP response code             │
         │  -                              response flags: "-" = none     │
         │                                 (UH/UO/NR/DC = Envoy-level     │
         │                                 failures — memorize these!)    │
         │  via_upstream                   response came from the app     │
         │  0 4523                         bytes received / bytes sent    │
         │  45 43                          total ms / upstream ms →       │
         │                                 45-43 = 2ms spent inside Envoy │
         │  "10.244.0.1"                   X-Forwarded-For (caller IP)    │
         │  "curl/8.5.0"                   user-agent header              │
         │  "d209e46f-...-702a"            x-request-id → paste into      │
         │                                 Jaeger to find the trace       │
         │  "anihpj.example.com"           :authority / Host header       │
         │  "10.244.1.5:8000"              upstream host (the app itself) │
         │  inbound|8000||                 Envoy cluster: direction+port  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ANNOTATED YAML — Telemetry: switch on access logs (envoy)]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1     # v1 since Istio 1.22   │
         │  kind: Telemetry                                               │
         │  metadata:                                                     │
         │    name: anihpj-access-logs                                    │
         │    namespace: anihpj            ← apply here: anihpj pods only │
         │  spec:                          #  (istio-system = mesh-wide)  │
         │    accessLogging:                                              │
         │    - providers:                                                │
         │      - name: envoy              ← built-in stdout text logger  │
         │      filter:                    ← OPTIONAL — drop this block   │
         │        expression: response.code >= 400    # to log everything │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - JSON instead of text: set meshConfig.accessLogEncoding: JSON —
           easier for Loki/EFK to parse field-by-field
         - ⚠️ volume warning: at 847 req/s, full access logging writes
           ~73M lines/day for jobpost-api alone — keep the errors-only
           filter in production, log everything only while debugging

    9.7  Control Plane Metrics — Monitoring istiod Health
         - DEFINITION: control-plane metrics — istiod monitoring ITSELF,
           exposed on the istiod pod at :15014/metrics (Prometheus
           scrapes it automatically alongside the sidecars)
         - Why it matters: 9.1-9.6 watch anihpj's health; these watch
           ISTIO's health — a sick istiod means STALE proxies, stuck
           cert rotation, and route changes that never arrive
         - Where to look: Grafana "Istio Control Plane Dashboard"
           (pre-built) → istiod CPU/memory, push latency, error counters
         - Quick manual peek:
           kubectl exec deploy/istiod -n istio-system -- \
             curl -s localhost:15014/metrics | grep pilot_
         - Three metric LEVELS — keep them apart on the exam:
           • proxy-level: raw Envoy stats (envoy_cluster_*, connection +
             TLS handshake errors) — deepest debugging layer
           • service-level: the istio_* RED metrics of 9.2-9.3 — what
             the Grafana dashboards show; the most-tested on the ICA
           • control-plane: istiod's own health — this section

         [ISTIOD SELF-MONITORING METRICS — watchlist + alert thresholds]
         ┌────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
         │  METRIC (istiod :15014)        │  WHAT IT MEANS / ALERT WHEN      │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  pilot_proxy_convergence_time  │  time from config change to      │
         │  (histogram)                   │  proxy ACK — ALERT: p99 > 1s     │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  pilot_xds_pushes (counter)    │  xDS pushes by type (cds/eds/    │
         │                                │  lds/rds) — sudden spike =       │
         │                                │  config churn storm              │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  pilot_total_xds_rejects       │  Envoy REJECTED a config push    │
         │  (counter)                     │  — ALERT: any increase (> 0)     │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  citadel_server_csr_count      │  workload cert CSRs signed —     │
         │  (counter)                     │  a FLAT line = rotation stuck    │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  citadel_server_root_cert_     │  root CA expiry (unix time) —    │
         │  expiry_timestamp (gauge)      │  ALERT: fewer than 30 days left  │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  galley_validation_failed      │  CRD rejected by the validating  │
         │  (counter)                     │  webhook — ALERT: any increase   │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │  pilot_xds (gauge)             │  proxies connected to THIS       │
         │                                │  istiod — should equal your      │
         │                                │  sidecar count                   │
         └────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

         [PROMETHEUS ALERT RULES — istiod health, copy-paste PromQL]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1) proxies rejecting config (should ALWAYS be zero)       │
         │  increase(pilot_total_xds_rejects[10m]) > 0                  │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 2) config slow to converge (p99 above 1 second)           │
         │  histogram_quantile(0.99, sum(rate(                          │
         │    pilot_proxy_convergence_time_bucket[5m])) by (le)) > 1    │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 3) root CA certificate expiring within 30 days            │
         │  citadel_server_root_cert_expiry_timestamp - time()          │
         │    < 30 * 24 * 3600                                          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.8  Deep Dive — Counters, Gauges, Histograms in PromQL
         - Istio emits three Prometheus metric TYPES — using the wrong
           PromQL function on the wrong type produces a meaningless graph
         - DEFINITION: COUNTER — a value that only ever goes UP (and
           resets to 0 when the pod restarts); istio_requests_total
           → NEVER graph it raw: you get "145,632 and climbing"
           → wrap it in rate() / increase() to get per-second truth
         - DEFINITION: GAUGE — a snapshot that goes up AND down;
           pilot_xds (connected proxies), envoy_cluster_upstream_cx_active
           → query it raw, or smooth it with avg_over_time()
         - DEFINITION: HISTOGRAM — observations sorted into cumulative
           "le" (less-or-equal) buckets; the buckets themselves are
           counters; istio_request_duration_milliseconds_bucket
           → extract percentiles with histogram_quantile()
         - Real scenario: during the skills-service incident the on-call
           graphed istio_requests_total raw and saw a line climbing
           forever → panic; wrapped in rate() it showed a steady 89 req/s
           — traffic was normal, the 8.1% errors were the real problem

         [THREE METRIC TYPES — the right PromQL for each]
         ┌──────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  COUNTER     │  istio_requests_total                          │
         │              │  only increases → use rate() / increase()      │
         │              │  raw query = a meaningless growing number      │
         ├──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  GAUGE       │  pilot_xds, envoy_cluster_upstream_cx_active   │
         │              │  up AND down → query raw or avg_over_time()    │
         ├──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  HISTOGRAM   │  istio_request_duration_milliseconds_bucket    │
         │              │  cumulative buckets → histogram_quantile()     │
         │              │  bucket label: le="5","10",...,"+Inf" (ms)     │
         └──────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [SAMPLE PROMQL — the RED queries for jobpost-api]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # RATE — requests per second (COUNTER + rate)                 │
         │  sum(rate(istio_requests_total{reporter="destination",         │
         │    destination_service=~"jobpost-api.*"}[5m]))                 │
         │  → 847 req/s for jobpost-api                                   │
         │                                                                │
         │  # ERRORS — share of 5xx responses                             │
         │  sum(rate(istio_requests_total{reporter="destination",         │
         │    destination_service=~"jobpost-api.*",                       │
         │    response_code=~"5.."}[5m]))                                 │
         │  / sum(rate(istio_requests_total{reporter="destination",       │
         │    destination_service=~"jobpost-api.*"}[5m])) * 100           │
         │  → 2.1 (%)                                                     │
         │                                                                │
         │  # DURATION — p99 latency (HISTOGRAM + histogram_quantile)     │
         │  histogram_quantile(0.99, sum(rate(                            │
         │    istio_request_duration_milliseconds_bucket{                 │
         │      destination_service=~"jobpost-api.*"}[5m])) by (le))      │
         │  → 234 (ms)                                                    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [CARDINALITY WARNING — labels multiply time series]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  istio_requests_total already carries ~15 labels               │
         │  (source_workload, destination_service, response_code, ...)    │
         │                                                                │
         │  time series ≈ every UNIQUE label combination that occurs      │
         │  anihpj today:  10 workloads × 10 services × 10 codes × 2      │
         │                 protocols × 2 TLS modes ≈ 4,000 series ✅ fine  │
         │                                                                │
         │  now add a BAD tagOverride: user_id from the x-user-id header  │
         │  50,000 users × 4,000 = 200,000,000 time series                │
         │  → Prometheus eats RAM → OOMKilled → NO metrics for anyone ❌   │
         │                                                                │
         │  RULE: only add LOW-cardinality dimensions (category, region,  │
         │  tier — a handful of values); NEVER user IDs, request IDs,     │
         │  session tokens, or full URLs                                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Chapter 10: Distributed Tracing — Follow Any Request Across All Services
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    10.1 What is Distributed Tracing?
         - A single HTTP request to anihpj /jobs/ triggers 5+ service calls
         - Without tracing: you see an error but have no idea WHICH service caused it
         - With tracing: see the ENTIRE call chain — every hop, every duration
         - Key concepts:
           • Trace: the entire journey of one request (root span + child spans)
           • Span: one unit of work (one service call) with start time + duration
           • Trace ID: unique ID linking all spans of one request
           • Parent/Child: shows the hierarchy of calls
         [TRACE DIAGRAM: one request → many spans visualized as Gantt chart]

         [DISTRIBUTED TRACE — anihpj GET /jobs/ request]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Trace ID: 7f3e9a12b4c06d8e  Duration: 342ms                │
         │                                                              │
         │  ▶ jobpost-api (GET /jobs/)                    0ms → 342ms  │
         │    ▶ user-service (GET /users/42/)            12ms → 87ms   │
         │      ▶ auth-service (POST /validate-token/)  15ms → 45ms   │
         │    ▶ skills-service (GET /skills/?ids=1,2,3)  92ms → 287ms  │
         │      ← 195ms ← SLOW! This is the bottleneck                │
         │    ▶ location-service (GET /locations/7/)    295ms → 320ms  │
         │                                                              │
         │  🔍 skills-service call took 195ms — investigate!           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: a span is the atomic unit of tracing — ONE timed
           operation in ONE service, stamped with IDs that link it to the
           rest of the request's journey
         - Learn the five fields every span carries and every Jaeger
           screen becomes instantly readable:
           • traceId — shared by ALL spans of one request (the glue)
           • spanId — unique per span; identifies THIS hop only
           • parentSpanId — spanId of whoever called me (builds the tree)
           • duration — start time → end time of this one hop
           • tags — key/value metadata: http.method, http.url, status code
         - The root span has NO parentSpanId — that is how Jaeger knows
           where the call tree starts (jobpost-api in the trace above)

         [SPAN ANATOMY — the skills-service span from the trace above]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SPAN = one unit of work (one service call)                  │
         │  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────  │
         │  traceId:      7f3e9a12b4c06d8e  ← same on ALL 5 spans       │
         │  spanId:       a1b2c3d4e5f60718  ← unique to THIS span       │
         │  parentSpanId: 9f8e7d6c5b4a3928  ← the jobpost-api span      │
         │  operation:    GET /skills/?ids=1,2,3                        │
         │  startTime:    T=92ms            duration: 195ms  ← SLOW!    │
         │                                                              │
         │  tags:                                                       │
         │    http.method       GET                                     │
         │    http.url          /skills/?ids=1,2,3                      │
         │    http.status_code  200                                     │
         │    component         proxy   ← span created by Envoy        │
         │    upstream_cluster  outbound|8090||skills-service.anihpj   │
         │                                                              │
         │  ROOT SPAN rule: empty parentSpanId → jobpost-api is root   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    10.2 How Istio Enables Tracing (Almost Automatically)
         - Istio's Envoy injects tracing headers automatically:
           x-request-id, x-b3-traceid, x-b3-spanid, x-b3-parentspanid
         - Your Django code MUST forward these headers between services!
         - Istio does the heavy lifting; you just propagate the headers
         [CODE EXAMPLE: Django middleware to forward Istio tracing headers]

         [REQUIRED: Header propagation in Django views.py]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Add this to your Django views that call services:   │
         │                                                        │
         │  ISTIO_HEADERS = [                                     │
         │      'x-request-id',                                   │
         │      'x-b3-traceid',                                   │
         │      'x-b3-spanid',                                    │
         │      'x-b3-parentspanid',                              │
         │      'x-b3-sampled',                                   │
         │      'x-b3-flags',                                     │
         │  ]                                                     │
         │                                                        │
         │  def get_jobs(request):                                │
         │      # Forward headers to propagate trace context:     │
         │      headers = {h: request.META.get(                   │
         │          'HTTP_' + h.upper().replace('-', '_'))        │
         │          for h in ISTIO_HEADERS                        │
         │          if request.META.get(                          │
         │              'HTTP_' + h.upper().replace('-', '_'))    │
         │      }                                                 │
         │      users = requests.get('http://user-service/users/',│
         │                           headers=headers)             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Real anihpj scenario: SREs suddenly saw five 1-span traces per
           GET /jobs/ instead of one 5-span chain — user-service had
           swapped its HTTP client library and silently stopped forwarding
           the headers → every hop began a brand-new trace
           → Envoy CREATES spans automatically, but it cannot see INSIDE
             your process — only your code can tie inbound to outbound
         - Rule of thumb: Istio does 80% (span creation, header injection,
           reporting); your Django code does the critical 20% (forwarding)
           — see 10.5 for the full contract and a middleware version

         [BROKEN vs CONNECTED TRACE — same request, two outcomes]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ ❌ HEADERS DROPPED (no propagation)                          │
         │    Jaeger shows FIVE separate traces, one span each:         │
         │    trace 8a01…  jobpost-api       GET /jobs/         342ms   │
         │    trace 3f77…  user-service      GET /users/42/      75ms   │
         │    trace c2b9…  auth-service      POST /validate/     30ms   │
         │    trace 91d4…  skills-service    GET /skills/       195ms   │
         │    trace 6e05…  location-service  GET /locations/7/   25ms   │
         │    → no hierarchy, no bottleneck view, no story              │
         │                                                              │
         │ ✅ HEADERS FORWARDED (10.2 snippet / 10.5 middleware)        │
         │    ONE trace 7f3e9a12…, 5 spans, full Gantt waterfall —      │
         │    the skills-service 195ms bottleneck is instantly visible  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    10.3 Jaeger — Visualizing Traces
         - Jaeger is the default tracing backend for Istio (demo profile)
         - Open with: istioctl dashboard jaeger
         - Features: trace search, service dependency graph, latency histograms
         - Find the slowest trace: sort by duration → identify bottleneck
         [JAEGER UI MOCKUP with anihpj traces annotated]

         [JAEGER UI MOCKUP — trace search + Gantt waterfall, annotated]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ JAEGER UI ▸ Search                                           │
         │   Service:   jobpost-api.anihpj    Lookback: last 15 min     │
         │   Operation: GET /jobs/            Sort: Longest Duration ▼  │
         │   Tags: (none)                     [ Find Traces ]           │
         ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │ ▸ 7f3e9a12b4c06d8e  jobpost-api: GET /jobs/  342ms  5 spans  │
         │                                                              │
         │                          0ms       114ms     228ms  342ms    │
         │ jobpost-api GET /jobs/   ██████████████████████████████ 342ms│
         │  ├ user-service /users/   ███████ 75ms                       │
         │  │  └ auth-svc /validate/ ███ 30ms                           │
         │  ├ skills-service /skills/       ██████████████████ 195ms    │
         │  │                               ^^ WIDEST BAR = BOTTLENECK  │
         │  └ location-service /loc/7/                        ██ 25ms   │
         │                                                              │
         │ Click the widest bar → span detail shows its tags:           │
         │   http.url = /skills/?ids=1,2,3    http.status_code = 200    │
         │   → 200 = slow, NOT failing → suspect the PostgreSQL query   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Bottleneck workflow: Search → sort by Longest Duration → open
           the slowest trace → widest bar = bottleneck → click that span
           → read its tags → cross-reference logs at that timestamp
         - Span tags to read first: http.method, http.url,
           http.status_code, component=proxy, upstream_cluster
         - The skills-service bar is wide but returns 200 → slow, not
           failing → next stop is its PostgreSQL query plan (psql:
           EXPLAIN ANALYZE), not its error logs
         - Dependency graph view: Jaeger draws the real anihpj service
           map from traces — compare it with your architecture diagram
           to spot calls you didn't know existed

    10.4 Tracing Sampling Rate
         - By default, Istio traces 1% of requests (to reduce overhead)
         - For development: set to 100% (trace everything)
         - For production: 1-5% is sufficient (100% is too expensive)
         [YAML: Configure sampling rate in Istio Telemetry resource]

         [TELEMETRY YAML — 100% sampling in dev, 1% mesh-wide default]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # DEV — trace EVERYTHING in the anihpj namespace:            │
         │ apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1                            │
         │ kind: Telemetry                                              │
         │ metadata:                                                    │
         │   name: dev-tracing                                          │
         │   namespace: anihpj            # ← namespace-scoped override │
         │ spec:                                                        │
         │   tracing:                                                   │
         │   - randomSamplingPercentage: 100.0  # ← every request       │
         │ ---                                                          │
         │ # PROD — 1% mesh-wide default (root namespace):              │
         │ apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1                            │
         │ kind: Telemetry                                              │
         │ metadata:                                                    │
         │   name: mesh-default-tracing                                 │
         │   namespace: istio-system      # ← applies to whole mesh     │
         │ spec:                                                        │
         │   tracing:                                                   │
         │   - randomSamplingPercentage: 1.0    # ← 1 in 100 traced     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Namespace-scoped Telemetry overrides the istio-system default:
           anihpj traces everything while the rest of the mesh stays at 1%
         - 0% = tracing off: Envoy stamps x-b3-sampled: 0, headers still
           flow, but no spans are reported to Jaeger
         - Need ONE traced request in prod without touching any config?
           → send x-b3-flags: 1 — the debug flag FORCES sampling for that
             request (and every downstream hop), overriding the
             randomSamplingPercentage

         [FORCE-SAMPLING — x-b3-flags: 1 beats the sampling percentage]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # trace ONE request in prod without changing any config:     │
         │ kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \               │
         │   curl -s -H "x-b3-flags: 1" \                               │
         │           -H "x-user-id: test-user-42" \                     │
         │           http://skills-service:8090/skills/                 │
         │                                                              │
         │ # x-b3-flags: 1 = debug flag → Envoy ALWAYS samples this     │
         │ # trace, even at randomSamplingPercentage: 1.0 — and the     │
         │ # flag propagates, so every downstream hop is captured too   │
         │ # Debug tool only — never set it fleet-wide in production    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    10.5  Deep Dive — The B3 Header Propagation Contract
         - DEFINITION: B3 is the trace-context header format (born in
           Zipkin; "B3" = its internal codename "BigBrotherBird") that
           carries trace identity between services as plain HTTP headers
         - Why it matters: a trace survives only if EVERY hop carries the
           same context — the x-b3-* headers ARE that context; drop them
           once and the chain snaps into disconnected spans
         - How it works:
           → the FIRST Envoy in the chain (usually the ingress gateway)
             mints x-b3-traceid and makes the sampling decision
           → every later Envoy creates a child span and UPDATES
             x-b3-spanid / x-b3-parentspanid for its own hop
           → your app's only job: copy the headers from the inbound
             request onto every outbound call, UNCHANGED
         - x-request-id rides along too: not a B3 header, but Envoy's
           per-request UUID — forward it so access logs in every service
           can be joined to the trace afterwards

         [B3 HEADER CONTRACT — the 5 B3 headers + x-request-id]
         ┌────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┬───────────────┐
         │ Header             │ Purpose                  │ Who sets it   │
         ├────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ x-b3-traceid       │ 64/128-bit trace ID —    │ first Envoy   │
         │                    │ the glue on ALL spans    │ (gateway)     │
         ├────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ x-b3-spanid        │ ID of THIS hop's span    │ every Envoy,  │
         │                    │                          │ each hop      │
         ├────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ x-b3-parentspanid  │ spanId of the caller —   │ every Envoy   │
         │                    │ builds the call tree     │               │
         ├────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ x-b3-sampled       │ 1 = trace, 0 = skip      │ first Envoy   │
         │                    │ (the sampling verdict)   │               │
         ├────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ x-b3-flags         │ 1 = debug — FORCES       │ you, manually │
         │                    │ sampling (beats the %)   │ (curl -H)     │
         ├────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ x-request-id       │ Envoy request UUID for   │ first Envoy   │
         │                    │ log-to-trace joins       │               │
         └────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┴───────────────┘

         [DIVISION OF LABOUR — Envoy does 80%, your code does 20%]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ENVOY SIDECAR — automatic (the 80%)                         │
         │  ✅ creates span context (traceId, spanId, timing)           │
         │  ✅ injects x-b3-* headers into requests                     │
         │  ✅ updates spanid/parentspanid at every hop                 │
         │  ✅ reports finished spans to the tracing backend            │
         │                                                              │
         │  YOUR DJANGO CODE — manual (the critical 20%)                │
         │  □ read x-b3-* + x-request-id from the inbound request       │
         │  □ attach them, UNCHANGED, to every outbound HTTP call       │
         │                                                              │
         │  ❌ skip the 20% → every hop starts a NEW trace:             │
         │     Jaeger shows five 1-span traces instead of one 5-span    │
         │     call chain — the #1 tracing failure in the field         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Per-view forwarding (10.2) works, but relies on every
           developer remembering it in every view — the production answer
           is a Django MIDDLEWARE that captures the headers once per
           request, automatically
         - Never generate your own IDs — Envoy owns span creation; your
           code is only the courier

         [DJANGO MIDDLEWARE — automatic B3 propagation for anihpj]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # anihpj/middleware.py — capture once, use everywhere        │
         │ TRACE_HEADERS = [                                            │
         │     'x-request-id',      'x-b3-traceid',   'x-b3-spanid',    │
         │     'x-b3-parentspanid', 'x-b3-sampled',   'x-b3-flags',     │
         │ ]                                                            │
         │                                                              │
         │ class IstioTracingMiddleware:                                │
         │     def __init__(self, get_response):                        │
         │         self.get_response = get_response                     │
         │                                                              │
         │     def __call__(self, request):                             │
         │         request.trace_headers = {      # ← UNCHANGED copy    │
         │             h: request.META['HTTP_' +                        │
         │                             h.upper().replace('-', '_')]     │
         │             for h in TRACE_HEADERS                           │
         │             if 'HTTP_' + h.upper().replace('-', '_')         │
         │                in request.META                               │
         │         }                                                    │
         │         return self.get_response(request)                    │
         │                                                              │
         │ # settings.py                                                │
         │ MIDDLEWARE = [                                               │
         │     'anihpj.middleware.IstioTracingMiddleware',              │
         │     # ... the rest ...                                       │
         │ ]                                                            │
         │                                                              │
         │ # any views.py — no per-view header plumbing needed:         │
         │ requests.get('http://skills-service:8090/skills/',           │
         │              headers=request.trace_headers)                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    10.6  Deep Dive — Tracing Providers: Zipkin, Jaeger, OpenTelemetry
         - DEFINITION: a tracing provider is the backend Envoy ships
           finished spans to; Istio can speak several dialects — Zipkin,
           OpenTelemetry (OTLP), Datadog, and more
         - Why it matters: "Jaeger" in the demo profile is really Envoy
           speaking the ZIPKIN protocol to a Jaeger collector on port
           9411 — knowing which protocol and port are in play is half of
           every "no traces appearing" debug session
         - How it works: declare providers once under meshConfig
           .extensionProviders, then a Telemetry resource selects WHICH
           provider is active (plus its sampling rate) — mesh-wide from
           istio-system or per namespace
         - The industry direction is OpenTelemetry: Envoy sends OTLP to
           one collector, and the collector fans out to Jaeger, Tempo,
           or a vendor APM — swap backends without touching Envoy again

         [MESHCONFIG — extensionProviders + Telemetry selecting one]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # IstioOperator excerpt (istioctl install -f iop.yaml):      │
         │ meshConfig:                                                  │
         │   extensionProviders:                                        │
         │   - name: jaeger-zipkin                                      │
         │     zipkin:                                                  │
         │       service: zipkin.istio-system.svc.cluster.local         │
         │       port: 9411               # ← Zipkin v2 span API        │
         │   - name: otel                                               │
         │     opentelemetry:                                           │
         │       service: otel-collector.istio-system.svc.cluster.local │
         │       port: 4317               # ← OTLP over gRPC            │
         │ ---                                                          │
         │ # A Telemetry resource picks the ACTIVE provider:            │
         │ apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1                            │
         │ kind: Telemetry                                              │
         │ metadata:                                                    │
         │   name: mesh-tracing                                         │
         │   namespace: istio-system                                    │
         │ spec:                                                        │
         │   tracing:                                                   │
         │   - providers:                                               │
         │     - name: otel               # ← switch backends HERE      │
         │     randomSamplingPercentage: 1.0                            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [OTEL COLLECTOR PIPELINE — one receiver, many exporters]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  anihpj Envoy sidecars (every namespace, every sidecar pod)  │
         │                        │                                     │
         │                        │ OTLP gRPC :4317                     │
         │                        ▼                                     │
         │         ┌─────────────────────────────────┐                  │
         │         │ otel-collector (istio-system)   │                  │
         │         │   receivers:  [otlp]            │                  │
         │         │   processors: [batch]           │                  │
         │         │   exporters:  [jaeger, tempo]   │                  │
         │         └───────┬─────────────────┬───────┘                  │
         │                 │                 │                          │
         │                 ▼                 ▼                          │
         │         Jaeger (today's UI)   Grafana Tempo / vendor APM     │
         │                                                              │
         │  Swap or add a backend = edit ONLY the collector config —    │
         │  Envoy keeps sending OTLP :4317 to the same address          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [HEADER FORMATS — B3 (Zipkin) vs W3C traceparent (OTel)]
         ┌───────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
         │               │ B3 (Zipkin-style)     │ W3C Trace Context    │
         ├───────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ headers       │ x-b3-traceid,         │ traceparent +        │
         │               │ -spanid,              │ tracestate           │
         │               │ -parentspanid,        │ (2 headers total)    │
         │               │ -sampled, -flags      │                      │
         ├───────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ format        │ five separate         │ one combined value:  │
         │               │ headers (or the       │ 00-<traceid>-        │
         │               │ compact single "b3")  │ <spanid>-<flags>     │
         ├───────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ used by       │ classic Zipkin /      │ OTel SDKs and the    │
         │               │ Jaeger path (demo)    │ otel provider        │
         ├───────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ standard?     │ de-facto (Zipkin)     │ official W3C rec     │
         └───────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

         - Migration guidance (Zipkin/B3 → OpenTelemetry), the safe order:
           → 1. deploy an OTel collector; add the "otel" provider in
                meshConfig NEXT TO the existing Zipkin one (they coexist)
           → 2. point the collector's exporter at the EXISTING Jaeger —
                same UI, new pipeline; verify traces still assemble
           → 3. flip the Telemetry provider for the anihpj namespace
                first, watch for broken chains, then go mesh-wide
           → 4. teach apps to forward traceparent + tracestate IN
                ADDITION to x-b3-* during the transition; drop B3 only
                after every service and client has switched
         - Gotcha: a mixed mesh where jobpost-api forwards only B3 and
           skills-service forwards only traceparent → the chain snaps at
           that boundary; forwarding BOTH during migration is the fix


Chapter 11: Kiali — Visualizing Your Entire Service Mesh
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11.1 What is Kiali?
         - Kiali is the "control panel" for your Istio mesh
         - Shows: service topology, real-time traffic flow, config validation
         - Not just a dashboard — it can GENERATE Istio YAML from a UI
         - Open with: istioctl dashboard kiali
         [KIALI DASHBOARD MOCKUP with anihpj service graph]

         - DEFINITION: Kiali is the open-source management console for
           the Istio mesh — it merges Prometheus metrics, Istio config,
           and Kubernetes state into one live, clickable picture
         - Why it matters: "which anihpj service is unhealthy RIGHT NOW?"
           is answered in seconds by looking at colored nodes and edges
         - Kiali stores nothing itself — it is a read-mostly UI over data
           that already exists (deleting Kiali loses no telemetry)
         - The four capabilities you will use daily:
           • Topology   → live service graph built from real traffic
           • Health     → RED metrics per node (service) and edge (call)
           • Validation → catches broken VirtualService/DestinationRule
           • Wizards    → generate correct Istio YAML from the UI

         [KIALI OVERVIEW PAGE — first screen after istioctl dashboard kiali]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  KIALI                                     Namespace: [anihpj ▼]   │
         ├──────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  ▸ Overview  │  anihpj                                7 services   │
         │  ▸ Graph     │  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  ▸ Apps      │  │  Health:   6 OK   1 failing (skills-service)  │  │
         │  ▸ Workloads │  │  Traffic:  847 req/s      Errors: 2.1%        │  │
         │  ▸ Services  │  │  Config:   ❌ 1 error   ⚠️ 2 warnings         │  │
         │  ▸ Istio     │  └───────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │    Config    │  mTLS: 🔒 enabled namespace-wide                    │
         ├──────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Open with: istioctl dashboard kiali                               │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.2 Service Graph — See Your Architecture Live
         - Kiali draws a real-time graph of all your anihpj services
         - Shows: which services talk to which, request rate, error rate
         - Color coding: green (healthy), red (errors), orange (degraded)
         - Click on any edge → see the RED metrics for that exact connection
         - anihpj use case: instantly see that skills-service is red
         [SERVICE GRAPH MOCKUP: anihpj microservices as a live topology]

         [KIALI SERVICE GRAPH — anihpj topology view]

         [KIALI ACTIONS YOU CAN TAKE directly from the graph]
         ┌────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Action                        │  What it does                     │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Click a service node          │  See RED metrics for that service  │
         │  Click an edge (arrow)         │  See metrics for that call path    │
         │  Right-click → Traffic Shifting│  Open wizard to set canary weights │
         │  Right-click → Fault Injection │  Add delays/aborts via UI          │
         │  Right-click → Suspend Traffic │  Block traffic to a service        │
         │  Click "Graph Settings"        │  Show circuit breaker badges       │
         │  Click "Validation" tab        │  See YAML config errors            │
         └────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘

    11.3 Validating Istio Configuration
         - Kiali validates your VirtualService and DestinationRule YAML
         - Common errors Kiali detects:
           • VirtualService references a subset not in DestinationRule
           • Gateway references a port not open in the gateway pod
           • Conflicting VirtualService rules
         - Kiali shows errors inline in the YAML editor
         [KIALI VALIDATION SCREENSHOT MOCKUP with error annotations]

         - How it works: Kiali cross-checks every Istio object against
           the live service registry and against every OTHER Istio
           object — most real mistakes are dangling references between
           two objects that are each valid on their own
         - Errors show as ❌ in the Istio Config list AND inline next to
           the offending line in Kiali's YAML editor
         - Real anihpj story: a DestinationRule renamed subset "v2" to
           "canary" but the VirtualService still said "v2" → every
           canary request failed → Kiali flagged KIA1107 seconds after
           the apply, long before anyone read a log

         [KIALI CONFIG VALIDATION — three real anihpj errors annotated]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Istio Config — Namespace: anihpj          ❌ 2 errors ⚠️ 1 warning│
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │                                                                    │
         │  ❌ VirtualService/jobpost-api          KIA1107                    │
         │     route[0].destination.subset: "v2"                              │
         │     └─▶ subset not found in any DestinationRule                    │
         │         (DR "jobpost-api" only defines subsets: v1, canary)        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ❌ Gateway/anihpj-gateway              KIA0303                    │
         │     servers[0].port: 8443                                          │
         │     └─▶ port 8443 not exposed by the istio-ingressgateway          │
         │         Service (open ports: 80, 443, 15021)                       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ⚠️ VirtualService/jobpost-api-legacy   KIA1106                    │
         │     hosts: ["jobpost-api"]                                         │
         │     └─▶ two VirtualServices define routes for the same host —      │
         │         rules may conflict; merge them into a single VS            │
         │                                                                    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [KIALI VALIDATION vs ISTIOCTL ANALYZE — pick the right tool]
         ┌──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
         │  Aspect              │  Kiali Validation    │  istioctl analyze    │
         ├──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │  Interface           │  Web UI, visual      │  CLI, text output    │
         │  Runs                │  continuously, live  │  on demand           │
         │  Scope               │  all namespaces      │  -n <ns> or -A       │
         │  Offline YAML check  │  ❌ live only        │  ✅ file.yaml        │
         │  CI/CD pipelines     │  ❌ not scriptable   │  ✅ exit code ≠ 0    │
         │  Error detail        │  click for docs      │  IST code + message  │
         │  Best for            │  discovery, triage   │  automation, pre-    │
         │                      │                      │  apply gate          │
         ├──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┤
         │  Different code prefixes (KIA1107 vs IST0101) — same class of      │
         │  checks; on the exam run istioctl analyze -n anihpj, then Kiali    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.4 Kiali Wizards — Generate Istio YAML From UI
         - Without writing YAML: use Kiali wizard to create:
           • Traffic Shifting (canary): slide percentage bars
           • Request Routing (A/B test): header-based routing
           • Fault Injection: add delays/aborts via checkbox
           • Traffic Suspension: block traffic to a service
         - Kiali generates the correct VirtualService + DestinationRule YAML
         [WIZARD FLOW MOCKUP: Kiali UI → generated YAML comparison]

         - Why wizards matter: hand-written canary YAML is the #1 source
           of subset typos — the wizard reads LIVE pod labels, so the
           subsets it generates always match running pods
         - How it works: right-click the service node → Actions → pick a
           wizard → fill the form → Preview shows the exact YAML →
           Create applies it to the cluster
         - DEFINITION: a Kiali wizard is a form that outputs standard
           VirtualService/DestinationRule objects — nothing proprietary;
           kubectl can read, edit, or delete them like any YAML you wrote
         - Update or delete wizard-created rules later from the same
           Actions menu — Kiali labels the objects it manages

         [KIALI WIZARDS — the four wizards and the YAML each generates]
         ┌───────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Wizard               │  Generated Istio YAML                      │
         ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Traffic Shifting     │  VirtualService with weight: fields +      │
         │  (canary %)           │  DestinationRule with one subset per       │
         │                       │  version label found on live pods          │
         ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Request Routing      │  VirtualService with match: blocks         │
         │  (A/B by header)      │  (headers/uri) routed to per-version       │
         │                       │  subsets in a DestinationRule              │
         ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Fault Injection      │  VirtualService with fault: block —        │
         │  (delay / abort)      │  fixedDelay + percentage, or abort         │
         │                       │  with httpStatus for chaos testing         │
         ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Traffic Suspension   │  VirtualService with a fault abort —       │
         │  (block a service)    │  httpStatus: 503 on 100% of requests       │
         │                       │  so every caller gets an instant error     │
         └───────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [WIZARD OUTPUT — Traffic Shifting 90/10 on jobpost-api]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Wizard form:  v1 ██████████████████░░ 90%                         │
         │                v2 ██░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░ 10%    [Preview]  [Create]  │
         ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: VirtualService                                              │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: jobpost-api            ← named after the service          │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    hosts:                                                          │
         │    - jobpost-api                                                   │
         │    http:                                                           │
         │    - route:                                                        │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: jobpost-api                                         │
         │          subset: v1                                                │
         │        weight: 90               ← slider position → weight         │
         │      - destination:                                                │
         │          host: jobpost-api                                         │
         │          subset: v2                                                │
         │        weight: 10               ← weights must sum to 100          │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: jobpost-api                                               │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    host: jobpost-api                                               │
         │    subsets:                                                        │
         │    - name: v1                                                      │
         │      labels:                                                       │
         │        version: v1              ← read from LIVE pod labels        │
         │    - name: v2                                                      │
         │      labels:                                                       │
         │        version: v2                                                 │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.5  The Observability Debug Flow — From Alert to Root Cause
         - DEFINITION: the debug flow is a fixed four-step route through
           the observability stack: Grafana → Kiali → Jaeger → logs
         - Why it matters: without a fixed route you tab-hop between
           dashboards for an hour; with it, most anihpj incidents go
           from alert to root cause in minutes
         - How it works — each tool answers exactly ONE question:
           • Grafana answers WHAT is wrong (which metric crossed a line)
           • Kiali answers WHERE it lives (which service/edge is red)
           • Jaeger answers WHY it is slow (which span eats the time)
           • Logs answer HOW to fix it (the exact application error)
         - Never skip a step: jumping straight to logs means grepping
