           seven services blind instead of one service, one timestamp

         [OBSERVABILITY DEBUG FLOW — 4 steps from alert to root cause]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │   ALERT: "anihpj p99 latency > 500ms for 5 minutes"                │
         │      │                                                             │
         │      ▼                                                             │
         │   STEP 1  GRAFANA — WHAT is wrong?                                 │
         │           Istio Service Dashboard → skills-service p99 = 1240ms    │
         │      │                                                             │
         │      ▼                                                             │
         │   STEP 2  KIALI — WHERE is it?                                     │
         │           Graph → red edge jobpost-api → skills-service, 8.1%      │
         │      │                                                             │
         │      ▼                                                             │
         │   STEP 3  JAEGER — WHY is it slow?                                 │
         │           Widest span: skills-service PostgreSQL query = 1,100ms   │
         │      │                                                             │
         │      ▼                                                             │
         │   STEP 4  LOGS — HOW to fix it?                                    │
         │           psql EXPLAIN shows a sequential scan → missing index     │
         │                                                                    │
         │   Each step narrows the search: mesh → service → span → SQL line   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [WORKED INCIDENT — anihpj p99 alert, from page to fix]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  T+0min : PagerDuty fires: "anihpj p99 > 500ms for 5 minutes"      │
         │  T+1min : GRAFANA — jobpost-api p99 = 234ms ✅ ... but             │
         │           skills-service p99 = 1240ms 🔴 → suspect found           │
         │  T+2min : KIALI — edge jobpost-api → skills-service is red:        │
         │           89 req/s at 8.1% errors (rest of the mesh green)         │
         │  T+4min : JAEGER — sort by duration, open top trace (1,340ms):     │
         │           span "SELECT ... FROM skills WHERE user_id" = 1,100ms    │
         │  T+6min : LOGS — kubectl logs deploy/skills-service -n anihpj      │
         │           shows the slow query; psql EXPLAIN: Seq Scan on          │
         │           skills — there is no index on user_id                    │
         │  T+9min : FIX — psql: CREATE INDEX idx_skills_user_id              │
         │                       ON skills(user_id);                          │
         │  T+12min: VERIFY — Grafana p99 falls 1240ms → 87ms; the Kiali      │
         │           edge turns green; incident closed in 12 minutes          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.6  Deep Dive — How Kiali Builds the Service Graph
         - DEFINITION: the service graph is COMPUTED, never configured —
           Kiali assembles it on every refresh from three live sources
         - Why it matters: when the graph looks wrong, fix the SOURCE:
           • empty graph      → Prometheus is down or not scraping
           • versions missing → pods lack app/version labels
           • no config badges → Kiali cannot reach the Istio config
         - How it works — three sources merged on every refresh:
           • Prometheus     → istio_requests_total counters become EDGES
             (source_workload → destination_workload label pairs)
           • Kubernetes API → pods/services/deployments become NODES,
             with readiness feeding node health
           • Istio config   → VS/DR/Gateway objects add badges and the
             validation results from 11.3
         - Health scoring: a node's color combines request error rate
           AND pod readiness — traffic can look clean while pods
           crash-loop, and Kiali still flags the workload
         - anihpj gotcha: a service with zero traffic shows GRAY (idle),
           not green — edges come from Prometheus counters, so no
           requests means no edges, even when pods are perfectly fine

         [KIALI GRAPH ASSEMBLY — three sources, one picture]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SOURCE 1  PROMETHEUS      → EDGES   istio_requests_total pairs,   │
         │            (telemetry)               req/s, error %, p99 per edge  │
         │  SOURCE 2  KUBERNETES API  → NODES   pods, services, versions,     │
         │            (state)                   readiness → node health       │
         │  SOURCE 3  ISTIO CONFIG    → BADGES  VS/DR/Gateway icons plus      │
         │            (CRDs)                    validation errors (see 11.3)  │
         │                                                                    │
         │                    merged on every refresh (15s)                   │
         │                              │                                     │
         │                              ▼                                     │
         │              ┌───────────────────────────────┐                     │
         │              │      KIALI GRAPH ENGINE       │                     │
         │              │  1. edges  ← telemetry pairs  │                     │
         │              │  2. nodes  ← Kubernetes state │                     │
         │              │  3. badges ← Istio CRDs       │                     │
         │              │  4. colors ← health scoring   │                     │
         │              └───────────────────────────────┘                     │
         │                              │                                     │
         │                              ▼                                     │
         │               colored, clickable service graph                     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [KIALI HEALTH LEGEND — one legend for nodes AND edges]
         ┌───────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Color    │  Meaning — one legend for nodes and edges              │
         ├───────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  🟢 green │  healthy — errors under threshold, pods ready          │
         │  🟠 orange│  degraded — error rate above 10% of requests           │
         │  🔴 red   │  failing — no healthy pods, or requests failing        │
         │  ⚪ gray  │  idle — no traffic in the selected time window         │
         ├───────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │  Edge extras: thickness = request volume, 🔒 = mTLS on that hop    │
         │  Thresholds are tunable in Kiali's health config — anihpj sets     │
         │  "failing" at 5% errors, which is why skills-service (8.1%)        │
         │  shows RED in this chapter's graphs                                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTERS 9-11: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS (Observability)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After these chapters, in your daily work you will:
      • Check Grafana every morning: RED metrics for all anihpj services
      • Know which service is slowest, which has most errors, at a glance
      • Use Jaeger to trace a slow request across ALL services in one view
      • Find bottlenecks: "skills-service DB query takes 1,100ms → add index"
      • Use Kiali service graph as your daily dashboard — see live topology
      • Validate Istio config with istioctl analyze before applying
      • Kiali can catch: missing subsets, port mismatches, VS without DR
      • Remember: metrics = WHAT, traces = WHY, logs = DETAILS
      • Propagate Istio tracing headers in your Django code (x-b3-* headers)
      • Set tracing sampling: 100% in dev, 1-5% in production

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTERS 9-11: ICA EXAM TIPS & QUESTIONS (Observability — feeds Troubleshooting 20%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS — Observability is not its own graded domain; it is
       how you FIND the root cause tested by Troubleshooting (20%, see
       17.9-17.11). Skipping this chapter cluster costs you there instead:
      • "Check RED metrics for a service" = Grafana Istio dashboards
      • "Find why a request is slow across services" = Jaeger distributed tracing
      • "See live service topology" = Kiali service graph
      • "Validate Istio config" = istioctl analyze -n <namespace>

    Q1: How do you open the Kiali dashboard?
    A1: istioctl dashboard kiali

    Q2: What are the three RED metrics Istio captures automatically?
    A2: Rate (requests/sec), Errors (4xx/5xx %), Duration (latency p50/p95/p99)

    Q3: How does Istio enable distributed tracing without code changes?
    A3: Envoy automatically injects tracing headers (x-b3-traceid, x-b3-spanid)
        into every request. Your app just needs to propagate these headers.

    Q4: Jaeger shows ZERO traces for the anihpj namespace. Walk the debug
        chain that finds the cause.
    A4: Check, cheapest first:
        1) Generate traffic — traces only exist for sampled requests:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ for i in $(seq 1 100); do                                │
        │   curl -s -o /dev/null http://$GATEWAY_IP/api/jobs/      │
        │ done                                                     │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        2) Sampling too low? kubectl get telemetry -A — at the default
           1% you need ~100 requests to see a single trace
        3) Tracing provider configured? Check meshConfig
           extensionProviders + a Telemetry resource that references it
        4) App dropping headers? Verify x-b3-* propagation (see Q10).
        Why: no sampling, no provider, or dropped headers all produce an
        empty Jaeger — this order tests them fastest.

    Q5: EXAM TASK: set trace sampling to 100% for the anihpj namespace
        only, leaving the mesh-wide default untouched.
    A5: Apply a namespace-scoped Telemetry resource:
        ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1          │
        │ kind: Telemetry                            │
        │ metadata:                                  │
        │   name: full-sampling                      │
        │   namespace: anihpj   # ← scope = this ns  │
        │ spec:                                      │
        │   tracing:                                 │
        │   - randomSamplingPercentage: 100          │
        └────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: a Telemetry object in a workload namespace overrides the
        mesh default only there — dev debugging gets every trace while
        production namespaces keep their cheap 1-5%.

    Q6: EXAM TASK: add a "request_host" label to istio_requests_total
        for anihpj workloads, without touching application code.
    A6: Use the Telemetry API's tagOverrides:
        ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1                  │
        │ kind: Telemetry                                    │
        │ metadata:                                          │
        │   name: custom-labels                              │
        │   namespace: anihpj                                │
        │ spec:                                              │
        │   metrics:                                         │
        │   - providers:                                     │
        │     - name: prometheus                             │
        │     overrides:                                     │
        │     - match:                                       │
        │         metric: REQUEST_COUNT  # requests_total    │
        │       tagOverrides:                                │
        │         request_host:                              │
        │           value: request.host  # CEL expression    │
        └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: Envoy stamps the new label on every sample; Prometheus picks
        it up from the merged metrics endpoint (istio-agent :15020) on
        the next scrape — zero app redeploys.

    Q7: EXAM TASK: enable Envoy access logs for the anihpj namespace
        only, then read jobpost-api's access log.
    A7: Apply namespace-scoped access logging:
        ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1          │
        │ kind: Telemetry                            │
        │ metadata:                                  │
        │   name: access-logs                        │
        │   namespace: anihpj                        │
        │ spec:                                      │
        │   accessLogging:                           │
        │   - providers:                             │
        │     - name: envoy                          │
        └────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Then read the log:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ kubectl logs deploy/jobpost-api -c istio-proxy -n anihpj │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: a namespace-scoped Telemetry beats mesh-wide
        meshConfig.accessLogFile — you pay the log volume only in the
        namespace you are debugging.

    Q8: Kiali shows a red edge from jobpost-api to skills-service. Give
        the triage sequence.
    A8: 1) Click the edge → per-hop RED metrics: 89 req/s, 8.1% errors,
           p99 1240ms
        2) Check OTHER inbound edges to skills-service — all red means a
           callee (skills-service) problem; only this one red means a
           caller (jobpost-api) problem
        3) Edge panel → "View Traces" → Jaeger opens pre-filtered on
           this hop; open the widest span
        4) Confirm in the proxy log:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ kubectl logs deploy/skills-service -c istio-proxy \      │
        │   -n anihpj | grep " 503 "                               │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: edge → traces → logs narrows topology → request → cause
        without ever guessing.

    Q9: Alert: "istiod config push latency high, proxies STALE." What do
        you check, in order?
    A9: 1) Grafana → Istio Control Plane Dashboard: istiod CPU/memory,
           push latency, connected proxy count
        2) istioctl proxy-status — any proxy not SYNCED for
           CDS/LDS/EDS/RDS is running on stale routing config
        3) kubectl top pod -n istio-system -l app=istiod (at its limit?)
        4) Fix — scale out or raise limits:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ kubectl scale deploy/istiod -n istio-system --replicas=2 │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: STALE proxies silently ignore new VirtualServices — a
        routing change that "does nothing" is often an istiod push
        problem, not a YAML problem.

    Q10: Jaeger shows every anihpj service as its own one-span trace
         instead of one connected chain. Fix it in the Django code.
    A10: The apps are dropping trace context between inbound and
         outbound calls. Propagate the B3 headers:
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ # anihpj/tracing.py                                        │
         │ TRACE_HEADERS = ["x-request-id", "x-b3-traceid",           │
         │     "x-b3-spanid", "x-b3-parentspanid",                    │
         │     "x-b3-sampled", "x-b3-flags"]                          │
         │                                                            │
         │ def trace_headers(request):                                │
         │     return {h: request.headers[h]                          │
         │             for h in TRACE_HEADERS                         │
         │             if h in request.headers}                       │
         │                                                            │
         │ # jobpost/views.py — pass context downstream               │
         │ requests.get(                                              │
         │     "http://skills-service:8090/api/skills/",              │
         │     headers=trace_headers(request))                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         Why: Envoy creates a span per hop, but only the APP can link an
         inbound request to its outbound calls — forwarding x-b3-* joins
         the per-hop spans into one end-to-end trace.


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 7: SECURITY — ZERO-TRUST WITH ISTIO                                 🟡
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Chapter 12: Mutual TLS (mTLS) — Encrypting Everything
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12.1 The Security Problem Without mTLS
         - Inside a Kubernetes cluster: traffic is plain HTTP by default
         - Any compromised pod can sniff traffic from other services
         - Anyone with access to the network can read jobpost DB passwords,
           user tokens, session data passing between anihpj services
         - Traditional solution: implement TLS in every service → complex
         [THREAT DIAGRAM: rogue pod sniffing plain HTTP between services]

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     VISUAL: SECURITY THREAT MODEL — BEFORE vs AFTER ISTIO
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WITHOUT ISTIO — 3 ATTACK VECTORS OPEN                              │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ATTACK 1: Sniffing (Man-in-the-Middle)                             │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  [jobpost-api] ──── plain HTTP ────▶ [user-service]     │      │
         │  │       │              ▲                                    │      │
         │  │       │              │ tcpdump captures:                  │      │
         │  │       │         ┌────┴────────┐                          │      │
         │  │       │         │ 😈 ROGUE POD │  ← compromised pod     │      │
         │  │       │         │  on same node│    on same network     │      │
         │  │       │         └─────────────┘                          │      │
         │  │       │                                                  │      │
         │  │       ▼  CAPTURED DATA:                                  │      │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │      │
         │  │  │ GET /users/42 HTTP/1.1                      │        │      │
         │  │  │ Host: user-service                          │        │      │
         │  │  │ Authorization: Bearer eyJhbGciOiJSUzI1Ni...  │  ← JWT │      │
         │  │  │ X-User-Data: {"email":"admin@anihpj.com"}   │  ← PII │      │
         │  │  │ Cookie: sessionid=a8f5c30d9e2b...           │  ← SESS│      │
         │  │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │      │
         │  │  ❌ ALL user data, tokens, sessions VISIBLE in plaintext │      │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ATTACK 2: Service Impersonation (Fake Service)                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  [jobpost-api] ──── plain HTTP ────▶ [😈 fake-user-svc] │      │
         │  │                                         │                │      │
         │  │                            "I am user-service!"          │      │
         │  │                            Returns: fake user data       │      │
         │  │                            Steals: real user requests    │      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  ❌ No way to verify the other service's identity       │      │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ATTACK 3: Lateral Movement (Compromised Service)                   │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  [😈 compromised skills-svc] ──HTTP──▶ [user-service]   │      │
         │  │  "GET /users/ (all users)"                               │      │
         │  │  "GET /users/42/payment-info"                            │      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  ❌ Any service can call any other service               │      │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════    │
         │                                                                     │
         │  WITH ISTIO — ALL 3 ATTACKS BLOCKED                                 │
         │                                                                     │
         │  DEFENSE 1: mTLS Encryption (PeerAuthentication)                    │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  [jobpost-api] ──🔒 mTLS tunnel ──▶ [user-service]      │      │
         │  │       │              ▲              │                    │      │
         │  │       │              │ tcpdump:     │                    │      │
         │  │       │         ┌────┴────────┐     │                    │      │
         │  │       │         │ 😈 ROGUE POD │     │                    │      │
         │  │       │         │ sees:        │     │                    │      │
         │  │       │         │ ENCRYPTED    │     │  Decrypts with    │      │
         │  │       │         │ BINARY DATA  │     │  SPIFFE cert ✅   │      │
         │  │       │         │ (garbage!)   │     │                    │      │
         │  │       │         └─────────────┘     │                    │      │
         │  │       │                              │                    │      │
         │  │       │  ✅ Encrypted — attacker sees NOTHING           │      │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
         │                                                                     │
         │  DEFENSE 2: SPIFFE Identity (prevents impersonation)               │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  [jobpost-api] ──mTLS──▶ [😈 fake-user-svc]             │      │
         │  │                              │                           │      │
         │  │                "Show me your SPIFFE certificate!"        │      │
         │  │                              │                           │      │
         │  │                fake-svc presents: NO VALID CERT ❌       │      │
         │  │                (or presents: spiffe://.../ns/hack/sa/x)  │      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  ✅ Envoy REJECTS — certificate not from anihpj/user-svc │      │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
         │                                                                     │
         │  DEFENSE 3: AuthorizationPolicy (prevents lateral movement)        │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │                                                          │      │
         │  │  [😈 compromised skills-svc] ──mTLS──▶ [user-service]   │      │
         │  │                                      │                   │      │
         │  │           Envoy inbound checks:      │                   │      │
         │  │           "Caller: spiffe://.../ns/anihpj/sa/skills-svc" │      │
         │  │           "AuthzPolicy says:                             │      │
         │  │              ALLOW from: jobpost-api ONLY"               │      │
         │  │           "skills-svc is NOT in the ALLOW list"          │      │
         │  │                                      │                   │      │
         │  │           ❌ 403 Forbidden — BLOCKED! ✅                 │      │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [THREAT MODEL — QUICK RECAP]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ATTACK 1: Network Sniffing (Man-in-the-Middle)                    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [jobpost-api] ──HTTP──▶ [user-service]                           │
         │                    │                                               │
         │              [😈 rogue-pod]                                        │
         │               (on same node)                                       │
         │               captures plain HTTP:                                 │
         │               "Authorization: Bearer eyJhbGci...  ← JWT STOLEN!   │
         │               "password=SuperSecret123"            ← DATA EXPOSED  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ATTACK 2: Service Impersonation                                   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [jobpost-api] ──HTTP──▶ [😈 fake-user-service]                   │
         │                          (attacker deployed a pod                  │
         │                           named "user-service" in a                │
         │                           different namespace)                     │
         │                          "I'm the real user-service — trust me!"  │
         │                          → Returns fake user data                  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ATTACK 3: Lateral Movement                                        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [😈 compromised skills-svc] ──HTTP──▶ [user-service]            │
         │  "Please give me all users' personal data..."                      │
         │  → No AuthorizationPolicy = request ALLOWED!                      │
         │  → All user data exfiltrated                                       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITH ISTIO mTLS + AuthorizationPolicy:                            │
         │  ✅ Attack 1 blocked: all traffic is encrypted                     │
         │  ✅ Attack 2 blocked: fake service has no valid SPIFFE cert        │
         │  ✅ Attack 3 blocked: AuthzPolicy denies skills-svc→user-svc call  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.2 What is Mutual TLS (mTLS)?
         - Regular TLS: server proves identity to client (HTTPS website)
         - Mutual TLS: BOTH sides prove identity to each other
         - Istio issues X.509 SVID certificates (SPIFFE standard) to every workload
         - Certificate contains: spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api
         - This certificate identifies the service, not just the node/IP
         - Rotation: certificates expire and rotate every 24 hours automatically
         [mTLS HANDSHAKE DIAGRAM: jobpost-api ↔ user-service certificate exchange]

         [mTLS HANDSHAKE LADDER — jobpost-api ↔ user-service, step by step]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │   Envoy @ jobpost-api                 Envoy @ user-service      │
         │       │                                         │               │
         │       │ ① ClientHello — TLS 1.3, ALPN "istio"   │               │
         │       │────────────────────────────────────────▶│               │
         │       │                                         │               │
         │       │ ② ServerHello + server SVID certificate │               │
         │       │◀────────────────────────────────────────│               │
         │       │      spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/  │               │
         │       │      sa/user-service                    │               │
         │       │                                         │               │
         │       │ ③ client verifies the server cert:      │               │
         │       │    • signed by the mesh root CA?        │               │
         │       │    • SAN = the SPIFFE ID istiod says    │               │
         │       │      user-service must have? (→ 12.5)   │               │
         │       │    • not expired?  → all checks ✅       │               │
         │       │                                         │               │
         │       │ ④ client sends its OWN SVID certificate │               │
         │       │────────────────────────────────────────▶│               │
         │       │      spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/  │               │
         │       │      sa/jobpost-api                     │               │
         │       │                                         │               │
         │       │ ⑤ server verifies chain + records the   │               │
         │       │    caller principal for AuthzPolicy     │               │
         │       │                                         │               │
         │       │ ⑥ 🔒 encrypted tunnel established        │               │
         │       │◀═══════════════════════════════════════▶│               │
         │       │    "GET /users/42" now travels encrypted│               │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [mTLS EXPLAINED — what happens in each handshake]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WITHOUT mTLS (plain HTTP):                            │
         │  jobpost-api ──────────────────────────▶ user-service  │
         │               "GET /users/42 HTTP/1.1"                 │
         │               (anyone can read this!)                  │
         │                                                        │
         │  WITH mTLS (Istio automatic):                          │
         │                                                        │
         │  ① Envoy(jobpost) → "Hello, I am jobpost-api"         │
         │                    presents certificate (SVID)         │
         │  ② Envoy(user)   → "I am user-service"                │
         │                    presents certificate (SVID)         │
         │  ③ BOTH verify each other's certificates with istiod   │
         │  ④ Encrypted tunnel established                        │
         │  ⑤ "GET /users/42" sent through encrypted tunnel      │
         │                                                        │
         │  ✅ No code change in Django to get mTLS!              │
         │  ✅ Certificates auto-issued and rotated by istiod     │
         │  ✅ SPIFFE identity — tied to service, not IP          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.3 PeerAuthentication — Configuring mTLS Mode
         - PeerAuthentication is the Istio CRD that controls mTLS
         - Three modes:
           • PERMISSIVE: accept both plain HTTP and mTLS (migration mode)
           • STRICT: reject all plain HTTP — enforce mTLS everywhere
           • DISABLE: turn off mTLS (for debugging only)
         - Apply per namespace or per workload
         - anihpj: start with PERMISSIVE during migration, then STRICT
         [YAML: PeerAuthentication for anihpj namespace in STRICT mode]

         [PeerAuthentication YAML — enforce mTLS for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1                 │
         │  kind: PeerAuthentication                              │
         │  metadata:                                             │
         │    name: default                                       │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                   │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    mtls:                                               │
         │      mode: STRICT   # ALL traffic must use mTLS       │
         │                     # Plain HTTP will be rejected      │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Alternative: per-workload only                      │
         │  spec:                                                 │
         │    selector:                                           │
         │      matchLabels:                                      │
         │        app: user-service   # Only for user-service     │
         │    mtls:                                               │
         │      mode: STRICT                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.4 Certificate Lifecycle — How istiod Issues Certs
         - Every Pod gets an X.509 certificate at startup via istiod CA
         - Certificate Authority is built into istiod (no external CA needed)
         - SPIFFE Verifiable Identity Document (SVID) format
         - Rotation: 24-hour default, rotated 5 minutes before expiry
         - Plugging in external CAs (AWS ACM, Vault, Cert-Manager)
         [CERT ISSUANCE FLOW DIAGRAM: Pod starts → istiod issues cert → mTLS ready]

         [CERTIFICATE LIFECYCLE — full flow for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  T=0s:   kubectl apply deploys jobpost-api Pod                     │
         │  T=0.1s: Istio Mutating Webhook INJECTS istio-proxy sidecar       │
         │  T=0.2s: Pod starts — istio-proxy starts alongside Django          │
         │  T=0.3s: istio-proxy sends CSR to istiod via port 15012           │
         │          CSR contains: spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/          │
         │                        sa/jobpost-api-serviceaccount               │
         │  T=0.5s: istiod (Citadel CA) validates ServiceAccount identity    │
         │          istiod signs X.509 cert with 24h expiry                  │
         │  T=0.6s: Certificate delivered to istio-proxy (in-memory)         │
         │  T=0.7s: mTLS ready! All outbound calls from this pod now use     │
         │          the certificate to authenticate                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  T=23h55m: 5 minutes before expiry → rotation begins              │
         │  T=23h56m: New CSR sent → new cert issued → old cert replaced      │
         │  T=24h:    Old cert expired — rotation seamless, no downtime       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  CERT ANATOMY:                                                     │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
         │  │  Subject:  spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/             │     │
         │  │            sa/jobpost-api-serviceaccount                  │     │
         │  │  Issuer:   istiod (cluster.local root CA)                │     │
         │  │  Valid:    24 hours                                       │     │
         │  │  SANs:     URI:spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/...      │     │
         │  │  Uses:     mTLS authentication only                      │     │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PERMISSIVE vs STRICT mTLS — migration path for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WEEK 1: Add Istio, keep PERMISSIVE mode                          │
         │  ────────────────────────────────────                             │
         │  • Sidecars injected, no traffic disruption                       │
         │  • Existing services still use plain HTTP internally              │
         │  • Kiali shows: some connections encrypted, some plain            │
         │  • Validate: all services working → check Kiali graph             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WEEK 2: Switch to STRICT mode per namespace                      │
         │  ────────────────────────────────────────────                     │
         │  • kubectl apply PeerAuthentication with mode: STRICT             │
         │  • Any non-sidecar service → 403 errors start appearing           │
         │  • Fix: ensure ALL consumers have Istio sidecars injected         │
         │  • Validate: no plain HTTP in Kiali → all edges show lock icon    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  RESULT: Every service-to-service call in anihpj is encrypted     │
         │  without a single line of Django code changed ✅                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.5  Secure Naming — Preventing Service Impersonation
         - DEFINITION: secure naming is istiod's built-in mapping of
           "service name → the SPIFFE identities allowed to SERVE it";
           client Envoys enforce it on every mTLS handshake
         - Why it matters: a VALID certificate alone only proves mesh
           membership, not the right to answer as user-service — without
           secure naming, ANY workload's cert could front any service
         - How it works:
           • istiod watches Services and the ServiceAccounts behind them
           • it compiles the map and pushes it to every client Envoy (xDS)
           • in the handshake, the client compares the presented SVID
             against the identities registered for the service it dialed
           → mismatch = handshake aborted before any app data is sent
         - Network tricks are useless against it: stolen ClusterIPs,
           poisoned DNS and spoofed IPs all fail the cryptographic check

         [IMPERSONATION ATTACK — stolen ClusterIP, defeated by secure naming]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  GOAL: attacker wants to receive jobpost-api → user-service     │
         │  traffic (sessions, JWTs, PII)                                  │
         │                                                                 │
         │  STEP 1  compromise the "test-team" ServiceAccount              │
         │          → attacker now owns a VALID mesh certificate:          │
         │            spiffe://cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/test-team        │
         │  STEP 2  deploy a fake pod and hijack user-service's            │
         │          ClusterIP (or poison DNS) to redirect traffic          │
         │  STEP 3  jobpost-api dials "user-service:8080"                  │
         │          → packets land on the fake pod — the NETWORK           │
         │            layer has been fooled successfully 😈                 │
         │  STEP 4  mTLS handshake — fake pod presents its SVID:           │
         │            …/ns/anihpj/sa/test-team  (valid! trusted CA!)       │
         │                                                                 │
         │  WITHOUT secure naming:                                         │
         │  cert is valid + chains to the mesh root → accepted             │
         │  → live anihpj traffic flows to the attacker ❌                  │
         │                                                                 │
         │  WITH secure naming (Istio default, always on):                 │
         │  client Envoy consults the istiod-pushed map:                   │
         │    "user-service" ⇒ only …/ns/anihpj/sa/user-service            │
         │  presented …/sa/test-team ⇒ MISMATCH                            │
         │  → handshake ABORTED at STEP 4 — the TCP connection is          │
         │    torn down before a single application byte is sent ✅         │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [SECURE NAMING MAP — pushed by istiod to every client Envoy]
         ┌───────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┐
         │ Service being dialed          │ authorized server identity      │
         ├───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
         │ user-service.anihpj:8080      │ …/ns/anihpj/sa/user-service ✅   │
         │ jobpost-api.anihpj:8000       │ …/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api ✅    │
         │ skills-service.anihpj:8090    │ …/ns/anihpj/sa/skills-service ✅ │
         │ user-service.anihpj:8080      │ …/sa/test-team ❌ NOT AUTHORIZED │
         └───────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┘

    12.6  PeerAuthentication Policy Hierarchy — Mesh → Namespace → Workload
         - DEFINITION: PeerAuthentication can be declared at three scopes:
           mesh-wide (root namespace istio-system, no selector),
           namespace-wide (any namespace, no selector), and
           workload-specific (spec.selector.matchLabels set)
         - Why it matters: production meshes layer all three — a mesh-wide
           PERMISSIVE fallback, STRICT per hardened namespace, plus the
           odd workload exemption; you must predict which one applies
         - The rule: the NARROWEST match wins — workload beats namespace,
           namespace beats mesh (think CSS specificity, but for mTLS)
         - Only ONE mesh-wide and ONE namespace-wide policy take effect
           (convention: name them "default") — if duplicates exist, the
           OLDEST wins and newer ones are silently ignored
         - Several workload policies may exist; if two select the same
           pod, again the OLDEST wins — delete stale ones to avoid
           mystery behaviour

         [POLICY PRECEDENCE — narrowest match wins]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Pod: user-service-6b9c…  (app: user-service, ns: anihpj)       │
         │                                                                 │
         │  candidate policies:                            mode            │
         │  ① MESH-WIDE   istio-system, no selector       PERMISSIVE       │
         │  ② NAMESPACE   anihpj, no selector             STRICT           │
         │  ③ WORKLOAD    selector app: user-service      DISABLE          │
         │                (legacy port exemption)                          │
         │                                                                 │
         │  precedence:  ③ beats ② beats ①                                 │
         │  → effective mode for THIS pod:  DISABLE   (from ③)             │
         │  → every other anihpj pod:       STRICT    (from ②)             │
         │  → pods in other namespaces:     PERMISSIVE (from ①)            │
         │                                                                 │
         │  delete ③ → pod falls back to ②  (STRICT)                       │
         │  delete ② → namespace falls back to ①  (PERMISSIVE)             │
         │  delete ① → Istio built-in default:  PERMISSIVE                 │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [THE 3 SCOPES — YAML differences at a glance]
         ┌──────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────────┬──────────────┐
         │ Scope        │ namespace:      │ selector:       │ priority     │
         ├──────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────┤
         │ Mesh-wide    │ istio-system    │ (none)          │ LOWEST       │
         │              │ (the root ns)   │                 │ (fallback)   │
         ├──────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────┤
         │ Namespace    │ anihpj          │ (none)          │ MIDDLE       │
         ├──────────────┼─────────────────┼─────────────────┼──────────────┤
         │ Workload     │ anihpj          │ matchLabels:    │ HIGHEST      │
         │              │                 │   app: user-svc │              │
         └──────────────┴─────────────────┴─────────────────┴──────────────┘

         [3-SCOPE YAML — same CRD, three different reaches]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1   # v1 GA since Istio 1.22    │
         │  kind: PeerAuthentication                                       │
         │  metadata:                                                      │
         │    name: default                                                │
         │    namespace: istio-system    ← root namespace = MESH-WIDE      │
         │  spec:                                                          │
         │    mtls:                                                        │
         │      mode: PERMISSIVE         # mesh-level fallback             │
         │  ---                                                            │
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                               │
         │  kind: PeerAuthentication                                       │
         │  metadata:                                                      │
         │    name: default                                                │
         │    namespace: anihpj          ← no selector = NAMESPACE-WIDE    │
         │  spec:                                                          │
         │    mtls:                                                        │
         │      mode: STRICT             # all of anihpj must use mTLS     │
         │  ---                                                            │
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                               │
         │  kind: PeerAuthentication                                       │
         │  metadata:                                                      │
         │    name: legacy-batch-exempt                                    │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                            │
         │  spec:                                                          │
         │    selector:                                                    │
         │      matchLabels:                                               │
         │        app: legacy-batch      ← selector = WORKLOAD scope       │
         │    mtls:                                                        │
         │      mode: PERMISSIVE         # this ONE app may still get      │
         │                               # plaintext (overrides STRICT)    │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.7  mTLS Troubleshooting — Common Scenarios and Solutions
         - Golden rule 1: STRICT rejects plaintext at the TCP layer — the
           caller sees "connection reset by peer", NEVER an HTTP 403
           → if you see 403 "RBAC: access denied", the problem is an
             AuthorizationPolicy (Chapter 13), not mTLS
         - Golden rule 2: 503 (response flag UC) minutes after enabling
           STRICT almost always means a stale DestinationRule still says
           tls.mode: DISABLE on the client side (deep dive in 12.8)
         - Golden rule 3: measure before guessing — four modern probes:
           istioctl x describe pod, istioctl proxy-config secret, the
           Kiali lock icons, and a raw openssl s_client handshake

         [mTLS TROUBLESHOOTING MATRIX — symptom → root cause → fix]
         ┌─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
         │ SYMPTOM             │ ROOT CAUSE          │ FIX                 │
         ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ 503 (flag UC) right │ stale Destination-  │ set tls.mode:       │
         │ after enabling      │ Rule with tls.mode: │ ISTIO_MUTUAL or     │
         │ STRICT              │ DISABLE (client)    │ delete the tls      │
         │                     │                     │ block entirely      │
         ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ curl: "connection   │ plaintext hit a     │ inject sidecar into │
         │ reset by peer" —    │ STRICT port — mTLS  │ the caller pod; or  │
         │ NOT an HTTP 403!    │ rejects at the TCP  │ PERMISSIVE while    │
         │                     │ layer               │ migrating           │
         ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ sidecar-less pod    │ STRICT demands a    │ label namespace     │
         │ can't reach anihpj  │ client SVID cert    │ istio-injection=    │
         │ services            │ it does not have    │ enabled + rollout   │
         │                     │                     │ restart             │
         ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ calls OK from ns A, │ PA scope differs    │ align PeerAuthN;    │
         │ fail from ns B      │ between namespaces  │ check istioctl x    │
         │                     │                     │ describe pod        │
         ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ HTTP 403 "RBAC:     │ NOT mTLS! An        │ fix the Authz-      │
         │ access denied"      │ AuthorizationPolicy │ Policy (Ch 13)      │
         │                     │ denied the caller   │                     │
         ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ TLS handshake       │ istiod down >24h —  │ restore istiod;     │
         │ fails, cert expired │ no CSR renewals     │ restart pod for a   │
         │                     │ happened            │ fresh CSR + cert    │
         └─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

         [mTLS VERIFICATION TOOLBOX — modern commands only]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1 — one-shot mTLS posture of a pod (BEST first move)         │
         │  istioctl x describe pod jobpost-api-7d9f4b-x2v4c -n anihpj     │
         │  #   → prints effective PeerAuthentication + DR TLS mode        │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # 2 — inspect the live SPIFFE cert Envoy holds in memory       │
         │  istioctl proxy-config secret deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj      │
         │  #   → shows "default" cert + ROOTCA, ~24h validity window      │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # 3 — Kiali:  Graph → Display → Security                       │
         │  #   → every encrypted edge shows a 🔒 padlock icon              │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # 4 — raw probe: openssl against the service port              │
         │  openssl s_client -connect user-service.anihpj:8080             │
         │  #   → shows the presented chain; SAN = SPIFFE URI              │
         │                                                                 │
         │  ⚠️  "istioctl authn tls-check" was REMOVED long ago —          │
         │      if a runbook still mentions it, use #1/#2 instead          │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.8  Deep Dive — The DR/PA TLS Contract (Why 503s Happen)
         - DEFINITION: mTLS is a CONTRACT between two independent configs:
           • DestinationRule trafficPolicy.tls.mode → what the CLIENT
             Envoy sends (ISTIO_MUTUAL, MUTUAL, SIMPLE, DISABLE)
           • PeerAuthentication mtls.mode → what the SERVER Envoy
             accepts (STRICT, PERMISSIVE, DISABLE)
         - 503s are born when the two sides disagree — the client-side
           Envoy fails to open the upstream connection and synthesizes
           a 503 (flag UC); the server never even logs a request
         - Best practice: write NO tls block in DRs at all — sidecar-to-
           sidecar traffic then auto-negotiates mTLS ("auto mTLS") and
           tracks the PA mode with zero extra configuration

         [DR × PA TLS MATRIX — client mode vs server mode]
         ┌─────────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────┬───────────────┐
         │ DR tls.mode     │ PA: STRICT    │ PA: PERMISSIVE│ PA: DISABLE   │
         ├─────────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ ISTIO_MUTUAL    │ ✅ mTLS 🔒      │ ✅ mTLS 🔒      │ ❌ handshake   │
         │ (mesh certs)    │               │               │ fails         │
         ├─────────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ DISABLE         │ ❌ TCP reset   │ ✅ works       │ ✅ works       │
         │ (plaintext)     │ client: 503   │ ⚠️ UNENCRYPTED│ ⚠️ UNENCRYPTED│
         ├─────────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────┼───────────────┤
         │ (no tls block)  │ ✅ auto-mTLS   │ ✅ auto-mTLS   │ ✅ plaintext   │
         │ = safest DR     │               │               │               │
         └─────────────────┴───────────────┴───────────────┴───────────────┘

         [THE 3 CLASSIC TRAPS — memorize these before the exam]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  TRAP 1 — the invisible DISABLE:                                │
         │  a DR written pre-Istio with tls.mode: DISABLE sits             │
         │  harmless under PERMISSIVE for months… the day PA flips         │
         │  to STRICT, every call through it dies with 503 UC              │
         │  → audit BEFORE enabling STRICT:                                │
         │    kubectl get destinationrules -A -o yaml | grep -B5 \         │
         │      "mode: DISABLE"                                            │
         │                                                                 │
         │  TRAP 2 — ISTIO_MUTUAL ≠ MUTUAL:                                │
         │  ISTIO_MUTUAL = mesh's auto-issued SPIFFE certs (normal)        │
         │  MUTUAL       = certs YOU mount yourself (external TLS)         │
         │  picking MUTUAL without providing certs = broken handshake      │
         │                                                                 │
         │  TRAP 3 — blaming the server:                                   │
         │  the 503 is generated CLIENT-side (Envoy could not open         │
         │  the upstream TLS connection) — the server never saw the        │
         │  request; its access log stays empty. Read the CALLER's         │
         │  logs and response flags (UC/UF) first                          │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    12.9  Deep Dive — External CAs: SPIRE Integration and Plug-in CA Certificates
         - By default istiod IS the CA: it creates a self-signed root at
           install and signs every workload cert in-process — perfect
           for anihpj-sized shops, zero configuration required
         - When security/PKI teams demand more, three upgrade paths:
           • PLUG-IN CA CERTS — istiod keeps signing, but with YOUR
             enterprise root/intermediate (the "cacerts" secret)
           • SPIRE — the CNCF SPIFFE Runtime Environment takes over
             cert issuance entirely (HSM backends, FIPS 140-2,
             identity federation across clusters and clouds)
           • CERT-MANAGER (istio-csr) — istiod becomes a Registration
             Authority and forwards CSRs to cert-manager, which signs
             via Vault, Venafi, or a private ACME CA
         - Exam signals that point here: "FIPS compliance", "use our
           existing PKI", "HSM integration", "trust-domain federation"

         [SPIRE INTEGRATION FLOW — external SPIFFE CA replaces istiod CA]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ① SPIRE Agent (DaemonSet — one per node) ATTESTS each          │
         │     workload: k8s ServiceAccount? namespace? pod labels?        │
         │  ② SPIRE Server checks the attestation against its              │
         │     registration entries                                        │
         │       entry: spiffe://anihpj.com/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api       │
         │  ③ SPIRE Server signs an X.509 SVID — the signing key can       │
         │     live in an HSM or Vault (FIPS 140-2 territory)              │
         │  ④ SVID is pushed down to the node's SPIRE Agent, which         │
         │     exposes it on a Unix Domain Socket (UDS)                    │
         │  ⑤ istio-proxy fetches the SVID via SDS from that socket        │
         │     (istiod no longer issues certs — SPIRE owns rotation)       │
         │  ⑥ mTLS handshakes work EXACTLY as before — Envoy only          │
         │     cares that the cert chains to the trusted root 🔒            │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - Note the trust domain changes: SPIRE SVIDs carry your
           registered domain (spiffe://anihpj.com/…), not cluster.local

         [PLUG-IN CA CERTIFICATES — the cacerts secret, step by step]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # STEP 1 — generate a root CA (keep root-key.pem OFFLINE)      │
         │  make -f tools/certs/Makefile.selfsigned.mk root-ca             │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # STEP 2 — generate this cluster's intermediate CA             │
         │  make -f tools/certs/Makefile.selfsigned.mk \                   │
         │    cluster1-cacerts                                             │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # STEP 3 — create the magic secret; name MUST be               │
         │  #          "cacerts", namespace MUST be istio-system           │
         │  kubectl create secret generic cacerts -n istio-system \        │
         │    --from-file=cluster1/ca-cert.pem \                           │
         │    --from-file=cluster1/ca-key.pem \                            │
         │    --from-file=cluster1/root-cert.pem \                         │
         │    --from-file=cluster1/cert-chain.pem                          │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # STEP 4 — (re)install Istio — istiod auto-detects it          │
         │  istioctl install --set profile=default -y                      │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # STEP 5 — restart workloads to pick up new certs              │
         │  kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj                   │
         │                                                                 │
         │  RESULT: istiod still issues + rotates 24h workload             │
         │  certs, but every cert now chains to YOUR root ✅                │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - cert-manager route in one line: install istio-csr, point
           istiod at it, and every CSR flows to cert-manager's issuer
           → certificates audited and managed like the rest of your PKI

         [CA HIERARCHY — one offline root, per-cluster intermediates]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │              ROOT CA — offline, HSM-protected                   │
         │                          │ signs                                │
         │          ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐                      │
         │          ▼               ▼               ▼                      │
         │   Intermediate CA Intermediate CA Intermediate CA               │
         │     (cluster1)      (cluster2)      (cluster3)                  │
         │          │               │               │                      │
         │      istiod #1       istiod #2       istiod #3                  │
         │                                                                 │
         │   each istiod signs 24h workload SVIDs that chain to            │
         │   the SAME root → cross-cluster mTLS with one trust             │
         │   domain 🔒                                                      │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Chapter 13: Authorization Policies — Who Can Call What
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13.1 The Need for Service-Level Authorization
         - mTLS proves identity but doesn't restrict what is allowed
         - Without AuthorizationPolicy: any service can call any other service
         - anihpj threat: a compromised skills-service could call user-service
           and read all user data — even though it has no business doing so
         - Zero-trust principle: deny everything by default, allow explicitly
         [THREAT DIAGRAM: overly permissive mesh vs zero-trust mesh]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WITHOUT AuthorizationPolicy — mTLS-only 'permissive' mesh:        │
         │                                                                    │
         │   😈 compromised skills-service (attacker inside the pod)           │
         │      ├──✅──▶ user-service      GET /api/users/*  → dumps user PII  │
         │      ├──✅──▶ auth-service      probes /validate  → forges sessions │
         │      └──✅──▶ location-service  maps the internal network           │
         │                                                                    │
         │   mTLS happily says: 'this really IS skills-service' — then lets   │
         │   every call through. Identity ✅  Encryption 🔒  Permissions ❌      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WITH zero-trust (deny-all + explicit ALLOW policies):             │
         │                                                                    │
         │   😈 compromised skills-service                                     │
         │      ├──❌──▶ user-service      403 RBAC: access denied             │
         │      ├──❌──▶ auth-service      403 (not on the allow-list)         │
         │      └──❌──▶ location-service  403                                 │
         │                                                                    │
         │   jobpost-api ──✅──▶ skills-service  (explicitly allowed, GET)     │
         │                                                                    │
         │   Blast radius of one hacked pod: EVERYTHING → almost nothing      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    13.2 AuthorizationPolicy — The Istio Firewall
         - AuthorizationPolicy is Istio's access control CRD
         - Works at L7 (HTTP): can allow/deny based on:
           • Source service identity (SPIFFE certificate)
           • HTTP method (GET, POST, DELETE)
           • HTTP path (/jobs/, /admin/)
           • HTTP headers (Authorization, x-user-id)
           • Source namespace
         - Default behavior: ALLOW ALL (if no policy exists)
         - With a DENY-ALL policy: must explicitly allow each path

         [AUTHORIZATIONPOLICY EVALUATION — how Istio decides ALLOW or DENY]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Incoming request: skills-service → GET /users/42 → user-service  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  EVALUATION ORDER:                                                 │
         │                                                                    │
         │  1. Is there a CUSTOM policy (action: CUSTOM) for user-service?   │
         │     • YES → evaluate it first; if it rejects → DENY               │
         │     • NO  → continue                                               │
         │                                                                    │
         │  2. Is there a DENY policy matching this request?                  │
         │     • YES, DENY all traffic to user-service?                      │
         │       • Does it match source: skills-service? YES → DENY ❌        │
         │     • NO matching DENY → continue                                  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  3. Is there an ALLOW policy for user-service?                     │
         │     • YES, ALLOW from: jobpost-api only                           │
         │       • Does skills-service match? NO → implicit DENY ❌           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  4. No matching ALLOW found → DENY (when any policy exists)       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  RESULT: skills-service → user-service = ❌ DENIED (403)          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  KEY RULE: If ANY AuthorizationPolicy targets a workload,         │
         │  then all requests NOT explicitly ALLOWED are DENIED.             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [COMPLETE AUTHORIZATIONPOLICY YAML — deny-all then allow-list]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Step 1: Deny ALL traffic in anihpj namespace                   │
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1                            │
         │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                        │
         │  metadata:                                                        │
         │    name: deny-all                                                 │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                              │
         │  spec: {}   ← empty spec = deny everything                       │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  # Step 2: Allow ingress gateway → jobpost-api                    │
         │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                        │
         │  metadata:                                                        │
         │    name: allow-ingress-to-jobpost                                 │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                              │
         │  spec:                                                            │
         │    selector:                                                      │
         │      matchLabels: {app: jobpost-api}    ← applies to jobpost-api  │
         │    action: ALLOW                                                   │
         │    rules:                                                         │
         │    - from:                                                        │
         │      - source:                                                    │
         │          principals:                                              │
         │          - "cluster.local/ns/istio-system/sa/istio-ingressgateway"│
         │      to:                                                          │
         │      - operation:                                                 │
         │          methods: ["GET", "POST"]  ← only GET and POST           │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  # Step 3: Allow jobpost-api → user-service (GET only)            │
         │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                        │
         │  metadata:                                                        │
         │    name: allow-jobpost-to-users                                   │
         │  spec:                                                            │
         │    selector:                                                      │
         │      matchLabels: {app: user-service}                             │
         │    action: ALLOW                                                   │
         │    rules:                                                         │
         │    - from:                                                        │
         │      - source:                                                    │
         │          principals:                                              │
         │          - "cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api"              │
         │      to:                                                          │
         │      - operation:                                                 │
         │          methods: ["GET"]                                         │
         │          paths: ["/api/users/*"]   ← restrict to specific paths  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    13.3 Building a Zero-Trust Policy for anihpj
         [COMPLETE POLICY WALKTHROUGH]
         - Step 1: Deny everything in the anihpj namespace
         - Step 2: Allow external traffic in via ingressgateway only
         - Step 3: Allow jobpost-api to call user-service (GET only)
         - Step 4: Allow jobpost-api to call skills-service (GET only)
         - Step 5: Allow user-service to call auth-service (GET/POST)
         - Step 6: Allow NO cross-calls that aren't explicitly needed
         - Step 7: Allow metrics scraping (Prometheus to all services)
         [POLICY MATRIX DIAGRAM: who is allowed to call what]

         [ZERO-TRUST POLICY MATRIX for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SOURCE        │  TARGET          │  ALLOWED METHODS  │
         │  ──────────────│──────────────────│──────────────────  │
         │  ingressgw     │  jobpost-api     │  GET, POST        │
         │  jobpost-api   │  user-service    │  GET only         │
         │  jobpost-api   │  skills-service  │  GET only         │
         │  jobpost-api   │  location-svc    │  GET only         │
         │  user-service  │  auth-service    │  GET, POST        │
         │  prometheus    │  ALL services    │  GET /metrics     │
         │  ANY OTHER     │  ANY             │  ❌ DENIED        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Rollout order matters: apply the ALLOW policies FIRST, the
           deny-all LAST (or shadow it with dry-run, see 13.8) —
           applying deny-all before the ALLOWs exist will 403 every
           live call for a few seconds
         - Each ALLOW policy lives next to its TARGET service: the
           policy selecting user-service governs traffic INTO
           user-service, not traffic it sends out

         [ROLLOUT & VERIFY — applying the matrix without an outage]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. Apply every ALLOW policy from the matrix (rows above)        │
         │  $ kubectl apply -f authz/ -n anihpj      # 6 ALLOW policies       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 2. Apply deny-all in DRY-RUN mode first (see 13.8)              │
         │  #    metadata.annotations: "istio.io/dry-run": "true"             │
         │  $ kubectl apply -f deny-all-dryrun.yaml                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 3. Watch shadow-denies across live traffic (847 req/s)          │
         │  #    → zero legitimate flows would be denied? proceed             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 4. Enforce: remove the dry-run annotation                       │
         │  $ kubectl annotate authorizationpolicy/deny-all -n anihpj \       │
         │      istio.io/dry-run-                                             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 5. Verify one allowed and one denied path                       │
         │  $ kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \                  │
         │      curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \                      │
         │      http://user-service:8080/api/users/42        → 200 ✅          │
         │  $ kubectl exec deploy/skills-service -n anihpj -- \               │
         │      curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \                      │
         │      http://user-service:8080/api/users/42        → 403 ❌          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    13.4 RequestAuthentication — JWT Verification
         - RequestAuthentication: validate incoming JWT tokens at the mesh level
         - Verify JWTs from your auth-service without code in every Django service
         - Extract JWT claims for use in AuthorizationPolicy conditions
         - anihpj use case: only requests with valid JWT issued by auth-service
           can reach user-service or jobpost-api
         [YAML: RequestAuthentication + AuthorizationPolicy using JWT claims]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Step 1: teach the mesh how to VALIDATE anihpj JWTs              │
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                  │
         │  kind: RequestAuthentication                                       │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: jwt-auth                                                  │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      matchLabels: {app: jobpost-api}  ← guard the public API pods  │
         │    jwtRules:                                                       │
         │    - issuer: "https://auth.anihpj.com"   ← must equal iss claim    │
         │      jwksUri: "https://auth.anihpj.com/.well-known/jwks.json"      │
         │                ↑ public signing keys Envoy fetches to verify JWTs  │
         │  ---                                                               │
         │  # Step 2: REQUIRE the JWT — RequestAuthentication alone does NOT  │
         │  # block requests that simply OMIT the token!                      │
         │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                  │
         │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                         │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: require-jwt                                               │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    selector:                                                       │
         │      matchLabels: {app: jobpost-api}                               │
         │    action: ALLOW                                                   │
         │    rules:                                                          │
         │    - from:                                                         │
         │      - source:                                                     │
         │          requestPrincipals: ["https://auth.anihpj.com/*"]          │
         │            ↑ "issuer/subject" — only set when a VALID JWT exists   │
         │      when:                                                         │
         │      - key: request.auth.claims[role]     ← claim-based condition  │
         │        values: ["admin", "recruiter"]  ← Django roles in the JWT   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [JWT ENFORCEMENT MATRIX — 401 vs 403 vs silent pass-through]
         ┌────────────────┬────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
         │  TOKEN STATE   │ RequestAuthN alone     │ + AuthZ (reqPrincipals)  │
         ├────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │  valid JWT     │ ✅ pass, claims set     │ ✅ 200 (principal set)    │
         │  INVALID JWT   │ ❌ 401 rejected HERE    │ (never reaches AuthZ)    │
         │  NO token      │ ⚠️  passes through!     │ ❌ 403 (no principal)     │
         ├────────────────┴────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┤
         │  KEY EXAM FACT: RequestAuthentication only rejects requests that   │
         │  PRESENT a broken/expired/wrong-issuer token (→ 401). A request    │
         │  with NO token sails straight past it — only an AuthorizationPolicy│
         │  demanding requestPrincipals turns "missing token" into a 403.     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [VERIFY END-TO-END — three curls, three different verdicts]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. valid admin JWT → allowed                                    │
         │  $ TOKEN=$(curl -s https://auth.anihpj.com/token)   # demo helper  │
         │  $ curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \                        │
         │      http://jobpost-api.anihpj:8000/api/jobs/       → 200 OK ✅     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # 2. malformed token → RequestAuthentication rejects it           │
         │  $ curl -H "Authorization: Bearer not.a.jwt" \                     │
