         │     labels:                                                       │
         │       app: jobpost-api                                            │
         │   configPatches:                                                  │
         │   - applyTo: HTTP_FILTER                                          │
         │     match:                                                        │
         │       context: SIDECAR_INBOUND                                    │
         │       listener:                                                   │
         │         filterChain:                                              │
         │           filter:                                                 │
         │             name: envoy.filters.network.http_connection_manager   │
         │             subFilter:                                            │
         │               name: envoy.filters.http.router                     │
         │     patch:                                                        │
         │       operation: INSERT_BEFORE      # run before the router       │
         │       value:                                                      │
         │         name: envoy.lua                                           │
         │         typed_config:                                             │
         │           # NOTE: "@type" is ONE long line in the real file       │
         │           "@type": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions           │
         │                    .filters.http.lua.v3.Lua                       │
         │           inline_code: |            # ← the WHOLE program is here │
         │             function envoy_on_request(handle)                     │
         │               handle:headers():add("x-anihpj-lua", "seen")        │
         │             end                                                   │
         │             function envoy_on_response(handle)                    │
         │               handle:headers():add("x-served-by", "anihpj-mesh")  │
         │             end                                                   │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [WASM vs LUA — choosing your Envoy extension mechanism]
         ┌─────────────┬───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
         │ Dimension   │ WasmPlugin (Wasm module)  │ Lua EnvoyFilter          │
         ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Language    │ Rust, Go, C++, Assembly-  │ Lua only, inline in      │
         │             │ Script → compiled .wasm   │ the YAML resource        │
         ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Safety      │ sandboxed VM — a crash is │ runs in Envoy's own      │
         │             │ contained; failStrategy   │ process — a bad script   │
         │             │ FAIL_CLOSE / FAIL_OPEN    │ stalls worker threads    │
         ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Performance │ near-native; small VM     │ interpreted; fine for    │
         │             │ overhead per request      │ tiny header tweaks       │
         ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Hot-reload  │ push new oci:// tag +     │ edit inline_code →       │
         │             │ sha256 → agent re-pulls,  │ istiod pushes new        │
         │             │ no pod restart            │ config, no restart       │
         ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Best for    │ real logic: tests, CI/CD, │ one-line tweaks and      │
         │             │ versions, reuse across    │ temporary workarounds    │
         │             │ teams and clusters        │ you may delete soon      │
         └─────────────┴───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

         - Rule of thumb for anihpj: Lua for a one-line header tweak
           you might delete next sprint; WasmPlugin for anything with
           real logic, tests, or versions — or that must NOT be able
           to crash the proxy (failStrategy: FAIL_CLOSE contains it)

         [VERIFY — is the Wasm module actually loaded?]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ $ kubectl get wasmplugins -n anihpj                         │
         │ NAME                   AGE                                  │
         │ jobpost-header-guard   2m           ← CRD accepted          │
         │                                                             │
         │ $ istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/jobpost-api \       │
         │     -n anihpj -o json | grep -i wasm                        │
         │ "name": "envoy.filters.http.wasm"   ← module live in Envoy  │
         │                                                             │
         │ $ curl -sI http://jobpost-api:8000/jobs/ | grep x-anihpj    │
         │ x-anihpj-guard: checked             ← plugin ran on request │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Chapter 16: Multi-Cluster and Advanced Scenarios
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    16.1 Multi-Cluster Istio — Spanning Kubernetes Clusters
         - Single mesh spanning multiple Kubernetes clusters
         - Models: single control plane, replicated control planes,
           external control plane
         - Use case: anihpj deployed across EU and US clusters with
           geographic routing — send EU users to EU cluster
         [MULTI-CLUSTER DIAGRAM: two clusters with mesh federation]

         [MULTI-CLUSTER ISTIO — anihpj EU + US deployment]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │   🌍 EU Users              🌎 US Users                             │
         │        │                        │                                  │
         │        ▼                        ▼                                  │
         │   [EU LoadBalancer]        [US LoadBalancer]                        │
         │        │                        │                                  │
         │   ┌────▼────────────────────────▼─────────────────────────────┐   │
         │   │                   SINGLE ISTIO MESH                        │   │
         │   │                                                            │   │
         │   │  CLUSTER 1 (EU — Frankfurt)  │  CLUSTER 2 (US — Virginia) │   │
         │   │  ┌─────────────────────────┐ │  ┌────────────────────────┐│   │
         │   │  │ istiod (EU primary)     │ │  │ istiod (US replica)   ││   │
         │   │  │ jobpost-api:v1          │ │  │ jobpost-api:v1         ││   │
         │   │  │ user-service            │ │  │ user-service           ││   │
         │   │  │ skills-service          │ │  │ skills-service         ││   │
         │   │  └─────────────────────────┘ │  └────────────────────────┘│   │
         │   │           │    mTLS cross-cluster calls   │               │   │
         │   │           └──────────────────────────────┘               │   │
         │   │                                                            │   │
         │   │  VirtualService: locality-based failover                   │   │
         │   │  • EU users → EU cluster (low latency)                    │   │
         │   │  • EU cluster down → auto failover to US cluster          │   │
         │   │  • mTLS works ACROSS clusters seamlessly                  │   │
         │   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────-┘   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  DEPLOYMENT MODELS:                                                │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │  Primary-Remote:  One istiod manages both clusters           │ │
         │  │  Multi-Primary:   Each cluster has own istiod (recommended)  │ │
         │  │  External CP:     istiod outside any cluster                 │ │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: EAST-WEST GATEWAY — a dedicated Istio gateway that
           carries service-to-service traffic BETWEEN clusters
           ("east-west"), unlike the ingress gateway which carries user
           traffic INTO the mesh ("north-south"). It exposes port 15443
           in tls AUTO_PASSTHROUGH mode and forwards mTLS connections
           without ever decrypting them
         - Cross-cluster mTLS requirement: all clusters must share the
           SAME root CA → SPIFFE identities stay globally unique and a
           certificate minted in one cluster validates in the other
         → No shared root CA → cross-cluster handshakes fail → each
           cluster becomes an island that can only talk to itself
         - Service discovery across clusters: each istiod is given a
           "remote secret" (a kubeconfig) so it can read the OTHER
           cluster's API server and learn its endpoints

         [CROSS-CLUSTER PLUMBING — what multi-cluster really requires]
         ┌───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
         │ REQUIREMENT               │ WHY IT EXISTS                    │
         ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │ Shared root CA            │ certs from either cluster        │
         │                           │ validate in the other → mTLS     │
         ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │ East-west gateway :15443  │ dedicated gateway carrying       │
         │ (tls AUTO_PASSTHROUGH)    │ service-to-service traffic       │
         │                           │ BETWEEN clusters, unopened       │
         ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │ Remote secrets            │ lets each istiod read the OTHER  │
         │ (istioctl create-remote-  │ cluster's API server to discover │
         │  secret)                  │ its services and endpoints       │
         ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │ topology.istio.io/network │ tells Istio which network each   │
         │ label per cluster         │ cluster is on → route via the    │
         │                           │ east-west gateway when crossing  │
         └───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

         [LOCALITY FAILOVER — keep EU traffic in EU, fail over to US]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                          │
         │  kind: DestinationRule                                       │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: jobpost-api-locality                                │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    host: jobpost-api                                         │
         │    trafficPolicy:                                            │
         │      loadBalancer:                                           │
         │        localityLbSetting:                                    │
         │          enabled: true            ← prefer local endpoints   │
         │          failover:                                           │
         │          - from: eu-central-1     ← EU cluster's region      │
         │            to: us-east-1          ← where EU traffic goes    │
         │      outlierDetection:            ← REQUIRED for failover!   │
         │        consecutive5xxErrors: 5    ← marks region unhealthy   │
         │        interval: 30s                                         │
         │        baseEjectionTime: 30s                                 │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.2 VM Workloads in the Mesh
         - Add non-Kubernetes VMs (legacy apps, databases) to the Istio mesh
         - VM gets a WorkloadEntry and certificate — treated like a Pod
         - anihpj use case: legacy PostgreSQL VM joins the mesh for mTLS
         - 🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: this is the external → in-mesh direction of
           "Connecting In-Mesh Workloads to External Workloads and
           Services." The reverse direction (in-mesh → external, e.g.
           calling api.stripe.com) is ServiceEntry — see Ch8

         [VM IN THE MESH — legacy PostgreSQL VM joining anihpj mesh]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  BEFORE (VM outside mesh):                                         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [skills-service Pod] ──plain TCP──▶ [PostgreSQL VM: 10.0.5.20]   │
         │  No mTLS. No metrics. No circuit breaker. Just raw database.      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  AFTER (VM inside mesh):                                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Step 1: Install Envoy proxy on the VM (istio-proxy binary)       │
         │  Step 2: Apply WorkloadEntry in Kubernetes:                        │
         │    apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3                        │
         │    kind: WorkloadEntry                                             │
         │    metadata: {name: postgres-vm}                                  │
         │    spec:                                                           │
         │      address: 10.0.5.20          ← VM IP                         │
         │      labels: {app: postgres}                                      │
         │  Step 3: istiod issues certificate to VM's Envoy proxy            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  [skills-service Pod] ──mTLS──▶ [PostgreSQL VM: 10.0.5.20]        │
         │  ✅ Same mTLS as between pods                                      │
         │  ✅ Metrics visible in Grafana                                      │
         │  ✅ AuthorizationPolicy controls who can query the database        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: WORKLOADGROUP — the "Deployment template" for VMs:
           it declares the labels, network, and serviceAccount that every
           VM instance inherits. Think of it this way: WorkloadEntry is
           to a VM what a Pod is to a container; WorkloadGroup is the
           Deployment that stamps them out
         - How the VM earns its identity: the VM's istio-proxy sends a
           CSR to istiod:15012 → istiod validates it against the
           WorkloadGroup → signs and returns a SPIFFE SVID certificate
         - A ServiceEntry (location: MESH_INTERNAL, resolution: STATIC)
           gives the VM a stable DNS name that anihpj pods can call —
           psql connection strings keep working, now over mTLS

         [VM ONBOARDING FLOW — WorkloadGroup → proxy → SVID → mTLS]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. Apply WorkloadGroup (template: labels, serviceAccount)   │
         │  2. Generate VM bootstrap files:                             │
         │       istioctl x workload entry configure ...                │
         │  3. Copy files to the VM, install + start istio-proxy        │
         │  4. VM proxy sends CSR ──▶ istiod:15012                      │
         │  5. istiod validates vs WorkloadGroup ──▶ signs SPIFFE SVID  │
         │  6. WorkloadEntry auto-registered (one per live VM)          │
         │  7. ServiceEntry (MESH_INTERNAL, STATIC) gives it a DNS name │
         │  ──▶ skills-service ══mTLS══▶ postgres VM, psql unchanged    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.3 Istio Ambient Mesh — Sidecar-Free Mode
         - New Istio architecture (alpha in v1.15, GA since v1.22): no
           sidecar injection required!
         - Layer 4 (ztunnel): handles mTLS + basic routing without sidecar
         - Layer 7 (waypoint proxy): optional, per-namespace L7 features
         - Reduced overhead: no per-pod sidecar → less memory, faster startup
         - anihpj use case: ambient mode for lower-overhead deployments
         [AMBIENT vs SIDECAR ARCHITECTURE COMPARISON DIAGRAM]

         [SIDECAR vs AMBIENT MESH — architecture comparison]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SIDECAR MODE (classic Istio)    │  AMBIENT MODE (GA in 1.22)       │
         ├──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
         │                                  │                                  │
         │  [Pod: jobpost-api]              │  [Pod: jobpost-api]              │
         │  ┌────────┐ ┌──────────┐         │  ┌────────────────────────┐      │
         │  │Django  │ │istio-proxy│         │  │Django (no sidecar!)   │      │
         │  │:8000   │ │(Envoy)   │         │  └────────────────────────┘      │
         │  └────────┘ └──────────┘         │          │                       │
         │  Sidecar in EVERY pod            │          ▼ (via iptables)        │
         │  • ~128Mi memory/pod overhead    │  [ztunnel DaemonSet on Node]     │
         │  • ~50ms pod startup overhead    │  • One per NODE (not per pod)    │
         │  • Full L4 + L7 features         │  • Handles L4: mTLS, auth        │
         │                                  │                                  │
         │                                  │  [Waypoint Proxy - per namespace]│
         │                                  │  • Optional, for L7 features     │
         │                                  │  • Shared across namespace        │
         │                                  │                                  │
         │  OVERHEAD:                        │  OVERHEAD:                       │
         │  7 anihpj services × 128Mi =      │  1 ztunnel/node (~50Mi) +        │
         │  ~900Mi extra memory             │  1 waypoint/namespace (~100Mi)   │
         │  = 9× more memory for mesh!      │  = 150Mi total! 6× less ✅       │
         └──────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

         [AMBIENT MODE ENABLEMENT for anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Install Istio with ambient mode:                                │
         │  istioctl install --set profile=ambient -y                         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Enable ambient for anihpj namespace (no istio-injection label!) │
         │  kubectl label namespace anihpj istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient    │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Result: pods in anihpj get L4 mTLS via ztunnel automatically    │
         │  # No 2/2 containers — pods stay at 1/1 ← big difference!         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # To enable L7 features (traffic management, retries, etc.):      │
         │  kubectl apply -f - <<EOF                                          │
         │  apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1                         │
         │  kind: Gateway                                                     │
         │  metadata:                                                         │
         │    name: anihpj-waypoint                                           │
         │    namespace: anihpj                                               │
         │    annotations:                                                    │
         │      istio.io/service-account: anihpj-waypoint                    │
         │  spec:                                                             │
         │    gatewayClassName: istio-waypoint                               │
         │  EOF                                                               │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.4  Ambient Mesh Architecture — How ztunnel and Waypoint Work Together
         - DEFINITION: AMBIENT MESH splits the sidecar's job into TWO
           independent layers — a mandatory Layer-4 "zero-trust secure
           overlay" (ztunnel) and an optional Layer-7 processing tier
           (waypoint proxy). You pay only for the features you use.
         - Why it matters: with sidecars, EVERY anihpj pod carries a full
           Envoy even if it only needs mTLS. Ambient gives the whole
           namespace mTLS for the cost of one small per-node agent.
         - How the two layers divide the work:
           • ztunnel — a Rust-based DaemonSet, ONE per node. It captures
             the traffic of every ambient pod on that node and provides
             mTLS, L4 authorization, and TCP telemetry. Always on.
           • waypoint proxy — an Envoy Deployment, typically one per
             namespace. Only created when you need L7: VirtualService
             routing, retries, timeouts, circuit breaking, HTTP-level
             AuthorizationPolicy, and full RED metrics.
         → No waypoint = L4 only. Add a waypoint = full L7. Either way,
           application pods are never restarted or reconfigured.

         [AMBIENT TWO-LAYER DESIGN — L4 overlay + optional L7 tier]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  LAYER 2 — WAYPOINT PROXY (optional, per namespace)    L7    │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ Envoy Deployment, runs as a normal pod in anihpj       │  │
         │  │ • VirtualService routing (canary weights, headers)     │  │
         │  │ • Retries, timeouts, circuit breaking, fault injection │  │
         │  │ • L7 AuthorizationPolicy (methods, paths, JWT claims)  │  │
         │  │ • Full HTTP telemetry (RED metrics, tracing)           │  │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                    ▲ used only if deployed                   │
         │  LAYER 1 — ZTUNNEL (always on, one per NODE)         L4 🔒    │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ Rust-based DaemonSet — shared by ALL pods on the node  │  │
         │  │ • mTLS with SPIFFE identity (same certs as sidecars)   │  │
         │  │ • L4 AuthorizationPolicy (identity / port allow-deny)  │  │
         │  │ • TCP metrics (bytes, connections, mTLS status)        │  │
         │  │ • ~50Mi per node — pods stay 1/1, zero pod overhead    │  │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [AMBIENT TRAFFIC FLOW — jobpost-api calling skills-service]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  L4 PATH (no waypoint deployed):                             │
         │                                                              │
         │  [jobpost-api pod] ──▶ ztunnel(node A) ══mTLS/HBONE══▶       │
         │                        ztunnel(node B) ──▶ [skills-service]  │
         │  ✅ encrypted, identity-checked, TCP metrics collected        │
         │  ❌ no HTTP routing, no retries, no circuit breaking          │
         │                                                              │
         │  L7 PATH (waypoint deployed for anihpj):                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  [jobpost-api pod] ──▶ ztunnel ══▶ [waypoint proxy] ══▶      │
         │                        ztunnel ──▶ [skills-service pod]      │
         │  ✅ everything above PLUS VirtualService routing, retries,    │
         │     timeouts, circuit breaking, L7 authz, RED metrics        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [AMBIENT ENABLEMENT CHEAT SHEET — the three commands to know]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. Install Istio with the ambient profile                 │
         │  istioctl install --set profile=ambient -y                   │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 2. Opt a namespace into ambient (L4 overlay, no sidecar)  │
         │  kubectl label namespace anihpj \                            │
         │      istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 3. (Optional) add L7 — deploy a waypoint proxy            │
         │  istioctl waypoint apply -n anihpj --enroll-namespace        │
         │  # under the hood this creates a Gateway resource with       │
         │  #   gatewayClassName: istio-waypoint                        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [SIDECAR → AMBIENT MIGRATION — six safe steps for anihpj]
         ┌────┬───────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
         │STEP│ ACTION                        │ RESULT                  │
         ├────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 1  │ istioctl install --set        │ ztunnel DaemonSet runs  │
         │    │   profile=ambient -y          │ on every node           │
         ├────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 2  │ On a TEST namespace: remove   │ next rollout: pods come │
         │    │ istio-injection label, add    │ up 1/1 — no sidecar     │
         │    │ istio.io/dataplane-mode=      │ container at all        │
         │    │ ambient                       │                         │
         ├────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 3  │ kubectl get pods → verify 1/1 │ mTLS still enforced —   │
         │    │ + Kiali still shows 🔒 edges   │ now provided by ztunnel │
         ├────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 4  │ Need L7? istioctl waypoint    │ VS routing, retries, CB │
         │    │ apply -n anihpj               │ return via the waypoint │
         ├────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 5  │ Repeat namespace by namespace │ sidecar + ambient       │
         │    │ — no big-bang cutover needed  │ coexist during rollout  │
         ├────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
         │ 6  │ Retire unused sidecar         │ clean, all-ambient mesh │
         │    │ injection config/webhooks     │                         │
         └────┴───────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘

    16.5  Deep Dive — HBONE: HTTP-Based Overlay Network Encapsulation
         - DEFINITION: HBONE (HTTP-Based Overlay Network Encapsulation)
           is ambient mesh's tunneling protocol — every pod-to-pod
           connection is wrapped in an HTTP/2 CONNECT tunnel between
           ztunnels, secured with mTLS, on dedicated port 15008
         - Why it matters: it replaces the per-pod iptables + sidecar
           interception model; the application's bytes ride inside the
           tunnel unchanged, so no app code or port changes are needed
         - How it works: the source ztunnel opens ONE mTLS connection to
           the destination ztunnel and multiplexes many application
           streams over it as HTTP/2 streams → fewer TCP handshakes,
           fewer TLS sessions to manage
         - Identity is preserved: the mTLS handshake uses the SPIFFE
           certificate of the SOURCE POD's service account (not the
           node!) — AuthorizationPolicy still sees
           cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api as the caller

         [HBONE PROTOCOL STACK — layers of an ambient connection]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ Application data (HTTP / gRPC / psql wire bytes)       │  │
         │  ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤  │
         │  │ HTTP/2 CONNECT tunnel (HBONE) — one stream per app     │  │
         │  │ connection, many streams multiplexed per peer          │  │
         │  ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤  │
         │  │ mTLS — SPIFFE cert of the SOURCE POD's service account │  │
         │  ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤  │
         │  │ TCP → destination ztunnel, port 15008                  │  │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                                                              │
         │  KEY FACTS:                                                  │
         │  • 15008 = HBONE tunnel port (memorize alongside 15001,      │
         │    15006, 15020, 15021, 15090, 15443)                        │
         │  • Identity survives the tunnel: authz still evaluates the   │
         │    calling POD's SPIFFE ID, never the node's                 │
         │  • Waypoints speak HBONE too — same tunnel, extra L7 hop     │
         │  • Transparent to apps: no code change, no port change       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INTERCEPTION COMPARED — sidecar iptables vs ambient HBONE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SIDECAR MODE:                                               │
         │  app :8000 → pod iptables REDIRECT → localhost:15001         │
         │  → own Envoy sidecar → mTLS → peer sidecar :15006 → app      │
         │                                                              │
         │  AMBIENT MODE:                                               │
         │  app :8000 → node-level redirection → local ztunnel          │
         │  → HTTP/2 CONNECT over mTLS → peer ztunnel :15008 → app      │
         │                                                              │
         │  → Same zero-trust guarantees, none of the per-pod plumbing  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.6  Sidecar vs Ambient — Decision Guide
         - There is no single "right" mode — decide per namespace using
           four questions: per-pod L7 needs, resource budget, upgrade
           blast radius, and maturity requirements. Both modes coexist
           inside one mesh, so mixed deployments are normal.
         - Rule of thumb for anihpj: latency-critical public services
           that rely on canary weights and circuit breakers (jobpost-api,
           skills-service) → sidecar, or ambient + waypoint; internal
           low-traffic services that only need mTLS + identity allow/deny
           (notification-service, location-service) → ambient L4 only
         → When in doubt, start new namespaces in ambient L4 and add a
           waypoint the day you actually need L7 — it is a one-command,
           zero-restart upgrade

         [DECISION TABLE — choose the data-plane mode per namespace]
         ┌───────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
         │ QUESTION          │ SIDECAR              │ AMBIENT          │
         ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
         │ Per-pod L7 needs  │ Full L7 in every pod │ L7 only where a  │
         │ (VS, retries, CB, │ including per-pod    │ waypoint exists; │
         │  WASM/Lua, fault  │ EnvoyFilter / WASM   │ no EnvoyFilter   │
         │  injection)       │ customization        │ or WASM per pod  │
         ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
         │ Resource budget   │ ~128Mi × every pod   │ ~50Mi per node + │
         │                   │ (7 anihpj svcs ≈     │ ~100Mi/waypoint  │
         │                   │ 900Mi just for mesh) │ (≈6× cheaper)    │
         ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
         │ Upgrade blast     │ Proxy upgrade means  │ ztunnel upgraded │
         │ radius            │ restarting EVERY     │ per node — app   │
         │                   │ injected pod         │ pods never move  │
         ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
         │ Current maturity  │ Battle-tested for    │ GA since 1.22 —  │
         │                   │ years; every Istio   │ newer, a few     │
         │                   │ feature supported    │ features lag     │
         └───────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────┘

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTERS 14-16: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS (Advanced Patterns)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After these chapters, in your daily work you will:
      • Run a full canary: deploy v2 → 5% → 25% → 50% → 100% over days
      • Rollback instantly: change weight to 0% — one YAML edit, no downtime
      • Use traffic mirroring to test v2 with REAL production traffic safely
      • Do blue-green deployments: instant 100% switch, instant rollback
      • Add local rate limiting per pod (EnvoyFilter) to protect Django
      • Set up global rate limiting (Redis-backed) per user/IP
      • Understand multi-cluster: EU + US clusters with locality failover
      • Add legacy VMs to the mesh via WorkloadEntry
      • Evaluate Ambient mesh: sidecar-free mode, 6x less memory overhead
      • Know when to use sidecar mode vs ambient mode

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTERS 14-16: ICA EXAM TIPS & QUESTIONS (Advanced — extends Traffic 35% & Install 20%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS — Advanced Patterns is not its own domain; canary/
       blue-green (Ch14) is graded under Traffic Management's "Configuring
       Traffic Shifting," and ambient mode (16.3-16.6) is graded under
       Installation's "Sidecar or Ambient Mode" (see 4.11):
      • "Deploy new version gradually" = VirtualService weight-based canary
      • "Test v2 with real traffic safely" = VirtualService mirror
      • "Block excessive requests per pod" = EnvoyFilter local rate limiting
      • "Join a VM to the mesh" = WorkloadEntry
      • "Enable ambient / no sidecar" = istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient label
      • "Cross-cluster traffic" = east-west gateway :15443 + shared root CA

    Q1: How do you mirror 100% of production traffic to a v2 deployment?
    A1: In VirtualService, add mirror: {host: jobpost-api, subset: v2} with
        mirrorPercentage: {value: 100.0}. v1 handles all user responses; v2
        gets a silent copy for testing.

    Q2: What Istio resource registers a legacy VM in the service mesh?
    A2: WorkloadEntry — specifies the VM's IP address and labels so istiod can
        issue it a SPIFFE certificate and apply mesh policies.

    Q3: jobpost-api must reject anything above 100 requests/minute PER POD
        with HTTP 429, without deploying any extra service. What do you use?
    A3: An EnvoyFilter that inserts envoy.filters.http.local_ratelimit into
        HTTP_FILTER (INSERT_BEFORE envoy.filters.http.router) with
        token_bucket: {max_tokens: 100, tokens_per_fill: 100,
        fill_interval: 60s}. Local rate limiting is per-pod and in-memory —
        no Redis or external rate-limit service needed, unlike global.

    Q4: You mirrored production traffic to jobpost-api v2 for a week and
        Grafana shows v2's error rate matches v1. Promote v2 as a canary —
        what exactly changes in the VirtualService?
    A4: Delete the mirror:/mirrorPercentage: block and replace the single
        route with weighted destinations: subset v1 weight: 95, subset v2
        weight: 5, then raise v2 (25 → 50 → 100) while watching dashboards.
        Mirroring proved correctness silently; weights now shift REAL user
        responses gradually.

    Q5: During a blue-green cutover, v2 (green) starts throwing 8.1%
        errors. Roll back with zero downtime — what is the fastest fix?
    A5: Edit the VirtualService weights back to v1: 100 / v2: 0 (kubectl
        edit virtualservice jobpost-api -n anihpj, or re-apply the old
        YAML). xDS propagates in ~1-2 seconds and no pods restart — that
        instant, restart-free rollback is the whole point of blue-green
        behind Istio.

    Q6: EU users must be served by the EU cluster, failing over to the US
        cluster only when EU endpoints are unhealthy. What do you configure?
    A6: A DestinationRule on jobpost-api with trafficPolicy.loadBalancer.
        localityLbSetting: {enabled: true, failover: [{from: eu-central-1,
        to: us-east-1}]} PLUS an outlierDetection block (e.g.
        consecutive5xxErrors: 5). Locality failover only activates when
        outlier detection can mark the local endpoints unhealthy.

    Q7: Cross-cluster calls between the EU and US clusters fail their mTLS
        handshake. Name the two multi-cluster prerequisites you check first.
    A7: (1) Both clusters must share the SAME root CA — otherwise certs
        minted in one cluster do not validate in the other. (2) The
        east-west gateway must be deployed and exposing port 15443 in tls
        AUTO_PASSTHROUGH mode so cross-cluster mTLS can transit unopened.
        Then confirm remote secrets exist (istioctl create-remote-secret).

    Q8: Bring the legacy PostgreSQL VM at 10.0.5.20:5432 into the anihpj
        mesh so skills-service reaches it over mTLS. Outline the steps.
    A8: (1) Create a WorkloadGroup (labels app: postgres, serviceAccount:
        postgres-sa) and a WorkloadEntry with address: 10.0.5.20 using
        apiVersion networking.istio.io/v1. (2) Install istio-proxy on the
        VM; it sends a CSR to istiod:15012 and receives a SPIFFE SVID.
        (3) Add a ServiceEntry (location: MESH_INTERNAL, resolution:
        STATIC) so pods resolve the VM by name. psql traffic now rides
        mTLS and obeys AuthorizationPolicy.

    Q9: Enable sidecar-free Istio for the anihpj namespace and verify it
        worked. Which commands?
    A9: istioctl install --set profile=ambient -y, then kubectl label
        namespace anihpj istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient. Verify: kubectl
        get pods -n anihpj shows 1/1 (no istio-proxy container), the
        ztunnel DaemonSet is Running in istio-system, and Kiali still
        shows lock icons on edges. For L7 features add a waypoint:
        istioctl waypoint apply -n anihpj.

    Q10: One team runs 40 low-traffic internal services needing only mTLS
         and identity-based allow/deny; another team needs per-pod WASM
         filters plus canary weights on jobpost-api. Which data-plane mode
         fits each, and can they share a cluster?
    A10: The 40 internal services → ambient L4 only (ztunnel gives mTLS +
         L4 authz for ~50Mi per node instead of 40 × 128Mi of sidecars).
         The jobpost-api team → sidecar mode (per-pod WASM/EnvoyFilter is
         not available in ambient), or ambient + waypoint if plain canary
         weights suffice. Yes — sidecar and ambient namespaces coexist in
         the same mesh with working mTLS between them.
