━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Chapter 17: Running Istio in Production
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17.1 Upgrading Istio
         - Istio release cycle: minor release every ~3 months
         - Supported versions: latest + previous (N-1)
         - In-place upgrade: istioctl upgrade (risky — avoid in production)
         - Canary upgrade: run two istiod versions simultaneously
           • New istiod runs alongside old one
           • Migrate namespaces one by one to new version
           • Old istiod removed when all namespaces migrated
         [CANARY UPGRADE FLOW DIAGRAM: old + new istiod running in parallel]

         [CANARY UPGRADE FLOW — old 1-22 and new 1-23 istiod in parallel]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  STEP 1: INSTALL the new revision NEXT TO the old one              │
         │  istioctl install --set revision=1-23 -y                           │
         │                                                                    │
         │   ┌────────────────────────┐       ┌────────────────────────┐      │
         │   │ istiod-1-22 (OLD)      │       │ istiod-1-23 (NEW)      │      │
         │   │ serves every existing  │       │ serves nothing yet —   │      │
         │   │ sidecar (unchanged)    │       │ waits for namespaces   │      │
         │   └────────────────────────┘       └────────────────────────┘      │
         │       BOTH run in parallel → zero impact on live traffic           │
         │                                                                    │
         │  STEP 2: MIGRATE one namespace at a time to the new revision       │
         │  kubectl label ns anihpj istio-injection- istio.io/rev=1-23        │
         │  kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj                      │
         │    → restarted pods (jobpost-api, user-service, ...) come back     │
         │      with 1-23 sidecars connected to the NEW istiod                │
         │                                                                    │
         │  STEP 3: VERIFY before touching the next namespace                 │
         │  istioctl proxy-status | grep anihpj  → all SYNCED, version 1.23   │
         │  istioctl analyze -n anihpj           → 0 errors, 0 warnings       │
         │  Grafana: still ~847 req/s, ~2.1% errors, p99 ~234ms? ✅            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  STEP 4: DECOMMISSION the old revision (ALL namespaces migrated)   │
         │  istioctl uninstall --revision=1-22 -y                             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ROLLBACK (any time before Step 4) = relabel back:                 │
         │  kubectl label ns anihpj istio.io/rev=1-22 --overwrite             │
         │  kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj                      │
         │    → pods reconnect to the OLD istiod — instant, no reinstall      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - DEFINITION: istioctl upgrade performs an IN-PLACE upgrade —
           it replaces the image on the SAME istiod Deployment/revision;
           no second control plane is ever created, unlike canary
         - Why it still matters for the exam: "Upgrading Istio (Canary,
           In-Place)" is a NAMED exam subtopic — you're expected to know
           both mechanics, even though canary is the safer production
           choice
         - How it works: istioctl upgrade (or helm upgrade istiod)
           overwrites the istiod Deployment's image tag directly and lets
           Kubernetes run a normal rolling update of the istiod pod(s) —
           there is only ever ONE control-plane version any sidecar can
           talk to, before, during, and after the upgrade

         [IN-PLACE UPGRADE — istioctl upgrade, one control plane]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  COMMAND (either is equivalent):                                   │
         │  istioctl upgrade -y                                               │
         │  helm upgrade istiod istio/istiod -n istio-system --set ...        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  DURING THE UPGRADE WINDOW:                                        │
         │  - istiod pod(s) roll one at a time (normal K8s rolling            │
         │    update); EXISTING sidecar connections drain gracefully          │
         │    and reconnect once the new istiod pod is Ready                  │
         │  - brand-new connections/pods that start in the instant no         │
         │    istiod replica is Ready yet can transiently fail to get         │
         │    xDS config (worse with replicas: 1)                             │
         │                                                                    │
         │  WHY CANARY IS PREFERRED IN PRODUCTION:                            │
         │  - Canary: OLD + NEW istiod run side by side — a bad NEW           │
         │    build only serves namespaces you deliberately migrate           │
         │    to it; live traffic on the OLD revision stays untouched         │
         │  - In-place: only ONE istiod version ever exists — a bad           │
         │    build breaks xDS pushes for EVERY namespace in the mesh         │
         │    AT ONCE, with no older control-plane version to fall            │
         │    back to except a full re-upgrade                                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         🎯 ICA EXAM NOTE: Upgrading Istio (Canary, In-Place) is a named
         exam topic — know BOTH commands (istioctl upgrade for in-place,
         istioctl install --set revision=X for canary) and be ready to
         explain why canary is the production-safe choice.

    17.2 Performance Tuning and Resource Sizing
         - istiod CPU/memory: scale based on number of sidecars
         - Sidecar proxy overhead: ~100m CPU, 128Mi memory per pod
         - Reduce overhead: concurrency setting in sidecar, request compression
         - Protocol optimization: use HTTP/2 for gRPC services
         - Tracing sampling: reduce from 100% (dev) to 1% (production)
         [RESOURCE SIZING TABLE: 10 pods / 50 pods / 100 pods / 500 pods]

         [RESOURCE SIZING TABLE — plan your cluster for Istio overhead]
         ┌─────────────┬───────────┬───────────┬───────────┬───────────────┐
         │  Scale      │  Pods     │  istiod   │  Sidecars │  Total extra  │
         ├─────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────────┤
         │  Dev/test   │  ~10      │  1 replica│  10×128Mi │  ~2Gi RAM     │
         │  (anihpj)   │           │  500m/2Gi │  + 10×100m│  ~1.5c CPU    │
         ├─────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────────┤
         │  Small prod │  ~50      │  2 replicas│ 50×128Mi │  ~8Gi RAM     │
         │             │           │  1c/4Gi   │  + 50×100m│  ~6c CPU      │
         ├─────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────────┤
         │  Medium prod│  ~100     │  3 replicas│100×128Mi │  ~15Gi RAM    │
         │             │           │  1.5c/6Gi │  +100×100m│  ~11.5c CPU   │
         ├─────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────────┤
         │  Large prod │  ~500     │  3+ replicas│500×128Mi│  ~67Gi RAM    │
         │             │           │  3c/12Gi  │  +500×100m│  ~53c CPU     │
         └─────────────┴───────────┴───────────┴───────────┴───────────────┘

    17.3 High Availability for istiod
         - Run 3 istiod replicas for HA (anti-affinity across nodes)
         - PodDisruptionBudget for istiod: minAvailable: 2
         - If istiod goes down: existing proxies KEEP working (cached config)
         - New proxies: can't start until istiod recovers
         [HA DIAGRAM: 3 istiod replicas across 3 availability zones]

         [istiod HA ARCHITECTURE — 3 replicas across availability zones]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │   Zone A              Zone B              Zone C                  │
         │   ┌────────────┐      ┌────────────┐      ┌────────────┐          │
         │   │ Node 1     │      │ Node 2     │      │ Node 3     │          │
         │   │            │      │            │      │            │          │
         │   │ ┌────────┐ │      │ ┌────────┐ │      │ ┌────────┐ │          │
         │   │ │ istiod │ │      │ │ istiod │ │      │ │ istiod │ │          │
         │   │ │replica1│ │      │ │replica2│ │      │ │replica3│ │          │
         │   │ └────────┘ │      │ └────────┘ │      │ └────────┘ │          │
         │   │            │      │            │      │            │          │
         │   │ [sidecars] │      │ [sidecars] │      │ [sidecars] │          │
         │   └────────────┘      └────────────┘      └────────────┘          │
         │         │                   │                   │                  │
         │         └─────── All 3 serve xDS to all sidecars ────────────────┘│
         │                                                                    │
         │  WHAT HAPPENS IF Zone A node fails?                                │
         │  • istiod replica 1 is gone                                       │
         │  • Existing Envoy sidecars: KEEP WORKING with cached config ✅    │
         │  • istiod replicas 2 and 3 take over new connections ✅            │
         │  • PDB ensures at least 2 replicas always up                      │
         │  • New pods deploying: get config from replicas 2 or 3 ✅         │
         │  • Service mesh: NO DOWNTIME during istiod failure ✅              │
         │                                                                    │
         │  istiod HA YAML:                                                   │
         │  kubectl patch deployment istiod -n istio-system \                 │
         │    -p '{"spec":{"replicas":3}}'                                    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.4 Debugging and Troubleshooting Istio
         [COMMON PROBLEMS + SOLUTIONS TABLE]
         - Problem 1: Pod not in mesh (missing sidecar)
           → Check: kubectl get pod -o jsonpath='{.spec.containers[*].name}'
           → Fix: ensure namespace has istio-injection=enabled label
         - Problem 2: mTLS connection refused (503 errors)
           → Check: istioctl proxy-status (SYNCED?)
           → Check: PeerAuthentication mode (STRICT vs PERMISSIVE)
         - Problem 3: VirtualService not routing (traffic goes to wrong version)
           → Check: DestinationRule subsets match Pod labels exactly
           → Run: istioctl analyze -n anihpj (find config errors)
         - Problem 4: High latency after adding Istio
           → Check: sidecar concurrency setting
           → Check: tracing sampling rate (100% = expensive)
         - Problem 5: Circuit breaker tripping too often
           → Check: outlierDetection thresholds (may be too sensitive)

         [COMMON PROBLEMS + SOLUTIONS — Problem | First check | Fix]
         ┌────────────────────┬───────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
         │ Problem            │ First check           │ Fix                  │
         ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ Pod not in mesh    │ kubectl get ns        │ label namespace      │
         │ (READY 1/1)        │ --show-labels         │ istio-injection=     │
         │                    │                       │ enabled + restart    │
         ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ mTLS conn refused  │ istioctl proxy-status │ align DR tls mode    │
         │ (503 / TCP reset)  │ + PeerAuthentication  │ ISTIO_MUTUAL with    │
         │                    │ mode (STRICT?)        │ PA STRICT            │
         ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ VS routes to wrong │ istioctl analyze      │ make DR subset       │
         │ version            │ -n anihpj             │ labels = pod labels  │
         ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ High latency after │ tracing sampling      │ sample 1% in prod    │
         │ adding Istio       │ rate (100%?) and      │ via Telemetry        │
         │                    │ sidecar concurrency   │                      │
         ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
         │ Circuit breaker    │ outlierDetection      │ raise consecutive-   │
         │ trips too often    │ thresholds in DR      │ 5xxErrors/interval   │
         └────────────────────┴───────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
         [TROUBLESHOOTING DECISION FLOWCHART: symptom → diagnosis → fix]

         [ISTIO TROUBLESHOOTING COMMANDS REFERENCE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Check proxy sync status (all should be SYNCED)      │
         │  istioctl proxy-status                                  │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Check proxy config for a specific pod               │
         │  istioctl proxy-config cluster <pod-name> -n anihpj    │
         │  istioctl proxy-config route <pod-name> -n anihpj      │
         │  istioctl proxy-config listener <pod-name> -n anihpj   │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Validate Istio configuration                        │
         │  istioctl analyze -n anihpj                            │
         │  istioctl analyze --all-namespaces                     │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Debug mTLS issues                                   │
         │  istioctl x describe pod <pod-name> -n anihpj          │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Check which version of Envoy is running             │
         │  istioctl version                                      │
         │                                                        │
         │  # Open dashboards                                     │
         │  istioctl dashboard kiali                              │
         │  istioctl dashboard grafana                            │
         │  istioctl dashboard jaeger                             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.5 Istio in CI/CD — Testing Service Mesh Config
         - Test VirtualService YAML before applying with istioctl analyze
         - Integration tests with Istio: verify routing, retries, mTLS in staging
         - Shift-left: catch Istio config errors in PR before cluster apply
         - GitOps with Istio: ArgoCD applies Istio CRDs alongside app manifests

         [SHIFT-LEFT PIPELINE — istioctl analyze as a PR gate]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  PR opened (change to istio/virtualservice.yaml)                   │
         │     │                                                              │
         │     ▼                                                              │
         │  STEP 1  istioctl analyze -f istio/ --use-kube=false               │
         │          → pure lint: bad YAML, missing fields (no cluster)        │
         │     │                                                              │
         │     ▼                                                              │
         │  STEP 2  istioctl analyze -f istio/ -n staging                     │
         │          → checks live staging state: subsets, hosts, ports        │
         │     │                                                              │
         │     ▼                                                              │
         │  STEP 3  exit code != 0 → pipeline FAILS → PR blocked ❌            │
         │          exit code 0    → merge → ArgoCD syncs to cluster ✅        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         (chapter-level reference — applies to all of Chapter 17)

         [ISTIO TROUBLESHOOTING DECISION FLOWCHART]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                                    │
         │  🚨 Something is wrong with anihpj in Istio                       │
         │                    │                                               │
         │  ┌─────────────────┴──────────────────────┐                       │
         │  │           What kind of problem?         │                       │
         │  └──────┬───────────┬──────────────┬───────┘                      │
         │         │           │              │                               │
         │   503 errors   Traffic not    Slow latency   Pods not in mesh      │
         │         │      routing              │               │              │
         │         ▼           ▼               ▼               ▼              │
         │   AuthzPolicy?  Check DR       Check sampling   Check label       │
         │   tls-check     subsets=Pod    rate (100%?)     istio-injection   │
         │         │       labels              │               │              │
         │         │           │               ▼               ▼              │
         │         │           ▼          Reduce to 1%    Label namespace     │
         │         ▼       istioctl       in Telemetry    kubectl label ns    │
         │   istioctl      analyze           resource     istio-injection=    │
         │   authn         -n anihpj                      enabled            │
         │   tls-check                                                        │
         │                                                                    │
         │  ALWAYS START WITH:  istioctl analyze -n anihpj                   │
         │  This catches 80% of configuration errors before deep debugging   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [PRODUCTION CHECKLIST — before go-live with Istio on anihpj]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  □ All pods show 2/2 READY (sidecar injected)                     │
         │  □ istioctl proxy-status: all SYNCED                               │
         │  □ istioctl analyze: 0 errors, 0 warnings                         │
         │  □ PeerAuthentication: STRICT mode enabled for anihpj namespace   │
         │  □ Deny-all AuthorizationPolicy applied, allow-rules tested       │
         │  □ VirtualService: all routes tested (curl through ingress)       │
         │  □ DestinationRule: subsets match real pod label versions         │
         │  □ istiod: 3 replicas, PodDisruptionBudget applied               │
         │  □ Tracing sampling: set to 1% for production                     │
         │  □ Grafana alerts configured for: error rate > 1%, p99 > 500ms   │
         │  □ ServiceEntry: all external APIs registered                     │
         │  □ Upgrade plan documented: canary upgrade process tested         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.6  ICA Exam Troubleshooting Command Reference Sheet
         - DEFINITION: the memorize-this sheet of MODERN istioctl commands the
           ICA exam expects, each paired with what healthy output looks like
         - Why it matters: exam tasks are time-boxed — if you know the healthy
           baseline for each command, the broken line jumps out in seconds
         - How it works: analyze validates your YAML against istiod's view of
           the mesh; proxy-status compares istiod vs every Envoy; proxy-config
           dumps what each Envoy is REALLY running right now
         - Heads-up: "istioctl authn tls-check" no longer exists in modern
           istioctl — for mTLS/cert questions use "istioctl x describe pod"
           or "istioctl proxy-config secret" instead

         [ICA COMMAND REFERENCE — modern commands + what healthy looks like]
         ┌────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
         │ Command                        │ Healthy output looks like         │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl analyze -n anihpj     │ "No validation issues             │
         │                                │ found" — run this FIRST           │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl proxy-status          │ every proxy row shows CDS/        │
         │                                │ LDS/EDS/RDS all SYNCED            │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl x describe pod        │ lists the VS/DR/PA in force;      │
         │ <jobpost-api-pod> -n anihpj    │ mTLS mode reported STRICT         │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl proxy-config route    │ your VirtualService routes,       │
         │ deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj   │ with the weights you set          │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl proxy-config cluster  │ one cluster per DR subset,        │
         │ deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj   │ outlier settings applied          │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl proxy-config endpoint │ upstream pod IPs listed,          │
         │ deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj   │ all marked HEALTHY                │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl proxy-config secret   │ default cert VALID + ROOTCA       │
         │ deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj   │ present (~24h workload TTL)       │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ kubectl logs <pod> -n anihpj   │ no reset/TLS/cert errors in       │
         │ -c istio-proxy --tail=50       │ the recent Envoy log tail         │
         ├────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
         │ istioctl dashboard kiali       │ green graph + mTLS padlocks;      │
         │ (also: grafana, jaeger)        │ RED metrics near baseline         │
         └────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘

    17.7  Deep Dive — Day-2 Operations: Monitoring, Emergency Diagnostics, and the Golden Debug Sequence
         - DEFINITION: Day-2 operations = everything AFTER install day — the
           watching, sizing, upgrading, and 3am-fixing of a live mesh
         - Why it matters: Istio failures rarely announce themselves; they
           show up first as drifting signals (sidecar memory creep, xDS push
           latency, a STALE proxy) long before users open tickets
         - How it works: continuously watch a handful of signals (table
           below), and when an incident hits, run the SAME 8-step checklist
           every time — never improvise during an outage

         [DAY-2 MONITORING — what to watch on the anihpj mesh]
         ┌────────────────┬────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
         │ Signal         │ Healthy baseline       │ Alert / act when         │
         ├────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Sidecar memory │ ~128Mi per pod         │ > 200Mi — leak? each     │
         │                │                        │ Wasm filter adds ~30Mi   │
         ├────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ istiod CPU     │ ~1 vCPU per 500        │ xDS push latency         │
         │                │ sidecars               │ rising, CPU pegged       │
         ├────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Config volume  │ ~5KB per service       │ > 1000 VS/DR — scope     │
         │                │ per proxy              │ with Sidecar resource    │
         ├────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Proxy sync     │ proxy-status: all      │ any proxy STALE for      │
         │                │ SYNCED                 │ more than a minute       │
         ├────────────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
         │ Cert health    │ workload certs renew   │ istiod logs show CA /    │
         │                │ ~every 12h             │ cert-signing errors      │
         └────────────────┴────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

         [EMERGENCY DIAGNOSTIC CHECKLIST — run in this exact order]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. istioctl analyze -A                                            │
         │       → YAML validation — ~80% of issues are caught right here     │
         │  2. istioctl proxy-status                                          │
         │       → all SYNCED? STALE = istiod failed to push config           │
         │  3. kubectl logs -l app=istiod -n istio-system --tail=100 \        │
         │       | grep -i error        → control-plane errors?               │
         │  4. istioctl proxy-config cluster deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj     │
         │       → does Envoy know the skills-service upstream cluster?       │
         │  5. istioctl proxy-config route deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj       │
         │       → are your VirtualService routes actually in Envoy?          │
         │  6. istioctl proxy-config listener deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj    │
         │       → are the expected ports (8000/8080/8090) bound?             │
         │  7. kubectl get peerauthentication,authorizationpolicy -A          │
         │       → is a STRICT/deny policy conflicting with your change?      │
         │  8. istioctl x describe pod <jobpost-api-pod> -n anihpj            │
         │       → cross-check: which VS/DR/PA apply + effective mTLS mode    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [THE GOLDEN DEBUG SEQUENCE — cheapest layer first]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. istioctl analyze          YAML valid?         ← ~80% caught    │
         │  2. istioctl proxy-status     config pushed to every Envoy?        │
         │  3. istioctl proxy-config     what is REALLY inside Envoy?         │
         │  4. kubectl logs -c istio-proxy   what errors does Envoy see?      │
         │  5. Kiali / Grafana           visual confirmation of the fix       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  GOLDEN RULE: analyze comes FIRST, every single time — it          │
         │  catches ~80% of configuration errors before any deep debugging.   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.8  Production Best Practices Reference
         - DEFINITION: the distilled operating rules from the official Istio
           production guidance — defaults you adopt BEFORE the first incident
         - Why it matters: most production Istio outages trace back to one of
           these being skipped (in-place upgrade, no PDB, missing timeouts)
         - How it works: three cards — Deployment, Traffic, Security — treat
           each line as a policy for the anihpj mesh, not a suggestion

         [BEST-PRACTICE CARD — DEPLOYMENT]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ✅ Revisioned (canary) control planes for EVERY upgrade            │
         │  ✅ 3 istiod replicas + PodDisruptionBudget minAvailable: 2         │
         │  ✅ Resource limits on istiod: ~2Gi RAM / 2 CPU per replica         │
         │  ✅ holdApplicationUntilProxyStarts: true — the app never           │
         │     races its own sidecar at pod startup                           │
         │  ❌ NEVER run an in-place "istioctl upgrade" in production          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [BEST-PRACTICE CARD — TRAFFIC]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ✅ Every VirtualService carries retries (with a sane retryOn)      │
         │  ✅ Timeouts SHORTER than the app's own timeouts — fail fast        │
         │  ✅ Circuit breaker (outlierDetection) on every upstream DR         │
         │  ✅ Header-based canary routing for testing, weights for rollout    │
         │  ✅ istioctl analyze before every apply — a CI gate, not a habit    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [BEST-PRACTICE CARD — SECURITY]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ✅ STRICT mTLS via PeerAuthentication in every prod namespace      │
         │  ✅ AuthorizationPolicy: default deny-all, then explicit ALLOWs     │
         │  ✅ Plan root CA rotation BEFORE expiry — never discover it         │
         │  ✅ Separate namespaces per team / trust boundary                   │
         │  ✅ Lock egress: outboundTrafficPolicy REGISTRY_ONLY + explicit     │
         │     ServiceEntry for each external API                             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.9  Troubleshooting Configuration — The ICA Exam Topic
         - DEFINITION: "configuration troubleshooting" on the exam almost
           always means a VirtualService / DestinationRule / Gateway /
           AuthorizationPolicy that is syntactically VALID YAML but
           semantically WRONG — a bad selector, a missing subset, a host
           that doesn't match anything real
         - Why it matters: this is the single most common ICA task
           pattern — "the YAML applied cleanly but traffic isn't doing
           what it says" — it's a config-reading skill, not a deep
           networking-debugging skill
         - The golden rule: run istioctl analyze BEFORE looking at a
           single log line — it catches roughly 80% of these errors by
           statically cross-checking every CRD against the others
         - How it works: analyze loads every Istio CRD in the namespace
           (or cluster) and re-runs istiod's own semantic checks against
           them — dangling subset references, hosts with no backing
           Service, gateways nothing selects, and more

         [CONFIG TROUBLESHOOTING — SYMPTOM TO FIX]
         ┌─────────────────────┬────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
         │ Symptom             │ analyze says       │ Check this CRD       │ Fix                 │
         ├─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ VS never matches -- │ usually 0          │ VirtualService hosts │ fix match rules /   │
         │ traffic stays 100%  │ issues (analyze    │ + http.match/route   │ route weights;      │
         │ on the old version  │ checks syntax,     │ rules                │ re-run analyze      │
         │                     │ not intent)        │                      │                     │
         ├─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ DR subset mismatch  │ error: referenced  │ DestinationRule      │ make subset labels  │
         │ -- 503, no healthy  │ subset not found   │ subsets vs the Pod's │ match Pod labels    │
         │ upstream for the    │                    │ actual labels        │ exactly             │
         │ subset              │                    │                      │                     │
         ├─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ Gateway host        │ warning: no        │ Gateway hosts+       │ align Gateway hosts │
         │ mismatch -- 404     │ matching workload  │ selector AND VS      │ to the VS hosts +   │
         │ straight from the   │ / host for Gateway │ gateways: field      │ gateways: entry     │
         │ ingress gateway     │ selector           │                      │                     │
         ├─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
         │ AuthorizationPolicy │ 0 issues (analyze  │ AuthorizationPolicy  │ fix selector scope; │
         │ blocks traffic      │ does not evaluate  │ selector +           │ add the missing     │
         │ unexpectedly -- 403 │ authz intent)      │ rules.from/to        │ explicit ALLOW rule │
         │ RBAC deny           │                    │                      │                     │
         └─────────────────────┴────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘

         → install-time config issues (webhook, injection, pending
           pods): full depth in 4.9
         → mTLS / authorization config issues (DR×PA TLS contract,
           RBAC deny): full depth in 12.7

    17.10  Troubleshooting the Mesh Control Plane — The ICA Exam Topic
         - DEFINITION: a control-plane problem means istiod ITSELF is
           unhealthy, unreachable, or has stopped pushing config (an xDS
           problem) — it is NOT one app or one Envoy acting up; it's the
           brain of the mesh
         - Why it matters: control-plane incidents are rarer than data-
           plane ones but far more severe — one broken istiod can freeze
           config delivery to every proxy in every namespace at once
         - How it works: istiod watches the Kubernetes API (CRDs,
           Services, Endpoints), computes each proxy's config, and pushes
           it over xDS on :15012 — if any of those three legs (watch,
           compute, push) breaks, the symptoms show up mesh-wide, not on
           one pod

         [CONTROL PLANE SYMPTOMS — CHECK, FIX]
         ┌──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
         │ Symptom                  │ Check command               │ Fix                        │
         ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ istiod pod               │ kubectl describe pod -n     │ raise istiod memory/CPU    │
         │ CrashLoopBackOff /       │ istio-system -l app=istiod; │ limits; check for a        │
         │ OOMKilled                │ kubectl top pod -n istio-   │ config-churn storm (too    │
         │                          │ system                      │ many VS/DR objects)        │
         ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ istiod unreachable --    │ istioctl proxy-status;      │ restore istiod (scale up   │
         │ proxy-status shows STALE │ kubectl get pods -n istio-  │ / fix crash loop); proxies │
         │ / NOT SENT for MANY      │ system                      │ self-heal once it is       │
         │ proxies at once          │                             │ reachable                  │
         ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ webhook timeout during   │ kubectl describe pod        │ confirm istiod:15017       │
         │ pod creation -- new pods │ <new-pod> -n anihpj, read   │ reachable from the API     │
         │ stuck, no sidecar        │ the Events section          │ server; re-run istioctl x  │
         │ injected                 │                             │ precheck                   │
         ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ xDS push failures in     │ kubectl logs -l app=istiod  │ find + fix the rejected    │
         │ istiod logs              │ -n istio-system | grep -i   │ config object (bad CRD     │
         │ (pilot_total_xds_rejects │ reject                      │ field), then re-apply      │
         │ climbing)                │                             │                            │
         └──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

         [CONTROL PLANE vs DATA PLANE — WHICH IS IT?]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  THE CORE DIAGNOSTIC INSTINCT:                                     │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Symptom affects MANY services / namespaces at once                │
         │  → CONTROL PLANE problem (istiod itself)                           │
         │     e.g. every proxy goes STALE together, every new pod            │
         │     fails injection, config stops propagating mesh-wide            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Symptom affects ONE service / ONE pod, rest is fine               │
         │  → DATA PLANE problem (that pod's Envoy, or ztunnel in             │
         │     ambient mode)                                                  │
         │     e.g. only jobpost-api returns 503s while every other           │
         │     anihpj service stays healthy                                   │
         │                                                                    │
         │  QUICK TEST: istioctl proxy-status — MANY rows STALE /             │
         │  NOT SENT together = suspect istiod; ONE odd row = that            │
         │  pod's data plane                                                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         → istiod internals (what actually runs inside the pod): 3.2
         → istiod HA architecture (replicas, PDB, zone spread): 17.3
         → control-plane metrics to watch (pilot_*, citadel_*): 9.7

    17.11  Troubleshooting the Mesh Data Plane — The ICA Exam Topic
         - DEFINITION: a data-plane problem means ONE specific Envoy
           sidecar (or ztunnel, in ambient mode) is misbehaving — wrong
           routing, dropped connections, a missing sidecar, a cert
           problem on ONE pod — even though istiod and the rest of the
           mesh are completely healthy
         - Why it matters: this is where most day-to-day tickets live —
           "jobpost-api is throwing 503s" is almost always a data-plane
           question, answered by reading that ONE proxy's live config
         - How it works: every istioctl proxy-config command reads what
           is ACTUALLY loaded in that pod's Envoy right now — proxy-status
           SYNCED only proves istiod pushed something, not that the
           pushed config is the config you intended; STRICT mTLS also
           rejects plaintext with a TCP connection reset, never an HTTP
           403 — a 403 always means an AuthorizationPolicy, not mTLS

         [DATA PLANE SYMPTOMS — CHECK, FIX]
         ┌──────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
         │ Symptom                  │ Check command              │ Fix                        │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ pod stuck 1/1 -- no      │ kubectl get ns anihpj      │ label namespace istio-     │
         │ sidecar was ever         │ --show-labels; kubectl get │ injection=enabled (or      │
         │ injected                 │ pod <pod> -o jsonpath      │ istio.io/rev=<rev>);       │
         │                          │ (container names)          │ rollout restart            │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ istio-proxy              │ kubectl logs <pod> -c      │ read the crash reason (bad │
         │ CrashLoopBackOff         │ istio-proxy -n anihpj      │ config, cert error); fix   │
         │                          │                            │ the cause, then delete pod │
         │                          │                            │ to force re-inject         │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ proxy-status says SYNCED │ istioctl proxy-config      │ SYNCED only means istiod   │
         │ but traffic is still     │ route/cluster/endpoint     │ pushed something; verify   │
         │ wrong                    │ deploy/<name> -n anihpj    │ the pushed rules are the   │
         │                          │ (the LIVE config, not      │ ones you intended          │
         │                          │ just the sync flag)        │                            │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ connection reset (not a  │ istioctl x describe pod    │ align client DR tls.mode   │
         │ clean HTTP error)        │ <pod> -n anihpj; check DR  │ with server PA mode (or    │
         │                          │ tls.mode vs                │ drop the tls block for     │
         │                          │ PeerAuthentication mode    │ auto mTLS)                 │
         ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
         │ 503, response flag UC or │ istioctl proxy-config      │ UC: no healthy endpoint    │
         │ UF                       │ endpoint deploy/<name> -n  │ (check the actual          │
         │                          │ anihpj -- any healthy pod  │ pod/Service); UF: upstream │
         │                          │ IPs at all?                │ refused the connection     │
         └──────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

         [BOTTOM-UP ENVOY DEBUG ORDER — fastest route to root cause]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Always debug bottom-up — the deepest layer explains the           │
         │  most common failures fastest (EDS before CDS before RDS           │
         │  before LDS):                                                      │
         │                                                                    │
         │  1. EDS (endpoints)  — healthy pod IPs behind the cluster?         │
         │  2. CDS (clusters)   — does the target cluster exist?              │
         │  3. RDS (routes)     — is your VirtualService rule present?        │
         │  4. LDS (listeners)  — is Envoy listening on the right port?       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  istioctl proxy-config endpoint|cluster|route|listener \           │
         │    deploy/<name> -n anihpj      (one command per layer)            │
         │                                                                    │
         │  Full xDS-to-CRD mapping + a worked walkthrough: see 3.8           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         → sidecar debugging checklist (six repeat symptoms): 3.7
         → Envoy config hierarchy in depth (LDS/RDS/CDS/EDS): 3.8
         → the full modern command reference sheet: 17.6

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 17: DAILY WORK TAKEAWAYS (Production Operations)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    After this chapter, in your daily work you will:
      • Run 3 istiod replicas for HA with PDB (minAvailable: 2)
      • Know that existing proxies keep working even if istiod goes down
      • Execute a canary upgrade of Istio itself (old+new istiod in parallel)
      • Size Istio for your cluster: sidecars cost ~128Mi each
      • Tune tracing sampling: 1% in prod (100% = expensive)
      • Troubleshoot: istioctl proxy-status → istioctl analyze → check logs
      • Know the top Istio problems: missing sidecar, mTLS mismatch,
        VirtualService subsets not matching pod labels
      • Use the production checklist before go-live (12 items)
      • Read Grafana alerts: error rate, p99 latency, circuit breaker status
      • Sleep better: circuit breakers isolate failures, mTLS encrypts everything,
        retries absorb transient errors — your system heals itself

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CHAPTER 17: ICA EXAM TIPS & QUESTIONS (Troubleshooting — 20%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    🎯 ICA EXAM FOCUS — This is 20% of the exam, split into 3 named
       subtopics (see 17.9-17.11): Configuration, Control Plane, Data
       Plane. Troubleshooting instinct is also tested inside every other
       domain, not just this chapter:
      • "Why isn't my VirtualService routing?" = Check DestinationRule subsets
        match pod labels. Run: istioctl analyze -n <namespace>
      • "Why is mTLS failing?" = istioctl x describe pod <pod>
      • "Why isn't the sidecar injected?" = Check namespace label
        kubectl get namespace <ns> --show-labels
      • "How to upgrade Istio?" = Canary upgrade: run old+new istiod
        simultaneously, migrate namespaces one by one

    Q1: What is the FIRST command you should run when Istio isn't working?
    A1: istioctl analyze -n <namespace> — catches 80% of configuration errors.

    Q2: How do you check if a specific pod has a valid mTLS configuration?
    A2: istioctl x describe pod <pod-name> -n <namespace> — shows the pod's
        effective mTLS mode. (The old "istioctl authn tls-check" was removed.)

    Q3: What's the safest way to upgrade Istio in production?
    A3: Canary upgrade — install new istiod version alongside the old one
        (using revision labels), migrate namespaces one by one, then remove
        old istiod when all namespaces are migrated.

    Q4: TASK — upgrade the mesh from Istio 1.22 to 1.23 with zero downtime
        and prove the anihpj namespace now runs on the new control plane.
    A4: Canary upgrade with a revisioned control plane:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ istioctl install --set revision=1-23 -y                      │
        │ kubectl label ns anihpj istio-injection- istio.io/rev=1-23   │
        │ kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj                 │
        │ istioctl proxy-status | grep anihpj   # all SYNCED on 1.23   │
        │ istioctl uninstall --revision=1-22 -y # only after ALL ns    │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: both istiods run in parallel, so live traffic never loses
        its control plane; each namespace migrates on its own schedule.

    Q5: TASK — make istiod highly available so a node drain can never
        drop the control plane below two replicas.
    A5: Scale to 3 replicas and protect them with a PodDisruptionBudget:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ kubectl scale deployment istiod -n istio-system --replicas=3 │
        │ kubectl create pdb istiod-pdb -n istio-system \              │
        │   --selector=app=istiod --min-available=2                    │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: the PDB blocks voluntary evictions (drains, node upgrades)
        from dropping istiod below 2; spread replicas across zones.

    Q6: TASK — capacity-plan a 50-pod production cluster: how much extra
        RAM and CPU does adding Istio sidecars cost?
    A6: Sidecars: 50 × 128Mi ≈ 6.4Gi RAM and 50 × 100m = 5 CPU cores;
        add istiod (2 replicas at 1c/4Gi) → budget ≈ 8Gi RAM / ~6 CPU.
        Why: ~128Mi + 100m per sidecar is the planning rule of thumb, and
        pod requests must cover app needs PLUS sidecar overhead.

    Q7: SCENARIO — istioctl proxy-status shows jobpost-api as STALE while
        every other proxy is SYNCED. Diagnose and fix it.
    A7: STALE = istiod failed to push the latest config to that Envoy:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ kubectl logs -l app=istiod -n istio-system --tail=100 \      │
        │   | grep -i -e error -e push                                 │
        │ kubectl logs <jobpost-api-pod> -c istio-proxy -n anihpj \    │
        │   | grep -i -e xds -e error                                  │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: look for xDS push errors (istiod overloaded / OOM) or the
        proxy losing its :15012 stream to istiod; scale istiod or
        restart the pod so it reconnects and resyncs.

    Q8: TASK — add a CI gate so a broken VirtualService can never merge.
    A8: Run istioctl analyze against the manifests in the PR pipeline:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ istioctl analyze -f istio/ --use-kube=false  # pure lint     │
        │ istioctl analyze -f istio/ -n staging        # vs staging    │
        │ # non-zero exit code → pipeline fails → PR is blocked        │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: analyze exits non-zero on errors, so the pipeline fails
        the PR before broken config ever reaches the cluster.

    Q9: SCENARIO — after migrating anihpj to revision 1-23, the error
        rate jumps from 2.1% to 8%. Roll back NOW.
    A9: Relabel the namespace back to the old revision and restart:
        ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │ kubectl label ns anihpj istio.io/rev=1-22 --overwrite        │
        │ kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj                 │
        └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
        Why: the old istiod is still running (never uninstall it until
        burn-in ends), so rollback is a relabel + restart — minutes,
        no reinstall.

    Q10: SCENARIO — all 3 istiod replicas crash at once. What breaks for
        anihpj, and what keeps working?
    A10: Keeps working: ALL existing traffic — every Envoy keeps serving
        with its last cached config (routing, mTLS, retries intact).
        Breaks: new pods start without sidecars, config changes stop
        propagating, and workload cert rotation halts (certs expire in
        ~24h, so recovery is urgent but not an instant outage).
        Why: the control plane and data plane are decoupled by design.
