    │          │  Execute a canary upgrade of Istio itself               │
    │          │  Troubleshoot any common Istio problem independently    │
    │          │  ★ READY FOR ISTIO CERTIFICATION EXAM ★                 │
    └──────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WEEK 1-2: Foundations
      • Read Part 1-3 (The Problem, What is Istio, Architecture)
      • Install Istio with demo profile on minikube
      • Deploy anihpj with sidecar injection
      • Verify 2/2 containers in pods, explore Kiali topology

    WEEK 3-4: Traffic Management
      • Part 4-5: Install + Traffic Management
      • Write your first VirtualService (path-based routing)
      • Implement canary: 90/10 split for jobpost-api
      • Test fault injection: verify retries work
      • Test circuit breaking: inject errors and watch Kiali

    WEEK 5-6: Observability and Security
      • Part 6-7: Observability + Security
      • Set up Grafana dashboards for all anihpj services
      • Create a distributed trace for a complex request
      • Enable STRICT mTLS for anihpj namespace
      • Implement zero-trust AuthorizationPolicy

    WEEK 7-8: Advanced and Production
      • Part 8-9: Advanced Patterns + Production
      • Implement shadow traffic testing for v2
      • Practice upgrading Istio (canary upgrade method)
      • Tune sidecar resource limits
      • Run istioctl analyze on full cluster — fix all warnings

         - HOW TO USE THIS PATH: reading alone will not pass the ICA — each
           fortnight pairs the chapters above with hands-on labs against the
           anihpj cluster, and every □ item names the proof that it worked
         - DEFINITION: A lab checklist is a "done-when-proven" list — an item
           only counts once its verify command succeeds, which stops "I read
           about it" from masquerading as "I can do it under exam pressure"
         - Print the box, run the labs on your minikube cluster (Appendix G),
           and tick items only after the arrow condition is true on screen

    [HANDS-ON LAB CHECKLIST — anihpj tasks to tick off each fortnight]
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  WEEKS 1-2  FOUNDATIONS — get a working anihpj mesh                    │
    │  □ minikube start --cpus=4 --memory=8192 → istiod reaches Running      │
    │  □ istioctl install --set profile=demo -y → verify-install is clean    │
    │  □ kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled              │
    │  □ Deploy jobpost-api + user-service → every pod shows 2/2 Running     │
    │  □ istioctl proxy-status → all anihpj proxies report SYNCED            │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEKS 3-4  TRAFFIC MANAGEMENT — route, split, protect                 │
    │  □ Gateway + VS applied → curl anihpj.example.com/jobs/ gives 200      │
    │  □ Canary 90/10 live → Kiali edge shows ~10% of traffic on v2          │
    │  □ Circuit breaker: a load test trips outlierDetection ejection        │
    │  □ ServiceEntry api.stripe.com → egress call from jobpost works        │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEKS 5-6  OBSERVABILITY + SECURITY — see it, then lock it            │
    │  □ Grafana: read jobpost-api baseline (847 req/s, p99 234ms)           │
    │  □ Jaeger: follow one /jobs/ request across 3+ anihpj services         │
    │  □ STRICT mTLS on → plaintext probe dies with connection reset         │
    │  □ deny-all AuthorizationPolicy then allow rules: 403 → 200            │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  WEEKS 7-8  ADVANCED + PRODUCTION — operate like SRE                   │
    │  □ Mirror jobpost-api traffic to v2 — copies appear in v2 logs         │
    │  □ Canary-upgrade istiod to a new revision — zero dropped calls        │
    │  □ istioctl analyze --all-namespaces → zero warnings remain            │
    │  □ Timed mock exam: 10 anihpj tasks in 80 minutes ★ exam-ready         │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix G: Local Development Quick Start (Minikube + Istio + anihpj)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    [COMPLETE LOCAL SETUP — from zero to Istio mesh in 10 commands]

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  # 1. Start minikube with enough resources for Istio + anihpj          │
    │  minikube start --cpus=4 --memory=8192 --disk-size=40g                 │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 2. Install Istio (demo profile = all tools included)               │
    │  curl -L https://istio.io/downloadIstio | sh -                         │
    │  cd istio-1.22.*                                                       │
    │  export PATH=$PWD/bin:$PATH                                            │
    │  istioctl install --set profile=demo -y                                │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 3. Verify installation                                              │
    │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system                                      │
    │  # Expected: istiod, ingressgateway, egressgateway, kiali,            │
    │  #           jaeger, grafana, prometheus — all Running                 │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 4. Create anihpj namespace with Istio injection                     │
    │  kubectl create namespace anihpj                                       │
    │  kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled                │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 5. Build Docker images (see Appendix B.2 for Dockerfiles)          │
    │  docker build -t anihpj/jobpost-api:v1 ./anihpj/jobpost/              │
    │  docker build -t anihpj/user-service:v1 ./anihpj/user-service/        │
    │  # ... repeat for all services                                        │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 6. Load images into minikube (skip if using remote registry)       │
    │  minikube image load anihpj/jobpost-api:v1                             │
    │  minikube image load anihpj/user-service:v1                            │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 7. Deploy anihpj services (see Appendix B.3 for YAML)              │
    │  kubectl apply -f anihpj/k8s/ -n anihpj                                │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 8. Verify pods have sidecars (2/2 containers)                      │
    │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj                                            │
    │  # Expected: jobpost-api-xxx  2/2  Running                             │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 9. Verify Istio sync status                                        │
    │  istioctl proxy-status                                                 │
    │  # Expected: ALL pods show SYNCED                                      │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # 10. Open dashboards                                                │
    │  istioctl dashboard kiali      # Service topology graph               │
    │  istioctl dashboard grafana    # RED metrics dashboards               │
    │  istioctl dashboard jaeger     # Distributed traces                   │
    │                                                                        │
    │  # BONUS: Generate some traffic to see in dashboards                  │
    │  kubectl port-forward svc/istio-ingressgateway -n istio-system 8080:80 │
    │  for i in {1..100}; do curl http://localhost:8080/jobs/; done         │
    │  # Now refresh Kiali — you'll see live traffic!                       │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [LOCAL DEV WORKFLOW — daily routine for anihpj development with Istio]
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  1.  kubectl get pods -n anihpj           # All 2/2?                  │
    │  2.  istioctl proxy-status                 # All SYNCED?               │
    │  3.  istioctl dashboard kiali              # Open topology             │
    │  4.  kubectl apply -f canary-vs.yaml       # Deploy canary            │
    │  5.  watch Kiali as traffic splits         # Monitor live              │
    │  6.  istioctl dashboard grafana            # Check RED metrics         │
    │  7.  istioctl analyze -n anihpj            # Validate config           │
    │  8.  kubectl logs deploy/jobpost-api -c istio-proxy  # Debug proxy    │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Minikube is the quickest personal Istio lab, but it has sharp
           edges that cost newcomers hours — the four traps below explain
           almost every "Istio is broken on my laptop" report
         - DEFINITION: minikube tunnel is a helper process that routes real
           traffic from your host into LoadBalancer Services, so the
           istio-ingressgateway gets an EXTERNAL-IP instead of <pending>

    [MINIKUBE GOTCHAS — the four traps that break local Istio labs]
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  GOTCHA 1: MEMORY TOO LOW → istiod stuck in Pending                    │
    │    Symptom: kubectl get pods -n istio-system → istiod 0/1 Pending      │
    │    Cause:   minikube's default 2GB VM cannot fit istiod + addons       │
    │    Fix:     minikube delete, then restart with more resources:         │
    │             minikube start --cpus=4 --memory=8192                      │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  GOTCHA 2: ErrImagePull FOR anihpj/jobpost-api:v1                      │
    │    minikube's Docker daemon is NOT your host's — pick ONE fix:         │
    │    a) eval $(minikube docker-env)    # build INSIDE minikube's Docker  │
    │       docker build -t anihpj/jobpost-api:v1 .                          │
    │    b) minikube image load anihpj/jobpost-api:v1   # copy from host     │
    │    Either way set imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent (Always forces pull)   │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  GOTCHA 3: EXTERNAL-IP STUCK <pending> ON istio-ingressgateway         │
    │    minikube has no cloud LoadBalancer — open a second terminal:        │
    │    minikube tunnel           # keeps running; asks for sudo/admin      │
    │    kubectl get svc istio-ingressgateway -n istio-system                │
    │    → EXTERNAL-IP now shows a real address you can curl                 │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  GOTCHA 4: TUNNEL PROCESS ANNOYING? USE METALLB INSTEAD                │
    │    minikube addons enable metallb                                      │
    │    minikube addons configure metallb   # then give a small IP range    │
    │    e.g. 192.168.49.100-192.168.49.110 (inside the minikube ip subnet)  │
    │    → LoadBalancer Services get stable IPs, no tunnel process needed    │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix H: Istio Quick-Reference Cheat Sheet
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    [ONE-PAGE VISUAL CHEAT SHEET — print-ready reference card]

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  ISTIO QUICK-REFERENCE CARD                                            │
    ├──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  INSTALL                 │  NAMESPACE SETUP                            │
    │  istioctl install        │  kubectl create ns anihpj                   │
    │    --set profile=demo    │  kubectl label ns anihpj                    │
    │                          │    istio-injection=enabled                  │
    ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  CHECK STATUS            │  DIAGNOSE PROBLEMS                          │
    │  istioctl proxy-status   │  istioctl analyze -n anihpj                 │
    │  kubectl get pods -n     │  istioctl x describe pod \                  │
    │    istio-system          │    <pod> -n anihpj                          │
    │  kubectl get pods 2/2    │  istioctl proxy-config route \              │
    │    -n anihpj             │    <pod> -n anihpj                          │
    ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  TRAFFIC MANAGEMENT CRDs │  SECURITY CRDs                              │
    │  Gateway — mesh entry    │  PeerAuthentication — mTLS mode             │
    │  VirtualService — routes │  AuthorizationPolicy — who can call         │
    │  DestinationRule — policy│  RequestAuthentication — JWT verify         │
    │  ServiceEntry — external │                                             │
    ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  OPEN DASHBOARDS         │  KEY CONCEPTS                               │
    │  istioctl dashboard kiali│  Sidecar = Envoy next to your app           │
    │  istioctl dashboard      │  Data Plane = all sidecars                  │
    │    grafana               │  Control Plane = istiod                     │
    │  istioctl dashboard      │  xDS = how istiod → Envoy                   │
    │    jaeger                │  iptables = traffic interception            │
    ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  mTLS MODES              │  CANARY PATTERN                             │
    │  PERMISSIVE — allow both │  1. Deploy v2 (replicas: 2)                 │
    │  STRICT — mTLS only      │  2. VS weight: v1=90%, v2=10%               │
    │  DISABLE — no TLS        │  3. Monitor Kiali / Grafana                 │
    │                          │  4. Gradually: 10→25→50→100%               │
    │  Apply: PeerAuth CRD     │  Rollback: set v2=0% instantly             │
    ├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  CIRCUIT BREAKER         │  RED METRICS (auto from Envoy)              │
    │  outlierDetection:       │  R = Rate (req/s)                           │
    │    consecutiveErrors: 5  │  E = Errors (% 4xx/5xx)                     │
    │    interval: 30s         │  D = Duration (latency p50/p95/p99)         │
    │    baseEjectionTime: 30s │  No code change needed!                     │
    └──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [DEPRECATED COMMAND WATCH — removed istioctl tools & modern equivalents]
    ┌──────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Removed / legacy command    │  Modern replacement (Istio 1.22+)       │
    ├──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  istioctl authn tls-check    │  istioctl x describe pod <pod>          │
    │  (removed years ago —        │  istioctl proxy-config secret <pod>     │
    │   ignore old guides using it)│  Kiali graph lock icons (mTLS view)     │
    │                              │  openssl s_client to a pod port         │
    └──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [ISTIO CRD SELECTION GUIDE — which resource solves which problem]
    ┌──────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  I want to...                    │  Use this Istio resource           │
    ├──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  Accept external HTTPS traffic   │  Gateway                           │
    │  Route by URL path               │  VirtualService (http.match.uri)   │
    │  Do a canary deployment          │  VirtualService (weight) +         │
    │                                  │  DestinationRule (subsets)         │
    │  Add retry logic                 │  VirtualService (retries)          │
    │  Add timeout                     │  VirtualService (timeout)          │
    │  Inject faults for testing       │  VirtualService (fault)            │
    │  Load balance requests           │  DestinationRule (loadBalancer)    │
    │  Add circuit breaker             │  DestinationRule (outlierDetection)│
    │  Encrypt service-to-service      │  PeerAuthentication (STRICT)       │
    │  Block a service from calling    │  AuthorizationPolicy (DENY)        │
    │  Validate JWT tokens             │  RequestAuthentication             │
    │  Call an external API            │  ServiceEntry                      │
    │  Audit all egress traffic        │  ServiceEntry + EgressGateway      │
    │  See service topology            │  Kiali dashboard                   │
    │  See request traces              │  Jaeger (istioctl dashboard jaeger)│
    │  See latency / error metrics     │  Grafana Istio dashboards          │
    │  Stop cascade failures           │  DestinationRule (outlierDetection)│
    │  Test resilience                 │  VirtualService (fault)            │
    │  Control resource usage          │  Sidecar (resource) + EnvoyFilter  │
    │  Add non-K8s workloads to mesh   │  WorkloadEntry                     │
    │  Run the mesh with no sidecars   │  Ambient mode: label namespace     │
    │  (ztunnel handles L4 + mTLS)     │  istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient   │
    │  Add L7 policy in ambient mode   │  Waypoint proxy per namespace      │
    │                                  │  (istioctl waypoint apply -n ns)   │
    │  Extend Envoy with custom code   │  WasmPlugin                        │
    │                                  │  (extensions.istio.io/v1alpha1)    │
    └──────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘


Appendix I: Full-Length ICA Mock Exam — 2-Hour Simulation
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    [HOW TO USE THIS APPENDIX — READ BEFORE YOU START THE CLOCK]
    - The REAL ICA exam is 2 hours, hands-on and performance-based — you
      work inside a LIVE Istio cluster typing real kubectl/istioctl
      commands and YAML, not answering multiple-choice questions. The
      official istio.io documentation is allowed open in another tab, so
      every task below is phrased the way the real exam phrases work:
      "configure this, verify it works" — not "which of the following..."
    - These are 25 FRESH scenarios built for this simulation — they reuse
      anihpj's services and the same commands taught throughout the
      guide, but they are NOT copies of the per-chapter Q&A blocks. Do
      not expect to recognize the exact wording from earlier chapters.
    - Timed self-practice rules:
      • Budget ~4-5 minutes per task (25 tasks ≈ 105-125 minutes,
        leaving 15-35 minutes to flag-review at the end — the same math
        the real exam rewards)
      • If you're stuck past 5 minutes: FLAG it, move on, come back with
        fresh eyes — this is mistake #2 from the Top 10 Mistakes panel
        near the front of this guide, and it costs candidates the most
      • Always verify after every change (kubectl get/describe,
        istioctl proxy-status, or a real curl) — an applied YAML that
        was never verified is an unfinished task on the real exam
      • Set your working namespace once and stop typing -n anihpj:
        kubectl config set-context --current --namespace=anihpj
    - Domain weighting below matches the OFFICIAL Linux Foundation ICA
      blueprint exactly (see the ICA EXAM OVERVIEW panel at the front of
      this guide) — NOT an even split across the 4 domains:

    ┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────┬────────────┐
    │ DOMAIN                         │ WEIGHT     │ TASKS      │
    ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────┤
    │ Installation, Upgrade & Config │ 20%        │ 5  (1-5)   │
    │ Traffic Management             │ 35%        │ 9  (6-14)  │
    │ Securing Workloads             │ 25%        │ 6  (15-20) │
    │ Troubleshooting                │ 20%        │ 5  (21-25) │
    ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────┤
    │ TOTAL                          │ 100%       │ 25 tasks   │
    └────────────────────────────────┴────────────┴────────────┘

    - Score yourself against the rubric at the end of this appendix, then
      route any weak domain back to the chapters listed there.

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     DOMAIN: INSTALLATION, UPGRADE & CONFIGURATION (20%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

    TASK 1: You have been handed a brand-new, empty Kubernetes cluster for
        anihpj. Install Istio with the demo profile, confirm every
        control-plane component is Running, then enable automatic sidecar
        injection for a new anihpj namespace. Prove injection actually worked
        before moving on.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  kubectl cluster-info && kubectl get nodes                          │
        │  istioctl install --set profile=demo -y                             │
        │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system   # istiod, gateways, addons Up   │
        │  kubectl create namespace anihpj                                    │
        │  kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled             │
        │  kubectl apply -f jobpost-api-deploy.yaml -n anihpj                 │
        │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj   # expect 2/2 READY, not 1/1           │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: profile=demo installs istiod plus both gateways plus
        Kiali/Jaeger/Prometheus/Grafana in one step; istio-injection=enabled
        is what makes the mutating webhook add the Envoy sidecar to every NEW
        pod — 2/2 READY is the proof, 1/1 means injection never fired.

    TASK 2: Company policy requires all cluster software to be installed as
        tracked, diffable Helm releases so Argo CD can manage Istio like
        everything else it runs. Install Istio's control plane and an ingress
        gateway using Helm ONLY — no istioctl install anywhere in the flow.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  helm repo add istio \                                              │
        │      https://istio-release.storage.googleapis.com/charts            │
        │  helm repo update                                                   │
        │  helm install istio-base istio/base -n istio-system \               │
        │      --create-namespace                                             │
        │  helm install istiod istio/istiod -n istio-system --wait            │
        │  helm install istio-ingress istio/gateway -n istio-system           │
        │  helm list -n istio-system   # STATUS: deployed, REVISION: 1        │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: the three charts must land in dependency order — base (CRDs) then
        istiod (control plane) then gateway (data plane) — installing out of
        order CrashLoops on "no matches for kind VirtualService"; each helm
        install becomes a versioned release Argo CD can diff and roll back,
        which istioctl install never creates.

    TASK 3: The platform team wants to pilot ambient mode on the low-traffic
        anihpj-canary namespace to cut sidecar RAM overhead before deciding
        whether to migrate the whole mesh. Install Istio in ambient mode and
        move anihpj-canary into it, then prove no pod there runs a sidecar.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl install --set profile=ambient -y                          │
        │  kubectl label namespace anihpj-canary \                            │
        │      istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient                                │
        │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system   # ztunnel DaemonSet, 1/node     │
        │  kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj-canary                │
        │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj-canary                                  │
        │  # expect 1/1 READY, NO istio-proxy container — correct by design   │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: ambient mode uses istio.io/dataplane-mode=ambient, NOT
        istio-injection=enabled — that label drives a completely different
        (sidecar) data plane. ztunnel supplies mTLS and L4 policy with zero
        per-pod proxy, so 1/1 READY here is success, not a bug.

    TASK 4: Load testing shows istiod falling behind during anihpj's Friday
        traffic-spike windows. Using the IstioOperator API (not ad-hoc --set
        flags), raise istiod's CPU/memory requests and enable 100% trace
        sampling for the debugging window, then apply the change.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: install.istio.io/v1alpha1                              │
        │  kind: IstioOperator                                                │
        │  metadata:                                                          │
        │    name: anihpj-istio                                               │
        │    namespace: istio-system                                          │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    profile: demo                                                    │
        │    meshConfig:                                                      │
        │      enableTracing: true                                            │
        │    components:                                                      │
        │      pilot:                                                         │
        │        k8s:                                                         │
        │          resources:                                                 │
        │            requests: {cpu: 1000m, memory: 4096Mi}                   │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  istioctl install -f operator.yaml -y                               │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: every istioctl --set flag is a shortcut into this same IstioOperator
        document — spec.components.pilot.k8s.resources is the durable,
        GitOps-friendly way to size istiod, and istioctl manifest generate -f
        operator.yaml lets you review the rendered YAML before it ever touches
        the cluster.

    TASK 5: anihpj runs three namespaces: anihpj, anihpj-staging,
        anihpj-batch. Upgrade ONLY anihpj-staging to Istio 1.23 as a burn-in
        canary while anihpj and anihpj-batch stay on 1.22 — with an instant,
        namespace-scoped rollback path if anything regresses.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl install --set revision=1-23 -y                            │
        │  kubectl label ns anihpj-staging istio-injection- \                 │
        │      istio.io/rev=1-23 --overwrite                                  │
        │  kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj-staging               │
        │  istioctl proxy-status | grep anihpj-staging   # SYNCED on 1.23     │
        │  # rollback if the burn-in regresses:                               │
        │  kubectl label ns anihpj-staging istio.io/rev=1-22 --overwrite      │
        │  kubectl rollout restart deployment -n anihpj-staging               │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: revisioned control planes run side by side, so migration is
        per-namespace and reversible with a relabel plus restart; anihpj and
        anihpj-batch never lose their control plane because 1-22's istiod
        keeps running untouched throughout the whole burn-in.

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     DOMAIN: TRAFFIC MANAGEMENT (35%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

    TASK 6: Expose two anihpj APIs on one public hostname over HTTPS:
        api.anihpj.com/jobs/* must reach jobpost-api:8000 and
        api.anihpj.com/users/* must reach user-service:8080. TLS terminates at
        the edge using an existing secret named anihpj-cert.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: Gateway                                                      │
        │  metadata: {name: anihpj-gateway, namespace: anihpj}                │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {istio: ingressgateway}                                │
        │    servers:                                                         │
        │    - port: {number: 443, name: https, protocol: HTTPS}              │
        │      tls: {mode: SIMPLE, credentialName: anihpj-cert}               │
        │      hosts: ["api.anihpj.com"]                                      │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata: {name: anihpj-routes, namespace: anihpj}                 │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: ["api.anihpj.com"]                                        │
        │    gateways: [anihpj-gateway]                                       │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - match: [{uri: {prefix: /jobs}}]                                │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination: {host: jobpost-api, port: {number: 8000}}       │
        │    - match: [{uri: {prefix: /users}}]                               │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination: {host: user-service, port: {number: 8080}}      │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: one Gateway terminates TLS once at the edge; the VirtualService's
        gateways: field binds it to that Gateway, and uri.prefix match rules
        fan the SAME hostname out to two backend Services by path — order
        matters, Envoy walks rules top-to-bottom, first match wins.

    TASK 7: skills-service:v2 fixes a scoring bug but is unproven under real
        load. Start it at exactly 10% of live traffic behind the existing v1,
        with an instant single-field rollback ready if the error rate spikes.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: DestinationRule                                              │
        │  metadata: {name: skills-service, namespace: anihpj}                │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    host: skills-service                                             │
        │    subsets:                                                         │
        │    - {name: v1, labels: {version: v1}}                              │
        │    - {name: v2, labels: {version: v2}}                              │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata: {name: skills-service, namespace: anihpj}                │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: [skills-service]                                          │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - route:                                                         │
        │      - destination: {host: skills-service, subset: v1}              │
        │        weight: 90                                                   │
        │      - destination: {host: skills-service, subset: v2}              │
        │        weight: 10                                                   │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  # rollback: weight v2 to 0, one JSON patch, ~2 seconds             │
        │  kubectl patch vs skills-service -n anihpj --type=json \            │
        │    -p='[{"op":"replace",                                            │
        │         "path":"/spec/http/0/route/1/weight","value":0}]'           │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: the split lives entirely in the VirtualService weight fields; the
        DestinationRule subsets MUST match the pods' version: v1/v2 labels or
        the split silently fails with "no healthy upstream" — rollback is a
        two-second YAML edit, no redeploy needed.

    TASK 8: notification-service occasionally hangs for 20-30 seconds when the
        upstream email provider degrades, and the struggling pods keep
        receiving new requests, backing up the whole queue. Eject a pod after
        5 consecutive 5xx errors for at least 30 seconds, but never eject more
        than half the pool at once.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: DestinationRule                                              │
        │  metadata: {name: notification-service, namespace: anihpj}          │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    host: notification-service                                       │
        │    trafficPolicy:                                                   │
        │      outlierDetection:                                              │
        │        consecutive5xxErrors: 5                                      │
        │        interval: 30s                                                │
        │        baseEjectionTime: 30s                                        │
        │        maxEjectionPercent: 50                                       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: consecutive5xxErrors trips the breaker, interval sets Envoy's scan
        cadence, baseEjectionTime is the first (doubling) ejection window, and
        maxEjectionPercent is the guard rail stopping the breaker from
        ejecting the whole pool during a correlated outage.

    TASK 9: jobpost-api now needs to call a third-party job board API at
        api.jobboard.io over HTTPS. The mesh runs in REGISTRY_ONLY mode, so
        the call currently fails. Register the host so the call succeeds —
        without switching the whole mesh back to ALLOW_ANY.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: ServiceEntry                                                 │
        │  metadata: {name: jobboard-api, namespace: anihpj}                  │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: ["api.jobboard.io"]                                       │
        │    ports: [{number: 443, name: https, protocol: HTTPS}]             │
        │    location: MESH_EXTERNAL                                          │
        │    resolution: DNS                                                  │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  # verify — was 502 BlackHoleCluster, now 200:                      │
        │  kubectl exec deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \                     │
        │    curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \                         │
        │    https://api.jobboard.io                                          │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: REGISTRY_ONLY black-holes any host not in the service registry; a
        ServiceEntry with location: MESH_EXTERNAL and resolution: DNS adds
        exactly one new Envoy cluster via xDS without reopening the gate to
        every other unregistered external host.

    TASK 10: skills-service calls location-service to resolve job coordinates.
        Under load, transient 503s spike. Add a 3-second total call budget, up
        to 2 retries only on 5xx, with each retry attempt capped so the
        retries can never blow past the outer timeout.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata: {name: location-service, namespace: anihpj}              │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: [location-service]                                        │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - route:                                                         │
        │      - destination: {host: location-service}                        │
        │      timeout: 3s                                                    │
        │      retries:                                                       │
        │        attempts: 2                                                  │
        │        perTryTimeout: 1s                                            │
        │        retryOn: 5xx                                                 │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: 3 tries (1 original plus 2 retries) times a 1s perTryTimeout = 3s,
        exactly matching the 3s outer timeout — so retries can never silently
        exceed the budget the caller was promised.

    TASK 11: Before a Black-Friday-scale traffic day, prove that jobpost-api's
        timeout/retry config actually protects it. Inject a 2-second delay on
        10% of calls from jobpost-api to auth-service, with no code changes to
        either service.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata: {name: auth-service, namespace: anihpj}                  │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: [auth-service]                                            │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - fault:                                                         │
        │        delay:                                                       │
        │          percentage: {value: 10}                                    │
        │          fixedDelay: 2s                                             │
        │      route:                                                         │
        │      - destination: {host: auth-service}                            │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: fault injection lives entirely in the VirtualService and only hits
        the matched percentage of calls; if jobpost-api's own timeout to
        auth-service is under 2s, this proves the timeout — not the delay
        itself — fires correctly. Remove the fault block once the test is
        done, it is not a permanent policy.

    TASK 12: anihpj now runs user-service pods split across us-east and
        us-west. When us-east's pods start failing, traffic should
        automatically fail over to us-west instead of continuing to hammer the
        unhealthy region.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: DestinationRule                                              │
        │  metadata: {name: user-service, namespace: anihpj}                  │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    host: user-service                                               │
        │    trafficPolicy:                                                   │
        │      loadBalancer:                                                  │
        │        localityLbSetting: {enabled: true}                           │
        │      outlierDetection:                                              │
        │        consecutive5xxErrors: 5                                      │
        │        interval: 30s                                                │
        │        baseEjectionTime: 30s                                        │
        │        maxEjectionPercent: 50                                       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: localityLbSetting alone only PREFERS same-locality pods — it does not
        fail over by itself. outlierDetection is what actually ejects the
        unhealthy us-east endpoints, so LEAST_REQUEST load balancing has no
        remaining choice but healthy us-west pods.

    TASK 13: location-service:v2 rewrites the geocoding engine. Validate it
        against 100% of real production traffic without it affecting a single
        real user — v2's responses must never reach a real client.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: VirtualService                                               │
        │  metadata: {name: location-service, namespace: anihpj}              │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    hosts: [location-service]                                        │
        │    http:                                                            │
        │    - route:                                                         │
        │      - destination: {host: location-service, subset: v1}            │
        │        weight: 100                                                  │
        │      mirror: {host: location-service, subset: v2}                   │
        │      mirrorPercentage: {value: 100.0}                               │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: mirror sends an async copy of every request to v2 and discards its
        response — v1 alone answers the real caller, so v2 sees full
        production load with zero user-facing risk. Watch v2's own error rate
        and latency in Grafana before ever giving it real weight.

    TASK 14: user-service keeps Django session state in-memory per pod during
        a migration window. Until that's fixed, route each user consistently
        to the SAME pod based on their x-user-id header, instead of the mesh's
        default load-balancing algorithm.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: DestinationRule                                              │
        │  metadata: {name: user-service, namespace: anihpj}                  │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    host: user-service                                               │
        │    trafficPolicy:                                                   │
        │      loadBalancer:                                                  │
        │        consistentHash:                                              │
        │          httpHeaderName: x-user-id                                  │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: consistentHash replaces the default LEAST_REQUEST algorithm with
        sticky routing keyed on a header — the same x-user-id always hashes to
        the same pod (until the pool changes), a stopgap until sessions move
        to a shared store.

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     DOMAIN: SECURING WORKLOADS (25%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

    TASK 15: Lock down location-service so the ONLY caller allowed to reach it
        is jobpost-api, and only with GET requests. Everything else —
        including every other anihpj service — must be denied by default.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                          │
        │  metadata: {name: deny-all, namespace: anihpj}                      │
        │  spec: {}   # empty spec = deny everything in the namespace         │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                          │
        │  metadata: {name: allow-jobpost-to-location, namespace: anihpj}     │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {matchLabels: {app: location-service}}                 │
        │    action: ALLOW                                                    │
        │    rules:                                                           │
        │    - from:                                                          │
        │      - source:                                                      │
        │          principals:                                                │
        │          - "cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api"                 │
        │      to:                                                            │
        │      - operation: {methods: ["GET"]}                                │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: an empty-spec AuthorizationPolicy denies everything it targets; once
        ANY policy targets a workload, every request not explicitly ALLOWED is
        denied — apply the ALLOW policy first and deny-all last (or dry-run
        it) to avoid a self-inflicted outage.

    TASK 16: Only requests carrying a valid JWT issued by auth-service, with
        role admin or recruiter, may reach jobpost-api's /api/admin/* paths.
        Requests with NO token at all must also be rejected, not silently
        passed through.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: RequestAuthentication                                        │
        │  metadata: {name: jwt-auth, namespace: anihpj}                      │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {matchLabels: {app: jobpost-api}}                      │
        │    jwtRules:                                                        │
        │    - issuer: "https://auth.anihpj.com"                              │
        │      jwksUri: "https://auth.anihpj.com/.well-known/jwks.json"       │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                          │
        │  metadata: {name: require-admin-jwt, namespace: anihpj}             │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {matchLabels: {app: jobpost-api}}                      │
        │    action: ALLOW                                                    │
        │    rules:                                                           │
        │    - from:                                                          │
        │      - source:                                                      │
        │          requestPrincipals: ["https://auth.anihpj.com/*"]           │
        │      to:                                                            │
        │      - operation: {paths: ["/api/admin/*"]}                         │
        │      when:                                                          │
        │      - key: request.auth.claims[role]                               │
        │        values: ["admin", "recruiter"]                               │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: RequestAuthentication only rejects tokens that ARE presented but
        broken (401) — it never blocks a MISSING token. Only an
        AuthorizationPolicy demanding requestPrincipals turns "no token" into
        a 403, and the when: clause enforces the role claim.

    TASK 17: Encrypt all service-to-service traffic inside anihpj. Enforce
        STRICT mTLS for the whole namespace and prove it is active on
        jobpost-api specifically, using a current (non-removed) istioctl
        command.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: PeerAuthentication                                           │
        │  metadata: {name: default, namespace: anihpj}                       │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    mtls: {mode: STRICT}                                             │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  istioctl x describe pod deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj               │
        │  # shows the effective PeerAuthentication mode for the pod          │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: a PeerAuthentication named "default" with no selector applies to
        every workload in that namespace; STRICT then rejects any plaintext
        caller at the TCP layer (connection reset, never an HTTP code).
        istioctl x describe pod is the modern check — the old istioctl authn
        tls-check was removed.

    TASK 18: A recruiting partner needs API access to jobpost-api over HTTPS,
        but anihpj security requires the partner to present their OWN client
        certificate at the edge — anonymous HTTPS is not acceptable for this
        endpoint.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1                                 │
        │  kind: Gateway                                                      │
        │  metadata: {name: partner-gateway, namespace: anihpj}               │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {istio: ingressgateway}                                │
        │    servers:                                                         │
        │    - port: {number: 443, name: https-partner, protocol: HTTPS}      │
        │      tls: {mode: MUTUAL, credentialName: partner-mtls-cert}         │
        │      hosts: ["partner.anihpj.com"]                                  │
        ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
        │  kubectl create secret generic partner-mtls-cert \                  │
        │    -n istio-system \                                                │
        │    --from-file=tls.crt --from-file=tls.key --from-file=ca.crt       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: MUTUAL is the only edge TLS mode that demands a client certificate.
        The secret needs a THIRD key beyond SIMPLE's usual pair — ca.crt, the
        CA that signed the partner's client cert — forget it and every partner
        handshake fails before any HTTP request happens.

    TASK 19: require-jwt now guards every path on skills-service, but
        kubelet's liveness probe carries no JWT and the pods are
        restart-looping. Exempt ONLY /healthz from the JWT requirement — every
        other path must still demand a valid token.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: AuthorizationPolicy                                          │
        │  metadata: {name: require-jwt, namespace: anihpj}                   │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {matchLabels: {app: skills-service}}                   │
        │    action: ALLOW                                                    │
        │    rules:                                                           │
        │    - from:                                                          │
        │      - source:                                                      │
        │          requestPrincipals: ["https://auth.anihpj.com/*"]           │
        │      to:                                                            │
        │      - operation: {notPaths: ["/healthz"]}                          │
        │    - to:                                                            │
        │      - operation: {paths: ["/healthz"]}                             │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: notPaths carves /healthz out of the JWT-required rule, and a second
        rule explicitly ALLOWs /healthz with no from: block at all — rules
        inside one policy are OR'd, so the probe matches rule 2 while every
        other path still needs rule 1's valid JWT.

    TASK 20: anihpj is moving to STRICT mTLS namespace-wide (see the previous
        task), but a legacy nightly batch job (app: legacy-batch) has no
        sidecar yet and cannot present a client certificate. Keep the rest of
        the namespace STRICT while this ONE workload still accepts plaintext
        during the migration.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1                                   │
        │  kind: PeerAuthentication                                           │
        │  metadata: {name: legacy-batch-exempt, namespace: anihpj}           │
        │  spec:                                                              │
        │    selector: {matchLabels: {app: legacy-batch}}                     │
        │    mtls: {mode: PERMISSIVE}                                         │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: PeerAuthentication resolves most-specific-wins: a WORKLOAD-selector
        policy overrides the namespace-wide "default" STRICT policy for just
        that one app label, without touching the blanket rule every other
        anihpj service still follows.

    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     DOMAIN: TROUBLESHOOTING (20%)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

    TASK 21: istioctl proxy-status suddenly shows 40 of anihpj's 42 proxies as
        STALE at the same moment, across every service at once. Determine
        whether this is a control-plane or a data-plane problem, and find the
        root cause.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl proxy-status                                              │
        │  # MANY rows STALE together, not one odd row -> control plane       │
        │  kubectl get pods -n istio-system -l app=istiod                     │
        │  kubectl top pod -n istio-system                                    │
        │  kubectl logs -l app=istiod -n istio-system --tail=100 \            │
        │    | grep -i -e error -e push                                       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: many proxies going STALE together, mesh-wide, is the signature of a
        control-plane problem — istiod itself is unhealthy or overloaded and
        stopped pushing xDS updates. A single odd pod would instead point at
        that one Envoy (data plane). Fix by restoring istiod capacity, not by
        touching individual app pods.

    TASK 22: Minutes after applying STRICT mTLS for anihpj, jobpost-api's
        calls to user-service start failing with "Recv failure: Connection
        reset by peer" instead of any HTTP status code. Diagnose and fix it.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl x describe pod deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj               │
        │  kubectl get destinationrules -n anihpj -o yaml \                   │
        │    | grep -B5 "mode: DISABLE"                                       │
        │  # fix — remove the stale tls block, or set ISTIO_MUTUAL:           │
        │  kubectl patch destinationrule user-service -n anihpj \             │
        │    --type merge \                                                   │
        │    -p '{"spec":{"trafficPolicy":{"tls":                             │
        │         {"mode":"ISTIO_MUTUAL"}}}}'                                 │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: "connection reset by peer" with no HTTP code at all means STRICT
        rejected plaintext at the TCP layer. The classic cause is a
        client-side DestinationRule left over from before STRICT with
        tls.mode: DISABLE — clearing the tls block (or ISTIO_MUTUAL) lets
        auto-mTLS take over and match the server's STRICT PA.

    TASK 23: You applied a VirtualService splitting jobpost-api traffic 80/20
        between v1 and v2. It applies with no errors, but 100% of traffic
        keeps going to v1. Find out why.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl analyze -n anihpj                                         │
        │  kubectl get destinationrule jobpost-api -n anihpj -o yaml          │
        │  # check subsets vs the pods' actual labels:                        │
        │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj -l app=jobpost-api --show-labels        │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: istioctl analyze checks syntax and cross-references, not routing
        intent, so a VirtualService that "applies cleanly" can still be wrong.
        The #1 cause of a stuck split is a DestinationRule subset whose labels
        (version: v2) do not match the actual Pod labels — the subset selector
        and the Pod labels must agree exactly.

    TASK 24: A teammate's pull request added a VirtualService with a typo'd
        host and it merged straight into production. Add a pipeline gate so a
        broken Istio manifest can never merge again.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  istioctl analyze -f istio/ --use-kube=false   # pure lint          │
        │  istioctl analyze -f istio/ -n staging   # cross-check vs live      │
        │  # non-zero exit code fails the CI step and blocks the PR           │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: analyze exits non-zero the instant it finds an error — exactly the
        signal a CI pipeline step needs to fail the build and block the merge
        before broken config ever reaches a real cluster.

    TASK 25: You scaled jobpost-api from 3 to 4 replicas. The new pod shows
        1/1 READY while the other three show 2/2, and traffic never reaches it
        through Istio routing. The namespace already has
        istio-injection=enabled. Diagnose and fix it.

    SOLUTION:
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj -l app=jobpost-api                      │
        │  # spot the 1/1 pod among the 2/2 pods                              │
        │  kubectl describe pod <new-pod> -n anihpj | grep -A3 Events         │
        │  kubectl rollout restart deployment jobpost-api -n anihpj           │
        │  kubectl get pods -n anihpj -l app=jobpost-api   # now all 2/2      │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WHY: sidecar injection happens ONLY through the mutating webhook at pod
        CREATE time; a pod created while the webhook was momentarily
        unavailable can come up without a sidecar even with a correct
        namespace label. A rollout restart recreates the pod through the
        webhook — a single-pod data-plane issue, not a control-plane one.
